## Craig Kennedy’s Theories / 克雷格·肯尼迪的理论

===Original===
“It has always seemed strange to me that no one has ever endowed a professorship in criminal science in any of our large universities.”

Craig Kennedy laid down his evening paper and filled his pipe with my tobacco. In college we had roomed together, had shared everything, even poverty, and now that Craig was a professor of chemistry and I was on the staff of the *Star*, we had continued the arrangement. Prosperity found us in a rather neat bachelor apartment on the Heights, not far from the University.

“Why should there be a chair in criminal science?” I remarked argumentatively, settling back in my chair. “I’ve done my turn at police headquarters reporting, and I can tell you, Craig, it’s no place for a college professor. Crime is just crime. And as for dealing with it, the good detective is born and bred to it. College professors for the sociology of the thing, yes; for the detection of it, give me a Byrnes.”

“On the contrary,” replied Kennedy, his clean-cut features betraying an earnestness which I knew indicated that he was leading up to something important, “there is a distinct place for science in the detection of crime. On the Continent they are far in advance of us in that respect. We are mere children beside a dozen crime-specialists in Paris, whom I could name.”

“Yes, but where does the college professor come in?” I asked, rather doubtfully.

“You must remember, Walter,” he pursued, warming up to his subject, “that it’s only within the last ten years or so that we have had the really practical college professor who could do it. The silk-stockinged variety is out of date now. Today it is the college professor who is the third arbitrator in labour disputes, who reforms our currency, who heads our tariff commissions, and conserves our farms and forests. We have professors of everything﻿—why not professors of crime?”

===Chinese===
「在我看来，我们那些名牌大学里居然从来没人捐设一个刑事科学的教授席位，这实在奇怪。」

克雷格·肯尼迪放下晚报，用我的烟草装满烟斗。大学时代我们同住一室，同甘共苦，连穷日子也一起熬过；如今克雷格当了化学教授，我进了《明星报》（*Star*）做事，这个安排也就延续了下来。境况好转后，我们在离大学不远的高地合租了一套相当整洁的单身公寓。

「为什么要有刑事科学的教授席位？」我不服气地顶了一句，往椅背上一靠。「我在警察总局跑过轮班报道，我可以告诉你，克雷格，那地方可不是大学教授待的。犯罪就是犯罪。要说对付它，好侦探是天生的、从小磨出来的。研究犯罪的社会学，交给教授们没问题；至于侦破犯罪，还是给我一个伯恩斯（Byrnes）那样的人。」

「恰恰相反，」肯尼迪答道。他轮廓分明的脸上流露出一种认真劲儿——我知道这预示着他要引出什么要紧事了——「科学在犯罪侦破中自有一席明确的地位。在这方面，欧洲大陆远走在我们前面。比起巴黎那十来位我能叫得出名字的犯罪专家，我们不过是小孩子。」

「不错，可大学教授又能派上什么用场？」我将信将疑地问。

「你得记住，沃尔特，」他越说越起劲，「真正能派上用场的实干型大学教授，不过是最近十年左右才有的。那种穿丝绸长袜的老派先生早已过时。如今充当劳资纠纷第三方仲裁人的是大学教授，改革币制的是大学教授，领导关税委员会的是大学教授，保护农田森林的也是大学教授。我们什么都有教授——为什么就没有犯罪教授呢？」

===Original===
Still, as I shook my head dubiously, he hurried on to clinch his point. “Colleges have gone a long way from the old ideal of pure culture. They have got down to solving the hard facts of life﻿—pretty nearly all, except one. They still treat crime in the old way, study its statistics and pore over its causes and the theories of how it can be prevented. But as for running the criminal himself down, scientifically, relentlessly﻿—bah! we haven’t made an inch of progress since the hammer and tongs method of your Byrnes.”

“Doubtless you will write a thesis on this most interesting subject,” I suggested, “and let it go at that.”

“No, I am serious,” he replied, determined for some reason or other to make a convert of me. “I mean exactly what I say. I am going to apply science to the detection of crime, the same sort of methods by which you trace out the presence of a chemical, or run an unknown germ to earth. And before I have gone far, I am going to enlist Walter Jameson as an aide. I think I shall need you in my business.”

“How do I come in?”

“Well, for one thing, you will get a scoop, a beat﻿—whatever you call it in that newspaper jargon of yours.”

I smiled in a skeptical way, such as newspapermen are wont to affect toward a thing until it is done﻿—after which we make a wild scramble to exploit it.

Nothing more on the subject passed between us for several days.

===Chinese===
见我仍将信将疑地摇头，他赶紧趁热打铁。「大学早已远离纯教养的旧理想，转而下力气解决生活中的种种难题——差不多样样都解决了，只除了一样。对犯罪，大学仍沿用老一套：研究统计数字，钻研犯罪成因，推敲预防犯罪的各种理论。可要说科学地、毫不留情地亲手把罪犯追缉归案——呸！自从你那位伯恩斯大刀阔斧的老办法以来，我们就没有前进过一寸。」

「不用说，你会就这个顶有趣的题目写一篇论文，」我打趣道，「写完也就罢了。」

「不，我是认真的，」他答道，不知怎么铁了心要把我争取过去。「我说到做到。我要把科学应用于犯罪侦破，用的正是你追踪一种化学物质、把一种未知细菌查个水落石出的那套方法。不用多久，我还要拉沃尔特·詹姆森入伙当帮手。我看我这一行少不了你。」

「那我能帮上什么忙？」

「嗯，头一条，你能捞到独家新闻，抢个头条——用你们报馆那套行话说，管它叫什么来着。」

我报以新闻人惯有的那种怀疑的微笑——事情没办成之前我们总摆出这副面孔，而一旦办成，便又争先恐后地大写特写。

此后好几天，我们再没谈起过这个话题。

## “Spontaneous Combustion” / 人体自燃

===Original===
Kennedy and I had risen early, for we were hustling to get off for a weekend at Atlantic City. Kennedy was tugging at the straps of his grip and remonstrating with it under his breath, when the door opened and a messenger-boy stuck his head in.

“Does Mr. Kennedy live here?” he asked.

===Chinese===
我和肯尼迪一早便起了床，正忙忙叨叨地收拾行装，准备去大西洋城度周末。肯尼迪一边使劲拽着旅行提包（grip）的搭扣带子，一边低声跟它较劲，正在这时，房门开了，一个送电报的男孩探进头来。

「肯尼迪先生住在这儿吗？」他问。

===Original===
Craig impatiently seized the pencil, signed his name in the book, and tore open a night letter. From the prolonged silence that followed I felt a sense of misgiving. I, at least, had set my heart on the Atlantic City outing, but with the appearance of the messenger-boy I intuitively felt that the boardwalk would not see us that week.

“I’m afraid the Atlantic City trip is off, Walter,” remarked Craig seriously. “You remember Tom Langley in our class at the university? Well, read that.”

===Chinese===
克雷格不耐烦地抓过铅笔，在签收簿上签了名，撕开一封夜间电报（night letter）。随后那阵久久的沉默，让我心里升起一股不安。至少我自己是一心盼着大西洋城之行的，可送信男孩一露面，我就直觉地感到，那个星期木板路（boardwalk）上是见不着我们俩了。

「恐怕大西洋城之行要告吹了，沃尔特，」克雷格神色郑重地说。「你还记得咱们大学同班的汤姆·兰利（Tom Langley）吧？喏，你自己看。」

===Original===
I laid down my safety razor and took the message. Tom had not spared words, and I could see at a glance at the mere length of the thing that it must be important. It was from Camp Hangout in the Adirondacks.

“Dear old K.,” it began, “regardless of expense, can you arrange to come up here by next train after you receive this? Uncle Lewis is dead. Most mysterious. Last night after we retired noticed peculiar odour about house. Didn’t pay much attention. This morning found him lying on floor of living-room, head and chest literally burned to ashes, but lower part of body and arms untouched. Room shows no evidence of fire, but full of sort of oily soot. Otherwise nothing unusual. On table near body siphon of seltzer, bottle of imported limes, and glass for rickeys. Have removed body, but am keeping room exactly as found until you arrive. Bring Jameson. Wire if you cannot come, but make every effort and spare no expense. Anxiously, Tom Langley.”

===Chinese===
我放下安全剃刀，接过电文。汤姆不惜笔墨，单看那长度我便一眼断定必有要事。电报是从阿迪朗达克山脉的逍遥营地（Camp Hangout）发来的。

电文开头写道：「亲爱的老肯：不计花费，你能否在收到此电后搭下一班火车赶来？刘易斯叔父死了。死得极为离奇。昨晚我们就寝后，察觉屋里有一股怪味。没太在意。今晨发现他躺在客厅地板上，头部和胸口烧成了灰，但身体下半部和双臂完好无损。房间不见失火痕迹，却满是油性的烟灰。其余一切如常。遗体旁的桌上有一瓶塞尔查苏打水（seltzer）的虹吸壶、一瓶进口青柠，还有一只调里基酒（rickeys）用的杯子。遗体已移走，但房间保持发现时的原样，等你到来。带詹姆森同来。若实在不能来请拍电报，但务必竭尽全力，不惜花费。心急如焚。汤姆·兰利。」

===Original===
Craig was impatiently looking at his watch as I hastily ran through the letter.

“Hurry, Walter,” he exclaimed. “We can just catch the Empire State. Never mind shaving﻿—we’ll have a stopover at Utica to wait for the Montreal express. Here, put the rest of your things in your grip and jam it shut. We’ll get something to eat on the train﻿—I hope. I’ll wire we’re coming. Don’t forget to latch the door.”

Kennedy was already halfway to the elevator, and I followed ruefully, still thinking of the ocean and the piers, the bands and the roller chairs.

===Chinese===
我匆匆浏览电文时，克雷格已经不耐烦地看起了表。

「快点，沃尔特，」他嚷道。「咱们还赶得上『帝国州号』（Empire State）快车。胡子别刮了——反正要在尤蒂卡（Utica）中途停留，等蒙特利尔快车。来，把剩下的东西塞进提包，使劲扣上。吃的东西到车上再想办法——但愿有卖的。我这就拍电报说我们动身了。别忘了把门锁好。」

肯尼迪已经走到了电梯半道，我怏怏地跟在后面，心里还惦念着大海和码头、乐队和滚轮椅（roller chairs）。

===Original===
It was a good ten-hour journey up to the little station nearest Camp Hangout and at least a two hour ride after that. We had plenty of time to reflect over what this death might mean to Tom and his sister and to speculate on the manner of it. Tom and Grace Langley were relatives by marriage of Lewis Langley, who, after the death of his wife, had made them his protégés. Lewis Langley was principally noted, as far as I could recall, for being a member of some of the fastest clubs of both New York and London. Neither Kennedy nor myself had shared in the world’s opinion of him, for we knew how good he had been to Tom in college and, from Tom, how good he had been to Grace. In fact, he had made Tom assume the Langley name, and in every way had treated the brother and sister as if they had been his own children.

===Chinese===
到离逍遥营地最近的那个小站，足足要坐十个钟头的火车，下车后至少还得再乘两个钟头的车。我们有大把时间来琢磨这场死亡对汤姆和他妹妹意味着什么，也足以对死亡的方式作种种推测。汤姆和格蕾丝·兰利（Grace Langley）本是刘易斯·兰利（Lewis Langley）的姻亲，刘易斯丧妻之后，便把兄妹俩收在了自己翼下。就我记得的来说，刘易斯·兰利的出名之处，主要在于他是纽约和伦敦两地几个最放浪的俱乐部的会员。我和肯尼迪却都不苟同世人对他的看法，因为我们知道他在大学时代待汤姆有多好，又从汤姆口中得知他待格蕾丝有多好。事实上，他让汤姆改姓了兰利，处处都把这对兄妹当亲生儿女看待。

===Original===
Tom met us with a smart trap at the station, a sufficient indication, if we had not already known, of the “roughing it” at such a luxurious Adirondack “camp” as Camp Hangout. He was unaffectedly glad to see us, and it was not difficult to read in his face the worry which the affair had already given him.

“Tom; I’m awfully sorry to﻿—” began Craig when, warned by Langley’s look at the curious crowd that always gathers at the railroad station at train time, he cut it short. We stood silently a moment while Tom was arranging the trap for us.

===Chinese===
汤姆驾着一辆漂亮的轻便马车（trap）到车站接我们——就算我们原本不知道，这也足以说明，在逍遥营地这样奢华的阿迪朗达克「营地」里，所谓「吃苦露营」（“roughing it”）是怎么一回事。他见到我们是发自真心的高兴，而他脸上那副已经被这事压出来的忧色，也不难读出来。

「汤姆，我实在太难过了——」克雷格刚一开口，瞥见兰利朝那群每逢火车到站必聚到车站上来的好奇看客递了个眼色，便把话咽了回去。我们默默站了一会儿，等汤姆替我们安顿马车。

===Original===
As we swung around the bend in the road that cut off the little station and its crowd of lookers-on, Kennedy was the first to speak. “Tom,” he said, “first of all, let me ask that when we get to the camp we are to be simply two old classmates whom you had asked to spend a few days before the tragedy occurred. Anything will do. There may be nothing at all to your evident suspicions, and then again there may. At any rate, play the game safely﻿—don’t arouse any feeling which might cause unpleasantness later in case you are mistaken.”

===Chinese===
马车转过那道把小车站和围观人群一并遮断的弯路之后，肯尼迪先开了口。「汤姆，」他说，「首先我得跟你讲好：到了营地，我们只是你早在惨剧发生之前就请来小住几日的两个老同学。随便怎么说都行。你心里那点明显的疑心，也许根本就是子虚乌有，也许又确有其事。无论如何，要稳着来——别激起什么情绪，免得日后万一你猜错了，落得大家不痛快。」

===Original===
“I quite agree with you,” answered Tom. “You wired, from Albany, I think, to keep the facts out of the papers as much as possible. I’m afraid it is too late for that. Of course the thing became vaguely known in Saranac, although the county officers have been very considerate of us, and this morning a New York *Record* correspondent was over and talked with us. I couldn’t refuse, that would have put a very bad face on it.”

===Chinese===
「我完全同意，」汤姆答道。「你从奥尔巴尼（Albany）拍来的电报——我记得是——叮嘱尽量别让事情见报。恐怕为时已晚。这事当然已经在萨拉纳克（Saranac）传得隐隐约约，好在县里的警官们待我们非常体谅，可今天早上，纽约《纪事报》（*Record*）的一个记者跑了来，找我们聊了聊。我总不能拒人千里——那样反倒把事情弄得更难看。」

===Original===
“Too bad,” I exclaimed. “I had hoped, at least, to be able to keep the report down to a few lines in the *Star*. But the *Record* will have such a yellow story about it that I’ll simply have to do something to counteract the effect.”

“Yes,” assented Craig. “But﻿—wait. Let’s see the *Record* story first. The office doesn’t know you’re up here. You can hold up the *Star* and give us time to look things over, perhaps get in a beat on the real story and set things right. Anyhow, the news is out. That’s certain. We must work quickly. Tell me, Tom, who are at the camp﻿—anyone except relatives?”

===Chinese===
「糟了，」我脱口而出。「我本来还指望，至少能把报道压到《明星报》（*Star*）上寥寥几行。可《纪事报》准会拿它做一篇耸人听闻的黄色报道，我只好多做点什么，好抵消它的影响。」

「是啊，」克雷格附和道。「不过——先等等。咱们先瞧瞧《纪事报》怎么写的。报馆还不知道你到了这儿。你可以把《明星报》那头压一压，给我们留点查看的时间，说不定还能在真相上抢个独家，把事情扳正。不管怎么说，消息已经传出去了，这是板上钉钉的。我们必须抓紧。告诉我，汤姆，营地里都有些什么人——除了亲戚，还有外人吗？」

===Original===
“No,” he replied, guardedly measuring his words. “Uncle Lewis had invited his brother James and his niece and nephew, Isabelle and James, junior﻿—we call him Junior. Then there are Grace and myself and a distant relative, Harrington Brown, and﻿—oh, of course, uncle’s physician, Doctor Putnam.”

“Who is Harrington Brown?” asked Craig.

===Chinese===
「没有，」他答道，字斟句酌。「刘易斯叔父请了他弟弟詹姆斯（James）、侄女伊莎贝尔（Isabelle）和侄子小詹姆斯（James, junior）——我们都管他叫『小詹』（Junior）。再有就是格蕾丝和我，还有一位远亲，哈林顿·布朗（Harrington Brown），另外——哦，当然，还有叔父的医生，帕特南医生（Doctor Putnam）。」

「哈林顿·布朗是什么人？」克雷格问。

===Original===
“He’s on the other side of the Langley family, on Uncle Lewis’s mother’s side. I think, or at least Grace thinks, that he is quite in love with Isabelle. Harrington Brown would be quite a catch. Of course he isn’t wealthy, but his family is mighty well connected. Oh, Craig,” sighed Langley, “I wish he hadn’t done it﻿—Uncle Lewis, I mean. Why did he invite his brother up here now when he needed to recover from the swift pace of last winter in New York? You know﻿—or you don’t know, I suppose, but you’ll know it now﻿—when he and Uncle Jim got together there was nothing to it but one drink after another. Doctor Putnam was quite disgusted, at least he professed to be, but, Craig,” he lowered his voice to a whisper, as if the very forest had ears, “they’re all alike﻿—they’ve been just waiting for Uncle Lewis to drink himself to death. Oh,” he added bitterly, “there’s no love lost between me and the relatives on that score, I can assure you.”

===Chinese===
「他算是兰利家的另一支，是刘易斯叔父母亲那边的亲戚。我看得出来——至少格蕾丝这么认为——他已经深深恋上了伊莎贝尔。哈林顿·布朗可是个抢手的乘龙快婿。他本人算不上有钱，可他家的人脉极广。唉，克雷格，」兰利叹了口气，「我真希望他没有这么办——我是说刘易斯叔父。他自己正需要从去年冬天纽约那种快马加鞭的挥霍日子里缓过来，为什么偏偏这时候把他弟弟请上山来？你是知道的——不，恐怕你不知道，往后就知道了——他跟吉姆叔叔凑到一块儿，就只剩下一杯接一杯地灌。帕特南医生厌恶得很——至少嘴上说得厌恶。可是克雷格，」他把声音压成耳语，仿佛这林子里都长着耳朵，「他们全是一路货——他们就巴望着刘易斯叔父把自己喝死。唉，」他又咬牙切齿地补了一句，「在这一点上，我跟那帮亲戚之间谈不上半点情分，这你可以放心。」

===Original===
“How did you find him that morning?” asked Kennedy, as if to turn off this unlocking of family secrets to strangers.

===Chinese===
「那天早晨你是怎么发现他的？」肯尼迪问道，像是存心把这场向外人开启家门秘辛的话头岔开。

===Original===
“That’s the worst part of the whole affair,” replied Tom, and even in the dusk I could see the lines of his face tighten. “You know Uncle Lewis was a hard drinker, but he never seemed to show it much. We had been out on the lake in the motorboat fishing all the afternoon and﻿—well, I must admit both my uncles had had frequent recourse to ‘pocket pistols,’ and I remember they referred to it each time as ‘bait.’ Then after supper nothing would do but fizzes and rickeys. I was disgusted, and after reading a bit went to bed. Harrington and my uncles sat up with Doctor Putnam﻿—according to Uncle Jim﻿—for a couple of hours longer. Then Harrington, Doctor Putnam, and Uncle Jim went to bed, leaving Uncle Lewis still drinking. I remember waking in the night, and the house seemed saturated with a peculiar odour. I never smelt anything like it in my life. So I got up and slipped into my bathrobe. I met Grace in the hall. She was sniffing.

“ ‘Don’t you smell something burning?’ she asked.

“I said I did and started downstairs to investigate. Everything was dark, but that smell was all over the house. I looked in each room downstairs as I went, but could see nothing. The kitchen and dining-room were all right. I glanced into the living-room, but, while the smell was more noticeable there, I could see no evidence of a fire except the dying embers on the hearth. It had been coolish that night, and we had had a few logs blazing. I didn’t examine the room﻿—there seemed no reason for it. We went back to our rooms, and in the morning they found the gruesome object I had missed in the darkness and shadows of the living-room.”

===Chinese===
「整件事里数这段最惨，」汤姆答道。即便在暮色里，我也看得出他脸上的线条绷紧了。「你们知道，刘易斯叔父是个贪杯的人，可表面上看不大出来。那天下午我们一直坐摩托艇在湖上钓鱼，呃——我得承认，两位叔父频频求助于『口袋小酒壶』（‘pocket pistols’），而且我记得他们每一回都管它叫『鱼饵』（‘bait’）。晚饭后更是非喝菲兹酒（fizzes）和里基酒不可。我看得腻烦，看了会儿书就上床了。哈林顿陪我两位叔父、还有帕特南医生又坐了——照吉姆叔叔的说法——两个来钟头。随后哈林顿、帕特南医生和吉姆叔叔也都去睡了，只剩刘易斯叔父一个人还在喝。我记得半夜里醒来，整座房子似乎都被一股怪味浸透了。我这辈子从没闻过那样的味道。于是我起身披上浴袍，在过道里碰见了格蕾丝。她正仰着鼻子嗅。

「『你没闻到什么东西烧焦了吗？』她问。

「我说闻到了，便下楼去查看。到处一片漆黑，那股味道却弥漫了整座房子。我一路把楼下的每间屋子都看了一眼，什么也没发现。厨房和餐厅都好好的。我朝客厅里扫了一眼，虽然那股味儿在客厅里更重，可除了壁炉里将熄的余烬，看不出半点着过火的痕迹。那晚有点凉，我们生了几块木柴取暖。我没细看那屋子——似乎也没有理由细看。我们各自回房睡了，到了早晨，他们就在那间客厅里，发现了我黑夜里在暗影中漏看的那个骇人之物。」

===Original===
Kennedy was intently listening. “Who found him?” he asked.

“Harrington,” replied Tom. “He roused us. Harrington’s theory is that uncle set himself on fire with a spark from his cigar﻿—a charred cigar butt was found on the floor.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪听得全神贯注。「是谁发现他的？」他问。

「哈林顿，」汤姆答道。「是他把我们叫醒的。哈林顿的看法是，叔父被雪茄上的火星点着了自己——地板上确实找到一支烧焦的雪茄烟蒂。」

===Original===
We found Tom’s relatives a saddened, silent party in the face of the tragedy. Kennedy and I apologised very profusely for our intrusion, but Tom quickly interrupted, as we had agreed, by explaining that he had insisted on our coming, as old friends on whom he felt he could rely, especially to set the matter right in the newspapers.

I think Craig noticed keenly the reticence of the family group in the mystery﻿—I might almost have called it suspicion. They did not seem to know just whether to take it as an accident or as something worse, and each seemed to entertain a reserve toward the rest which was very uncomfortable.

Mr. Langley’s attorney in New York had been notified, but apparently was out of town, for he had not been heard from. They seemed rather anxious to get word from him.

===Chinese===
我们见到汤姆的亲属们时，这一群人正面对惨剧黯然神伤、默不作声。我和肯尼迪为我们的唐突到访再三致歉，但汤姆按照事先的约定赶紧接过话头，解释说是他执意请我们来的——我们是他觉得靠得住的老朋友，尤其是可以帮他在报纸上把事情摆平。

我想克雷格敏锐地注意到了这一家人在这桩疑案面前的缄默——几乎可以称之为猜忌。他们似乎拿不准该把这当作一场意外，还是更糟的事，而且人人对其余各位都怀着一层保留，弄得彼此极不自在。

兰利先生在纽约的律师已接到通知，但他人显然不在城里，因为一直没有音信。他们似乎急盼着他的消息。

===Original===
Dinner over, the family group separated, leaving Tom an opportunity to take us into the gruesome living-room. Of course the remains had been removed, but otherwise the room was exactly as it had been when Harrington discovered the tragedy. I did not see the body, which was lying in an anteroom, but Kennedy did, and spent some time in there.

After he rejoined us, Kennedy next examined the fireplace. It was full of ashes from the logs which had been lighted on the fatal night. He noted attentively the distance of Lewis Langley’s chair from the fireplace, and remarked that the varnish on the chair was not even blistered.

Before the chair, on the floor where the body had been found, he pointed out to us the peculiar ash-marks for some space around, but it really seemed to me as if something else interested him more than these ash-marks.

===Chinese===
晚饭过后，一家人各自散去，汤姆便得了空带我们进那间骇人的客厅。遗体自然是已经移走了，但除此之外，屋子完全是哈林顿发现惨剧时的原样。我没有去看停在前厅里的遗体，肯尼迪去了，还在里面待了不短的工夫。

他回到我们身边后，接着检查壁炉。炉膛里满是出事那晚生过的木柴灰烬。他留心记下了刘易斯·兰利的椅子离壁炉的距离，又指出椅子上的清漆连一个泡都没起。

在椅子前面的地板上，也就是发现遗体的地方，他指给我们看四周一片面积里那些古怪的灰痕，不过说实话，我总觉得比起这些灰痕来，另有什么东西更让他上心。

===Original===
We had been engaged perhaps half an hour in viewing the room. At last Craig suddenly stopped.

“Tom,” he said, “I think I’ll wait till daylight before I go any further. I can’t tell with certainty under these lights, though perhaps they show me some things the sunlight wouldn’t show. We’d better leave everything just as it is until morning.”

So we locked the room again and went into a sort of library across the hall.

===Chinese===
我们在这间屋子里前前后后看了约莫半个钟头，克雷格终于突然停了下来。

「汤姆，」他说，「我想先等天亮再往下查。在这种灯光下我没法看得确切，虽说灯光或许也让我看到了些阳光底下看不到的东西。咱们最好让一切原样不动，等到早晨再说。」

于是我们重新锁上客厅的门，进了过道对面一间类似书房的屋子。

===Original===
We were sitting in silence, each occupied with his own thoughts on the mystery, when the telephone rang. It proved to be a long-distance call from New York for Tom himself. His uncle’s attorney had received the news at his home out on Long Island and had hurried to the city to take charge of the estate. But that was not the news that caused the grave look on Tom’s face as he nervously rejoined us.

“That was uncle’s lawyer, Mr. Clark, of Clark & Burdick,” he said. “He has opened uncle’s personal safe in the offices of the Langley estate﻿—you remember them, Craig﻿—where all the property of the Langley heirs is administered by the trustees. He says he can’t find the will, though he knows there was a will and that it was placed in that safe some time ago. There is no duplicate.”

The full purport of this information at once flashed on me, and I was on the point of blurting out my sympathy, when I saw by the look which Craig and Tom exchanged that they had already realised it and understood each other. Without the will the blood-relatives would inherit all of Lewis Langley’s interest in the old Langley estate. Tom and his sister would be penniless.

===Chinese===
我们正默然坐着，各自琢磨这桩疑案，电话铃响了。原来是纽约打给汤姆本人的长途。他叔父的律师在长岛的家中得到消息，已赶进城去料理产业事务。但这并不是让汤姆神色不安地重回我们身边时、脸色那般凝重的原因。

「是叔父的律师，克拉克-伯迪克律师事务所（Clark & Burdick）的克拉克先生，」他说。「他打开了叔父在兰利产业办事处里的私人保险柜——那地方你记得的，克雷格——兰利家继承人的全部产业都由受托人在那里经管。他说遗嘱找不到了，尽管他明知立过遗嘱，而且前些时候就存放在那只保险柜里。也没有副本。」

这消息的全部含义登时在我脑中闪过，我差一点就脱口说出宽慰的话，却看见克雷格和汤姆交换的那一下眼色——他们早已明白过来，彼此心照不宣。遗嘱一丢，血亲们就将继承刘易斯·兰利在老兰利产业里的全部权益。汤姆和他妹妹将一文不名。

===Original===
It was late, yet we sat for nearly an hour longer, and I don’t think we exchanged a half-dozen sentences in all that time. Craig seemed absorbed in thought. At length, as the great hall-clock sounded midnight, we rose as if by common consent.

“Tom,” said Craig, and I could feel the sympathy that welled up in his voice, “Tom, old man, I’ll get at the bottom of this mystery if human intelligence can do it.”

“I know you will, Craig,” responded Tom, grasping each of us by the hand. “That’s why I so much wanted you fellows to come up here.”

===Chinese===
时间已晚，可我们又坐了将近一个钟头，我记得这段时间里前后说不上六句话。克雷格似乎沉浸在思绪里。终于，大厅里的大座钟敲响午夜，我们不约而同地站起身来。

「汤姆，」克雷格说，我能感觉到他话音里涌上来的那份情谊，「汤姆，老伙计，只要人类的智慧办得到，我就一定把这桩疑案查个水落石出。」

「我知道你办得到，克雷格，」汤姆答道，把我们的手挨个儿握住。「所以我才这么盼着你们两位上山来。」

===Original===
Early in the morning Kennedy aroused me. “Now, Walter, I’m going to ask you to come down into the living-room with me, and we’ll take a look at it in the daytime.”

I hurried into my clothes, and together we quietly went down. Starting with the exact spot where the unfortunate man had been discovered, Kennedy began a minute examination of the floor, using his pocket lens. Every few moments he would stop to examine a spot on the rug or on the hardwood floor more intently. Several times I saw him scrape up something with the blade of his knife and carefully preserve the scrapings, each in a separate piece of paper.

Sitting idly by, I could not for the life of me see just what good it did for me to be there, and I said as much. Kennedy laughed quietly.

“You’re a material witness, Walter,” he replied. “Perhaps I shall need you some day to testify that I actually found these spots in this room.”

===Chinese===
一清早，肯尼迪便叫醒了我。「听着，沃尔特，我要请你跟我下楼到客厅去，咱们趁白天把它看上一看。」

我匆匆穿好衣服，两人悄悄下了楼。从那位不幸者被发现的准确地点着手，肯尼迪用袖珍放大镜开始对地板做细致入微的检查。每隔一会儿，他就要停下来，更专注地察看地毯上或硬木地板上的某个斑点。有几次，我看见他用刀刃刮起一点什么东西，把刮屑小心地包起来，各用一张纸分开保存。

我闲坐在一旁，实在想不通我在场究竟有什么用处，便照实说了。肯尼迪轻轻笑了。

「你是个重要证人，沃尔特，」他答道。「说不定哪天我要你出庭作证，证明这些斑点确实是我在这间屋子里发现的。」

===Original===
Just then Tom stuck his head in. “Can I help?” he asked. “Why didn’t you tell me you were going at it so early?”

“No, thanks,” answered Craig, rising from the floor. “I was just making a careful examination of the room before anyone was up so that nobody would think I was too interested. I’ve finished. But you can help me, after all. Do you think you could describe exactly how everyone was dressed that night?”

“Why, I can try. Let me see. To begin with, uncle had on a shooting-jacket﻿—that was pretty well burnt, as you know. Why, in fact, we all had our shooting-jackets on. The ladies were in white.”

Craig pondered a little, but did not seem disposed to pursue the subject further, until Tom volunteered the information that since the tragedy none of them had been wearing their shooting jackets.

“We’ve all been wearing city clothes,” he remarked.

===Chinese===
正这时汤姆探进头来。「要帮忙吗？」他问。「怎么不告诉我你们起这么早就动手了？」

「不用，谢了，」克雷格从地板上站起身答道。「我不过是趁大家没起床，先把屋子仔细查一遍，免得人家觉得我太上心。已经查完了。不过你倒真能帮个忙。你能不能把那天晚上每个人穿的衣着一五一十地描述出来？」

「嗯，我可以试试。让我想想。头一个，叔父穿了件猎装（shooting-jacket）——那件烧得差不多了，你们是知道的。呃，其实那天我们人人都穿着猎装。女士们一身白衣。」

克雷格略一沉吟，似乎不打算再深究这个话头，直到汤姆主动补充说，惨剧发生之后，他们就再没穿过那些猎装。

「我们都改穿城里便装了。」他说。

===Original===
“Could you get your Uncle James and your Cousin Junior to go with you for an hour or two this morning on the lake, or on a tramp in the woods?” asked Craig after a moment’s thought.

“Really, Craig,” responded Tom doubtfully, “I ought to go to Saranac to complete the arrangements for taking Uncle Lewis’s body to New York.”

“Very well, persuade them to go with you. Anything, so long as you keep me from interruption for an hour or two.”

They agreed on doing that, and as by that time most of the family were up, we went in to breakfast, another silent and suspicious meal.

===Chinese===
「今天上午，你能不能把你詹姆斯叔叔和小詹表弟拉上，跟你一起到湖上走走，或者到林子里徒步一两个钟头？」克雷格想了片刻问道。

「说真的，克雷格，」汤姆迟疑着答道，「我本该去萨拉纳克，把送刘易斯叔父遗体回纽约的事宜办妥。」

「那正好，劝他们同你一道去。去哪儿都行，只要能保我一两个钟头不受打扰。」

事情就这么说定了。那时一家人多半已经起身，我们便进去吃早饭——又是一场沉默而互怀猜忌的早餐。

===Original===
After breakfast Kennedy tactfully withdrew from the family, and I did the same. We wandered off in the direction of the stables and there fell to admiring some of the horses. The groom, who seemed to be a sensible and pleasant sort of fellow, was quite ready to talk, and soon he and Craig were deep in discussing the game of the north country.

“Many rabbits about here?” asked Kennedy at length, when they had exhausted the larger game.

“Oh, yes. I saw one this morning, sir,” replied the groom.

“Indeed?” said Kennedy. “Do you suppose you could catch a couple for me?”

===Chinese===
早饭后，肯尼迪得体地从家人中间抽身出来，我也照做了。我们信步朝马厩那边走去，到了那儿便赏玩起几匹马来。那位马夫看上去是个明白事理、和蔼可亲的人，也很乐意搭话，不多时，他和克雷格便热络地聊起了北乡的野味猎物。

等大个头的猎物聊尽了，肯尼迪终于问道：「这一带兔子多吗？」

「哦，多，先生。今儿早上我还见着一只呢。」马夫答道。

「当真？」肯尼迪说。「你看你能替我捉两只来吗？」

===Original===
“Guess I could, sir﻿—alive, you mean?”

“Oh, yes, alive﻿—I don’t want you to violate the game laws. This is the closed season, isn’t it?”

“Yes, sir, but then it’s all right, sir, here on the estate.”

“Bring them to me this afternoon, or﻿—no, keep them here in the stable in a cage and let me know when you have them. If anybody asks you about them, say they belong to Mr. Tom.”

Craig handed a small treasury note to the groom, who took it with a grin and touched his hat.

“Thanks,” he said. “I’ll let you know when I have the bunnies.”

===Chinese===
「我想行，先生——您是说，要活的？」

「对，要活的——我可不想让你触犯狩猎法。现在是禁猎期，不是吗？」

「是，先生，不过在庄园地界里没事，先生。」

「今天下午给我送来，或者——不，就把它们关在笼子里搁在马厩，捉到了知会我一声。要是有人问起，就说是汤姆先生的。」

克雷格递给马夫一张小面额纸币，马夫咧嘴笑着接过去，抬手碰了碰帽檐。

「谢谢，」他说。「兔宝宝一到手，我就知会您。」

===Original===
As we walked slowly back from the stables we caught sight of Tom down at the boathouse just putting off in the motorboat with his uncle and cousin. Craig waved to him, and he walked up to meet us.

“While you’re in Saranac,” said Craig, “buy me a dozen or so test-tubes. Only, don’t let anyone here at the house know you are buying them. They might ask questions.”

===Chinese===
我们从马厩慢慢往回走时，望见汤姆正在下面船屋那儿，同他叔叔和表弟一起登上摩托艇准备出发。克雷格朝他挥了挥手，他便走上坡来迎我们。

「你到了萨拉纳克，」克雷格说，「替我买一打上下的试管。只是别让宅子里任何人知道你买这个。人家会问东问西的。」

===Original===
While they were gone Kennedy stole into James Langley’s room and after a few minutes returned to our room with the hunting-jacket. He carefully examined it with his pocket lens. Then he filled a drinking-glass with warm boiled water and added a few pinches of table salt. With a piece of sterilised gauze from Doctor Putnam’s medicine-chest, he carefully washed off a few portions of the coat and set the glass and the gauze soaking in it aside. Then he returned the coat to the closet where he had found it. Next, as silently, he stole into Junior’s room and repeated the process with his hunting-jacket, using another glass and piece of gauze.

===Chinese===
趁他们外出的当口，肯尼迪溜进了詹姆斯·兰利的房间，几分钟后拿着那件猎装回到我们屋里。他用袖珍放大镜细细察看了一遍，然后倒了一玻璃杯温开水，撒进几撮食盐。他又从帕特南医生的药箱里取了一块消毒纱布，蘸着盐水仔细擦洗了外套的几处地方，把纱布泡进那杯水里，连杯子一起搁在一边。随后他把外套放回壁橱原处。接着他又悄没声儿地溜进小詹的房间，用另一只杯子和另一块纱布，把同样的过程又做了一遍。

===Original===
“While I am out of the room, Walter,” he said, “I want you to take these two glasses, cover them, and number them and on a slip of paper which you must retain, place the names of the owners of the respective coats. I don’t like this part of it﻿—I hate to play spy and would much rather come out in the open, but there is nothing else to do, and it is much better for all concerned that I should play the game secretly just now. There may be no cause for suspicion at all. In that case I’d never forgive myself for starting a family row. And then again but we shall see.”

After I had numbered and recorded the glasses Kennedy returned, and we went downstairs again.

“Curious about the will, isn’t it?” I remarked as we stood on the wide verandah a moment.

“Yes,” he replied. “It may be necessary to go back to New York to delve into that part of it before we get through, but I hope not. We’ll wait.”

===Chinese===
「趁我出去的工夫，沃尔特，」他说，「你把这两只杯子收起来，盖上，编上号，再在一张你必须自己留着的纸条上，写下各件外套主人的名字。这一步我并不喜欢——我不愿做密探，宁可凡事摆到明处——可眼下别无他法，而且为了所有相关的人着想，此刻还是暗中行事为好。也许根本就不存在值得怀疑的事。果真如此，却由我惹起一场家庭内讧，那我这辈子都不能原谅自己。不过——再说吧，咱们走着瞧。」

我把杯子编号登记完毕，肯尼迪回来了，我们便又下了楼。

「遗嘱的事，蹊跷不蹊跷？」我们此刻正站在宽阔的游廊上，我说了这么一句。

「是啊，」他答道。「事情了结之前，说不定还得回纽约去专门查一查那一头，但我希望不至于。再等等看。」

===Original===
At this point the groom interrupted us to say that he had caught the rabbits. Kennedy at once hurried to the stable. There he rolled up his sleeves, pricked a vein in his arm, and injected a small quantity of his own blood into one of the rabbits. The other he did not touch.

It was late in the afternoon when Tom returned from town with his uncle and cousin. He seemed even more agitated than usual. Without a word he hurried up from the landing and sought us out.

“What do you think of that?” he cried, opening a copy of the *Record*, and laying it flat on the library table.

===Chinese===
正说到这里，马夫来打断我们，说兔子捉到了。肯尼迪立刻赶到马厩。他在那里挽起袖子，刺破臂上的一根血管，把自己少量的血注射进其中一只兔子体内。另一只他碰也没碰。

傍晚时分，汤姆才同他叔叔和表弟从镇上回来。他看上去比平时更加烦躁不安。他一言不发地从泊船处急急赶上坡来，径直找到我们。

「你们瞧瞧这个！」他喊道，摊开一份《纪事报》，平平地铺在书房桌上。

===Original===
There on the front page was Lewis Langley’s picture with a huge scare-head:

Mysterious Case of Spontaneous Combustion

“It’s all out,” groaned Tom, as we bent over to read the account. “And such a story!”

Under the date of the day previous, a Saranac despatch ran:

===Chinese===
头版上印着刘易斯·兰利的照片，配着一条吓人的通栏大标题：

人体自燃离奇案

「全捅出去了，」汤姆呻吟道，我们俯身去读那篇报道。「还编成了这么一个故事！」

报纸日期是前一天，其中一条萨拉纳克电讯写道：

===Original===
Lewis Langley, well known as sporting man and club member in New York, and eldest son of the late Lewis Langley, the banker, was discovered dead under the most mysterious circumstances this morning at Camp Hangout, twelve miles from this town.

The Death of “Old Krook” in Dickens’s *Bleak House* or of the victim in one of Marryat’s most thrilling tales was not more gruesome than this actual fact. It is without doubt a case of spontaneous human combustion, such as is recorded beyond dispute in medical and medico-legal textbooks of the past two centuries. Scientists in this city consulted for the *Record* agree that, while rare, spontaneous human combustion is an established fact and that everything in this curious case goes to show that another has been added to the already well-authenticated list of cases recorded in America and Europe. The family refuse to be interviewed, which seems to indicate that the rumours in medical circles in Saranac have a solid basis of fact.

Then followed a circumstantial account of the life of Langley and the events leading up to the discovery of the body﻿—fairly accurate in itself, but highly coloured.

===Chinese===
以运动界人士和俱乐部会员身份闻名纽约、系已故银行家刘易斯·兰利长子的刘易斯·兰利，今晨在距本镇十二英里的逍遥营地，被人发现死于最为神秘的状况之中。

狄更斯《荒凉山庄》（*Bleak House*）里「老克鲁克」（“Old Krook”）之死，或是马里亚特（Marryat）最惊悚的某篇故事里那位遇难者的下场，都不及这桩实事来得骇人。这无疑是人体自燃的一例——过去两个世纪的医学与法医学教科书中，不乏无可争辩的此类记载。本报为此咨询的本地科学家们一致认为，人体自燃虽属罕见，却是确凿成立的事实，而眼下这桩奇案的种种情形都表明，欧美已有翔实记载的案例名录上，又将增添一例。死者家属拒绝接受采访，这似乎恰恰说明，萨拉纳克医学界流传的种种传闻，有着坚实的实事依据。

接下来是一篇关于兰利生平和发现遗体前后经过的详详细细的报道——内容大体属实，却极尽渲染之能事。

===Original===
“The *Record* man must have made good use of his time here,” I commented, as I finished reading the despatch. “And﻿—well, they must have done some hard work in New York to get this story up so completely﻿—see, after the despatch follow a lot of interviews, and here is a short article on spontaneous combustion itself.”

Harrington and the rest of the family had just come in.

“What’s this we hear about the *Record* having an article?” Harrington asked. “Read it aloud, Professor, so we can all hear it.”

===Chinese===
「那位《纪事报》记者在这儿的时间真没白费，」我读完电讯评论道。「还有——喏，纽约那边想必也下了一番苦功，才把这篇东西拼得这么齐全——瞧，电讯后面跟着一大串访谈，这儿还有一篇讲人体自燃本身的小文章。」

哈林顿和家里其他人刚刚进来。

「我们听说《纪事报》登了篇文章，是怎么回事？」哈林顿问。「教授，念出来吧，好让大家都能听见。」

===Original===
“ ‘Spontaneous human combustion, or *catacausis ebriosus*,’ ” began Craig, “ ‘is one of the baffling human scientific mysteries. Indeed, there can be no doubt but that individuals have in some strange and inexplicable manner caught fire and been partially or almost wholly consumed.

“ ‘Some have attributed it to gases in the body, such as carbureted hydrogen. Once it was noted at the Hôtel Dieu in Paris that a body on being dissected gave forth a gas which was inflammable and burned with a bluish flame. Others have attributed the combustion to alcohol. A toper several years ago in Brooklyn and New York used to make money by blowing his breath through a wire gauze and lighting it. Whatever the cause, medical literature records seventy-six cases of *catacausis* in two hundred years.

“ ‘The combustion seems to be sudden and is apparently confined to the cavities, the abdomen, chest, and head. Victims of ordinary fire accidents rush hither and thither frantically, succumb from exhaustion, their limbs are burned, and their clothing is all destroyed. But in *catacausis* they are stricken down without warning, the limbs are rarely burned, and only the clothing in contact with the head and chest is consumed. The residue is like a distillation of animal tissue, grey and dark, with an overpoweringly fetid odour. They are said to burn with a flickering stifled blue flame, and water, far from arresting the combustion, seems to add to it. Gin is particularly rich in inflammable, empyreumatic oils, as they are called, and in most cases it is recorded that the *catacausis* took place among gin-drinkers, old and obese.

“ ‘Within the past few years cases are on record which seem to establish *catacausis* beyond doubt. In one case the heat was so great as to explode a pistol in the pocket of the victim. In another, a woman, the victim’s husband was asphyxiated by the smoke. The woman weighed, one hundred and eighty pounds in life, but the ashes weighed only twelve pounds: In all these cases the proof of spontaneous combustion seems conclusive.’ ”

As Craig finished reading, we looked blankly, horrified, at one another. It was too dreadful to realise.

===Chinese===
「『人体自燃，即所谓 *catacausis ebriosus*（醉后自燃），』」克雷格念道，「『是人类科学上最难解的谜团之一。的确，无可怀疑，曾有人以某种离奇而无法解释的方式自行着火，被烧去一部分，乃至几乎烧尽。

「『有人把它归因于体内的气体，譬如碳化氢（carbureted hydrogen）。巴黎主宫医院（Hôtel Dieu）曾有一次在解剖尸体时注意到，尸身放出一种可燃气体，点燃后呈蓝色火焰。也有人把这种燃烧归因于酒精。几年前，布鲁克林和纽约有个酒徒，惯于隔着铁丝网吹气点火，以此卖艺挣钱。无论成因何在，两百年间，医学文献记载了七十六例 *catacausis*（自燃）。

「『这种燃烧似乎骤然而起，而且显然只限于体腔——腹腔、胸腔和头部。寻常火灾的遇难者会疯狂地四处奔突，力竭而亡，四肢烧毁，衣衫尽焚。可在 *catacausis*（自燃）中，患者毫无预兆地颓然倒毙，四肢很少烧及，只有贴着头胸部的衣物被烧掉。残存的物质好似动物组织蒸馏所得，灰黑一团，散发出一股难以抵挡的恶臭。据说他们燃烧时火焰窒闷发蓝、摇曳不定，而水非但止不住燃烧，似乎反而助长它。金酒（gin）尤其富含那种所谓易燃的焦臭油类（empyreumatic oils），而且大多数记载都说，*catacausis*（自燃）发生在年老肥胖的金酒酒徒身上。

「『近几年来，又有几宗记录在案的病例，似乎已使 *catacausis*（自燃）确凿无疑。有一例，热度之高，竟把死者口袋里的一支手枪烤炸走了火。另一例的死者是位妇人，死者的丈夫被浓烟活活熏死。那妇人生前体重一百八十磅，遗灰却只称得十二磅：在所有这些病例中，自燃的证明看来都无懈可击。』」

克雷格念完，我们面面相觑，人人惊骇得失了神。这事可怕得叫人无法信以为真。

===Original===
“What do you think of it, Professor?” asked James Langley, at length. “I’ve read somewhere of such cases, but to think of its actually happening﻿—and to my own brother. Do you really think Lewis could have met his death in this terrible manner?”

Kennedy made no reply. Harrington seemed absorbed in thought. A shudder passed over us as we thought about it. But, gruesome as it was, it was evident that the publication of the story in the *Record* had relieved the feelings of the family group in one respect﻿—it at least seemed to offer an explanation. It was noticeable that the suspicious air with which everyone had regarded everyone else was considerably dispelled.

===Chinese===
「教授，您怎么看？」詹姆斯·兰利终于开口问。「这样的病例，我在哪儿读到过，可想不到它竟真会发生——还偏偏落在我亲兄弟身上。您当真认为，刘易斯可能就是这样惨死的吗？」

肯尼迪没有作答。哈林顿似乎陷入了沉思。我们一想起来，便都打了个寒噤。然而，这事尽管骇人，《纪事报》这篇报道的刊出，显然让一家人的情绪在某一方面松了下来——它至少像是给出了一种解释。可以看得出，先前人人打量人人时那股猜疑的气氛，已经消散了大半。

===Original===
Tom said nothing until the others had withdrawn. “Kennedy,” he burst out, then, “do you believe that such combustion is absolutely *spontaneous*? Don’t you believe that something else is necessary to start it?”

“I’d rather not express an opinion just yet, Tom,” answered Craig carefully. “Now, if you can get Harrington and Doctor Putnam away from the house for a short time, as you did with your uncle and cousin this morning, I may be able to tell you something about this case soon.”

===Chinese===
汤姆一直没吭声，直到旁人都退了出去。「肯尼迪，」他这才脱口而出，「你相信这种燃烧真是完全『自发的』（*spontaneous*）吗？你不觉得，还得有别的什么才能点着它吗？」

「眼下我还是不忙表态的好，汤姆，」克雷格谨慎地答道。「这样吧，你要是能像今早打发你叔叔和表弟那样，把哈林顿和帕特南医生也引出门去一小会儿，这案子，我很快就能给你们一点说法了。」

===Original===
Again Kennedy stole into another bedroom, and returned to our room with a hunting-jacket. Just as he had done before, he carefully washed it off with the gauze soaked in the salt solution and quickly returned the coat, repeating the process with Doctor Putnam’s coat and, last, that of Tom himself. Finally he turned his back while I sealed the glasses and marked and recorded them on my slip.

The next day was spent mainly in preparations for the journey to New York with the body of Lewis Langley. Kennedy was very busy on what seemed to me to be preparations for some mysterious chemical experiments. I found myself fully occupied in keeping special correspondents from all over the country at bay.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪再次溜进另一间卧室，拿着一件猎装回到我们房里。他像先前那样，用浸透盐水的纱布细细擦洗了外套，又赶紧把它送了回去，随后拿帕特南医生的外套照方抓药，最后处理的，是汤姆自己的那件。末了，他背过身去，由我把那几只杯子封口、编号、登记到我的纸条上。

第二天，时间大多花在护送刘易斯·兰利遗体回纽约的准备上。肯尼迪忙个不停，在我看来是在为某种神秘的化学实验做准备。我则发现自己全副精力，都用来招架从全国各地涌来的特派记者。

===Original===
That evening after dinner we were all sitting in the open summer house over the boathouse. Smudges of green pine were burning and smoking on little artificial islands of stone near the lake shore, lighting up the trees on every side with a red glare. Tom and his sister were seated with Kennedy and myself on one side, while some distance from us Harrington was engaged in earnest conversation with Isabelle. The other members of the family were further removed. That seemed typical to me of the way the family group split up.

“Mr. Kennedy,” remarked Grace in a thoughtful, low tone, “what do you make of that *Record* article?”

“Very clever, no doubt,” replied Craig.

“But don’t you think it strange about the will?”

“Hush,” whispered Tom, for Isabelle and Harrington had ceased talking and might perhaps be listening.

===Chinese===
当天晚饭后，大家都坐在船屋上方那座敞开的凉亭里。湖岸近处一座座人造的小石岛上，绿松枝的熏烟堆（smudges）燃着、冒着烟，红彤彤的光把四面的树木都照亮了。汤姆和他妹妹同肯尼迪和我坐在一侧；离我们不远，哈林顿正和伊莎贝尔谈得投入。家里其他人坐得更远。在我看来，这一家人这样分成几拨，倒颇具象征意味。

「肯尼迪先生，」格蕾丝若有所思地低声说，「您对《纪事报》那篇文章怎么看？」

「无疑写得很高明。」克雷格答道。

「可您不觉得遗嘱的事很蹊跷吗？」

「嘘，」汤姆低声道，因为伊莎贝尔和哈林顿已经住了口，说不定正听着呢。

===Original===
Just then one of the servants came up with a telegram.

Tom hastily opened it and read the message eagerly in the corner of the summer house nearest one of the glowing smudges. I felt instinctively that it was from his lawyer. He turned and beckoned to Kennedy and myself.

“What do you think of that?” he whispered hoarsely.

We bent over and in the flickering light read the message:

New York papers full of spontaneous combustion story. *Record* had exclusive story yesterday, but all papers today feature even more. Is it true? Please wire additional details at once. Also immediate instructions regarding loss of will. Has been abstracted from safe. Could Lewis Langley have taken it himself? Unless new facts soon must make loss public or issue statement Lewis Langley intestate.

Daniel Clark

===Chinese===
正在这时，一个仆人拿着电报走上前来。

汤姆急急拆开，就着凉亭里离一堆红彤彤的熏烟最近的角落，迫切地读起电文来。我本能地感觉到，这是他的律师发来的。他转过身，招手唤肯尼迪和我过去。

「你们看这事怎么办？」他哑着嗓子悄声问。

我们俯下身，就着摇曳的火光读那封电报：

纽约各报连篇累牍，尽是人体自燃。《纪事报》昨日独家，今日各报更加渲染。是否属实？请即刻电告详情。遗嘱遗失一节，亦请速电示方略。遗嘱已被人从保险柜中抽走。有无可能系刘易斯·兰利自取？若短期内无新情况，本人不得不公布遗失，或声明刘易斯·兰利死时未立遗嘱。

丹尼尔·克拉克（Daniel Clark）

===Original===
Tom looked blankly at Kennedy, and then at his sister, who was sitting alone. I thought I could read what was passing in his mind. With all his faults Lewis Langley had been a good foster-parent to his adopted children. But it was all over now if the will was lost.

“What can I do?” asked Tom hopelessly. “I have nothing to reply to him.”

“But I have,” quietly returned Kennedy, deliberately folding up the message and handing it back. “Tell them all to be in the library in fifteen minutes. This message hurries me a bit, but I am prepared. You will have something to wire Mr. Clark after that.” Then he strode off toward the house, leaving us to gather the group together in considerable bewilderment.

===Chinese===
汤姆失神地望了望肯尼迪，又望了望独自坐着的妹妹。我想我读得出他心里翻腾的是什么。刘易斯·兰利纵有千般不是，对这些养子女却当真尽到了养父的本分。可遗嘱一丢，这一切如今都完了。

「我能怎么办？」汤姆绝望地问。「我根本没什么可回复他的。」

「我有。」肯尼迪不动声色地答道，从容地折好电报递了回去。「叫大家一刻钟后都到书房来。这封电报催得紧了些，不过我已准备停当。到时候，你就有东西可以拍给克拉克先生了。」说罢他便大步朝宅子走去，留下我们满腹狐疑地把一家人聚拢到一处。

===Original===
A quarter of an hour later we had all assembled in the library, across the hall from the room in which Lewis Langley had been found. As usual Kennedy began by leaping straight into the middle of his subject.

“Early in the eighteenth century,” he commenced slowly, “a woman was found burned to death. There were no clues, and the scientists of that time suggested spontaneous combustion. This explanation was accepted. The theory always has been that the process of respiration by which the tissues of the body are used up and got rid of gives the body a temperature, and it has seemed that it may be possible, by preventing the escape of this heat, to set fire to the body.”

We were leaning forward expectantly, horrified by the thought that perhaps, after all, the *Record* was correct.

===Chinese===
一刻钟后，大家都聚在了书房里，就在发现刘易斯·兰利那间屋子的过道对面。肯尼迪照例一开口便直奔正题。

「十八世纪初，」他不紧不慢地讲起来，「有人发现一名妇女被烧死了。现场毫无线索，当时的科学家们便提出了自燃之说。这个解释被接受了。那一套理论向来是这么说的：人体组织在呼吸过程中不断消耗、排除，由此赋予人体一种温度；而只要不让这热量散逸出去，似乎就有可能把人体点着。」

我们满怀期待地倾身向前，一想到《纪事报》说不定终究是对的，又不禁心惊胆战。

===Original===
“Now,” resumed Kennedy, his tone changing, “suppose we try a little experiment﻿—one that was tried very convincingly by the immortal Liebig. Here is a sponge. I am going to soak it in gin from this bottle, the same that Mr. Langley was drinking from on the night of the﻿—er﻿—the tragedy.”

Kennedy took the saturated sponge and placed it in an agate-iron pan from the kitchen. Then he lighted it. The bluish flame shot upward, and in tense silence we watched it burn lower and lower, till all the alcohol was consumed. Then he picked up the sponge and passed it around. It was dry, but the sponge itself had not been singed.

“We now know,” he continued, “that from the nature of combustion it is impossible for the human body to undergo spontaneous ignition or combustion in the way the scientific experts of the past century believed. Swathe the body in the thickest of nonconductors of heat, and what happens? A profuse perspiration exudes, and before such an ignition could possibly take place all the moisture of the body would have to be evaporated. As seventy-five percent or more of the body is water, it is evident that enormous heat would be necessary﻿—moisture is the great safeguard. The experiment which I have shown you could be duplicated with specimens of human organs preserved for years in alcohol in museums. They would burn just as this sponge﻿—the specimen itself would be very nearly uninjured by the burning of the alcohol.”

===Chinese===
「现在，」肯尼迪接下去说，语气一变，「咱们来做个小实验——不朽的李比希（Liebig）就做过一个极有说服力的实验。这儿是一块海绵。我要把它用这只瓶子里的金酒浸透——正是惨案那天夜里兰利先生喝的那瓶——呃——那瓶酒。」

肯尼迪拿起浸透的海绵，放进一只从厨房拿来的搪瓷锅里，然后点着了它。蓝色的火焰腾地蹿起，我们在紧张的寂静中，望着它越烧越低，直到酒精燃尽。随后他拈起海绵，递给大家传看。海绵已经干了，可海绵本身一点没燎着。

「如今我们已经知道，」他继续说道，「就燃烧的本性而论，人体绝不可能像上一世纪的科学专家们所相信的那样，发生自燃或自焚。把人体裹上最厚的不导热材料，会怎么样？会大量渗出汗来；不等这种燃烧发生，全身的水分早就得蒸发干净。人体既然有百分之七十五以上是水，显然就需要极大的热量——水分正是最牢靠的保障。刚才给你们看的这个实验，拿博物馆里用酒精保存多年的人体器官标本来重做，也会是同样的结果。它们会像这块海绵一样燃烧——标本本身几乎不会因酒精的燃烧而受损。」

===Original===
“Then, Professor Kennedy, you maintain that my brother did not meet his death by such an accident?” asked James Langley.

“Exactly that, sir,” replied Craig. “One of the most important aspects of the historic faith in this phenomenon is that of its skilful employment in explaining away what would otherwise appear to be convincing circumstantial evidence in cases of accusations of murder.”

“Then how do you explain Mr. Langley’s death?” demanded Harrington. “My theory of a spark from a cigar may be true, after all.”

===Chinese===
「那么，肯尼迪教授，您是断定我兄弟并非死于这种意外了？」詹姆斯·兰利问。

「正是如此，先生，」克雷格答道。「世人历来笃信这种现象，其最重要的一个侧面就在于：它被巧妙地用来解释掉一些东西——若不是有它挡着，那些东西在谋杀指控中本会是极具说服力的间接证据。」

「那您怎么解释兰利先生的死？」哈林顿逼问道。「我的雪茄火星之说，说不定终究是对的。」

===Original===
“I am coming to that in a moment,” answered Kennedy quietly. “My first suspicion was aroused by what not even Doctor Putnam seems to have noticed. The skull of Mr. Langley, charred and consumed as it was, seemed to show marks of violence. It might have been from a fracture of the skull or it might have been an accident to his remains as they were being removed to the anteroom. Again, his tongue seemed as though it was protruding. That might have been natural suffocation, or it might have been from forcible strangulation. So far I had nothing but conjecture to work on. But in looking over the living-room I found near the table, on the hardwood floor, a spot﻿—just one little round spot. Now, deductions from spots, even if we know them to be blood, must be made very carefully. I did not know this to be a blood-spot, and so was very careful at first.

===Chinese===
「这个我马上就要讲到，」肯尼迪平静地答道。「我最初的怀疑，是由一件连帕特南医生似乎都未曾留意的事情引起的。兰利先生的颅骨，虽已烧焦焚毁，上面却似乎留有暴力的痕迹。那可能是颅骨骨折所致，也可能是遗体抬往前厅时磕碰所致。再者，他的舌头似乎有些外伸。那可能是自然的窒息，也可能是遭人强力扼勒。到这一步，我手里可凭的还只有揣测。但在检查客厅时，我在桌子近旁的硬木地板上发现了一个斑点——就那么一个小小的圆斑。要知道，从斑点作出的推断，即便明知是血，也须格外审慎。我当时并不确知那是血迹，所以起初慎之又慎。

===Original===
“Let us assume it was a blood-spot, however. What did it show? It was just a little regular round spot, quite thick. Now, drops of blood falling only a few inches usually make a round spot with a smooth border. Still the surface on which the drop falls is quite as much a factor as the height from which it falls. If the surface is rough the border may be irregular. But this was a smooth surface and not absorbent. The thickness of a dried blood-spot on a nonabsorbent surface is less the greater the height from which it has fallen. This was a thick spot. Now if it had fallen, say, six feet, the height of Mr. Langley, the spot would have been thin﻿—some secondary spatters might have been seen, or at least an irregular edge around the spot. Therefore, if it was a blood-spot, it had fallen only one or two feet. I ascertained next that the lower part of the body showed no wounds or bruises whatever.

===Chinese===
「不过，我们不妨假定它就是血迹。它说明了什么？那不过是一小点规整的圆斑，相当之厚。要知道，从不过几英寸高的地方落下的血滴，通常会形成一个边缘光洁的圆斑。但血滴落上去的表面，与坠落的高度同是决定因素：表面粗糙，边缘就可能参差不齐。而这里是光滑又不吸水的表面。在不吸水的表面上，干涸的血斑落点越高，便越薄。这一斑却是厚的。倘若它是从——比如说——六英尺高、也就是兰利先生的身高处落下的，斑点就该很薄，也许还能见到四散的次生溅点，至少斑点四周会有一圈不规则的边缘。因此，倘若这是血迹，它落下的高度不过一两英尺。接下来我又查明，遗体下半身没有任何伤痕或淤青。

===Original===
“Tracks of blood such as are left by dragging a bleeding body differ very greatly from tracks of arterial blood which are left when the victim has strength to move himself. Continuing my speculations, supposing it to be a blood-spot, what did it indicate? Clearly that Mr. Langley was struck by somebody on the head with a heavy instrument, perhaps in another part of the room, that he was choked, that as the drops of blood oozed from the wound on his head, he was dragged across the floor, in the direction of the fireplace﻿—”

“But, Professor Kennedy,” interrupted Doctor Putnam, “have you proved that the spot was a blood-spot? Might it not have been a paint-spot or something of that sort?”

Kennedy had apparently been waiting for just such a question.

===Chinese===
「拖拽一具流血的躯体所留下的血迹，与受害人尚有气力自行移动时留下的动脉血迹，是大不一样的。顺着我的揣测再往下走：假定那是血迹，它指向什么？分明是——有人用一件沉重的器械击打了兰利先生的头部，也许是在房间的另一处；随后又扼住了他；当他头部伤口渗出血珠时，他被人拖过地板，朝壁炉的方向拖去——」

「可是，肯尼迪教授，」帕特南医生插话道，「您证实了那个斑点是血迹吗？它会不会只是油漆斑之类的东西？」

看来肯尼迪等的就是这么一问。

===Original===
“Ordinarily, water has no effect on paint,” he answered. “I found that the spot could be washed off with water. That is not all. I have a test for blood that is so delicately sensitive that the blood of an Egyptian mummy thousands of years old will respond to it. It was discovered by a German scientist, Doctor Uhlenhuth, and was no longer ago than last winter applied in England in connection with the Clapham murder. The suspected murderer declared that stains on his clothes were only spatters of paint, but the test proved them to be spatters of blood. Walter, bring in the cage with the rabbits.”

I opened the door and took the cage from the groom, who had brought it up from the stable and stood waiting with it some distance away.

“This test is very simple, Doctor Putnam,” continued Craig, as I placed the cage on the table and Kennedy unwrapped the sterilised test-tubes. “A rabbit is inoculated with human blood, and after a time the serum that is taken from the rabbit supplies the material for the test.

===Chinese===
「寻常说来，水对油漆是不起作用的，」他答道。「我却发现，那个斑点用水就洗得掉。这还不算。我有一种验血的法子，灵敏到连几千年前的埃及木乃伊的血都能验出来。它是德国科学家乌伦胡特医生（Doctor Uhlenhuth）发现的，而就在去年冬天，英国审理克拉珀姆凶案（Clapham murder）时还用到过它。那个杀人嫌疑犯一口咬定，衣上的污迹不过是溅上的油漆，可这项检验证明，那是溅上的血。沃尔特，把装兔子的笼子提进来。」

我打开门，从马夫手里接过笼子——他已把它从马厩提了上来，一直站在不远处候着。

「这项检验非常简单，帕特南医生，」克雷格继续说道。这时我把笼子放到桌上，肯尼迪解开了那几支消毒试管。「把人的血注射给一只兔子，过些时候从它身上取出的血清，便是检验所用的材料。

===Original===
“I will insert this needle in one of these rabbits which has been so inoculated and will draw off some of the serum, which I place in this test-tube to the right. The other rabbit has not been inoculated. I draw off some of its serum and place that tube here on the left﻿—we will call that our ‘control tube.’ It will check the results of our tests.

“Wrapped up in this paper I have the scrapings of the spot which I found on the floor﻿—just a few grains of dark, dried powder. To show how sensitive the test is, I will take only one of the smallest of these minute scrapings. I dissolve it in this third tube with distilled water. I will even divide it in half, and place the other half in this fourth tube.

“Next I add some of the serum of the uninoculated rabbit to the half in this tube. You observe, nothing happens. I add a little of the serum of the inoculated rabbit to the other half in this other tube. Observe how delicate the test is﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我先把针头刺进这只接种过的兔子，抽出一部分血清，放进右边这支试管。另一只兔子没有接种过。我从它身上也抽出一部分血清，把那支试管搁在左边——我们姑且叫它『对照管』（‘control tube’）。它会替我们的检验结果把关。

「这张纸里包着的，是我从地板上取回的刮屑——不过几粒发黑的干粉。为了显一显这检验有多灵敏，我只取这些细屑中最小的一粒。我把它溶进第三支试管的蒸馏水里。索性再一分为二，把另一半放进第四支试管。

「接着，我往这支试管的那一半里，加入一些未接种兔子的血清。诸位请看，毫无动静。我再往另一支试管的另一半里，加少许接种过的兔子的血清。且看这检验何等精微——」

===Original===
Kennedy was leaning forward, almost oblivious of the rest of us in the room, talking almost as if to himself. We, too, had riveted our eyes on the tubes.

As he added the serum from the inoculated rabbit, a cloudy milky ring formed almost immediately in the hitherto colourless, very dilute blood-solution.

“That,” concluded Craig, triumphantly holding the tube aloft, “that conclusively proves that the little round spot on the hardwood floor was not paint, was not anything in this wide world but blood.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪俯身向前，几乎忘了屋里我们这些人，话音低得近乎自语。我们也全都目不转睛地盯住了那几支试管。

他把接种过的兔子的血清滴下去，那原本无色、极稀的血溶液里，几乎立刻生出了一圈浑浊的乳白环。

「这就，」克雷格高高举起试管，一锤定音地说，「这就确凿地证明了：硬木地板上那个小圆斑不是油漆，普天之下，它什么都不是，只可能是血。」

===Original===
No one in the room said a word, but I knew there must have been someone there who thought volumes in the few minutes that elapsed.

“Having found one blood-spot, I began to look about for more, but was able to find only two or three traces where spots seemed to have been. The fact is that the blood spots had been apparently carefully wiped up. That is an easy matter. Hot water and salt, or hot water alone, or even cold water, will make quite short work of fresh blood-spots﻿—at least to all outward appearances. But nothing but a most thorough cleaning can conceal them from the Uhlenhuth test, even when they are apparently wiped out. It is a case of Lady Macbeth over again, crying in the face of modern science, ‘Out, out, damned spot.’

“I was able with sufficient definiteness to trace roughly a course of blood-spots from the fireplace to a point near the door of the living-room. But beyond the door, in the hall, nothing.”

===Chinese===
屋里没有一个人作声，但我知道，在接下来那几分钟里，必定有人心里翻过了千言万语。

「找到了一处血迹，我便开始四处搜寻，却只找到两三处疑似斑点的残痕。事实上，那些血迹显然被人细心擦拭过。这并不费事：热水加盐，或者光用热水，甚至冷水，都能很快把新鲜血迹收拾干净——至少表面上看是干净了。可是除非清洗得彻头彻尾，任它擦拭得再干净，也瞒不过乌伦胡特试验——哪怕斑迹表面上已经除净。这又是麦克白夫人的故事重演，只能面对现代科学哭喊：『去吧，去吧，该死的污点！』

「我还算分明地追出了一条血迹的大致路线，从壁炉一直延到客厅门边近处。可一出了门，到了过道里，便什么也没有了。」

===Original===
“Still,” interrupted Harrington, “to get back to the facts in the case. They are perfectly in accord either with my theory of the cigar or the *Record*’s of spontaneous combustion. How do you account for the facts?”

“I suppose you refer to the charred head, the burned neck, the upper chest cavity, while the arms and legs were untouched?”

“Yes, and then the body was found in the midst of combustible furniture that was not touched. It seems to me that even the spontaneous-combustion theory has considerable support in spite of this very interesting circumstantial evidence about blood-spots. Next to my own theory, the combustion theory seems most in harmony with the facts.”

“If you will go over in your mind all the points proved to have been discovered﻿—not the added points in the *Record* story﻿—I think you will agree with me that mine is a more logical interpretation than spontaneous combustion,” reasoned Craig. “Hear me out and you will see that the facts are more in harmony with my less fanciful explanation. No, someone struck Lewis Langley down either in passion or in cold blood, and then, seeing what he had done, made a desperate effort to destroy the evidence of violence. Consider my next discovery.”

===Chinese===
「话虽如此，」哈林顿插嘴道，「还是回到本案的事实上来吧。这些事实，跟我的雪茄之说也好，跟《纪事报》的自燃之说也好，都完全吻合。您又怎么解释这些事实呢？」

「想必你们指的是：头颅烧焦，脖颈和上胸腔烧毁，而双臂双腿却毫发无损？」

「正是。还有，遗体是在一堆易燃家具中间发现的，家具却分毫未损。依我看，尽管有了这些关于血迹的有趣旁证，自燃之说仍旧大有市场。除开我自己的理论，就数燃烧之说最合乎事实了。」

「只要你们在心里，把已证明确系查明的一切要点过一遍——不是《纪事报》故事里添油加醋的那些——我想你们会同意，我的解释比自燃之说更合逻辑，」克雷格条分缕析地说。「听我讲完，你们便会看到，这些事实跟我这个较少奇思异想的解释更相吻合。不，是有人在一怒之下、或是蓄意冷血地击倒了刘易斯·兰利，随后眼见自己干下的事，便孤注一掷地想要毁掉暴行的痕迹。且听我的下一项发现。」

===Original===
Kennedy placed the five glasses which I had carefully sealed and labelled on the table before us.

“The next step,” he said, “was to find out whether any articles of clothing in the house showed marks that might be suspected of being blood-spots. And here I must beg the pardon of all in the room for intruding in their private wardrobes. But in this crisis it was absolutely necessary, and under such circumstances I never let ceremony stand before justice.

“In these five glasses on the table I have the washings of spots from the clothing worn by Tom, Mr. James Langley, Junior, Harrington Brown, and Doctor Putnam. I am not going to tell you which is which﻿—indeed I merely have them marked, and I do not know them myself. But Mr. Jameson has the marks with the names opposite on a piece of paper in his pocket. I am simply going to proceed with the tests to see if any of the stains on the coats were of blood.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪把那五只杯子摆到我们面前的桌上——都是我细心封口、编了号的。

「下一步，」他说，「是查明宅中是否有什么衣物上带有疑似血迹的斑痕。为此，我必须恳请屋里各位恕罪——我擅动了诸位的私人衣柜。但值此危局，这是万不得已；在这种时候，我从不让礼数挡在正义前头。

「桌上这五只杯子里，装的是从汤姆、詹姆斯·兰利先生、小詹、哈林顿·布朗和帕特南医生所穿衣物上取下的斑点洗液。我不打算告诉你们哪只是哪只——说实话，我只给它们编了号，连我自己也不知道谁是谁。但詹姆森先生口袋里有一张纸条，编号对着名字。我这就动手检验，看看外套上的污渍里，究竟有没有血。」

===Original===
Just then Doctor Putnam interposed. “One question, Professor Kennedy. It is a comparatively easy thing to recognise a bloodstain, but it is difficult, usually impossible, to tell whether the blood is that of a man or of an animal. I recall that we were all in our hunting-jackets that day, had been all day. Now, in the morning there had been an operation on one of the horses at the stable, and I assisted the veterinary from town. I may have got a spot or two of blood on my coat from that operation. Do I understand that this test would show that?”

“No,” replied Craig, “this test would not show that. Other tests would, but not this. But if the spot of human blood were less than the size of a pinhead, it would show﻿—it would show if the spot contained even so little as one twenty-thousandth of a gram of albumin. Blood from a horse, a deer, a sheep, a pig, a dog, could be obtained, but when the test was applied the liquid in which they were diluted would remain clear. No white precipitin, as it is called, would form. But let human blood, ever so diluted, be added to the serum of the inoculated rabbit, and the test is absolute.”

===Chinese===
就在这时，帕特南医生插了进来。「有个问题，肯尼迪教授。认出一块血斑并不太难，可要分辨那血是人的还是动物的，就难了，通常根本不可能。我记得那天我们都穿着猎装，穿了一整天。而那天早上，马厩里有一匹马动了手术，我给镇上来的兽医帮过手。我的外套上，说不定溅了一两滴手术的血。请问这项检验，连这个也验得出来吗？」

「验不出来，」克雷格答道，「这项检验分辨不了这个。别的法子能，这个不能。但只要有一处人血，哪怕比针尖还小，它也验得出——哪怕斑痕里只含两万分之一克的白蛋白（albumin），它也照样显形。马血、鹿血、羊血、猪血、狗血，都可以取来一试，可一加检验，稀释它们的液体依然清澈，不会形成那种所谓的白色沉淀素（precipitin）。但只要是人血，无论稀释到什么地步，一碰到接种过的兔子的血清，这检验便是绝对灵验的。」

===Original===
A deathlike silence seemed to pervade the room. Kennedy slowly and deliberately began to test the contents of the glasses. Dropping into each, as he broke the seal, some of the serum of the rabbit, he waited a moment to see if any change occurred.

It was thrilling. I think no one could have gone through that fifteen minutes without having it indelibly impressed on his memory. I recall thinking as Kennedy took each glass, “Which is it to be, guilt or innocence, life or death?” Could it be possible that a man’s life might hang on such a slender thread? I knew Kennedy was too accurate and serious to deceive us. It was not only possible, it was actually a fact.

The first glass showed no reaction. Someone had been vindicated.

The second was neutral likewise﻿—another person in the room had been proved innocent.

The third﻿—no change. Science had released a third.

===Chinese===
死一般的寂静仿佛弥漫了整个房间。肯尼迪缓慢而沉着地开始检验杯中之物。他每启封一只，便滴入一些那只兔子的血清，静候片刻，看有没有变化发生。

那真是惊心动魄。我想，谁经历了那十五分钟，都会把它不可磨灭地刻在记忆里。我记得，肯尼迪每端起一只杯子，我就在想：「这一只，定的是罪还是无辜，是生还是死？」一个人的性命，难道真能悬在这样一根细若游丝的线上？我深知肯尼迪行事精确、为人郑重，绝不会诓我们。这不仅可能，而且就是事实。

第一只杯子没有反应。有人洗清了嫌疑。

第二只同样毫无动静——屋里又有一人被证明清白。

第三只——没有变化。科学又开脱了第三个人。

===Original===
The fourth﻿—

Almost it seemed as if the record in my pocket burned﻿—spontaneously﻿—so intense was my feeling. There in the glass was that fatal, telltale white precipitate.

“My God, it’s the milk ring!” whispered Tom close to my ear.

Hastily Kennedy dropped the serum into the fifth. It remained as clear as crystal.

My hand trembled as it touched the envelope containing my record of the names.

===Chinese===
第四只——

我口袋里那张记录纸，仿佛也在燃烧——自发地燃烧——我的心情就是这般灼热。那只杯子里，赫然是那要命的、泄底的白色沉淀。

「我的天，是乳环（milk ring）！」汤姆凑在我耳边悄声说。

肯尼迪急忙往第五只杯子里滴下血清。杯中液体澄清如水晶，纹丝不变。

我的手触到那只装着名单记录的信封时，抖个不停。

===Original===
“The person who wore the coat with that bloodstain on it,” declared Kennedy solemnly, “was the person who struck Lewis Langley down, who choked him and then dragged his scarcely dead body across the floor and obliterated the marks of violence in the blazing log fire. Jameson, whose name is opposite the sign on this glass?”

I could scarcely tear the seal to look at the paper in the envelope. At last I unfolded it, and my eye fell on the name opposite the fatal sign. But my mouth was dry, and my tongue refused to move. It was too much like reading a death-sentence. With my finger on the name I faltered an instant.

Tom leaned over my shoulder and read it to himself. “For Heaven’s sake, Jameson,” he cried, “let the ladies retire before you read the name.”

===Chinese===
「那件带血外套的主人，」肯尼迪郑重宣布，「就是击倒刘易斯·兰利的人，就是扼住他、又把他那几乎刚断气的躯体拖过地板、再在熊熊柴火中毁去暴行痕迹的人。詹姆森，这只杯子上的记号，对着的是谁的名字？」

我几乎撕不开封口去掏信封里的纸条。好不容易展开来，目光落在那要命记号对着的名字上。可我口干舌燥，舌头怎么也不听使唤。这实在太像宣读死刑判决了。我的手指按着那个名字，踌躇了一瞬。

汤姆从我肩后探过头去，自己先看了。「看在老天的份上，詹姆森，」他喊道，「先让女士们退下去，再念那个名字。」

===Original===
“It’s not necessary,” said a thick voice. “We quarrelled over the estate. My share’s mortgaged up to the limit, and Lewis refused to lend me more even until I could get Isabelle happily married. Now Lewis’s goes to an outsider﻿—Harrington, boy, take care of Isabelle, fortune or no fortune. Good﻿—”

Someone seized James Langley’s arm as he pressed an automatic revolver to his temple. He reeled like a drunken man and dropped the gun on the floor with an oath.

“Beaten again,” he muttered. “Forgot to move the ratchet from ‘safety’ to ‘fire.’ ”

===Chinese===
「不必了。」一个含混的声音说道。「我们是为遗产吵的架。我名下的份额已经抵押到了顶，刘易斯连让我撑到把伊莎贝尔风风光光嫁出去，都不肯再借钱给我。如今刘易斯的那份，倒要落进外人口袋了——哈林顿，好孩子，照顾好伊莎贝尔，有钱没钱都一样。好了——」

詹姆斯·兰利把一支自动手枪顶上太阳穴时，有人一把攥住了他的手臂。他像个醉汉似的踉跄了一下，咒骂着把枪摔在了地板上。

「又输了，」他喃喃道。「忘了把棘轮从『保险』拨到『击发』了。」

===Original===
Like a madman he wrenched himself loose from us, sprang through the door, and darted upstairs. “I’ll show you some combustion!” he shouted back fiercely.

Kennedy was after him like a flash. “The will!” he cried.

We literally tore the door off its hinges and burst into James Langley’s room. He was bending eagerly over the fireplace. Kennedy made a flying leap at him. Just enough of the will was left unburned to be admitted to probate.

===Chinese===
他疯子一般从我们手里挣脱，蹿出门去，飞奔上楼。「我让你们瞧瞧什么叫燃烧！」他回过头来凶狠地吼道。

肯尼迪闪电般追了上去。「遗嘱！」他喊道。

我们简直是把门从合页上硬拽了下来，冲进詹姆斯·兰利的房间。他正急切地俯身对着壁炉。肯尼迪飞身扑了过去。遗嘱残留未烧的部分，恰好还够呈交法庭，认证生效。

## The Artificial Paradise / 人造天堂

===Original===
It was, I recall, at that period of the late unpleasantness in the little Central American republic of Vespuccia, when things looked darkest for American investors, that I hurried home one evening to Kennedy, bursting with news.

===Chinese===
我记得，那正是在中美洲小共和国维斯佩西亚（Vespuccia）「不久前那场乱子」闹得最凶、美国投资人处境最黑暗的当口，一天傍晚，我揣着满肚子新闻，急匆匆赶回家来找肯尼迪。

===Original===
By way of explanation, I may add that during the rubber boom Kennedy had invested in stock of a rubber company in Vespuccia, and that its value had been shrinking for some time with that elasticity which a rubber band shows when one party suddenly lets go his end. Kennedy had been in danger of being snapped rather hard by the recoil, and I knew he had put in an order with his broker to sell and take his loss when a certain figure was reached. My news was a first ray of light in an otherwise dark situation, and I wanted to advise him to cancel the selling order and stick for a rise.

===Chinese===
这里不妨补一句背景：橡胶热那阵子，肯尼迪买了一家维斯佩西亚橡胶公司的股票，而那行情缩水已有相当时日，缩得极有「弹性」——活像橡皮筋一头被人猛然撒了手。肯尼迪大有被回弹的鞭梢狠狠抽上一记的危险，我知道他已在经纪人那儿挂了卖单，跌到某个价位便止损认赔。我带来的新闻，算是漆黑一片里透出的头一线光，我想劝他撤掉卖单，持股待涨。

===Original===
Accordingly I hurried unceremoniously into our apartment with the words on my lips before I had fairly closed the door. “What do you think, Craig” I shouted. “It is rumoured that the revolutionists have captured half a million dollars from the government and are sending it to﻿—” I stopped short. I had no idea that Kennedy had a client, and a girl, too.

===Chinese===
于是我顾不得礼数，一头闯进我们的公寓，门还没关严，话已经出了口。「你猜怎么着，克雷格？」我嚷道。「据说革命党从政府手里夺下了五十万美元，正把这批钱运往——」我猛地收住话头。我万万没有想到，肯尼迪那里坐着一位委托人，而且还是位姑娘。

===Original===
With a hastily mumbled apology I checked myself and backed out toward my own room. I may as well confess that I did not retreat very fast, however. Kennedy’s client was not only a girl, but a very pretty one, I found, as she turned her head quickly at my sudden entrance and betray a lively interest at the mention of the revolution. She was a Latin-American, and the Latin-American type of feminine beauty is fascinating﻿—at least to me. I did not retreat very fast.

===Chinese===
我含混地嘟哝一句抱歉，收住话，朝自己的房间退去。不过我不妨坦白：我退得实在不快。肯尼迪的委托人不但是位姑娘，还是位十分标致的姑娘——她被我这突然一闯惊得霍然回头，一听人提起革命，眼里顿时燃起热切的兴味，这我都看在了眼里。她是位拉丁美洲人，而拉丁美洲型的女子之美自有其迷人的魔力——至少迷得住我。我退得实在不快。

===Original===
As I hoped, Kennedy rose to the occasion. “Miss Guerrero,” he said, “let me introduce Mr. Jameson, who has helped me very much in solving some of my most difficult cases. Miss Guerrero’s father, Walter, is the owner of a plantation which sells its product to the company I am interested in.”

===Chinese===
如我所愿，肯尼迪从容接住了场面。「格雷罗小姐（Miss Guerrero），」他说，「请允许我介绍詹姆森先生，我好几桩最棘手的案子，多亏他帮着才破的。沃尔特，格雷罗小姐的父亲拥有一座种植园，园里的出产正是卖给我入股的那家公司的。」

===Original===
She bowed graciously, but there was a moment of embarrassment until Kennedy came to the rescue.

===Chinese===
她落落大方地欠身还礼，但一时不免尴尬，直到肯尼迪再次出来解围。

===Original===
“I shall need Mr. Jameson in handling your case, Miss Guerrero,” he explained. “Would it be presuming to ask you to repeat to him briefly what you have already told me about the mysterious disappearance of your father? Perhaps some additional details will occur to you, things that you may consider trivial, but which, I assure you, may be of the utmost importance.”

===Chinese===
「办您这个案子，我会用得上詹姆森先生，格雷罗小姐，」他解释道，「可否冒昧请您把方才对我讲过的、令尊神秘失踪的经过，对他再简要讲一遍？也许您会顺势想起一些细节——那些您觉得琐碎、我却敢说可能至关重要的事情。」

===Original===
She assented, and in a low, tremulous, musical voice bravely went through her story.

===Chinese===
她应允了，用一种低微、颤动却如乐音般好听的声音，强自镇定地讲起了自己的遭遇。

===Original===
“We come,” she began, “my father and I﻿—for my mother died when I was a little girl﻿—we come from the northern part of Vespuccia, where foreign capitalists are much interested in the introduction of a new rubber plant. I am an only child and have been the constant companion of my father for years, ever since I could ride a pony, going with him about our hacienda and on business trips to Europe and the States.

===Chinese===
「我们，」她开口道，「我父亲和我——我母亲在我小时候便去世了——我们来自维斯佩西亚北部，那里的外国资本家正热衷于引进一种新的橡胶植物。我是独女，从能骑小马驹的年纪起，多年来一直是父亲形影不离的伴儿，跟着他在我们的庄园（hacienda）里巡看，也跟着他去欧洲和美国出差。

===Original===
“I may as well say at the start, Mr. Jameson, that although my father is a large landowner, he has very liberal political views and is deeply in sympathy with the revolution that is now going on in Vespuccia. In fact, we were forced to flee very early in the trouble, and as there seemed to be more need of his services here in New York than in any of the neighbouring countries, we came here. So you see that if the revolution is not successful his estate will probably be confiscated and we shall be penniless. He is the agent﻿—the head of the junta, I suppose you would call it﻿—here in New York.”

===Chinese===
「詹姆森先生，我不妨一开始就说明：虽然我父亲是个大地主，他的政治见解却十分开明，对眼下维斯佩西亚正进行的革命深为同情。事变刚起，我们便被迫出逃，而纽约比邻近各国都更用得着他，我们就来了这里。所以您明白，革命若是失败，他的产业多半要被充公，我们便一文不名了。他就是这里的代理人——我想你们会管它叫『洪塔』（junta）——的头目，坐镇纽约。」

===Original===
“Engaged in purchasing arms and ammunition,” put in Kennedy, as she paused, “and seeing that they are shipped safely to New Orleans as agricultural machinery, where another agent receives them and attends to their safe transit across the Gulf.”

===Chinese===
「专管采购军火弹药，」她略一停顿，肯尼迪接口道，「再设法把它们充作农业机器，安然运到新奥尔良（New Orleans），那边另有代理人接货，负责它们平安渡过墨西哥湾。」

===Original===
She nodded and after a moment resumed:

===Chinese===
她点了点头，稍停又接着说：

===Original===
“There is quite a little colony of Vespuccians here in New York, both revolutionists and government supporters. I suppose that neither of you has any idea of the intriguing that is going on under the peaceful surface right here in your own city. But there is much of it, more than even I know or can tell you. Well, my father lately has been acting very queerly. There is a group who meet frequently at the home of a Señora Mendez﻿—an insurrecto group, of course. I do not go, for they are all much older people than I. I know the señora well, but I prefer a different kind of person. My friends are younger and perhaps more radical, more in earnest about the future of Vespuccia.

===Chinese===
「纽约这儿聚着不小的一伙维斯佩西亚人，革命派、保政府派的都有。我想你们两位根本想不到，就在你们这座城市太平的表面底下，正进行着怎样的勾心斗角。那可多着呢，多到连我也不尽知晓，更说不全。喏，我父亲近来行事十分古怪。有一伙人常在门德斯夫人（Señora Mendez）家里聚会——当然是起义派（insurrecto）的一伙。我不去，因为那些人都比我年长得多。那位夫人我熟得很，可我喜欢的是另一路人。我的朋友们更年轻，兴许更激进，对维斯佩西亚的前途更较真。

===Original===
“For some weeks it has seemed to me that this Señora Mendez has had too much influence over my father. He does not seem like the same man he used to be. Indeed, some of the junta who do not frequent the house of the señora have remarked it. He seems moody, works by starts, then will neglect his work entirely. Often I see him with his eyes closed, apparently sitting quietly, oblivious to the progress of the cause﻿—the only cause now which can restore us our estate.

===Chinese===
「这几个星期以来，我总觉得这位门德斯夫人对我父亲的影响太深了。他不像从前那个人了。真的，洪塔里那些不常上那位夫人家的成员都这么议论。他郁郁寡欢，干事忽冷忽热，有时索性把正事整个撂下。我常见他闭着眼睛坐着，看似安安静静，实则对大业的进展浑然不觉——那可是如今唯一能替我们夺回产业的大业啊。

===Original===
“The other day we lost an entire shipment of arms﻿—the Secret Service captured them on the way from the warehouse on South Street to the steamer which was to take them to New Orleans. Only once before had it happened, when my father did not understand all the things to conceal. Then he was frantic for a week. But this time he seems not to care. Ah, señores,” she said, dropping her voice, “I fear there was some treachery there.”

===Chinese===
「前几天，我们整整一批军火全折了——特勤局（Secret Service）把它们截在了半路，就在南街（South Street）仓库到那艘原定开往新奥尔良的轮船之间。这种事从前只出过一回，那时我父亲还摸不清哪些地方需要遮掩，为那事他疯了似的急了一个星期。可这一回，他竟像是满不在乎。唉，先生们，」她压低声音说，「我怕那里头有内奸。」

===Original===
“Treachery?” I asked. “And have you any suspicions who might have played informer?”

===Chinese===
「内奸？」我问。「那您可怀疑过，是谁去充当告密者？」

===Original===
She hesitated. “I may as well tell you just what I suspect. I fear that the hold of Señora Mendez is somehow or other concerned with it all. I even have suspected that somehow she may be working in the pay of the government that she is a vampire, living on the secrets of the group who so trust her. I suspect anything, everybody﻿—that she is poisoning his mind, perhaps even whispering into his ear some siren proposal of amnesty and his estate again, if he will but do what she asks. My poor father﻿—I must save him from himself if it is necessary. Argument has no effect with him. He merely answers that the señora is a talented and accomplished woman, and laughs a vacant laugh when I hint to him to beware. I hate her.”

===Chinese===
她迟疑了一下。「我不妨把我心里的怀疑原原本本告诉你们。我怕门德斯夫人的掌控，同这一切脱不了干系。我甚至疑心，她说不定领着政府的津贴在办事——她是个吸血鬼，专靠吮吸那伙如此信任她的人的秘密过活。我什么都疑，人人都疑——疑她在毒化父亲的心智，甚至疑她凑在父亲耳边，吹着海妖一般甜的风：只要肯依她，特赦有了，产业也能失而复得。我可怜的父亲——必要的时候，我必须把他从他自己手里救出来。跟他讲道理全然无用。他只回一句，说那位夫人是个有才有识的女人；我暗示他要多提防，他也只是空落落地笑一笑。我恨她。

===Original===
The fiery animosity of her dark eyes boded ill, I felt, for the señora. But it flashed over me that perhaps, after all, the señora was not a traitress, but had simply been scheming to win the heart and hence the hacienda of the great landowner, when he came into possession of his estate if the revolution proved successful.

===Chinese===
她那双黑眼睛里烧起的恨火，我觉着，对那位夫人可不是好兆头。但我心头忽地一闪：说不定那位夫人终究算不得叛徒，不过是在处心积虑地博取这位大地主的心——进而博取他的庄园，只等革命成功、产业重归他手的那一天罢了。

===Original===
“And finally,” she concluded, keeping back the tears by an heroic effort, “last night he left our apartment, promising to return early in the evening. It is now twenty-four hours, and I have heard not a word from him. It is the first time in my life that we have ever been separated so long.”

===Chinese===
「最后，」她强忍着泪，硬生生把眼泪逼了回去，收尾道，「昨天晚上他离开我们的公寓，说好天一擦黑就回来。如今二十四个小时过去了，我却连他一个字也没有得到。长这么大，我们父女还从来不曾分开这么久。」

===Original===
“And you have no idea where he could have gone?” asked Craig.

===Chinese===
「那您想不出他可能去哪儿了吗？」克雷格问。

===Original===
“Only what I have learned from Señor Torreon, another member of the junta. Señor Torreon said this morning that he left the home of Señora Mendez last night about ten o’clock in company with my father. He says they parted at the subway, as they lived on different branches of the road. Professor Kennedy,” she added, springing up and clasping her hands tightly in an appeal that was irresistible, “you know what steps to take to find him. I trust all to you﻿—even the calling on the police, though I think it would be best if we could get along without them. Find my father, señores, and when we come into our own again you shall not regret that you befriended a lonely girl in a strange city, surrounded by intrigue and danger.” There were tears in her eyes as she stood swaying before us.

===Chinese===
「我只从托雷翁先生（Señor Torreon）那里打听到一点，他是洪塔的另一位成员。托雷翁先生说，昨晚十点钟左右，他同我父亲一道离开门德斯夫人家。他说两人是在地铁口分手的，因为他们住在地铁不同的支线沿线。肯尼迪教授，」她补充道，一边霍地站起身，双手紧紧交握，那恳求叫人无从拒绝，「您知道该走哪些步骤去找他。我一切都托付给您——连报警也包括在内，尽管我想，最好还是能不动用警察。找到我的父亲吧，先生们，等我们夺回属于我们的一切，你们绝不会后悔曾帮过一个孤身女孩——在这座举目无亲的城市里，被阴谋与危险团团围住的女孩。」她立在我们面前，身子微微发晃，眼里噙着泪。

===Original===
The tenseness of the appeal was broken by the sharp ringing of the telephone bell. Kennedy quickly took down the receiver.

===Chinese===
一阵骤响的电话铃，划破了这恳求的紧张。肯尼迪赶忙摘下听筒。

===Original===
“Your maid wishes to speak to you,” he said, handing the telephone to her.

===Chinese===
「您的女仆要同您讲话。」他说着，把电话递给她。

===Original===
Her face brightened with that nervous hope that springs in the human breast even in the blackest moments. “I told her if any message came for me she might find me here,” explained Miss Guerrero. “Yes, Juanita, what is it﻿—a message for me?”

===Chinese===
她的脸倏地一亮，亮出那种哪怕在最漆黑的关头也会在人心底冒出来的、忐忑不安的希望。「我吩咐过她，若有我的口信，到这儿找我便是。」格雷罗小姐解释道。「喂，胡安妮塔（Juanita），什么事——有我的口信？」

===Original===
My Spanish was not quite good enough to catch more than a word here and there in the low conversation, but I could guess from the haggard look which overspread her delicate face that the news was not encouraging.

===Chinese===
她们低声通着话，我的西班牙语不济，只零星听懂几个词，但从那张娇美面庞上骤然笼开的憔悴之色，我猜得出这消息不妙。

===Original===
“Oh!” she cried, “this is terrible﻿—terrible! What shall I do? Why did I come here? I don’t believe it. I don’t believe it.”

===Chinese===
「哦！」她叫道，「这太可怕了——太可怕了！我可怎么办？我干吗要来这儿？我不信。我不信。」

===Original===
“Don’t believe what, Miss Guerrero?” asked Kennedy reassuringly. “Trust me.”

===Chinese===
「不信什么，格雷罗小姐？」肯尼迪安抚道。「相信我。」

===Original===
“That he stole the money﻿—oh, what am I saying? You must not look for him﻿—you must forget that I have been here. No, I don’t believe it.”

===Chinese===
「不信他偷了那笔钱——哦，我在说什么呀？你们不能去找他——你们必须忘了我来过这里。不，我不信。」

===Original===
“What money?” asked Kennedy, disregarding her appeal to drop the case. “Remember, it may be better that we should know it now than the police later. We will respect your confidence.”

===Chinese===
「什么钱？」肯尼迪问，全不理会她撤销委托的恳求。「记住，与其让警察事后知晓，不如让我们现在就知道，兴许倒好些。我们绝不辜负您的信任。」

===Original===
“The junta had been notified a few days ago, they say, that a large sum﻿—five hundred thousand silver dollars﻿—had been captured from the government and was on its way to New York to be melted up as bullion at the subtreasury,” she answered, repeating what she had heard over the telephone as if in a dream. “Mr. Jameson referred to the rumour when he came in. I was interested, for I did not know the public had heard of it yet. The junta has just announced that the money is missing. As soon as the ship docked in Brooklyn this morning an agent appeared with the proper credentials from my father and a guard, and they took the money away. It has not been heard of since﻿—and they have no word from my father.”

===Chinese===
「他们说，洪塔几天前得到通知：一大笔款子——五十万银元——已从政府手里夺下，正在运往纽约的途中，要送到财政部分库（subtreasury）熔成银锭。」她答道，恍如梦中一般，复述着电话里听来的话。「詹姆森先生进门时提到的那个谣言，说的正是它。我上了心，因为我还不知道外界已经听到了风声。洪塔刚刚宣布，那笔钱不见了。今天早晨船一靠上布鲁克林的码头，就来了一个人，拿着我父亲签发的合格凭证，还带着一名警卫，他们把钱提走了。此后再无音讯——我父亲也杳无音信。」

===Original===
Her face was blanched as she realised what the situation was. Here she was, setting people to run down her own father, if the suspicions of the other members of the junta were to be credited.

===Chinese===
等她明白过来这是怎样一种处境，脸刷地惨白。瞧吧，倘若洪塔其他成员的怀疑作数，她这不是在雇人追查自己的父亲么。

===Original===
“You﻿—you do not think my father﻿—stole the money?” she faltered pitifully. “Say you do not think so.”

===Chinese===
「你们——你们不会以为，我父亲——偷了那笔钱吧？」她可怜巴巴地讷讷道。「你们说，不会这么想。」

===Original===
“I think nothing yet,” replied Kennedy in an even voice. “The first thing to do is to find him﻿—before the detectives of the junta do so.”

===Chinese===
「眼下我什么结论也没有，」肯尼迪平声答道，「头一件事是找到他——要赶在洪塔自己的侦探前头。」

===Original===
I felt a tinge﻿—I must confess it﻿—of jealousy as Kennedy stood beside her, clasping her hand in both of his and gazing earnestly down into the rich flush that now spread over her olive cheeks.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪立在她身旁，双手把她的手攥住，热切地俯视着她橄榄色双颊上漫开的浓浓红晕——我得承认，我心里泛起了一丝醋意。

===Original===
“Miss Guerrero,” he said, “you may trust me implicitly. If your father is alive I will do all that a man can do to find him. Let me act﻿—for the best. And,” he added, wheeling quickly toward me, “I know Mr. Jameson will do likewise.”

===Chinese===
「格雷罗小姐，」他说，「您尽管全心信任我。只要令尊还活着，凡人力所能及，我必竭尽一切把他找到。放手让我来办——一定往最好处办。」说着，他猛地转向我，补了一句：「我知道詹姆森先生也会同样尽力。」

===Original===
I was pulled two ways at once. I believed in Miss Guerrero, and yet the flight of her father and the removal of the bullion swallowed up, as it were, instantly, without so much as a trace in New York﻿—looked very black for him. And yet, as she placed her small hand tremblingly in mine to say goodbye, she won another knight to go forth and fight her battle for her, nor do I think that I am more than ordinarily susceptible, either.

===Chinese===
我一时被扯向两头。我相信格雷罗小姐，可她父亲的出走，连同那批转眼间在纽约吞没得无影无踪、连点渣都不剩的银锭——对他都大为不利。然而，当她道别时把小小的手颤巍巍放进我的掌心，她又赢得了一位骑士，愿为她出马征战——我倒不觉得自己比常人更多情善感。

===Original===
When she had gone, I looked hopelessly at Kennedy. How could we find a missing man in a city of four million people, find him without the aid of the police﻿—perhaps before the police could themselves find him?

===Chinese===
她走后，我一脸无望地望着肯尼迪。在一座四百万人的城市里，我们怎么去找一个失踪的人？不靠警察去找——说不定还要抢在警察前头找到他？

===Original===
Kennedy seemed to appreciate my perplexity as though he read my thoughts. “The first thing to do is to locate this Señor Torreon from whom the first information came,” he remarked as we left the apartment. “Miss Guerrero told me that he might possibly be found in an obscure boardinghouse in the Bronx where several members of the junta live. Let us try, anyway.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪像是看透了我的困惑，仿佛读出了我的心思。「头一件事，是找到那位最先送来消息的托雷翁先生。」我们离开公寓时，他说，「格雷罗小姐告诉我，他兴许能在布朗克斯（the Bronx）一家不起眼的寄宿公寓里找到，洪塔的几位成员都住在那儿。不管怎样，去试试。」

===Original===
Fortune favoured us to the extent that we did find Torreon at the address given. He made no effort to evade us, though I noted that he was an unprepossessing looking man﻿—undersized and a trifle over-stout, with an eye that never met yours as you talked with him. Whether it was that he was concealing something, or whether he was merely fearful that we might after all be United States Secret Service men, or whether it was simply a lack of command of English, he was uncommonly uncommunicative at first. He repeated sullenly the details of the disappearance of Guerrero, just as we had already heard them.

===Chinese===
运气待我们不薄，我们果然照地址找到了托雷翁。他无意躲我们，不过我看这人其貌不扬——个子矮小，身量微微发胖，同他说话时，那双眼睛从不与你的目光相接。不知是他藏着什么事，还是只怕我们终究是美国特勤局的人，又或者干脆是英文不灵，起初他嘴闭得死紧。他把格雷罗失踪的细节闷闷地重述了一遍，同我们已听到的毫无二致。

===Original===
“And you simply bade him goodbye as you got on a subway train and that is the last you ever saw of him?” repeated Kennedy.

===Chinese===
「就这么着，您上了地铁，同他道了声再见，那便是您见他的最后一面？」肯尼迪复述道。

===Original===
“Yes,” he replied.

===Chinese===
「是。」他答。

===Original===
“Did he seem to be worried, to have anything on his mind, to act queerly in any way?” asked Kennedy keenly.

===Chinese===
「他当时像是有心事？忧心忡忡？举止可有一丝反常？」肯尼迪敏锐地问。

===Original===
“No,” came the monosyllabic reply, and there was just that shade of hesitation about it that made me wish we had the apparatus we used in the Bond case for registering association time. Kennedy noticed it, and purposely dropped the line of inquiry in order not to excite Torreon’s suspicion.

===Chinese===
「没有。」回答只有一个字，可其中恰带着那么一丝迟疑，让我直希望手边有办邦德案（Bond case）时用过的那套记录联想时间（association time）的装置。肯尼迪察觉了，故意撂下这条问话，免得引起托雷翁的疑心。

===Original===
“I understand no word has been received from him at the headquarters on South Street today.”

===Chinese===
「据我了解，今天南街总部没有收到他的任何音信。」

===Original===
“None,” replied Torreon sharply.

===Chinese===
「没有。」托雷翁尖声答道。

===Original===
“And you have no idea where he could have gone after you left him last night?”

===Chinese===
「昨晚您同他分手之后，他可能去了哪儿，您一点也想不出？」

===Original===
“No, señor, none.”

===Chinese===
「想不出，先生，一点也没有。」

===Original===
