This answer was given, I thought, with suspicious quickness.

===Chinese===
我心想，这回答来得快得可疑。

===Original===
“You do not think that he could be concealed by Señora Mendez, then?” asked Kennedy quietly.

===Chinese===
「那么，您不认为他可能是被门德斯夫人藏起来了？」肯尼迪不动声色地问。

===Original===
The little man jumped forward with his eyes flashing. “No,” he hissed, checking this show of feeling as quickly as he could.

===Chinese===
小个子猛地抢前一步，眼里冒火。「不。」他嘶声道，随即又急忙把这失态敛住。

===Original===
“Well, then,” observed Kennedy, rising slowly, “I see nothing to do but to notify the police and have a general alarm sent out.”

===Chinese===
「那么好吧，」肯尼迪缓缓起身，说道，「我看别无他法，只好报告警察，请他们发出全面通缉了。」

===Original===
The fire died in the eyes of Torreon. “Do not do that, Señor,” he exclaimed. “Wait at least one day more. Perhaps he will appear. Perhaps he has only gone up to Bridgeport to see about some arms and cartridges﻿—who can tell? No, sir, do not call in the police, I beg you﻿—not yet. I myself will search for him. It may be I can get some word, some clue. If I can I will notify Miss Guerrero immediately.”

===Chinese===
托雷翁眼里的火气灭了。「千万别，先生。」他嚷道。「至少再等一天。说不定他自己会露面。说不定他只是上布里奇波特（Bridgeport）去看一批枪支弹药去了——谁说得准呢？不，先生，千万别叫警察，求您了——先别叫。我自己去找他。说不定我能打听到点消息、点线索。一旦有了收获，我立刻通知格雷罗小姐。」

===Original===
Kennedy turned suddenly. “Torreon,” he flashed quickly, “what do you suspect about that shipment of half a million silver dollars? Where did it go after it left the wharf?”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪猛地转过身。「托雷翁，」他疾声问道，「那批五十万银元，您是作何猜想的？它离开码头以后，去了哪儿？」

===Original===
Torreon kept his composure admirably. An enigma of a smile flitted over his mobile features as he shrugged his shoulders. “Ah,” he said simply, “then you have heard that the money is missing? Perhaps Guerrero has not gone to Bridgeport, after all!”

===Chinese===
托雷翁的镇定功夫堪称一绝。他耸耸肩，一张生动的脸上掠过一丝谜样的笑。「啊，」他淡淡地说，「这么说，你们已经听说那笔钱丢了？也许格雷罗压根儿就没去布里奇波特！」

===Original===
“On condition that I do not notify the police yet﻿—will you take us to visit Señora Mendez, and let us learn from her what she knows of this strange case?”

===Chinese===
「条件是我暂且不报警——您肯带我们去拜访门德斯夫人，让我们从她口中听听，她对这桩奇案都知道些什么吗？」

===Original===
Torreon was plainly cornered. He sat for a moment biting his nails nervously and fidgeting in his chair. “It shall be as you wish,” he assented at length.

===Chinese===
托雷翁显然被逼进了死角。他坐了片刻，紧张地咬着指甲，在椅子里如坐针毡。「就照您的意思办。」他终于应承了。

===Original===
“We are to go,” continued Kennedy, “merely as friends of yours, you understand? I want to ask questions in my own way, and you are not to﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我们去，」肯尼迪接着说，「只以你朋友的身份，明白吗？我要按我自己的路数发问，你不许——」

===Original===
“Yes, yes,” he agreed. “Wait. I will tell her we are coming,” and he reached for the telephone.

===Chinese===
「是，是。」他连声应道。「等等，我打电话告诉她说我们要来。」说着他伸手去够电话。

===Original===
“No,” interrupted Kennedy. “I prefer to go with you unexpected. Put down the telephone. Otherwise, I may as well notify my friend Inspector O’Connor of the Central Office and go up with him.”

===Chinese===
「不必。」肯尼迪打断他。「我更想出其不意，同你一道登门。放下电话。不然的话，我不如索性知会我的朋友、中央办公厅的奥康纳警督，同他一起上门。」

===Original===
Torreon let the receiver fall back in its socket, and I caught just a glimpse of the look of hate and suspicion which crossed his face as he turned toward Kennedy. When he spoke it was as suavely as if he himself were the one who had planned this little excursion.

===Chinese===
托雷翁任听筒落回叉簧，他转向肯尼迪的一瞬，我恰好瞥见一丝又恨又疑的神色掠过他的脸。待他开口，已是满口的圆滑周到，倒仿佛张罗这趟小小出访的正是他自己。

===Original===
“It shall be as you wish,” he said, leading the way out to the crosstown surface cars.

===Chinese===
「就照您的意思办。」他说着，领着我们出门，去乘横贯城区的地面电车。

===Original===
Señora Mendez received us politely, and we were ushered into a large music-room in her apartment. There were several people there already. They were seated in easy chairs about the room.

===Chinese===
门德斯夫人客客气气地接待了我们，把我们让进她公寓里一间宽大的音乐室。屋里已有几位客人，分坐在四下的安乐椅里。

===Original===
One of the ladies was playing on the piano as we entered. It was a curious composition﻿—very rhythmic, with a peculiar thread of monotonous melody running through it.

===Chinese===
我们进去时，一位女士正在弹钢琴。那是支古怪的曲子——节奏感极强，一支单调的旋律如线一般贯穿始终。

===Original===
The playing ceased, and all eyes were fixed on us. Kennedy kept very close to Torreon, apparently for the purpose of frustrating any attempt at a whispered conversation with the señora.

===Chinese===
琴声一停，所有人的目光都钉在我们身上。肯尼迪紧贴着托雷翁，明摆着是要掐断他同夫人咬耳朵递话的任何机会。

===Original===
The guests rose and with courtly politeness bowed as Señora Mendez presented two friends of Señor Torreon, Señor Kennedy and Señor Jameson. We were introduced in turn to Señor and Señora Alvardo, Señor Gonzales, Señorita Reyes, and the player, Señora Barrios.

===Chinese===
客人们起身，风度翩翩地欠身为礼，门德斯夫人介绍说，这两位是托雷翁先生的朋友——肯尼迪先生和詹姆森先生。随后我们被逐一介绍给阿尔瓦拉多先生及夫人（Señor and Señora Alvardo）、冈萨雷斯先生（Señor Gonzales）、雷耶斯小姐（Señorita Reyes），还有那位抚琴的巴里奥斯夫人（Señora Barrios）。

===Original===
It was a peculiar situation, and for want of something better to say I commented on the curious character of the music we had overheard as we entered.

===Chinese===
场面有些微妙，我一时寻不出更好的话头，便就我们进门时无意听到的琴曲议论了几句，说它曲风古怪。

===Original===
The señora smiled, and was about to speak when a servant entered, bearing a tray full of little cups with a steaming liquid, and in a silver dish some curious, round, brown, disc-like buttons, about an inch in diameter and perhaps a quarter of an inch thick. Torreon motioned frantically to the servant to withdraw, but Kennedy was too quick for him. Interposing himself between Torreon and the servant, he made way for her to enter.

===Chinese===
夫人微微一笑，正待开口，一个女仆进来了，端着满满一盘冒着热气的小杯，银碟里还盛着些古怪的、圆滚滚的褐色盘状「纽扣」，直径约一英寸，厚度约莫四分之一英寸。托雷翁拼命朝女仆打手势，要她退下，肯尼迪却比他快了一步，一身子插到托雷翁与女仆之间，给她让开了进屋的路。

===Original===
“You were speaking of the music,” replied Señora Mendez to me in rich, full tones. “Yes, it is very curious. It is a song of the Kiowa Indians of New Mexico which Señora Barrios has endeavoured to set to music so that it can be rendered on the piano. Señora Barrios and myself fled from Vespuccia to Mexico at the start of our revolution, and when the Mexican government ordered us to leave on account of our political activity we merely crossed the line to the United States, in New Mexico. It was there that we ran across this very curious discovery. The monotonous beat of that melody you heard is supposed to represent the beating of the tom-toms of the Indians during their mescal rites. We are having a mescal evening here, whiling away the hours of exile from our native Vespuccia.”

===Chinese===
「您方才说到那音乐，」门德斯夫人以丰润饱满的嗓音对我答道。「是啊，它确实很奇特。那是新墨西哥州凯奥瓦印第安人（Kiowa）的一支歌，巴里奥斯夫人试着把它编配成曲，好搬到钢琴上弹。革命初起时，巴里奥斯夫人同我一道从维斯佩西亚逃到墨西哥，后来墨西哥政府因我们的政治活动下逐客令，我们便索性越境进了美国，落在新墨西哥州。就是在那儿，我们撞见了这桩顶新奇的发现。你方才听到的那单调的节拍，据说是在模拟印第安人举行麦斯卡尔（mescal）祭祀时手鼓（tom-tom）的敲击。今晚我们正办一场麦斯卡尔晚会，排遣远离故土维斯佩西亚的流亡光阴。」

===Original===
“Mescal?” I repeated blankly at first, then feeling a nudge from Kennedy, I added hastily: “Oh, yes, to be sure. I think I have heard of it. It’s a Mexican drink, is it not? I have never had the pleasure of tasting it or of tasting that other drink, pulque﻿—poolkay﻿—did I get the accent right?”

===Chinese===
「麦斯卡尔？」我先是一脸茫然，随即觉出肯尼迪在暗中碰我，忙不迭添上一句：「哦，对，当然。我想我听说过。是墨西哥的一种酒吧？我一直无缘品尝它，也无缘品尝另外那种酒——普尔克（pulque）——普尔凯——重音我念对了吗？」

===Original===
I felt another, sharper nudge from Kennedy, and knew that I had only made matters worse.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪又碰了我一下，这回更重，我知道自己越描越黑了。

===Original===
“Mr. Jameson,” he hastened to remark, “confounds this mescal of the Indians with the drink of the same name that is common in Mexico.”

===Chinese===
「詹姆森先生，」他赶忙说道，「把印第安人的这种麦斯卡尔，同墨西哥常见的那种同名酒弄混了。」

===Original===
“Oh,” she laughed, to my great relief, “but this mescal is something quite different. The Mexican drink mescal is made from the maguey-plant and is a frightfully horrid thing that sends the peon out of his senses and makes him violent. Mescal as I mean it is a little shrub, a god, a cult, a religion.”

===Chinese===
「哦，」她笑了——我大大松了口气，「不过这麦斯卡尔可完全是另一回事。墨西哥的那种麦斯卡尔酒是拿龙舌兰（maguey）酿的，是种顶顶可怕的玩意儿，能把雇农（peon）喝得神志全无、撒起野来。我说的麦斯卡尔呢，是一株小灌木，是一位神祇，一个教派，一门宗教。」

===Original===
“Yes,” assented Kennedy; “discovered by those same Kiowa Indians, was it not?”

===Chinese===
「不错，」肯尼迪附和道，「正是那些凯奥瓦印第安人发现的，对吧？」

===Original===
“Perhaps,” she admitted, raising her beautiful shoulders in polite deprecation. “The mescal religion, we found, has spread very largely in New Mexico and Arizona among the Indians, and with the removal of the Kiowas to the Indian reservation it has been adopted by other tribes even, I have heard, as far north as the Canadian border.”

===Chinese===
「也许吧。」她承认，谦和地耸了耸美丽的肩膀。「我们发现，麦斯卡尔教在新墨西哥州和亚利桑那州的印第安人中传播极广，后来凯奥瓦人迁往印第安人保留地，别的部落竟也皈依了它——我听说，往北一直传到了加拿大边境。」

===Original===
“Is that so?” asked Kennedy. “I understood that the United States government had forbidden the importation of the mescal plant and its sale to the Indians under severe penalties.”

===Chinese===
「是这样吗？」肯尼迪问。「据我所知，美国政府已明令禁止麦斯卡尔植株进口，也禁止把它卖给印第安人，违者重罚。」

===Original===
“It has, sir,” interposed Alvardo, who had joined us, “but still the mescal cult grows secretly. For my part, I think it might be more wise for your authorities to look to the whiskey and beer that unscrupulous persons are selling. Señor Jameson,” he added, turning to me, “will you join us in a little cup of this artificial paradise, as one of your English writers﻿—Havelock Ellis, I think﻿—has appropriately called it?”

===Chinese===
「确有其事，先生，」这时凑过来的阿尔瓦拉多插话道，「可麦斯卡尔教照样在暗地里滋长。依我看，贵国当局与其操心这个，不如去查一查那些奸商贩卖的威士忌和啤酒。詹姆森先生，」他转向我，又道，「可否赏光，陪我们饮上一小杯这『人造天堂』（artificial paradise）——贵国一位作家，我想是哈夫洛克·霭理士（Havelock Ellis）——给它起的这个名目，倒是贴切得很？」

===Original===
I glanced dubiously at Kennedy as Señora Mendez took one of the little buttons out of the silver tray. Carefully paring the fuzzy tuft of hairs off the top of it﻿—it looked to me very much like the tip of a cactus plant, which, indeed, it was﻿—she rolled it into a little pellet and placed it in her mouth, chewing it slowly like a piece of chicle.

===Chinese===
门德斯夫人从银碟里拈起一枚小「纽扣」时，我迟疑地瞟了肯尼迪一眼。只见她细心地削去顶上那一撮绒毛——在我看来活脱是仙人掌的芽尖，事实上也确是——把它搓成一个小丸，送进口中，像嚼一块糖胶（chicle）似的慢慢嚼着。

===Original===
“Watch me; do just as I do,” whispered Kennedy to me at a moment when no one was looking.

===Chinese===
「看着我，照我的样子做。」趁没人留意，肯尼迪对我耳语。

===Original===
The servant advanced towards us with the tray.

===Chinese===
女仆端着托盘朝我们走来。

===Original===
“The mescal plant,” explained Alvardo, pointing at the little discs, “grows precisely like these little buttons which you see here. It is a species of cactus which rises only half an inch or so from the ground. The stem is surrounded by a clump of blunt leaves which give it its button shape, and on the top you will see still the tuft of filaments, like a cactus. It grows in the rocky soil in many places in the state of Jalisco, though only recently has it become known to science. The Indians, when they go out to gather it, simply lop off these little ends as they peep above the earth, dry them, keep what they wish for their own use, and sell the rest for what is to them a fabulous sum. Some people chew the buttons, while a few have lately tried making an infusion or tea out of them. Perhaps to a beginner I had better recommend the infusion.”

===Chinese===
「麦斯卡尔这种植物，」阿尔瓦拉多指着那些小圆饼解释道，「长起来就同您眼前这些小纽扣一模一样。它是一种仙人掌，只从地里冒出半英寸上下。茎的四周簇拥着一圈钝圆的叶子，使它成了纽扣的形状，顶上您还能看到那撮绒须，也是仙人掌的模样。在哈利斯科州（Jalisco）许多地方的岩石土壤里都有它生长，只是近来才为科学界所知。印第安人出去采的时候，只把这些刚钻出土的小芽头削下来，晒干，留足自用的，余下的卖出去——那价钱对他们是天文数字。有人直接嚼这种纽扣，近来也有少数人试着拿它泡浸剂，或者泡茶。对初次尝试的人，我看还是推荐浸剂为妙。」

===Original===
I had scarcely swallowed the bitter, almost nauseous decoction than I began to feel my heart action slowing up and my pulse beating fuller and stronger. The pupils of my eyes expanded as with a dose of belladonna; at least, I could see that Kennedy’s did, and so mine must have done the same.

===Chinese===
那碗煎剂苦得几乎令人作呕，我刚咽下去，就觉出心跳缓了下来，脉搏却跳得更沉、更有力。瞳孔像服了一剂颠茄（belladonna）似的放大了——至少，我看见肯尼迪的瞳孔是这样，我的想必也不例外。

===Original===
I seemed to feel an elated sense of superiority﻿—really I almost began to feel that it was I, not Kennedy, who counted most in this investigation. I have since learned that this is the common experience of mescal-users, this sense of elation; but the feeling of physical energy and intellectual power soon wore off, and I found myself glad to recline in my easy chair, as the rest did, in silent indolence.

===Chinese===
我仿佛生出一种飘飘然的优越感——说真的，我几乎开始觉得，这桩案子里顶要紧的人物是我，不是肯尼迪。后来我才知道，这种亢扬之感，是麦斯卡尔服用者的共同体验；只是那股体力与智性上的豪情很快就退了潮，我发觉自己巴不得像旁人一样瘫进安乐椅里，慵懒得一言不发。

===Original===
Still, the display that followed for an enchanted hour or so was such as I find it hopeless to describe in language which shall convey to others the beauty and splendour of what I saw.

===Chinese===
然而，接下来大约一个钟头里那场如痴如醉的展演，我已断了用言语转述他人的念头——任何字句都载不动我所见的美与壮丽。

===Original===
I picked up a book lying on the table before me. A pale blue-violet shadow floated across the page before me, leaving an afterimage of pure colour that was indescribable. I laid down the book and closed my eyes. A confused riot of images and colours like a kaleidoscope crowded before me, at first indistinct, but, as I gazed with closed yes, more and more definite. Golden and red and green jewels seemed to riot before me. I bathed my hands in inconceivable riches of beauty such as no art-glass worker has ever produced. All discomfort ceased. I had no desire to sleep﻿—in fact, was hypersensitive. But it was a real effort to open my eyes; to tear myself away from the fascinating visions of shapes and colours.

===Chinese===
我拿起面前桌上的一本书。一抹淡蓝紫的影子从书页上飘过，留下一道纯色彩的残像，无可名状。我放下书，闭上眼睛。一幕幕图像与色彩如万花筒般纷乱地涌到眼前，起初模糊，可当我阖眼凝望，它们越来越清晰。金、红、绿的宝石仿佛在我眼前狂欢。我的双手浸在难以想象的美的富矿里，任何彩绘玻璃工匠都不曾造出那样的美。一切不适都止息了。我毫无睡意——恰恰相反，敏感到极点。可要睁开眼睛，却成了一桩真正的苦差；要把自身从那形状与色彩的迷人幻象里硬扯出来，谈何容易。

===Original===
At last I did open my eyes to gaze at the gas jets of the chandelier as they flickered. They seemed to send out waves, expanding and contracting, waves of colour. The shadows of the room were highly coloured and constantly changing as the light changed.

===Chinese===
我终于睁眼，去望枝形吊灯上摇曳的煤气灯嘴。它们仿佛送出一圈圈波，胀了又缩，全是色彩的波。屋里的影子也染着浓色，随着灯光的变化变个不停。

===Original===
Señora Barrios began lightly to play on the piano the transposed Kiowa song, emphasising the notes that represented the drumbeats. Strange as it may seem, the music translated itself into pure colour﻿—and the rhythmic beating of the time seemed to aid the process. I thought of the untutored Indians as they sat in groups about the flickering campfire while others beat the tom-toms and droned the curious melody. What were the visions of the red man, I wondered, as he chewed his mescal button and the medicine man prayed to Hikori, the cactus god, to grant a “beautiful intoxication?”

===Chinese===
巴里奥斯夫人轻轻奏起那支移了调的凯奥瓦歌，把代表鼓点的音符一个个加重。说来奇怪，那音乐竟自行化作了纯粹的色彩——节拍一下一下，似乎还在帮着这个转化。我想起那些未经开化的印第安人，成群围坐在忽闪的营火边，另一些人敲着手鼓，哼着那支古怪的曲子。那红种人嚼着麦斯卡尔纽扣、巫医（medicine man）向希科里（Hikori）——仙人掌之神——祈求一场「美的沉醉」时，他眼里的幻象，又该是什么模样呢？

===Original===
Under the gaslights of the chandelier hung a cluster of electric light bulbs which added to the flood of golden effulgence that bathed the room and all things in it. I gazed next intently at the electric lights. They became the sun itself in their steadiness, until I had to turn away my head and close my eyes. Even then the image persisted﻿—I saw the golden sands of Newport, only they were blazing with glory as if they were veritable diamond dust: I saw the waves, of incomparable blue, rolling up on the shore. A vague perfume was wafted on the air. I was in an orgy of vision. Yet there was no stage of maudlin emotion. It was at least elevating.

===Chinese===
枝形吊灯的煤气灯下，还悬着一簇电灯泡，为那片淹没房间与房中万物的金色光海更添几分辉煌。接着我凝神注视起那些电灯来。它们那种恒定，简直化作了太阳本身，逼得我不得不转开头、闭上眼。即便如此，那影像仍不散去——我看见纽波特（Newport）金色的沙滩，只是那沙滩正燃着荣光，仿佛当真是钻石的粉末；我看见蓝得无与伦比的波浪，一滚滚卷上岸边。一缕莫名的香气随空气飘来。我置身于一场视觉的狂欢。然而其间没有半点烂醉伤感的阶段。它至少是令人升华的。

===Original===
Kennedy’s experiences as he related them to me afterwards were similar, though sufficiently varied to be interesting. His visions took the forms of animals﻿—a Cheshire cat, like that in *Alice in Wonderland*, with merely a grin that faded away, changing into a lynx which in turn disappeared, followed by an unknown creature with short nose and pointed ears, then tortoises and guinea-pigs, a perfectly unrelated succession of beasts. When the playing began a beautiful panorama unfolded before him﻿—the regular notes in the music enhancing the beauty, and changes in the scenes, which he described as a most wonderful kinetoscopic display.

===Chinese===
事后肯尼迪向我讲起他的体验，同我相仿，却又不尽相同，自有其趣味。他的幻象取的是兽形——《爱丽丝梦游仙境》（Alice in Wonderland）里那种柴郡猫（Cheshire cat），只余一副笑容，渐渐淡去，化作一头猞猁，猞猁又隐没，接着是一头短鼻尖耳、叫不出名目的怪兽，再后是乌龟和豚鼠——全然不相干的一串走兽。琴声一起，一幅绚丽的画卷在他眼前展开——音乐里规整的音符为美境增色，场景流转不息，他形容说，那是一场妙到毫巅的「电影镜」（kinetoscope）演出。

===Original===
In fact, only De Quincey or Bayard Taylor or Poe could have done justice to the thrilling effects of the drug, and not even they unless an amanuensis had been seated by them to take down what they dictated, for I defy anyone to remember anything but a fraction of the rapid march of changes under its influence. Indeed, in observing its action I almost forgot for the time being the purpose of our visit, so fascinated was I. The music ceased, but not the visions.

===Chinese===
说实在的，这药的惊心夺魄之效，唯有德·昆西（De Quincey）、贝阿德·泰勒（Bayard Taylor）或爱伦·坡（Poe）才描摹得起，而且即便他们，也须有个笔录员坐在身旁记下口述才行——因为在它的作用下，那疾速行进的万千变化，谁也别想记住超过零星几分。真的，观察药力之时，我一时竟忘了我们此行的目的，实在是沉醉太深。音乐停了，幻象却没有停。

===Original===
Señora Mendez advanced toward us. The spangles on her net dress seemed to give her a fairy-like appearance; she seemed to float over the carpet like a glowing, fleecy, white cloud over a rainbow-tinted sky.

===Chinese===
门德斯夫人朝我们走来。网纱裙上的亮片仿佛给她添了一身仙气；她像一朵莹莹发亮的絮状白云，飘过一道虹彩的天空，那样飘过地毯。

===Original===
Kennedy, however, had not for an instant forgotten what we were there for, and his attention recalled mine. I was surprised to see that when I made the effort I could talk and think quite as rationally as ever, though the wildest pranks were going on in my mind and vision. Kennedy did not beat about in putting his question, evidently counting on the surprise to extract the truth.

===Chinese===
然而肯尼迪一刻也没忘我们登门的来意，他的专注把我的心神也召了回来。我惊讶地发现，只要肯使劲，我的谈吐和思维仍同往日一样清明，尽管脑海里、视野中正闹着最狂放的把戏。肯尼迪发问毫不拐弯，显是指望攻其无备，逼出真话。

===Original===
“What time did Señor Guerrero leave last night?”

===Chinese===
「格雷罗先生昨晚是什么时候走的？」

===Original===
The question came so suddenly that she had no time to think of a reply that would conceal anything she might otherwise have wished to conceal.

===Chinese===
这一问来得太突兀，她来不及斟酌出一个能遮掩点什么的回答——倘若她原本想遮掩点什么的话。

===Original===
“About ten o’clock,” she answered, then instantly was on her guard, for Torreon had caught her eye.

===Chinese===
「十点钟左右。」她答道，随即立刻警觉起来，因为托雷翁的目光已经勾住了她的眼睛。

===Original===
“And you have no idea where he went?” asked Kennedy.

===Chinese===
「那他去哪儿了，您一点儿也不知道？」肯尼迪问。

===Original===
“None, unless he went home,” she replied guardedly.

===Chinese===
「不知道，除非他是回家了。」她设防似的答道。

===Original===
I did not at the time notice the significance of her prompt response to Torreon’s warning. I did not notice, as did Kennedy, the smile that spread over Torreon’s features. The music had started again, and I was oblivious to all but the riot of colour.

===Chinese===
当时我并没有留意，她应托雷翁的警示应得那么快，其中大有文章；也没有像肯尼迪那样，留意到托雷翁脸上漾开的笑意。音乐又响起来了，除了那色彩的狂欢，我对一切都浑然不觉。

===Original===
Again the servant entered. She seemed clothed in a halo of light and colour, every fold of her dress radiating the most delicate tones. Yet there was nothing voluptuous or sensual about it. I was raised above earthly things. Men and women were no longer men and women﻿—they were brilliant creatures of whom I was one. It was sensuous, but not sensual. I looked at my own clothes. My everyday suit was idealised. My hands were surrounded by a glow of red fire that made me feel that they must be the hands of a divinity. I noticed them as I reached forward toward the tray of little cups.

===Chinese===
女仆又进来了。她仿佛裹在一团光与色彩的光晕里，衣裙的每一道褶都在辐射最精微的色调。然而其中没有丝毫淫靡与肉欲。我被高举到尘世之上。男男女女不复是男男女女——他们成了熠熠生辉的造物，而我也是其中之一。那是悦感官的，却不是纵肉欲的。我看看自己的衣服，我那套日常西服也被理想化了。我的双手笼在一团红火的光晕里，让我觉得它们必是神明的手。我朝那盘小杯探身过去时，注意到的正是这双手。

===Original===
There swam into my line of vision another such hand. It laid itself on my arm. A voice sang in my ear softly:

===Chinese===
又一只这样的手游进我的视野，轻轻搭上我的手臂。一个声音在我耳畔轻唱：

===Original===
“No, Walter, we have had enough. Come, let us go. This is not like any other known drug﻿—not even the famous *Cannabis indica*, hashish. Let us go as soon as we politely can. I have found out what I wanted to know. Guerrero is not here.”

===Chinese===
「行了，沃尔特，我们喝够了。来，走吧。这东西同已知的任何麻醉品都不一样——连大名鼎鼎的印度大麻（Cannabis indica）、哈希什（hashish）也比不得它。趁还能客气周旋，赶紧告辞吧。我想知道的，已经知道了。格雷罗不在这里。」

===Original===
We rose shortly and excused ourselves and, with general regrets in which all but Torreon joined, were bowed out with the same courtly politeness with which we had been received.

===Chinese===
不多时我们起身告罪，众人皆表惋惜——唯独托雷翁例外——随后我们被以迎客时那种彬彬有礼的礼数，躬身送了出去。

===Original===
As we left the house, the return to the world was quick. It was like coming out from the matinee and seeing the crowds on the street. They, not the matinee, were unreal for the moment. But, strange to say, I found one felt no depression as a result of the mescal intoxication.

===Chinese===
一出那宅子，回到人间竟是一瞬间的事。就像看完日场戏（matinee）出来，看见街上熙攘的人潮——那一刻，不真实的是人潮，倒不是那出戏。可奇怪的是，我发觉麦斯卡尔这场沉醉过后，人竟毫无抑郁消沉之感。

===Original===
“What is it about mescal that produces such results?” I asked.

===Chinese===
「麦斯卡尔里到底是什么东西，能造出这样的景象？」我问。

===Original===
“The alkaloids,” replied Kennedy as we walked slowly along. “Mescal was first brought to the attention of scientists by explorers employed by our bureau of ethnology. Dr. Weir Mitchell and Dr. Harvey Wiley and several German scientists have investigated it since then. It is well known that it contains half a dozen alkaloids and resins of curious and little-investigated nature. I can’t recall even the names of them offhand, but I have them in my laboratory.”

===Chinese===
「生物碱。」肯尼迪一边缓步走着，一边答道。「麦斯卡尔最早引起科学界注意，靠的是我们民族学局（bureau of ethnology）雇的那批探险家。后来威尔·米切尔医生（Weir Mitchell）、哈维·威利医生（Harvey Wiley），还有几位德国科学家都研究过它。众所周知，它含有六种之多的生物碱和树脂，性质古怪，少有人钻研。它们的名字我一时都背不全，不过实验室里都存着呢。」

===Original===
As the effect of the mescal began to wear off in the fresh air, I found myself in a peculiar questioning state. What had we gained by our visit? Looking calmly at it, I could not help but ask myself why both Torreon and Señora Mendez had acted as if they were concealing something about the whereabouts of Guerrero. Was she a spy? Did she know anything about the loss of the half-million dollars?

===Chinese===
清新的空气里，麦斯卡尔的药力渐渐退去，我陷入一种怪异的求解状态。我们这一趟，究竟捞到了什么？冷眼看去，我不禁自问：托雷翁和门德斯夫人为什么都像是藏着什么——关于格雷罗下落的什么？她是个暗探么？那五十万美元的失踪，她知情么？

===Original===
Of one thing I was certain. Torreon was an ardent admirer of the beautiful señora, equally ardent with Guerrero. Was he simply a jealous suitor, angry at his rival, and now glad that he was out of the way? Where had Guerrero gone? The question was still unanswered.

===Chinese===
有一件事我有把握。托雷翁是那位美丽夫人的热烈仰慕者，热得同格雷罗不相上下。莫非他不过是个争风吃醋的求婚者，怀恨情敌，如今乐得他碍不着事了？格雷罗到底去了哪里？这个问题仍悬着。

===Original===
Absorbed in these reveries, I did not notice particularly where Kennedy was hurrying me. In fact, finding no plausible answer to my speculations and knowing that it was useless to question Kennedy at this stage of his inquiry, I did not for the moment care where we went but allowed him to take the lead.

===Chinese===
我沉在这片遐想里，没怎么留意肯尼迪正匆匆领我去哪儿。说实在的，既然推测不出个所以然，又知道在他查案的这个节骨眼上问他也是白问，我一时也懒得管去哪儿，索性由他领路。

===Original===
We entered one of the fine apartments on the drive and rode up in the elevator. A door opened and, with a start, I found myself in the presence of Miss Guerrero again. The questioning look on her face recalled the object of our search, and its ill success so far. Why had Kennedy come back with so little to report?

===Chinese===
我们走进大道上一幢上等公寓，乘电梯上楼。一扇门开了，我猛一怔——又站到了格雷罗小姐面前。她脸上那探询的神情，让我想起我们此行的目标，也想起它至今的不得要领。肯尼迪怎么带回的汇报就这么一点点？

===Original===
“Have you heard anything?” she asked eagerly.

===Chinese===
「打听到什么了吗？」她急切地问。

===Original===
“Not directly,” replied Kennedy. “But I have a clue, at least. I believe that Torreon knows where your father is and will let you know any moment now. It is to his interest to clear himself before this scandal about the money becomes generally known. Would you allow me to search through your father’s desk?”

===Chinese===
「直接的没有。」肯尼迪答道。「但至少有了一条线索。我相信托雷翁知道令尊在哪里，而且随时会来告诉你。赶在这桩钱款丑闻闹得人尽皆知之前洗清自己，才符合他的利益。可否允许我翻一翻令尊的书桌？」

===Original===
For some moments Kennedy rummaged through the drawers and pigeonholes, silently.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪闷不作声，在抽屉和文件格里翻检了好一阵。

===Original===
“Where does the junta keep its arms stored﻿—not in the meeting-place on South Street does it?” asked Kennedy at length.

===Chinese===
「洪塔的军火存放在哪儿——总不会就存在南街的聚会处吧？」肯尼迪终于开口。

===Original===
“Not exactly; that would be a little too risky,” she replied. “I believe they have a loft above the office, hired in someone else’s name and not connected with the place downstairs at all. My father and Señor Torreon are the only ones who have the keys. Why do you ask?”

===Chinese===
「倒也不是；那也太悬了。」她答道。「我记得他们在办公处楼上租了一间阁楼，用的是别人的名义，同楼下那处地方完全不搭界。钥匙只有我父亲和托雷翁先生两人有。您问这个做什么？」

===Original===
“I ask,” replied Craig, “because I was wondering whether there might not be something that would take him down to South Street last night. It is the only place I can think of his going to at such a late hour, unless he has gone out of town. If we do not hear from Torreon soon I think I will try what. I can find down there. Ah, what is this?”

===Chinese===
「我这么问，」克雷格答道，「是因为我在琢磨，昨夜会不会有什么事把他引去了南街。深更半夜的，除非出城，我想得出他会去的，也只有那一个地方。托雷翁再不来信儿，我想我就亲自下去看看，能翻出点什么。咦，这是什么？」

===Original===
Kennedy drew forth a little silver box and opened it. Inside reposed a dozen mescal buttons.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪掏出一只小小的银盒，打开来。里面静静躺着十二枚麦斯卡尔纽扣。

===Original===
We both looked quickly at Miss Guerrero, but it was quite evident that she was unacquainted with them.

===Chinese===
我们俩都飞快地看向格雷罗小姐，可她分明不认得这东西。

===Original===
She was about to ask what Kennedy had found when the telephone rang and the maid announced that Miss Guerrero was wanted by Señor Torreon.

===Chinese===
她正要问肯尼迪找见了什么，电话铃响了，女仆来报，说托雷翁先生找格雷罗小姐听电话。

===Original===
A smile of gratification flitted over Kennedy’s face as he leaned over to me and whispered: “It is evident that Torreon is anxious to clear himself. I’ll wager he has done some rapid hustling since we left him.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪脸上掠过一丝得色，俯到我耳边低语：「看样子，托雷翁是急着洗清自己呢。我敢打赌，自打咱们同他分手，他可没少四处奔忙。」

===Original===
“Perhaps this is some word about my father at last,” murmured Miss Guerrero as she nervously hurried to the telephone, and answered, “Yes, this is Señorita Guerrero, Señor Torreon. You are at the office of the junta? Yes, yes, you have word from my father﻿—you went down there tonight expecting some guns to be delivered?﻿—and you found him there﻿—upstairs in the loft﻿—ill, did you say?﻿—unconscious?”

===Chinese===
「也许终于有我父亲的消息了。」格雷罗小姐喃喃着，慌慌张张奔到电话前应道：「喂，我就是格雷罗小姐，托雷翁先生。您在洪塔的办公处？对，对，您有我父亲的消息了——您今晚下去，是等着接一批要到的枪械？——结果您在那儿发现了他——在楼上的阁楼里——您说他病了？——不省人事？」

===Original===
In an instant her face was drawn and pale, and the receiver fell clattering to the hardwood floor from her nerveless fingers.

===Chinese===
一刹那间，她面容扭曲、煞白，听筒从她瘫软的手指间滑落，磕在硬木地板上啪啦作响。

===Original===
“He is dead!” she gasped as she swayed backward and I caught her. With Kennedy’s help I carried her, limp and unconscious, across the room, and placed her in a deep armchair. I stood at her side, but for the moment could only look on helplessly, blankly at the now stony beauty of her face.

===Chinese===
「他死了！」她气咽着向后便倒，被我一把扶住。在肯尼迪帮衬下，我把软作一团、人事不省的她抱过房间，安置进一张深座安乐椅。我立在她身旁，一时却只能束手地、怔怔地望着她那张此刻已如石像般凝固的脸。

===Original===
“Some water, Juanita, quick!” I cried as soon as I had recovered from the shock. “Have you any smelling-salts or anything of that sort? Perhaps you can find a little brandy. Hurry.”

===Chinese===
「拿水来，胡安妮塔，快！」我缓过神便喊。「有嗅盐（smelling-salts）那一类的东西吗？兴许还能找一点白兰地。快点。」

===Original===
While we were making her comfortable the telephone continued to tinkle.

===Chinese===
我们忙着让她安顿舒服，电话铃一直丁零零响个不停。

===Original===
“This is Kennedy,” I heard Craig say, as Juanita came hurrying in with water, smelling-salts, and brandy. “You fool. She fainted. Why couldn’t you break it to her gently? What’s that address on South Street? You found him over the junta meeting-place in a loft? Yes, I understand. What were you doing down there? You went down expecting a shipment of arms and saw a light overhead I see﻿—and suspecting something you entered with a policeman. You heard him move across the floor above and fall heavily? All right. Someone will be down directly. Ambulance surgeon has tried everything, you say? No heart action, no breathing? Sure. Very well. Let the body remain just where it is until I get down. Oh, wait. How long ago did it happen? Fifteen minutes? All right. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
「我是肯尼迪。」胡安妮塔端着水、嗅盐和白兰地匆匆进来时，我听见克雷格在说。「你这个蠢货。她晕过去了。这种事就不能婉转点说？南街那地址是多少？你在洪塔聚会处的楼上、一间阁楼里发现了他？好，明白了。你下去做什么？你下去等一批军火，看见头顶有灯光——明白了——起了疑心，就同一个警察进去了。你听见他在楼上地板上走动、随后重重摔倒？好。马上有人下去。你说救护车医生什么都试过了？没有心跳，没有呼吸？确定。很好。尸体原地别动，等我下去。哦，等等。出事多久了？十五分钟？好。再见。」

===Original===
Such restoratives as we had found we applied faithfully. At last we were rewarded by the first flutter of an eyelid. Then Miss Guerrero gazed wildly about.

===Chinese===
能找到的这些提神药剂，我们一样样都悉心用上了。终于，她的眼皮头一回颤了颤。随即，格雷罗小姐失魂般环顾四周。

===Original===
“He is dead,” she moaned. “They have killed him. I know it. My father is dead.” Over and over she repeated: “He is dead. I shall never see him again.”

===Chinese===
「他死了，」她呜咽道。「他们害死了他。我知道的。我父亲死了。」她翻来覆去只有一句：「他死了。我再也见不着他了。」

===Original===
Vainly I tried to soothe her. What was there to say? There could be no doubt about it. Torreon must have gone down directly after we left Señora Mendez. He had seen a light in the loft, had entered with a policeman﻿—as a witness, he had told Craig over the telephone﻿—had heard Guerrero fall, and had sent for the ambulance. How long Guerrero had been there he did not know, for while members of the junta had been coming and going all day in the office below none had gone up into the locked loft.

===Chinese===
我百般劝慰，终归徒劳。还有什么可说的呢？这事已无可置疑。托雷翁必定是在我们离开门德斯夫人之后径直下了南街。他看见阁楼里有灯光，便同一个警察进去了——他在电话里对克雷格说，是请来作见证的——听见格雷罗摔倒，又唤了救护车。格雷罗在那里待了多久，他说不上来，因为楼下的办公处里，洪塔成员整日进进出出，却没有一个人上过那间上着锁的阁楼。

===Original===
Kennedy with rare skill calmed Miss Guerrero’s dry-eyed hysteria into a gentle rain of tears, which relieved her overwrought feelings. We silently withdrew, leaving the two women, mistress and servant, weeping.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪拿出罕见的本事，把格雷罗小姐那欲哭无泪的癔症，抚成了一场簌簌的轻泪，让她过度紧绷的心绪得了纾解。我们悄悄退出去，留下主仆二人对泣。

===Original===
“Craig,” I asked when we had gained the street, “what do you make of it? We must lose no time. Arrest this Mendez woman before she has a chance to escape.”

===Chinese===
「克雷格，」到了街上，我问，「你怎么看？咱们一刻也不能耽搁。趁那门德斯女人还没来得及跑，先把她抓起来。」

===Original===
“Not so fast, Walter,” he cautioned as we spun along in a taxicab. “Our case isn’t very complete against anybody yet.”

===Chinese===
「别急，沃尔特，」出租车疾驰着，他提醒我，「眼下对任何一个人，咱们的案子都还不够瓷实。」

===Original===
“But it looks black for Guerrero,” I admitted. “Dead men tell no tales even to clear themselves.”

===Chinese===
「可对格雷罗的前景不太妙啊，」我承认道，「死人开不了口，连给自己辩白都不成。」

===Original===
“It all depends on speed now,” he answered laconically.

===Chinese===
「现在全看谁快了。」他言简意赅地答。

===Original===
We had reached the university, which was only a few blocks away, and Craig dashed into his laboratory while I settled with the driver. He reappeared almost instantly with some bulky apparatus under his arm, and we more than ran from the building to the nearby subway station. Fortunately there was an express just pulling in, as we tumbled down the steps.

===Chinese===
大学离这儿只隔几个街区，我们赶到了。克雷格一头扎进实验室，我则付车钱打发司机。几乎转眼的工夫，他腋下夹着几件笨重的器械重新出现，我们从楼里一路狂奔——何止是奔——赶到附近的地铁站。还算侥幸，我们连滚带跌下台阶时，正有一列快车进站。

===Original===
To one who knows South Street as merely a river-front street whose glory of other days has long since departed, where an antiquated horsecar now ambles slowly uptown, and trucks and carts all day long are in a perpetual jam, it is peculiarly uninteresting by day, and peculiarly deserted and vicious by night. But there is another fascination about South Street. Perhaps there has never been a revolution in Latin America which has not in some way or other been connected with this street, whence hundreds of filibustering expeditions have started. Whenever a dictator is to be overthrown, or half a dozen chocolate-skinned generals in the Caribbean become dissatisfied with their portions of gold lace, the arms- and ammunition-dealers of South Street can give, if they choose, an advance scenario of the whole tragedy or comic opera, as the case may be. Real war or opera-bouffe, it is all grist for the mills of these closemouthed individuals.

===Chinese===
在有些人眼里，南街不过是条滨河的街，昔日的荣光早已散尽，如今只有一辆老掉牙的有轨马车（horsecar）慢腾腾地朝上城溜达，卡车板车从早到晚堵成一锅粥——白天它无趣得出奇，夜里又冷僻凶险得出奇。可南街另有一股魔力。拉丁美洲的历次革命，恐怕没有一次不与这条街有这样那样的干系；数百次军事冒险（filibustering）远征，正是从这里扬帆启程的。哪回要是有独裁者该被推翻了，或是加勒比海那五六位巧克力肤色的将军，对自己分得的那份金穗带起了怨言，南街的军火弹药商们只要愿意，都能预先把这整出悲剧或喜剧——各视其情形——的剧本念给你听。真刀真枪的战争也罢，滑稽歌剧（opera-bouffe）也罢，对这些嘴严的家伙来说，统统是自家磨坊里的谷子。

===Original===
Our quest took us to a ramshackle building reminiscent of the days when the street bristled with bowsprits of ships from all over the world, an age when the American merchantman flew our flag on the uttermost of the seven-seas. On the ground floor was an apparently innocent junk dealer’s shop, in reality the meeting-place of the junta. By an outside stairway the lofts above were reached, hiding their secrets behind windows opaque with decades of dust.

===Chinese===
我们要找的地方，是一幢摇摇欲坠的楼，教人想起这条街当年船首斜桅（bowsprit）林立、泊满全世界帆船的岁月，想起美国商船把我们的国旗挂上七海之极的时代。底层是家看似清白的废品铺，实则是洪塔的聚会处。沿一道外挂的楼梯上去，便是楼上的几间阁楼，它们把各自的秘密，藏在被几十年尘土糊得不透光的窗户后面。

===Original===
At the door we were met by Torreon and the policeman. Both appeared to be shocked beyond measure. Torreon was profuse in explanations which did not explain. Out of the tangled mass of verbiage I did manage to extract, however, the impression that, come what might to the other members of the junta, Torreon was determined to clear his own name at any cost. He and the policeman had discovered Señor Guerrero only a short time before, upstairs. For all he knew, Guerrero had been there some time, perhaps all day, while the others were meeting downstairs. Except for the light he might have been there undiscovered still. Torreon swore he had heard Guerrero fall; the policeman was not quite so positive.

===Chinese===
门口，托雷翁和那名警察迎住我们。两人都显得震惊得无以复加。托雷翁滔滔不绝地解释，可解释来解释去，什么也没解释出来。不过，从那一团乱麻似的冗词里，我到底拈出这么个印象：洪塔其他成员是死是活且不论，托雷翁是铁了心要不惜一切洗清自己的名字。他和警察是刚刚不久前在楼上发现格雷罗先生的。据他所知，格雷罗可能已在里面待了些时候，也许一整天，而旁人全在楼下开会。若不是那灯光，说不定他至今还没被人发觉。托雷翁赌咒发誓，说亲耳听见格雷罗摔倒；警察就不那么肯定了。

===Original===
Kennedy listened impatiently, then sprang up the stairs, only to call back to the policeman: “Go call me a taxicab at the ferry, an electric cab. Mind, now, not a gasoline-cab﻿—electric.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪不耐烦地听着，随即蹬蹬蹬冲上楼去，只回头朝警察喊了一句：「去渡口给我叫辆出租车，要电动的。记住，别叫汽油车——要电动的。」

===Original===
We found the victim lying on a sort of bed of sailcloth in a loft apparently devoted to the peaceful purposes of the junk trade, but really a perfect arsenal and magazine. It was dusty and cobwebbed, crammed with stands of arms, tents, uniforms in bales, batteries of Maxims and mountain-guns, and all the paraphernalia for carrying on a real twentieth-century revolution.

===Chinese===
我们看见罹难者躺在阁楼里一张帆布铺就的床铺上。这间阁楼明面上做的是废品买卖的太平营生，实则是座地道的军火弹药库。屋里积尘结网，塞满了一架架枪支、成捆的帐篷军服、成排的马克沁机枪（Maxim）和山炮，还有操办一场真正的二十世纪革命所需的全副行头。

===Original===
The young ambulance surgeon was still there, so quickly had we been able to get downtown. He had his stomach-pump, hypodermic syringe, emetics, and various tubes spread out on a piece of linen on a packing-case. Kennedy at once inquired just what he had done.

===Chinese===
那位年轻的救护车医生还在——我们赶到下城竟有这么快。他把洗胃泵（stomach-pump）、皮下注射器、催吐剂和各式管子，摊在包装箱上铺的一块亚麻布上。肯尼迪立刻细问，他都做了些什么。

===Original===
“Thought at first it was only a bad case of syncope,” he replied, “but I guess he was dead some minutes before I got here. Tried rhythmic traction of the tongue, artificial respiration, stimulants, chest and heart massage﻿—everything, but it was no use:”

===Chinese===
「起先以为只是重症晕厥（syncope），」他答道，「可我估摸，我到之前他已断气好几分钟了。有节律地牵拉舌头、人工呼吸、强心剂、胸部与心脏按摩——什么法子都试了，全不管用：」

===Original===
“Have you any idea what caused his death?” asked Craig as he hastily adjusted his apparatus to an electric light socket﻿—a rheostat, an induction-coil of peculiar shape, and an “interrupter.”

===Chinese===
「那您可知道死因？」克雷格问，一面手忙脚乱地把器械往电灯插座上接——一只变阻器（rheostat）、一只形状特异的感应圈（induction-coil），还有一只「断续器」（interrupter）。

===Original===
“Poison of some kind﻿—an alkaloid. They say they heard him fall as they came upstairs, and when they got to him he was blue. His face was as blue as it is now when I arrived. Asphyxia, failure of both heart and lungs, that was what the alkaloid caused.”

===Chinese===
「某种毒药——一种生物碱。他们说上楼时听见他摔倒，赶到跟前，人已经发青了。我到的时候，他那张脸同现在一样青。窒息，心肺俱衰——那生物碱造成的正是这个。」

===Original===
The gong of the electric cab sounded outside. As Craig heard it he rushed with two wires to the window, threw them out, and hurried downstairs, attaching them to the batteries of the cab.

===Chinese===
外头响起电动出租车的铃声。克雷格一听，抓起两根电线奔到窗口，把线抛出去，随即匆匆下楼，把线接到车上的电瓶上。

===Original===
In an instant he was back again.

===Chinese===
转眼他又回来了。

===Original===
“Now, Doctor,” he said, “I’m going to perform a very delicate test on this man. Here I have the alternating city current and here a direct, continuous current from the storage-batteries of the cab below. Doctor, hold his mouth open. So. Now, have you a pair of forceps handy? Good. Can you catch hold of the tip of his tongue? There. Do just as I tell you. I apply this cathode to his skin in the dorsal region; under the back of the neck, and this anode in the lumbar region at the base of the spine﻿—just pieces of cotton soaked in salt solution and covering the metal electrodes, to give me a good contact with the body.”

===Chinese===
「好了，医生，」他说，「我要在这人身上做一个非常精细的试验。这边是城市的交流电，这边是楼下车子蓄电池里的直流电。医生，把他的嘴扳开。就这样。好，您手边有钳子吗？好。能夹住他的舌尖吗？行了。一切照我说的做。我把这枚阴极贴在他背部的皮肤上、颈后根，把这枚阳极放在脊柱底端的腰骶部——不过是几片浸了盐水的棉花，覆在金属电极上，好同身体贴合得紧实些。」

===Original===
I was fascinated. It was gruesome, and yet I could not take my eyes off it. Torreon stood blankly, in a daze. Craig was as calm as if his everyday work was experimenting on cadavers.

===Chinese===
我看得入了迷。那场面阴森可怖，我却移不开眼。托雷翁木然立着，如坠云雾。克雷格平静得很，仿佛拿尸体做试验就是他每日的家常功课。

===Original===
He applied the current, moving the anode and the cathode slowly. I had often seen the experiments on the nerves of a frog that had been freshly killed, how the electric current will make the muscles twitch, as discovered long ago by Galvani. But I was not prepared to see it on a human being. Torreon muttered something and crossed himself.

===Chinese===
他通上电流，缓缓移动着阳极和阴极。刚宰杀的青蛙神经上做的试验我见得多了——电流如何叫肌肉抽动，伽伐尼（Galvani）早就发现了。可我没料到，这一幕竟会发生在一个人身上。托雷翁咕哝了句什么，在胸前画了个十字。

===Original===
The arms seemed half to rise﻿—then suddenly to fall, flabby again. There was a light hiss like an inspiration and expiration of air, a ghastly sound.

===Chinese===
那双胳膊像是半抬了起来——忽又颓然落下，重新瘫软。一声轻嘶，像一次吸气又呼气，森然可怖。

===Original===
“Lungs react,” muttered Kennedy, “but the heart doesn’t. I must increase the voltage.”

===Chinese===
「肺有反应，」肯尼迪喃喃道，「心脏还没有。我得加大电压。」

===Original===
Again he applied the electrodes.

===Chinese===
他再次贴上电极。

===Original===
The face seemed a different shade of blue, I thought.

===Chinese===
我觉得，那张脸的青色似乎换了一个色调。

===Original===
“Good God, Kennedy,” I exclaimed, “do you suppose the effect of that mescal on me hasn’t worn off yet? Blue, blue everything blue is playing pranks before my eyes. Tell me, is the blue of that face﻿—his face﻿—is it changing? Do you see it, or do I imagine it?”

===Chinese===
「老天爷，肯尼迪，」我失声道，「莫非那麦斯卡尔的劲儿在我身上还没过去？蓝的，蓝的，满眼的蓝都在我眼前变戏法。你说，那张脸的青色——他的脸——是不是在变？你也看见了，还是我的幻觉？」

===Original===
“Blood asphyxiated,” was the disjointed reply. “The oxygen is clearing it.”

===Chinese===
「血液窒息了。」回答断断续续。「氧气正把它化开。」

===Original===
“But, Kennedy,” I persisted; “his face was dark blue, black a minute ago. The most astonishing change has taken place. Its colour is almost natural now. Do I imagine it or is it real?”

===Chinese===
「可是，肯尼迪，」我追着问，「一分钟前，他的脸还是深青，黑青。这一变可太惊人了。现在那颜色几乎就是活人的颜色。是我看花了眼，还是真的？」

===Original===
Kennedy was so absorbed in his work that he made no reply at all. He heard nothing, nothing save the slow, forced inspiration and expiration of air as he deftly and quickly manipulated the electrodes.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪全副心思都在手上的活计里，一言不发。他什么也没听见，什么也听不见，只听见那一口口缓慢而被动的进气出气——他手上利落迅捷地操纵着电极。

===Original===
“Doctor,” he cried at length, “tell me what is going on in that heart.”

===Chinese===
「医生，」他终于喊道，「告诉我，那颗心脏里现在是什么情形。」

===Original===
The young surgeon bent his head and placed his ear on the cold breast. As he raised his eyes and they chanced to rest on Kennedy’s hands, holding the electrodes dangling idly in the air, I think I never saw a greater look of astonishment on a human face. “It﻿—is﻿—almost﻿—natural,” he gasped.

===Chinese===
年轻的外科医生俯下头，把耳朵贴在那冰冷的胸口上。待他抬起眼，目光恰好落在肯尼迪那双手上——电极正闲悬在半空——我想我从没在一张人脸上见过比这更深的惊愕。「几——乎——正——常——了。」他倒吸一口气。

===Original===
“With great care and a milk diet for a few days Guerrero will live,” said Kennedy quietly. “It is natural.”

===Chinese===
「精心护理，再吃上几天奶食，格雷罗死不了。」肯尼迪平平静静地说。「是完全正常了。」

===Original===
“My God, man, but he was dead!” exclaimed the surgeon. “I know it. His heart was stopped and his lungs collapsed.”

===Chinese===
「老天，老兄，可他方才明明死了！」外科医生叫道。「这我知道。他心跳停了，肺也塌了。」

===Original===
“To all intents and purposes he was dead, dead as ever a man was,” replied Craig, “and would be now, if I hadn’t happened to think of this special induction-coil loaned to me by a doctor who had studied deeply the process of electric resuscitation developed by Professor Leduc of the Nantes École de Médicin. There is only one case I know of on record which compares with this﻿—a case of a girl resuscitated in Paris. The girl was a chronic morphine-eater and was ‘dead’ forty minutes.”

===Chinese===
「无论从哪方面讲，他都死了，死得不能再死了，」克雷格答道，「要不是我碰巧想起这只特制的感应圈——一位医生借给我的，他深入钻研过南特医学院（École de Médicin）勒杜克教授（Leduc）创立的电复苏法（electric resuscitation）——他这会儿还死着呢。据我所知，有案可查、能同这一例相提并论的只有一桩——巴黎救活一位姑娘的案子。那姑娘常年吞吗啡，『死』了四十分钟。」

===Original===
I stood like one frozen, the thing was so incomprehensible, after the many surprises of the evening that had preceded. Torreon, in fact, did not comprehend for the moment.

===Chinese===
我僵在原地——前半夜惊魂已经受了那么多，这件事仍旧太不可索解。至于托雷翁，一时压根儿没回过神来。

===Original===
As Kennedy and I bent over, Guerrero’s eyes opened, but he apparently saw nothing. His hand moved a little, and his lips parted. Kennedy quickly reached into the pockets of the man gasping for breath, one after another. From a vest pocket he drew a little silver case, identical with that he had found in the desk uptown. He opened it, and one mescal button rolled out into the palm of his hand. Kennedy regarded it thoughtfully.

===Chinese===
我和肯尼迪俯下身去，格雷罗睁开了眼，可显然什么也没看见。他的手动了动，嘴唇也翕动了一下。肯尼迪赶忙伸手，在那气息奄奄的人身上一个口袋一个口袋地掏。他从马甲口袋里摸出一只小银盒，同上城书桌里找到的那只一模一样。打开来，一枚麦斯卡尔纽扣滚进他的掌心。肯尼迪若有所思地端详着它。

===Original===
“I suspect there is at least one devotee of the vision-breeding drug who will no longer cultivate its use, as a result of this,” he added, looking significantly at the man before us.

===Chinese===
「我料想，经过这一场，那养幻之药的信徒里头，至少有一位再也不敢沾它了。」他补了一句，意味深长地望着眼前这个人。

===Original===
“Guerrero,” shouted Kennedy, placing his mouth close to the man’s ear, but muting his voice so that only I could distinguish what he said, “Guerrero, where is the money?”

===Chinese===
「格雷罗，」肯尼迪把嘴凑到那人耳边，嗓门却压着，压得只有我一人听得清他说什么，「格雷罗，钱在哪儿？」

===Original===
His lips moved trembling again, but I could not make out that he said anything.

===Chinese===
他的嘴唇又颤抖着动了动，可我听不出他说了什么没有。

===Original===
Kennedy rose and quietly went over to detach his apparatus from the electric light socket behind Torreon.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪起身，不动声色地走过去，从托雷翁身后的电灯插座上拆他的器械。

===Original===
“Car‑ramba!” I heard as I turned suddenly.

===Chinese===
我猛一回头，听见一声：「卡拉姆巴！（Car-ramba！）」

===Original===
Craig had Torreon firmly pinioned from behind by both arms. The policeman quickly interposed.

===Chinese===
克雷格已从背后死死反剪住托雷翁的双臂。警察赶忙上前拦阻。

===Original===
“It’s all right, officer,” exclaimed Craig. “Walter, reach into his inside pocket.”

===Chinese===
「没事，警官，」克雷格朗声道，「沃尔特，伸手掏他的内袋。」

===Original===
I pulled out a bunch of papers and turned them over.

===Chinese===
我掏出一沓文件，一页页翻检。

===Original===
“What’s that?” asked Kennedy as I came to something neatly enclosed in an envelope.

===Chinese===
「那是什么？」我翻到一样用信封装得整整齐齐的东西时，肯尼迪问。

===Original===
I opened it. It was a power of attorney from Guerrero to Torreon.

===Chinese===
我打开来。是格雷罗签给托雷翁的一份授权书（power of attorney）。

===Original===
“Perhaps it is no crime to give a man mescal if he wants it﻿—I doubt if the penal code covers that,” ejaculated Kennedy. “But it is conspiracy to give it to him and extract a power of attorney by which you can get control of trust funds consigned to him. Manuel Torreon, the game is up. You and Señora Mendez have played your parts well. But you have lost. You waited until you thought Guerrero was dead, then you took a policeman along as a witness to clear yourself. But the secret is not dead, after all. Is there nothing else in those papers, Walter? Yes? Ah, a bill of lading dated today? Ten cases of ‘scrap iron’ from New York to Boston﻿—a long chance for such valuable ‘scrap,’ señor, but I suppose you had to get the money away from New York, at any risk.”

===Chinese===
「人家自己想要麦斯卡尔，你给他，这兴许算不上罪——刑法管不管得着，我还犯嘀咕。」肯尼迪脱口道。「可一边给他下这药，一边骗出一纸授权书，好攥住托到他名下的信托资金，那就是阴谋了。曼努埃尔·托雷翁（Manuel Torreon），戏该收场了。你和门德斯夫人演得都不错。可惜你们输了。你们等吃准格雷罗死了，才拉个警察同行做见证，替自己开脱。可秘密到底没死。那沓文件里还有别的吗，沃尔特？有？啊，一张今天日期的提单（bill of lading）？十箱『废铁』，纽约运波士顿——这么值钱的『废铁』走这一步，可真是险棋啊，先生。不过我琢磨着，无论如何，你也得把这钱弄出纽约去，对吧。」

===Original===
“And Señora Mendez?” I asked as my mind involuntarily reverted to the brilliantly lighted room uptown. “What part did she have in the plot against Guerrero?”

===Chinese===
「那门德斯夫人呢？」我问，心思不由自主地飘回上城那间灯火辉煌的屋子。「针对格雷罗的这一局，她扮演的是什么角色？」

===Original===
Torreon stood sullenly silent. Kennedy reached in another of Torreon’s pockets and drew out a third little silver box of mescal buttons. Holding all three of the boxes, identically the same, before us he remarked: “Evidently Torreon was not averse to having his victim under the influence of mescal as much as possible. He must have forced it on him﻿—all’s fair in love and revolution, I suppose. I believe he brought him down here under the influence of mescal last night, obtained the power of attorney, and left him here to die of the mescal intoxication. It was just a case of too strong a hold of the mescal﻿—the artificial paradise was too alluring to Guerrero, and Torreon knew it and tried to profit by it to the extent of half a million dollars.”

===Chinese===
托雷翁闷着头，一言不发。肯尼迪又探进托雷翁的另一只口袋，摸出第三只盛麦斯卡尔纽扣的小银盒。他把三只一模一样的银盒一齐举到我们眼前，说道：「看得出来，托雷翁巴不得他的猎物尽量多泡在麦斯卡尔的药力里。他八成是硬灌的——恋爱与革命，兵不厌诈嘛。我料定，昨夜他就是让中了麦斯卡尔药力的格雷罗跟他到了这儿，到手了授权书，再把他撂在这里，让他死于麦斯卡尔的沉醉。说穿了，不过是麦斯卡尔攥住了人，攥得太紧——那座人造天堂对格雷罗太迷人了，托雷翁看准了这一点，就想借它发一笔五十万美元的横财。」

===Original===
It was more than I could grasp at the instant. The impossible had happened. I had seen the dead﻿—literally﻿—brought back to life and the secret which the criminal believed buried wrung from the grave.

===Chinese===
这一切，我一时实在消化不了。不可能的事发生了。我亲眼见一个死人——不折不扣的死人——被救活了，亲眼见那桩罪犯自以为埋进了坟墓的秘密，从坟墓里被生生逼了出来。

===Original===
Kennedy must have noted the puzzled look on my face. “Walter,” he said, casually, as he wrapped up his instruments, “don’t stand there gaping like Billikin. Our part in this case is finished﻿—at least mine is. But I suspect from some of the glances I have seen you steal at various times that﻿—well, perhaps you would like a few moments in a real paradise. I saw a telephone downstairs. Go call up Miss Guerrero and tell her her father is alive﻿—and innocent.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪想必瞧见了我脸上那副迷惑相。「沃尔特，」他一边收拾器械，一边漫不经心地说，「别杵在那儿，像尊比利金（Billikin）似的张着嘴发呆。这案子咱们的差事完了——至少我的完了。不过，你那些趁人不备偷偷瞟向人家的目光，我可瞧了不少回——这么说吧，也许你还想去真正的天堂里消磨片刻呢。楼下就有电话。去打给格雷罗小姐，告诉她，她父亲活着——而且是清白的。」

## The Azure Ring / 天青色戒指

===Original===

Files of newspapers and innumerable clippings from the press bureaus littered Kennedy’s desk in rank profusion. Kennedy himself was so deeply absorbed that I had merely said good evening as I came in and had started to open my mail. With an impatient sweep of his hand, however, he brushed the whole mass of newspapers into the wastebasket.

“It seems to me, Walter,” he exclaimed in disgust, “that this mystery is considered insoluble for the very reason which should make it easy to solve﻿—the extraordinary character of its features.”

Inasmuch as he had opened the subject, I laid down the letter I was reading. “I’ll wager I can tell you just why you made that remark, Craig,” I ventured. “You’re reading up on that Wainwright-Templeton affair.”

“You are on the road to becoming a detective yourself, Walter,” he answered with a touch of sarcasm. “Your ability to add two units to two other units and obtain four units is almost worthy of Inspector O’Connor. You are right, and within a quarter of an hour the district attorney of Westchester County will be here. He telephoned me this afternoon and sent an assistant with this mass of dope. I suppose he’ll want it back,” he added, fishing the newspapers out of the basket again. “But, with all due respect to your profession, I’ll say that no one would ever get on speaking terms with the solution of this case if he had to depend solely on the newspaper writers.”

===Chinese===

一摞摞报纸和数不清的通讯社剪报，乱七八糟地堆满了肯尼迪的书桌。他本人埋头其间，全神贯注，以致我进门时只道了声晚上好，便自顾自拆起自己的信件来。然而他不耐烦地一挥手，把整堆报纸全扫进了废纸篓。

「在我看来，沃尔特，」他厌恶地嚷道，「人们之所以把这桩谜案当作无从破解，恰恰是因为那个本应让它容易破解的原因——它的种种特征太不寻常了。」

既然是他先挑起话头，我便放下正读着的信。「我敢打赌，我知道你为什么发这通牢骚，克雷格，」我斗胆说，「你是在钻研温赖特—坦普尔顿（Wainwright-Templeton）那桩案子。」

「你呀，沃尔特，都快自己当上侦探了。」他答道，语带几分讥诮。「你这种把两个单位加到另外两个单位上、得出四个单位的本事，简直快赶上奥康纳警督了。你说对了：再过一刻钟，威斯特彻斯特县的检察官就要到这里来。他今天下午给我打了电话，还派了个助理送来这么一大堆材料。我猜他还要拿回去的。」说着，他又把报纸从纸篓里捞了出来。「不过，恕我对你这一行直言一句：谁要是只能指望报馆的那些写手，那他一辈子也休想同本案的谜底搭上话。」

===Original===

“No?” I queried, rather nettled at his tone.

“No,” he repeated emphatically. “Here one of the most popular girls in the fashionable suburb of Williston, and one of the leading younger members of the bar in New York, engaged to be married, are found dead in the library of the girl’s home the day before the ceremony. And now, a week later, no one knows whether it was an accident due to the fumes from the antique charcoal-brazier, or whether it was a double suicide, or suicide and murder, or a double murder, or﻿—or﻿—why, the experts haven’t even been able to agree on whether they have discovered poison or not,” he continued, growing as excited as the city editor did over my first attempt as a cub reporter.

“They haven’t agreed on anything except that on the eve of what was, presumably, to have been the happiest day of their lives two of the best known members of the younger set are found dead, while absolutely no one, as far as is known, can be proved to have been near them within the time necessary to murder them. No wonder the coroner says it is simply a case of asphyxiation. No wonder the district attorney is at his wits’ end. You fellows have hounded them with your hypotheses until they can’t see the facts straight. You suggest one solution and before﻿—”

The doorbell sounded insistently, and without waiting for an answer a tall, spare, loose-jointed individual stalked in and laid a green bag on the table.

===Chinese===

「是吗？」我反问，对他的口气颇为恼火。

「是吗。」他加重语气重复道。「时髦郊区威利斯敦（Williston）人缘最好的姑娘之一，同纽约律师界年轻一辈中的翘楚之一，两人已经订了婚，却在婚礼前一天双双死在姑娘家的书房里。如今一个星期过去了，谁也说不清这究竟是把古董炭盆（charcoal-brazier）冒出的烟引起的事故，是双双自杀，是自杀加谋杀，还是双重谋杀，还是——还是——嘿，专家们甚至连到底查没查到毒物，都还没吵出个结果来。」他越说越激动，活像当年我初出茅庐交头一篇稿子时的都市版主编（city editor）。

「他们只在一件事上达成了共识：在两个人此生——据推测——本该最幸福的一天的前夜，年轻交际圈里最有名的两位被发现死了，而就目前所知，根本无法证明有谁在行凶所需的时间里接近过他们。难怪验尸官说这不过是一桩窒息（asphyxiation）案。难怪检察官一筹莫展。你们这帮家伙拿种种假说穷追猛打，打得他们连事实都看不清了。你们刚提出一种解释，还没等——」

门铃执拗地响个不停，不等任何应答，一个瘦高个、关节松垮的男人已经大步流星地闯了进来，把一只绿色提包搁在桌上。

===Original===

“Good evening, Professor Kennedy,” he began brusquely. “I am District Attorney Whitney, of Westchester. I see you have been reading up on the case. Quite right.”

“Quite wrong,” answered Craig. “Let me introduce my friend, Mr. Jameson, of the *Star*. Sit down. Jameson knows what I think of the way the newspapers have handled this case. I was about to tell him as you came in that I intended to disregard everything that had been printed, to start out with you as if it were a fresh subject and get the facts at first hand. Let’s get right down to business. First tell us just how it was that Miss Wainwright and Mr. Templeton were discovered and by whom.”

The district attorney loosened the cords of the green bag and drew out a bundle of documents. “I’ll read you the affidavit of the maid who found them,” he said, fingering the documents nervously. “You see, John Templeton had left his office in New York early that afternoon, telling his father that he was going to visit Miss Wainwright. He caught the three-twenty train, reached Williston all right, walked to the Wainwright house, and, in spite of the bustle of preparation for the wedding, the next day, he spent the rest of the afternoon with Miss Wainwright. That’s where the mystery begins. They had no visitors. At least, the maid who answers the bell says they had none. She was busy with the rest of the family, and I believe the front door was not locked﻿—we don’t lock our doors in Williston, except at night.”

He had found the paper and paused to impress these facts on our minds.

===Chinese===

「晚上好，肯尼迪教授。」他开门见山。「我是威斯特彻斯特的检察官惠特尼（Whitney）。看来你已经在钻研这件案子了。非常好。」

「非常不好。」克雷格答道。「我来介绍一下，这位是我的朋友詹姆森先生，《明星报》（*Star*）的。请坐。詹姆森知道我对报纸处理这桩案子的方式有什么看法。你进来的时候，我正要告诉他：我打算把已经见报的东西统统撇开，同你一起把这事当作一个全新的题目从头做起，第一手地弄清事实。咱们直奔正题吧。先说说温赖特小姐（Miss Wainwright）和坦普尔顿先生（Mr. Templeton）是怎么被发现的，又是被谁发现的。」

检察官解开绿提包的系绳，抽出一卷文件。「我来给你们念一念发现他们俩的那位女仆的证词宣誓书（affidavit）。」他一边说，一边神经质地捻着那些文件。「情况是这样：那天下午，约翰·坦普尔顿（John Templeton）提早离开了在纽约的办公室，告诉他父亲说要去看温赖特小姐。他赶上了三点二十的火车，顺顺当当地到了威利斯敦，步行到温赖特家。尽管第二天就是婚礼、家里为筹备忙得团团转，他还是同温赖特小姐一起消磨了那个下午剩下的时光。谜就是从这里开始的。他们没有访客。至少，应门的女仆说没有。她同家里其他人一样忙，而且我记得前门没有上锁——在威利斯敦，除了夜里，我们是不锁门的。」

他找到了那页纸，停了一停，好让这些事实在我们脑子里扎下根。

===Original===

“Mrs. Wainwright and Miss Marian Wainwright, the sister, were busy about the house. Mrs. Wainwright wished to consult Laura about something. She summoned the maid and asked if Mr. Templeton and Miss Wainwright were in the house. The maid replied that she would see, and this is her affidavit. Ahem! I’ll skip the legal part: ‘I knocked at the library door twice, but obtaining no answer, I supposed they had gone out for a walk or perhaps a ride across country as they often did. I opened the door partly and looked in. There was a silence in the room, a strange, queer silence. I opened the door further and, looking toward the davenport in the corner, I saw Miss Laura and Mr. Templeton in such an awkward position. They looked as if they had fallen asleep. His head was thrown back against the cushions of the davenport, and on his face was a most awful look. It was discoloured. Her head had fallen forward on his shoulder, sideways, and on her face, too, was the same terrible stare and the same discolouration. Their right hands were tightly clasped.

“ ‘I called to them. They did not answer. Then the horrible truth flashed on me. They were dead. I felt giddy for a minute, but quickly recovered myself, and with a cry for help I rushed to Mrs. Wainwright’s room, shrieking that they were dead. Mrs. Wainwright fainted. Miss Marian called the doctor on the telephone and helped us restore her mother. She seemed perfectly cool in the tragedy, and I do not know what we servants should have done if she had not been there to direct us. The house was frantic, and Mr. Wainwright was not at home.

“ ‘I did not detect any odour when I opened the library door. No glasses or bottles or vials or other receptacles which could have held poison were discovered or removed by me, or to the best of my knowledge and belief by anyone else.’ ”

“What happened next?” asked Craig eagerly.

===Chinese===

「温赖特夫人和妹妹玛丽安·温赖特小姐（Miss Marian Wainwright）正在屋里忙前忙后。温赖特夫人有点事要同劳拉（Laura）商量。她把女仆叫来，问坦普尔顿先生和温赖特小姐在不在家。女仆答应去看看——这便是她的证词。啊咳！法律程式的部分我跳过去：『我敲了两下书房的门，没有人应。我以为他们像往常那样出去散步，或是到乡间骑马兜风去了。我把门推开一条缝，朝里看去。屋里一片寂静，一种奇怪的、异样的寂静。我把门再推大了些，朝角落里那张长靠椅（davenport）望去，只见劳拉小姐和坦普尔顿先生的姿势说不出的别扭。他们看上去像是睡着了。他的头向后仰在长靠椅的软垫上，脸上是一副骇人的神情，颜色都变了。她的头歪着，前倾在他肩上，她脸上也是同样可怕的眼神、同样的变色。他们的右手紧紧相握。

「『我喊他们。他们没有应声。刹那间，可怕的事实闪过我的脑海：他们死了。我头晕了一阵，但很快定下神来，一面呼救，一面冲向温赖特夫人的房间，尖声喊叫他们死了。温赖特夫人晕了过去。玛丽安小姐打电话请医生，又帮我们把夫人救醒。她在这场变故里镇定得出奇；要不是有她在那里指点，我们这些做下人的真不知如何是好。全家乱成了一锅粥，而温赖特先生偏偏不在家。

「『我打开书房门时没有察觉任何气味。没有发现、我也没有拿走任何玻璃杯、瓶子、小药瓶或其他可能盛过毒药的容器；据我所知所信，也没有别人发现或拿走过。』」

「后来呢？」克雷格急切地问。

===Original===

“The family physician arrived and sent for the coroner immediately, and later for myself. You see, he thought at once of murder.”

“But the coroner, I understand, thinks differently,” prompted Kennedy.

“Yes, the coroner has declared the case to be accidental. He says that the weight of evidence points positively to asphyxiation. Still, how can it be asphyxiation? They could have escaped from the room at any time; the door was not locked. I tell you, in spite of the fact that the tests for poison in their mouths, stomachs, and blood have so far revealed nothing, I still believe that John Templeton and Laura Wainwright were murdered.”

Kennedy looked at his watch thoughtfully. “You have told me just enough to make me want to see the coroner himself,” he mused. “If we take the next train out to Williston with you, will you engage to get us a half-hour talk with him on the case, Mr. Whitney?”

===Chinese===

「家庭医生赶到后，立刻派人去请验尸官（coroner），随后又通知了我。你瞧，他头一个念头就是他杀。」

「可据我所知，验尸官不这么想。」肯尼迪提了一句。

「不错，验尸官已经宣布这是意外。他说证据的分量确凿地指向窒息。可是，窒息又从何谈起呢？他们随时都可以走出那个房间，门并没有锁。我告诉你，尽管对他们口腔、胃部和血液的毒物检验至今毫无所获，我依然坚信约翰·坦普尔顿和劳拉·温赖特（Laura Wainwright）是被人谋害的。」

肯尼迪若有所思地看了看表。「你讲得恰到好处，正好让我想亲眼见见验尸官。」他沉吟道。「要是我们搭下一班火车随你去威利斯敦，惠特尼先生，你能不能保证安排我们同他就这案子谈上半个小时？」

===Original===

“Surely. But we’ll have to start right away. I’ve finished my other business in New York. Inspector O’Connor﻿—ah, I see you know him﻿—has promised to secure the attendance of anyone whom I can show to be a material witness in the case. Come on, gentlemen: I’ll answer your other questions on the train.”

As we settled ourselves in the smoker, Whitney remarked in a low voice, “You know, someone has said that there is only one thing more difficult to investigate and solve than a crime whose commission is surrounded by complicated circumstances and that is a crime whose perpetration is wholly devoid of circumstances.”

“Are you so sure that this crime is wholly devoid of circumstances?” asked Craig.

“Professor,” he replied, “I’m not sure of anything in this case. If I were I should not require your assistance. I would like the credit of solving it myself, but it is beyond me. Just think of it: so far we haven’t a clue, at least none that shows the slightest promise, although we have worked night and day for a week. It’s all darkness. The facts are so simple that they give us nothing to work on. It is like a blank sheet of paper.”

===Chinese===

「当然能。不过得马上动身。我在纽约的其他事都办完了。奥康纳警督——啊，看来你们认识他——已经答应负责传人到案，只要我能证明谁是本案的重要证人（material witness）。走吧，二位，其余的问题我在火车上回答。」

我们在吸烟车厢里坐定之后，惠特尼压低声音说：「你们知道，有人说过，世上只有一件事比案情扑朔迷离的罪案更难侦查破解，那就是作案过程全无线索可循的罪案。」

「你这么肯定，这桩罪案真的全无线索可循吗？」克雷格问。

「教授，」他答道，「这件案子里我对什么都拿不准。要是拿得准，我就不必劳你相助了。我本想独享破案的功劳，可它实在超出了我的本事。你想想：我们夜以继日干了整整一个星期，到现在连一条线索都没有——至少没有一条露出丝毫指望的。四处一片漆黑。事实简单得让我们无从下手。就像一张白纸。」

===Original===

Kennedy said nothing, and the district attorney proceeded: “I don’t blame Mr. Nott, the coroner, for thinking it an accident. But to my mind, some master criminal must have arranged this very baffling simplicity of circumstances. You recall that the front door was unlocked. This person must have entered the house unobserved, not a difficult thing to do, for the Wainwright house is somewhat isolated. Perhaps this person brought along some poison in the form of a beverage, and induced the two victims to drink. And then, this person must have removed the evidences as swiftly as they were brought in and by the same door. That, I think, is the only solution.”

“That is not the only solution. It is one solution,” interrupted Kennedy quietly.

“Do you think someone in the house did it?” I asked quickly.

“I think,” replied Craig, carefully measuring his words, “that if poison was given them it must have been by someone they both knew pretty well.”

===Chinese===

肯尼迪没有作声，检察官接着说：「我不怪验尸官诺特先生（Mr. Nott）把它当作事故。但依我看，这局外人看来一筹莫展的简单，多半是哪个高明罪犯一手安排的。你记得吧，前门没有上锁。此人必定是神不知鬼不觉地进了屋——这不难，温赖特家的房子位置有些偏僻。也许此人随身带来某种掺在饮料里的毒药，诱得两个被害人喝了下去。然后，此人必定又像来时一样迅捷、从同一扇门把罪证移走了。我想，解释只有这一个。」

「那不是唯一的解释。那是一种解释。」肯尼迪不动声色地插了进来。

「你认为是屋里的人干的？」我连忙问。

「我认为，」克雷格字斟句酌地答道，「如果他们真是被下了毒，那下手的人必定是他们两个都相当熟识的。」

===Original===

No one said a word, until at last I broke the silence. “I know from the gossip of the *Star* office that many Williston people say that Marian was very jealous of her sister Laura for capturing the catch of the season. Williston people don’t hesitate to hint at it.”

Whitney produced another document from that fertile green bag. It was another affidavit. He handed it to us. It was a statement signed by Mrs. Wainwright, and read:

“Before God, my daughter Marian is innocent. If you wish to find out all, find out more about the past history of Mr. Templeton before he became engaged to Laura. She would never in the world have committed suicide. She was too bright and cheerful for that, even if Mr. Templeton had been about to break off the engagement. My daughters Laura and Marian were always treated by Mr. Wainwright and myself exactly alike. Of course they had their quarrels, just as all sisters do, but there was never, to my certain knowledge, a serious disagreement, and I was always close enough to my girls to know. No, Laura was murdered by someone outside.”

Kennedy did not seem to attach much importance to this statement. “Let us see,” he began reflectively. “First, we have a young woman especially attractive and charming in both person and temperament. She is just about to be married and, if the reports are to be believed, there was no cloud on her happiness. Secondly, we have a young man whom everyone agrees to have been of an ardent, energetic, optimistic temperament. He had everything to live for, presumably. So far, so good. Everyone who has investigated this case, I understand, has tried to eliminate the double-suicide and the suicide-and-murder theories. That is all right, providing the facts are as stated. We shall see, later, when we interview the coroner. Now, Mr. Whitney, suppose you tell us briefly what you have learned about the past history of the two unfortunate lovers.”

===Chinese===

谁都没有接话，最后还是我打破了沉默：「我从《明星报》馆里的闲话听说，许多威利斯敦人都讲，玛丽安非常嫉妒妹妹劳拉抢走了当季最抢手的金龟婿。威利斯敦人暗示起这个来可毫不嘴软。」

惠特尼又从那只取之不尽的绿提包里掏出一份文件，是另一份证词宣誓书。他递给我们。那是温赖特夫人署名的一份陈述，写道：

「在上帝面前，我的女儿玛丽安是无辜的。你们若想查个水落石出，就去多查查坦普尔顿先生同劳拉订婚之前的经历。她无论如何也不会自杀。她那样开朗爱笑，绝不会走这条路，哪怕坦普尔顿先生真要毁约也罢。我的女儿劳拉和玛丽安，温赖特先生和我向来一视同仁。她们当然拌过嘴，天下姐妹都这样，但据我确切所知，从不曾有过一次认真的龃龉，而我的女儿们，我向来了如指掌。不，劳拉是外人谋害的。」

肯尼迪似乎并不怎么看重这份陈述。「我们来理一理，」他若有所思地开了腔。「首先，是一位容貌与性情都格外迷人的年轻姑娘。她就要出嫁了，而且——倘若报上说的可信——她的幸福美满无缺。其次，是一位人人都公认热情、干劲十足、乐观开朗的青年。照理说，他前程似锦，样样都值得留恋。到这里为止，都说得通。据我了解，凡是查过这件案子的人，都力图排除双双自杀与『一自杀一他杀』的理论。这没有问题，前提是事实确如所述。等会儿见了验尸官，自见分晓。好了，惠特尼先生，请你扼要讲讲，这对不幸恋人过去的经历，你都查到了些什么。」

===Original===

“Well, the Wainwrights are an old Westchester family, not very wealthy, but of the real aristocracy of the county. There were only two children, Laura and Marian. The Templetons were much the same sort of family. The children all attended a private school at White Plains, and there also they met Schuyler Vanderdyke. These four constituted a sort of little aristocracy in the school. I mention this, because Vanderdyke later became Laura’s first husband. This marriage with Templeton was a second venture.”

“How long ago was she divorced?” asked Craig attentively.

“About three years ago. I’m coming to that in a moment. The sisters went to college together, Templeton to law school, and Vanderdyke studied civil engineering. Their intimacy was pretty well broken up, all except Laura’s and Vanderdyke’s. Soon after he graduated he was taken into the construction department of the Central Railroad by his uncle, who was a vice-president, and Laura and he were married. As far as I can learn he had been a fellow of convivial habits at college, and about two years after their marriage his wife suddenly became aware of what had long been well known in Williston, that Vanderdyke was paying marked attention to a woman named Miss Laporte in New York.

“No sooner had Laura Vanderdyke learned of this intimacy of her husband,” continued Whitney, “than she quietly hired private detectives to shadow him, and on their evidence she obtained a divorce. The papers were sealed, and she resumed her maiden name.

===Chinese===

「嗯，温赖特家是威斯特彻斯特的老门户，算不上富有，却是县里真正的望族。家里只有劳拉和玛丽安两个女儿。坦普尔顿家也是差不多的人家。几个孩子都在白原（White Plains）一所私立学校念书，也正是在那里，他们认识了斯凯勒·范戴克（Schuyler Vanderdyke）。这四个人在学校里自成了一个小小的贵族圈子。我提这一笔，是因为范戴克后来成了劳拉的第一任丈夫。这回同坦普尔顿的婚事，是她的第二回。」

「她离婚是几年前的事？」克雷格留神问。

「大约三年前。这事我马上就要讲到。姐妹俩一起上了大学，坦普尔顿进了法学院，范戴克学的是土木工程。小时候那伙人的亲密大抵散了，唯独劳拉和范戴克的没有散。他毕业后不久，被当副总裁的舅舅延揽进中央铁路公司（Central Railroad）的工程部门，随后他和劳拉结了婚。据我了解，他在大学里本是个贪恋杯中之物的角色；婚后约莫两年，他的妻子突然察觉到一件威利斯敦早已尽人皆知的事——范戴克正对纽约一个名叫拉波尔特小姐（Miss Laporte）的女人大献殷勤。

「劳拉·范戴克一发觉丈夫的这层私情，」惠特尼接着说，「便不动声色地雇了私家侦探盯他的梢，并凭侦探取得的证据离了婚。案卷被封存，她也恢复了娘家的姓。

===Original===

“As far as I can find out, Vanderdyke then disappeared from her life. He resigned his position with the railroad and joined a party of engineers exploring the upper Amazon. Later he went to Venezuela. Miss Laporte also went to South America about the same time, and was for a time in Venezuela, and later in Peru.

“Vanderdyke seems to have dropped all his early associations completely, though at present I find he is back in New York raising capital for a company to exploit a new asphalt concession in the interior of Venezuela. Miss Laporte has also reappeared in New York as Mrs. Ralston, with a mining claim in the mountains of Peru.”

“And Templeton?” asked Craig. “Had he had any previous matrimonial ventures?”

“No, none. Of course he had had love affairs, mostly with the country-club set. He had known Miss Laporte pretty well, too, while he was in law school in New York. But when he settled down to work he seems to have forgotten all about the girls for a couple of years or so. He was very anxious to get ahead, and let nothing stand in his way. He was admitted to the bar and taken in by his father as junior member of the firm of Templeton, Mills & Templeton. Not long ago he was appointed a special master to take testimony in the get-rich-quick-company prosecutions, and I happen to know that he was making good in the investigation.”

===Chinese===

「据我查到的情况，范戴克从此便从她的生活里消失了。他辞去了铁路公司的职务，加入了一支勘探亚马逊河上游的工程队，后来去了委内瑞拉。拉波尔特小姐大约也在同一时期去了南美，先在委内瑞拉待了一阵，后来又到了秘鲁。

「范戴克似乎把早年的一切旧交都断得干干净净，不过眼下我发现他又回到了纽约，正替一家公司筹集资金，要开发委内瑞拉内陆一处新的沥青开采权。拉波尔特小姐也以拉尔斯顿夫人（Mrs. Ralston）的身份重新在纽约露面，手里握着秘鲁山区的一处矿权。」

「那坦普尔顿呢？」克雷格问。「他从前有过婚史没有？」

「没有，一回也没有。恋爱的风波当然有过，多半在乡村俱乐部的圈子里。他在纽约念法学院的时候，同拉波尔特小姐也相当熟。不过等他安下心来做事，大约有两三年，似乎把姑娘们忘了个干净。他一心想往上爬，什么都不肯挡道。他取得了律师资格，被他父亲吸收为坦普尔顿—米尔斯—坦普尔顿律师事务所（Templeton, Mills & Templeton）的年轻合伙人。不久前，他被任命为『快速致富公司』系列控案中受命取证的特别主事官（special master），而我恰好知道，他在那桩调查里干得有声有色。」

===Original===

Kennedy nodded. “What sort of fellow personally was Templeton?” he asked.

“Very popular,” replied the district attorney, “both at the country club and in his profession in New York. He was a fellow of naturally commanding temperament﻿—the Templetons were always that way. I doubt if many young men even with his chances could have gained such a reputation at thirty-five as his. Socially he was very popular, too, a great catch for all the sly mamas of the country club who had marriageable daughters. He liked automobiles and outdoor sports, and he was strong in politics, too. That was how he got ahead so fast.

“Well, to cut the story short, Templeton met the Wainwright girls again last summer at a resort on Long Island. They had just returned from a long trip abroad, spending most of the time in the Far East with their father, whose firm has business interests in China. The girls were very attractive. They rode and played tennis and golf better than most of the men, and this fall Templeton became a frequent visitor at the Wainwright home in Williston.

“People who know them best tell me that his first attentions were paid to Marian, a very dashing and ambitious young woman. Nearly every day Templeton’s car stopped at the house and the girls and some friend of Templeton’s in the country club went for a ride. They tell me that at this time Marian always sat with Templeton on the front seat. But after a few weeks the gossips﻿—nothing of that sort ever escapes Williston﻿—said that the occupant of the front seat was Laura. She often drove the car herself and was very clever at it. At any rate, not long after that the engagement was announced.”

===Chinese===

肯尼迪点点头。「坦普尔顿本人是个什么样的人？」他问。

「非常有人缘，」检察官答道，「在乡村俱乐部如此，在纽约的律师界也是如此。他天生一副发号施令的脾性——坦普尔顿家的人历来如此。我怀疑，纵然有他那样的机遇，又有几个年轻人能在三十五岁上挣到他那样的名声。社交场上他也很吃得开，是乡村俱乐部里那些膝下有待嫁女儿、心里打小算盘的妈妈们眼中的头号抢手货。他喜欢汽车和户外运动，政治上也有分量。他升得那么快，靠的就是这些。」

「咳，长话短说，去年夏天，坦普尔顿在长岛一处避暑地同温赖特家姐妹重逢。她们刚结束漫长的国外旅行回来，大部分时间同父亲待在远东——她们父亲的公司在中国有生意。两位姑娘十分迷人，骑马、网球、高尔夫，打得比大多数男人还漂亮。到今年秋天，坦普尔顿便成了威利斯敦温赖特家的常客。

「同他们最熟的人告诉我，他起初献殷勤的对象是玛丽安——一位英姿飒爽、野心勃勃的年轻姑娘。那阵子，几乎天天有坦普尔顿的汽车停在温赖特家门口，载上姐妹俩，再捎上他乡村俱乐部里的哪个朋友出去兜风。据他们说，起初总是玛丽安同坦普尔顿并排坐在前座。可几个星期之后，长舌妇们——这种事在威利斯敦从来瞒不住——咬定前座上的人换成了劳拉。她还常常亲自开车，手法十分娴熟。不管怎样，没过多久，订婚的消息便宣布了。」

===Original===

As he walked up from the pretty little Williston station Kennedy asked: “One more question, Mr. Whitney. How did Marian take the engagement?”

The district attorney hesitated. “I will be perfectly frank, Mr. Kennedy,” he answered. “The country-club people tell me that the girls were very cool toward each other. That was why I got that statement from Mrs. Wainwright. I wish to be perfectly fair to everyone concerned in this case.”

We found the coroner quite willing to talk, in spite of the fact that the hour was late. “My friend, Mr. Whitney, here, still holds the poison theory,” began the coroner, “in spite of the fact that everything points absolutely toward asphyxiation. If I had been able to discover the slightest trace of illuminating-gas in the room I should have pronounced it asphyxia at once. All the symptoms accorded with it. But the asphyxia was not caused by escaping illuminating-gas.

“There was an antique charcoal-brazier in the room, and I have ascertained that it was lighted. Now, anything like a brazier will, unless there is proper ventilation, give rise to carbonic oxide or carbon monoxide gas, which is always present in the products of combustion, often to the extent of from five to ten percent. A very slight quantity of this gas, insufficient even to cause an odour in a room, will give a severe headache, and a case is recorded where a whole family in Glasgow was poisoned without knowing it by the escape of this gas. A little over one percent of it in the atmosphere is fatal, if breathed for any length of time. You know, it is a product of combustion, and is very deadly﻿—it is the much-dreaded white damp or afterdamp of a mine explosion.

===Chinese===

从秀丽的小小威利斯敦车站走上坡时，肯尼迪问：「还有个问题，惠特尼先生。玛丽安对这门婚事是什么态度？」

检察官迟疑了一下。「我就对你完全直说吧，肯尼迪先生，」他答道，「乡村俱乐部的人告诉我，姐妹俩彼此十分冷淡。我之所以去讨温赖特夫人那份陈述，原因就在这里。凡是与本案有关的人，我都不偏不倚。」

虽然时候已晚，我们发现验尸官倒十分乐意开口。「我这位朋友惠特尼先生仍旧抱着投毒论不放，」验尸官开了腔，「尽管一切都确凿地指向窒息。倘若我能在那个房间里查到照明煤气（illuminating-gas）的丝毫痕迹，我早就可以断定为煤气窒息了。种种症状全都吻合。可这一回的窒息，并不是泄漏的照明煤气造成的。

「房间里有一只古董炭盆，我已查明那天它是点着的。要知道，凡是这类炭盆，只要通风不良，就会产生一氧化碳（carbonic oxide / carbon monoxide）——燃烧的产物里总少不了这种气体，常常占到百分之五到十。极少量的一氧化碳，甚至不足以让房里闻出气味，就会引起剧烈的头痛；有案可查，格拉斯哥（Glasgow）有一户人家就是被这种气体泄漏毒倒的，自己还浑然不觉。空气里这种气体只要略高于百分之一，呼吸上一阵子便会致命。你们知道，它是燃烧的产物，非常要命——就是矿井爆炸中令人谈之色变的『白湿气』（white damp），又称『爆后瓦斯』（afterdamp）。」

===Original===

“I’m going to tell you a secret which I have not given out to the press yet. I tried an experiment in a closed room today, lighting the brazier. Some distance from it I placed a cat confined in a cage so it could not escape. In an hour and a half the cat was asphyxiated.”

The coroner concluded with an air of triumph that quite squelched the district attorney.

Kennedy was all attention. “Have you preserved samples of the blood of Mr. Templeton and Miss Wainwright?” he asked.

“Certainly. I have them in my office.”

===Chinese===

「我要告诉你们一个还没有透露给报界的秘密。今天我在一间密闭的屋子里做了个实验，把炭盆点着，又在离它几步远的地方放了一只关在笼子里逃不出去的猫。一个半钟头之后，猫就窒息死了。」

验尸官说完，一副得胜的神气，噎得检察官半句话也答不上来。

肯尼迪聚精会神。「坦普尔顿先生和温赖特小姐的血样，你保存下来了没有？」他问。

「当然保存了。就在我的办公室里。」

===Original===

The coroner, who was also a local physician, led us back into his private office.

“And the cat?” added Craig.

Doctor Nott produced it in a covered basket.

Quickly Kennedy drew off a little of the blood of the cat and held it up to the light along with the human samples. The difference was apparent.

===Chinese===

这位验尸官兼本地医生领我们进了他里间的私人诊室。

「那只猫呢？」克雷格补了一句。

诺特医生（Doctor Nott）用一只盖着盖的提篮把它提了出来。

肯尼迪迅速抽取了一点猫血，同那几份人的血样一起举到光下比照。差别一目了然。

===Original===

“You see,” he explained, “carbon monoxide combines firmly with the blood, destroying the red colouring matter of the red corpuscles. No, Doctor, I’m afraid it wasn’t carbonic oxide that killed the lovers, although it certainly killed the cat.”

Doctor Nott was crestfallen, but still unconvinced. “If my whole medical reputation were at stake,” he repeated, “I should still be compelled to swear to asphyxia. I’ve seen it too often, to make a mistake. Carbonic oxide or not, Templeton and Miss Wainwright were asphyxiated.”

It was now Whitney’s chance to air his theory.

“I have always inclined toward the cyanide-of-potassium theory, either that it was administered in a drink or perhaps injected by a needle,” he said. “One of the chemists has reported that there was a possibility of slight traces of cyanide in the mouths.”

===Chinese===

「你们看，」他解释道，「一氧化碳会同血液牢牢结合，破坏红细胞的红色素。所以，医生，害死这对恋人的恐怕不是一氧化碳——虽然它确实害死了那只猫。」

诺特医生一脸沮丧，心里却仍不服。「哪怕搭上我行医一辈子的名声，」他还是那句话，「我也只能起誓：这是窒息。这种症候我见得太多，错不了。不管是不是一氧化碳，坦普尔顿和温赖特小姐就是窒息死的。」

这下轮到惠特尼来亮出他的理论了。

「我一向倾向于氰化钾（cyanide of potassium）的解释，要么掺在饮料里让他俩喝下，要么就是用针注射进去的，」他说。「有一位化验师报告说，两人口腔里可能存有微量的氰化物。」

===Original===

“If it had been cyanide,” replied Craig, looking reflectively at the two jars before him on the table, “these blood specimens would be blue in colour and clotted. But they are not. Then, too, there is a substance in the saliva which is used in the process of digestion. It gives a reaction which might very easily be mistaken for a slight trace of cyanide. I think that explains what the chemist discovered; no more, no less. The cyanide theory does not fit.”

“One chemist hinted at nux vomica,” volunteered the coroner. “He said it wasn’t nux vomica, but that the blood test showed something very much like it. Oh, we’ve looked for morphine chloroform, ether, all the ordinary poisons, besides some of the little known alkaloids. Believe me, Professor Kennedy, it *was* asphyxia.”

I could tell by the look that crossed Kennedy’s face that at last a ray of light had pierced the darkness. “Have you any spirits of turpentine in the office?” he asked.

The coroner shook his head and took a step toward the telephone as if to call the drugstore in town.

===Chinese===

「倘若真是氰化物，」克雷格答道，一面若有所思地望着面前桌上那两只广口瓶，「这些血样就会发蓝、结块。可它们没有。再者，唾液里有一种参与消化的物质，它会起一种反应，很容易被误当作氰化物的微量痕迹。我想那位化验师查明的正是这个，不多也不少。氰化物的说法对不上。」

「还有一位化验师提到过马钱子（nux vomica），」验尸官主动说。「他说那不是马钱子，但血液检验显示出某种非常相近的东西。唉，吗啡、氯仿、乙醚，凡是寻常的毒药我们全查过了，连几种鲜为人知的生物碱也查了。相信我吧，肯尼迪教授，这就是窒息。」

肯尼迪脸上掠过的神情让我看出，一线光明终于刺破了黑暗。「你诊室里有松节油（spirits of turpentine）吗？」他问。

验尸官摇了摇头，朝电话机迈出一步，像是要往镇上的药房挂个电话。

===Original===

“Or ether?” interrupted Craig. “Ether will do.”

“Oh, yes, plenty of ether.”

Craig poured a little of one of the blood samples from the jar into a tube and added a few drops of ether. A cloudy dark precipitate formed. He smiled quietly and said, half to himself, “I thought so.”

“What is it?” asked the coroner eagerly. “Nux vomica?”

===Chinese===

「那乙醚呢？」克雷格插话，「乙醚也行。」

「哦，乙醚有，有的是。」

克雷格从瓶里往试管中倒了一点血样，又滴入几滴乙醚。一种混浊的深色沉淀物析了出来。他淡淡一笑，半像自言自语地说：「果然如此。」

「那是什么？」验尸官急切地问。「马钱子？」

===Original===

Craig shook his head as he stared at the black precipitate. “You were perfectly right about the asphyxiation, Doctor,” he remarked slowly, “but wrong as to the cause. It wasn’t carbon monoxide or illuminating-gas. And you, Mr. Whitney, were right about the poison, too. Only it is a poison neither of you ever heard of.”

“What is it?” we asked simultaneously.

“Let me take these samples and make some further tests. I am sure of it, but it is new to me. Wait till tomorrow night, when my chain of evidence is completed. Then you are all cordially invited to attend at my laboratory at the university. I’ll ask you, Mr. Whitney, to come armed with a warrant for John or Jane Doe. Please see that the Wainwrights, particularly Marian, are present. You can tell Inspector O’Connor that Mr. Vanderdyke and Mrs. Ralston are required as material witnesses﻿—anything so long as you are sure that these five persons are present. Good night, gentlemen.”

We rode back to the city in silence, but as we neared the station, Kennedy remarked: “You see, Walter, these people are like the newspapers. They are floundering around in a sea of unrelated facts. There is more than they think back of this crime. I’ve been revolving in my mind how it will be possible to get some inkling about this concession of Vanderdyke’s, the mining claim of Mrs. Ralston, and the exact itinerary of the Wainwright trip in the Far East. Do you think you can get that information for me? I think it will take me all day tomorrow to isolate this poison and get things in convincing shape on that score. Meanwhile if you can see Vanderdyke and Mrs. Ralston you can help me a great deal. I am sure you will find them very interesting people.”

===Chinese===

克雷格盯着那黑色的沉淀物，摇了摇头。「说窒息，医生，你说得完全对，」他缓缓说道，「但死因找错了。不是一氧化碳，也不是照明煤气。而你，惠特尼先生，说投毒，你也说对了。只不过这毒，你们两位谁都从来没有听说过。」

「是什么？」我们异口同声地问。

「让我把这些血样带回去，再做几项检验。我有把握，只是这东西对我也新鲜。等到明天晚上，等我把证据链补齐全，就诚心邀请诸位光临我在大学的实验室。惠特尼先生，请你届时带着一张『无名氏』（John or Jane Doe）逮捕令来。务必让温赖特一家到场，尤其是玛丽安。你可以告诉奥康纳警督，本案需要范戴克先生和拉尔斯顿夫人作为重要证人——总而言之，务必保证这五个人到场。晚安，二位。」

我们默默乘车回城，快到站时，肯尼迪开了口：「你瞧，沃尔特，这些人跟报纸一个样，都在一片互不相干的事实之海里扑腾。这桩罪案背后的名堂，比他们想的要深。我一直在盘算，怎样才能摸到一点头绪，弄清范戴克那笔特许权、拉尔斯顿夫人的矿权，还有温赖特家远东之行的确切路线。你看你能替我弄到这些情报吗？明天一整天，我大概都得花在分离这种毒药、把这一头的证据做得令人信服上面。这期间，你若能去见见范戴克和拉尔斯顿夫人，可就帮了我的大忙。我敢说，你会发现他们两位非常有趣。」

===Original===

“I have been told that she is quite a female high financier,” I replied, tacitly accepting Craig’s commission. “Her story is that her claim is situated near the mine of a group of powerful American capitalists, who are opposed to having any competition, and on the strength of that story she has been raking in the money right and left. I don’t know Vanderdyke, never heard of him before, but no doubt he has some equally interesting game.”

“Don’t let them think you connect them with the case, however,” cautioned Craig.

Early the next morning I started out on my quest for facts, though not so early but that Kennedy had preceded me to his work in his laboratory. It was not very difficult to get Mrs. Ralston to talk about her troubles with the government. In fact, I did not even have to broach the subject of the death of Templeton. She volunteered the information that in his handling of her case he had been very unjust to her, in spite of the fact that she had known him well a long time ago. She even hinted that she believed he represented the combination of capitalists who were using the government to aid their own monopoly and prevent the development of her mine. Whether it was an obsession of her mind, or merely part of her clever scheme, I could not make out. I noted, however, that when she spoke of Templeton it was in a studied, impersonal way, and that she was at pains to lay the blame for the governmental interference rather on the rival mine-owners.

It quite surprised me when I found from the directory that Vanderdyke’s office was on the floor below in the same building. Like Mrs. Ralston’s, it was open, but not doing business, pending the investigation by the Post-Office Department.

===Chinese===

「听说她是个了不起的金融女强人，」我答道，默默认下了克雷格的差事。「她的说法是，她那处矿权紧挨着一伙有钱有势的美国资本家的大矿，而那伙人最怕有人来竞争。凭着这套说辞，她左右开弓，大把大把地捞钱。范戴克我不认识，从前连听也没听说过，不过不用问，他玩的把戏也同样有意思。」
