「只是，别让他们觉得你把他们同这桩案子挂上了钩。」克雷格叮嘱道。

第二天一早我就出门查访去了，不过再早也早不过肯尼迪——他已经先我一步进实验室干上了。要让拉尔斯顿夫人谈她同政府之间的纠纷，并不怎么费事。实际上，坦普尔顿的死，我压根用不着开口提。她主动说，坦普尔顿在经办她那桩案子时对她极不公道，尽管很久以前她就同他相熟。她甚至暗示，她相信坦普尔顿是那伙资本家联合体的代表，那伙人正利用政府维护自家的垄断，阻挠她那座矿的开发。这究竟是她的心病，还只是她那套精明把戏的一部分，我摸不透。不过我留意到，她谈起坦普尔顿时用的是一种刻意的不带感情的口吻，而且极力把政府刁难的罪责推到与她作对的矿主头上。

等我从人名地址录上查到范戴克的办公室就在同一幢楼的下一层时，着实吃了一惊。同拉尔斯顿夫人的办公室一样，门开着，却没有营业——都在等邮政部（Post-Office Department）的调查结果。

===Original===

Vanderdyke was a type of which I had seen many before. Well dressed to the extreme, he displayed all those evidences of prosperity which are the stock in trade of the man with securities to sell. He grasped my hand when I told him I was going to present the other side of the post-office cases and held it between both of his as if he had known me all his life. Only the fact that he had never seen me before prevented his calling me by my first name. I took mental note of his stock of jewellery, the pin in his tie that might almost have been the Hope diamond, the heavy watch chain across his chest, and a very brilliant seal ring of lapis lazuli on the hand that grasped mine. He saw me looking at it and smiled.

“My dear fellow, we have deposits of that stuff that would make a fortune if we could get the machinery to get at it. Why, sir, there is lapis lazuli enough on our claim to make enough ultramarine paint to supply all the artists to the end of the world. Actually we could afford to crush it up and sell it as paint. And that is merely incidental to the other things on the concession. The asphalt’s the thing. That’s where the big money is. When we get started, sir, the old asphalt trust will simply melt away, melt away.”

He blew a cloud of tobacco smoke and let it dissolve significantly in the air.

When it came to talking about the suits, however, Vanderdyke was not so communicative as Mrs. Ralston, but he was also not so bitter against either the post-office or Templeton.

===Chinese===

范戴克这种人，我从前见得多了。他衣着考究到了极点，浑身上下透着股兴旺气——这正是兜售证券的人的看家行头。听我说要报道邮政案件的另一方，他一把攥住我的手，把它夹在自己两手当中，仿佛跟我相识了一辈子；只因从前没有见过我，才没好意思直呼我的教名。我心里暗暗记下了他那一身行头：领带上那枚几乎赶得上『希望之钻』（Hope diamond）的别针，横过胸前的那条粗表链，还有攥住我那只手上亮闪闪的青金石（lapis lazuli）图章戒指。他见我盯着那戒指看，笑了。

「亲爱的朋友，那种矿藏我们多得是，只要弄到机器去开采，就是一笔天大的家业。嗬，先生，我们那块矿权上的青金石，足够制成群青（ultramarine）颜料，供全天下的画家用到世界末日。真到了那一步，我们尽可以把它碾碎了当颜料卖。而这在整份特许权里，不过是捎带的零头。沥青才是正经。大钱全在那上面。等我们一开工，先生，那家老沥青托拉斯就只有冰消瓦解的份儿——冰消瓦解。」

他喷出一团烟雾，意味深长地任它在空气里消散。

不过，一谈到那几桩官司，范戴克就不像拉尔斯顿夫人那样知无不言了，但对邮政部和坦普尔顿，他也不像她那样怨气冲天。

===Original===

“Poor Templeton,” he said. “I used to know him years ago when we were boys. Went to school with him and all that sort of thing, you know, but until I ran across him, or rather he ran across me, in this investigation I hadn’t heard much about him. Pretty clever fellow he was, too. The state will miss him, but my lawyer tells me that we should have won the suit anyhow, even if that unfortunate tragedy hadn’t occurred. Most unaccountable, wasn’t it? I’ve read about it in the papers for old time’s sake, and can make nothing out of it.”

I said nothing, but wondered how he could pass so lightheartedly over the death of the woman who had once been his wife. However, I said nothing. The result was he launched forth again on the riches of his Venezuelan concession and loaded me down with “literature,” which I crammed into my pocket for future reference.

My next step was to drop into the office of a Spanish-America paper whose editor was especially well informed on South American affairs.

“Do I know Mrs. Ralston?” he repeated, thoughtfully lighting one of those black cigarettes that look so vicious and are so mild. “I should say so. I’ll tell you a little story about her. Three or four years ago she turned up in Caracas. I don’t know who Mr. Ralston was﻿—perhaps there never was any Mr. Ralston. Anyhow, she got in with the official circle of the Castro government and was very successful as an adventuress. She has considerable business ability and represented a certain group of Americans. But, if you recall, when Castro was eliminated pretty nearly everyone who had stood high with him went, too. It seems that a number of the old concessionaires played the game on both sides. This particular group had a man named Vanderdyke on the anti-Castro side. So, when Mrs. Ralston went, she just quietly sailed by way of Panama to the other side of the continent, to Peru﻿—they paid her well﻿—and Vanderdyke took the title role.

===Chinese===

「可怜的坦普尔顿，」他说。「多年前我们还是半大孩子的时候，我就认识他了。跟他同过学，诸如此类，你知道的。可直到这回调查里撞见他——或者不如说他撞见我——之前，我几乎没听说过他的什么消息。他也是个相当精明的人。州里会想念他的。不过我的律师告诉我，即便没有出那场不幸的悲剧，那场官司我们横竖也是要赢的。真是匪夷所思，是不是？看在旧日情分上，我在报上读了相关的报道，可怎么也理不出头绪。」

我什么也没说，心里却纳闷：对曾经做自己妻子的那个女人的死，他怎么能这样轻飘飘地一笔带过。不过我还是什么都没说。结果，他又滔滔不绝地大谈起他那委内瑞拉特许权的财富，塞给我一大堆「宣传资料」，我把它们一股脑塞进口袋，留作日后参考。

我的下一站，是顺道走进一家西班牙语美洲报纸（Spanish-America paper）的报馆，那位主编对南美事务的消息格外灵通。

「我认不认识拉尔斯顿夫人？」他重复了一遍，若有所思地点起一支黑烟卷——那种烟看着凶，抽起来却极温和。「那还用说。我给你讲讲她的一段往事。三四年前，她现身加拉加斯（Caracas）。拉尔斯顿先生是谁，我不知道——也许压根就没有过什么拉尔斯顿先生。总之，她混进了卡斯特罗政府的官场圈子，当女冒险家（adventuress）当得顺风顺水。她颇有几分手腕，背后代表着一伙美国人。可是你总记得，卡斯特罗一垮台，凡是在他跟前得势的人差不多都跟着完蛋。看来那批老牌特许权商人惯于两头下注。这一伙在反卡斯特罗的那一头，就安插着一个叫范戴克的人。于是，拉尔斯顿夫人抽身而退，悄没声儿地取道巴拿马驶往大陆另一侧的秘鲁——那伙人给她的报酬颇为优厚——主角的位置便由范戴克接了过去。

===Original===

“Oh, yes, she and Vanderdyke were very good friends, very, indeed. I think they must have known each other here in the States. Still they played their parts well at the time. Since things have settled down in Venezuela, the concessionaires have found no further use for Vanderdyke either, and here they are, Vanderdyke and Mrs. Ralston, both in New York now, with two of the most outrageous schemes of financing ever seen on Broad Street. They have offices in the same building, they are together a great deal, and now I hear that the state attorney-general is after both of them.”

With this information and a very meagre report of the Wainwright trip to the Far East, which had taken in some out-of-the-way places apparently, I hastened back to Kennedy. He was surrounded by bottles, tubes, jars, retorts, Bunsen burners, everything in the science and art of chemistry, I thought.

I didn’t like the way he looked. His hand was unsteady, and his eyes looked badly, but he seemed quite put out when I suggested that he was working too hard over the case. I was worried about him, but rather than say anything to offend him I left him for the rest of the afternoon, only dropping in before dinner to make sure that he would not forget to eat something. He was then completing his preparations for the evening. They were of the simplest kind, apparently. In fact, all I could see was an apparatus which consisted of a rubber funnel, inverted and attached to a rubber tube which led in turn into a jar about a quarter full of water. Through the stopper of the jar another tube led to a tank of oxygen.

There were several jars of various liquids on the table and a number of chemicals. Among other things was a sort of gourd, encrusted with a black substance, and in a corner was a box from which sounds issued as if it contained something alive.

===Chinese===

「哦，没错，她同范戴克是老相好，交情笃得很呢。我看他们八成早就在美国本土相识了。不过当年各自的角色都演得滴水不漏。如今委内瑞拉的局势安定下来，特许权商人们也用不着范戴克了，于是乎，范戴克同拉尔斯顿夫人双双回了纽约，各揣一套百老街（Broad Street）上前所未见的、胆大包天的融资把戏。两人的办公室同在一幢楼里，过从甚密，眼下我还听说，州总检察长已经盯上他们两个了。」

揣着这些情报，外加一份关于温赖特家远东之行的语焉不详的报告——那趟行程看来到过几处偏僻地方——我匆匆赶回肯尼迪那里。只见他团团围在瓶子、试管、广口瓶、曲颈瓶、本生灯（Bunsen burners）中间，化学这门科学与技艺的家什，依我看是一样不缺。

他的气色我不喜欢。手不稳，眼神也不济。可我一劝他别为这案子太过劳神，他反倒老大不高兴。我很替他担心，但又不愿说冒犯他的话，便由他去了，直到晚饭前才又进去一趟，看看他别把吃饭忘了。那时他正在收拾晚上要用的家伙。表面上简单得很。说实话，我看得见的只有一套器具：一只倒置的橡胶漏斗，接在一根橡胶管上，管子又通进一只约盛了四分之一水的罐子；罐塞上另有一根管子，通向一罐氧气。

桌上摆着好几罐各式液体和一批药品。其中有一只小葫芦（gourd）模样的东西，外面结着一层黑色物质；屋角还有一只箱子，里面传出动静，像是装着什么活物。

===Original===

I did not trouble Kennedy with questions, for I was only too glad when he consented to take a brisk walk and join me in a thick porterhouse.

It was a large party that gathered in Kennedy’s laboratory that night, one of the largest he had ever had. Mr. and Mrs. Wainwright and Miss Marian came, the ladies heavily veiled. Doctor Nott and Mr. Whitney were among the first to arrive. Later came Mr. Vanderdyke and last of all Mrs. Ralston with Inspector O’Connor. Altogether it was an unwilling party.

“I shall begin,” said Kennedy, “by going over, briefly, the facts in this case.”

Tersely he summarised it, to my surprise laying great stress on the proof that the couple had been asphyxiated.

===Chinese===

我没有拿问题去烦肯尼迪——他总算肯出去快步走走，再陪我吃一块厚厚的上等腰肉牛排（porterhouse），我已经谢天谢地了。

那天晚上聚到肯尼迪实验室里的人可不少，算得上他历来聚会中数一数二的一次。温赖特先生偕夫人、玛丽安小姐都来了，两位女士都罩着厚面纱。诺特医生和惠特尼先生属于最先到的。随后是范戴克先生，最后到的是拉尔斯顿夫人，同来的还有奥康纳警督。总而言之，这是一场人人都不情愿的聚会。

「我先，」肯尼迪说，「把本案的事实简要过一遍。」

他简明扼要地作了归纳，让我惊讶的是，他特别强调了两人的确死于窒息这一点。

===Original===

“But it was no ordinary asphyxiation,” he continued. “We have to deal in this case with a poison which is apparently among the most subtle known. A particle of matter so minute as to be hardly distinguishable by the naked eye, on the point of a needle or a lancet, a prick of the skin scarcely felt under any circumstances and which would pass quite unheeded if the attention were otherwise engaged, and not all the power in the world﻿—unless one was fully prepared﻿—could save the life of the person in whose skin the puncture had been made.”

Craig paused a moment, but no one showed any evidence of being more than ordinarily impressed.

“This poison, I find, acts on the so-called endplates of the muscles and nerves. It produces complete paralysis, but not loss of consciousness, sensation, circulation, or respiration until the end approaches. It seems to be one of the most powerful sedatives I have ever heard of. When introduced in even a minute quantity it produces death finally by asphyxiation﻿—by paralysing the muscles of respiration. This asphyxia is what so puzzled the coroner.

“I will now inject a little of the blood serum of the victims into a white mouse.”

===Chinese===

「但这绝不是寻常的窒息，」他接着说。「在本案中，我们要对付的是一种显然属最隐秘难察之列的毒药。针尖或柳叶刀（lancet）尖上那一点点肉眼几乎分辨不出的东西，往皮肤上轻轻一刺，无论何时都几乎察觉不出，注意力若在别处更是全然无从留意——而一旦刺进谁的皮肤，天大的本领——除非早有万全准备——也救不回那人的性命。」

克雷格顿了顿，但谁也没有表现出超乎寻常的震动。

「我发现，这种毒药作用于肌肉和神经所谓的终板（endplates）。它造成彻底的瘫痪，却不损伤意识、感觉、血液循环和呼吸——直到终局临近。它似乎是我平生所闻最强效的镇静剂之一。哪怕只进入极微的量，最终也以窒息夺人性命——靠的是麻痹呼吸的肌肉。这种窒息，正是让验尸官大惑不解的地方。」

「现在，我来把两位被害人的一点血清注射进一只小白鼠体内。」

===Original===

He took a mouse from the box I had seen, and with a needle injected the serum. The mouse did not even wince, so lightly did he touch it, but as we watched, its life seemed gently to ebb away, without pain and without struggle. Its breath simply seemed to stop.

Next he took the gourd I had seen on the table and with a knife scraped off just the minutest particle of the black licorice-like stuff that encrusted it. He dissolved the particle in some alcohol and with a sterilised needle repeated his experiment on a second mouse. The effect was precisely similar to that produced by the blood on the first.

It did not seem to me that anyone showed any emotion except possibly the slight exclamation that escaped Miss Marian Wainwright. I fell to wondering whether it was prompted by a soft heart or a guilty conscience.

We were all intent on what Craig was doing, especially Doctor Nott, who now broke in with a question.

===Chinese===

他从我见过的那只箱子里取出一只小白鼠，用针把血清注了进去。他的手法轻得小白鼠连缩都没缩一下。就在我们眼前，它的生命仿佛被轻轻抽走，不痛苦，也不挣扎，呼吸就那么停了。

接着，他拿起我在桌上见过的那只葫芦，用刀从结在外面的那层黑色甘草状物质上刮下极细微的一点，把它溶进酒精，再用一根消过毒的针，在第二只小白鼠身上重复了实验。效果同血液在第一只身上引起的分毫不差。

依我看，没有人流露出什么情绪，唯一的例外，也许是玛丽安·温赖特小姐不由自主漏出的一声轻呼。我不禁琢磨：这一声，是出于心软，还是出于内疚。

大家的注意力都集中在克雷格手上的动作上，尤其是诺特医生，这时他插进来提了个问题。

===Original===

“Professor Kennedy, may I ask a question? Admitting that the first mouse died in an apparently similar manner to the second, what proof have you that the poison is the same in both cases? And if it is the same can you show that it affects human beings in the same way, and that enough of it has been discovered in the blood of the victims to have caused their death? In other words, I want the last doubt set aside. How do you know absolutely that this poison which you discovered in my office last night in that black precipitate when you added the ether﻿—how do you know that it asphyxiated the victims?”

If ever Craig startled me it was by his quiet reply. “I’ve isolated it in their blood, extracted it, sterilised it, and I’ve tried it on myself.”

In breathless amazement, with eyes riveted on Craig, we listened.

“Altogether I was able to recover from the blood samples of both of the victims of this crime six centigrams of the poison,” he pursued. “Starting with two centigrams of it as a moderate dose, I injected it into my right arm subcutaneously. Then I slowly worked my way up to three and then four centigrams. They did not produce any very appreciable results other than to cause some dizziness, slight vertigo, a considerable degree of lassitude, and an extremely painful headache of rather unusual duration. But five centigrams considerably improved on this. It caused a degree of vertigo and lassitude that was most distressing, and six centigrams, the whole amount which I had recovered from the samples of blood, gave me the fright of my life right here in this laboratory this afternoon.

===Chinese===

「肯尼迪教授，我能提个问题吗？就算第一只小白鼠的死状同第二只表面相似，你有什么证据，证明两种情况下是同一种毒药？退一步，就算相同，你又能证明它对人体起同样的作用吗？能证明被害人血液里发现的剂量足以致死吗？说白了，我要把最后一点疑心也除掉。昨晚在我办公室里，你往血样里加了乙醚、得到那黑色沉淀物时发现的这种毒——你怎么就绝对肯定，是它使被害人窒息而死？」

要说克雷格什么时候惊到过我，就是这句平静的回答。「我把它从他们的血液里分离了出来，提取，灭菌，然后——在我自己身上试过了。」

我们屏息凝神，目不转睛地盯着克雷格听下去。

「从这桩罪行两名被害人的血样里，我总共提取出六厘克（centigram）毒药，」他继续说。「我先以两厘克为适中的剂量，皮下注射进右臂。随后慢慢加到三厘克、四厘克。除了引起些许头晕、轻微的目眩、相当程度的倦怠，以及一场痛得厉害、缠绵许久不散的头痛之外，没有产生什么明显的反应。可五厘克就大大加重了：那目眩和倦怠令人苦不堪言；而六厘克——我从血样里提取出的全部剂量——今天下午就在这间实验室里，让我平生头一回吓破了胆。」

===Original===

“Perhaps I was not wise in giving myself so large an injection on a day when I was overheated and below par otherwise because of the strain I have been under in handling this case. However that may be, the added centigram produced so much more on top of the five centigrams previously taken that for a time I had reason to fear that that additional centigram was just the amount needed to bring my experiments to a permanent close.

“Within three minutes of the time of injection the dizziness and vertigo had become so great as to make walking seem impossible. In another minute the lassitude rapidly crept over me, and the serious disturbance of my breathing made it apparent to me that walking, waving my arms, anything, was imperative. My lungs felt glued up, and the muscles of my chest refused to work. Everything swam before my eyes, and I was soon reduced to walking up and down the laboratory with halting steps, only preventing falling on the floor by holding fast to the edge of this table. It seemed to me that I spent hours gasping for breath. It reminded me of what I once experienced in the Cave of the Winds of Niagara, where water is more abundant in the atmosphere than air. My watch afterward indicated only about twenty minutes of extreme distress, but that twenty minutes is one never to be forgotten, and I advise you all, if you ever are so foolish as to try the experiment, to remain below the five-centigram limit.

“How much was administered to the victims, Doctor Nott, I cannot say, but it must have been a good deal more than I took. Six centigrams, which I recovered from these small samples, are only nine-tenths of a grain. Yet you see what effect it had. I trust that answers your question.”

Doctor Nott was too overwhelmed to reply.

===Chinese===

「也许我不够明智：为这个案子连日操劳，本来就已经燥热体虚，偏又在这种日子给自己注射这么大的一剂。不过也罢，在先前五厘克的基础上，多出的这一厘克引起的反应重了许多，有一阵子，我真有理由担心，这额外的一厘克，恰恰就是让我的实验永远画上句号所需要的分量。」

「注射后不出三分钟，头晕和目眩就重得让我觉得连走路都不可能了。再过一分钟，倦怠迅速漫上全身，呼吸的严重紊乱让我明白：走动、抡胳膊、什么都行，非做不可。我的肺像被糊住了，胸部的肌肉也罢了工。眼前的一切都在打转，我很快就只剩下踉踉跄跄在实验室里来回走动的份儿，全靠死死抓住这张桌子的边，才不至于瘫倒在地。我觉得自己喘着粗气熬了几个钟头。这让我想起当年在尼亚加拉风之洞（Cave of the Winds）里的滋味——那地方的空气里，水汽比空气还多。事后一看表，极度难受的那段不过二十分钟上下，可那二十分钟，一辈子也忘不了。奉劝诸位，倘若哪天糊涂到也想试试这实验，千万守住五厘克以下的界限。」

「至于给两位被害人用了多少，诺特医生，我说不上来，但肯定比我用的多得多。六厘克——我从这几小份血样里提出来的量——不过十分之九格令（grain）。可你们也看到了它的效力。但愿这回答了你的问题。」

诺特医生被震得说不出话来。

===Original===

“And what is this deadly poison?” continued Craig, anticipating our thoughts. “I have been fortunate enough to obtain a sample of it from the Museum of Natural History. It comes in a little gourd, or often a calabash. This is in a gourd. It is blackish brittle stuff encrusting the sides of the gourd just as if it was poured in in the liquid state and left to dry. Indeed, that is just what has been done by those who manufacture this stuff after a lengthy and somewhat secret process.”

He placed the gourd on the edge of the table where we could all see it. I was almost afraid even to look at it.

“The famous traveller, Sir Robert Schomburgh first brought it into Europe, and Darwin has described it. It is now an article of commerce and is to be found in the United States Pharmacopoeia as a medicine, though of course it is used in only very minute quantities, as a heart stimulant.”

Craig opened a book to a place he had marked:

===Chinese===

「那么，这致命的毒药究竟是什么？」克雷格接着说，抢在了我们发问之前。「我有幸从自然历史博物馆（Museum of Natural History）弄到了一份样品。这东西装在小葫芦里，或者常常装在瓢瓜（calabash）里。这一份就在葫芦里。那是一种发黑的脆性物质，结在葫芦的四壁上，就像先以液态倒进去、又任其晾干似的。事实上，制造这东西的人在经过一道冗长而颇有几分隐秘的工序之后，干的正是这个。」

他把那只葫芦摆到桌沿上，让大家都看得见。我简直连朝它看一眼都发怵。

「当年，著名旅行家罗伯特·舒姆堡爵士（Sir Robert Schomburgh）头一个把它带回欧洲，达尔文也对它作过描述。如今它已是一种商品，作为药物收进了《美国药典》（United States Pharmacopoeia），当然，用量只限于极微，用作强心剂。」

克雷格翻开一本书，翻到他夹了记号的那一页：

===Original===

“At least one person in this room will appreciate the local colour of a little incident I am going to read﻿—to illustrate what death from this poison is like. Two natives of the part of the world whence it comes were one day hunting. They were armed with blowpipes and quivers full of poisoned darts made of thin charred pieces of bamboo tipped with this stuff. One of them aimed a dart. It missed the object overhead, glanced off the tree, and fell down on the hunter himself. This is how the other native reported the result:

“ ‘Quacca takes the dart out of his shoulder. Never a word. Puts it in his quiver and throws it in the stream. Gives me his blowpipe for his little son. Says to me goodbye for his wife and the village. Then he lies down. His tongue talks no longer. No sight in his eyes. He folds his arms. He rolls over slowly. His mouth moves without sound. I feel his heart. It goes fast and then slow. It stops. Quacca has shot his last *woorali* dart.’ ”

We looked at each other, and the horror of the thing sank deep into our minds. *Woorali.* What was it? There were many travellers in the room who had been in the Orient, home of poisons, and in South America. Which one had run across the poison?

“*Woorali*, or *curare*,” said Craig slowly, “is the well-known poison with which the South American Indians of the upper Orinoco tip their arrows. Its principal ingredient is derived from the Strychnos toxifera tree, which yields also the drug nux vomica.”

===Chinese===

「在座至少有一位，会领会我接下来要念的这段小小插曲的乡土风味——它正好说明，中这种毒而死是什么样子。在它出产的那地方，两个土人有一天出猎。他们带着吹箭筒（blowpipes）和装满毒镖的箭筒，镖是烧制的细竹片，尖端蘸着这种东西。其中一人瞄准放出一镖。镖没有射中头顶的猎物，擦着树身弹开，落在了猎人自己身上。另一个土人是这样讲述结果的：

「『夸卡（Quacca）把镖从肩膀上拔出来。一声不吭。把镖收进箭筒，又把它扔进河里。把吹箭筒交给我，留给他的小儿子。托我向他的老婆和村里人道别。然后他躺下。舌头不再说话了。眼里没有光了。他交叠起双臂。缓缓翻过身去。嘴动着，没有声音。我摸他的心口。跳得快，又跳得慢。停了。夸卡放完了他最后一支乌拉利（*woorali*）镖。』」

我们面面相觑，这件事的恐怖深深沉进了每个人的心底。乌拉利。那是什么东西？屋里到过毒药之乡东方、也到过南美洲的旅行者大有人在。是哪一位撞见过这种毒药呢？

「乌拉利，或称箭毒（*curare*），」克雷格缓缓说道，「是南美洲奥里诺科河（Orinoco）上游的印第安人蘸箭头用的那种有名的毒药。它的主要成分取自毒马钱树（Strychnos toxifera）——产马钱子这味药的，也是同一种树。」

===Original===

A great light dawned on me. I turned quickly to where Vanderdyke was sitting next to Mrs. Ralston, and a little behind her. His stony stare and laboured breathing told me that he had read the purport of Kennedy’s actions.

“For God’s sake, Craig,” I gasped. “An emetic, quick﻿—Vanderdyke.”

A trace of a smile flitted over Vanderdyke’s features, as much as to say that he was beyond our interference.

“Vanderdyke,” said Craig, with what seemed to me a brutal calmness, “then it was you who were the visitor who last saw Laura Wainwright and John Templeton alive. Whether you shot a dart at them I do not know. But you are the murderer.”

===Chinese===

我恍然大悟。我急忙转向范戴克——他坐在拉尔斯顿夫人身旁，略微靠后一点。他那呆滞的目光和吃力的呼吸告诉我：肯尼迪这一连串举动是什么用意，他已经看明白了。

「看在上帝份上，克雷格，」我倒抽一口气，「催吐剂！快——范戴克！」

一丝笑意掠过范戴克的脸，仿佛在说：他早已用不着我们来插手了。

「范戴克，」克雷格说，那份平静在我看来近乎残忍，「那么，最后见到活着的劳拉·温赖特和约翰·坦普尔顿的那个访客，就是你。你是不是朝他们放过毒镖，我不知道。但凶手就是你。」

===Original===

Vanderdyke raised his hand as if to assent. It fell back limp, and I noted the ring of the bluest lapis lazuli.

Mrs. Ralston threw herself toward him. “Will you not do something? Is there no antidote? Don’t let him die!” she cried.

“You are the murderer,” repeated Kennedy, as if demanding a final answer.

Again the hand moved in confession, and he feebly moved the finger on which shone the ring.

===Chinese===

范戴克抬起一只手，像是表示认账。手无力地垂了下去，我看见了那枚蓝得不能再蓝的青金石戒指。

拉尔斯顿夫人扑向了他。「你们不能想想办法吗？就没有解药吗？别让他死！」她喊道。

「你就是凶手。」肯尼迪重复了一遍，像是在索要最后的答复。

那只手再次动了一下，作为招认；他还软弱地动了动那根亮着戒指的手指。

===Original===

Our attention was centred on Vanderdyke. Mrs. Ralston, unobserved, went to the table and picked up the gourd. Before O’Connor could stop her she had rubbed her tongue on the black substance inside. It was only a little bit, for O’Connor quickly dashed it from her lips and threw the gourd through the window, smashing the glass.

“Kennedy,” he shouted frantically, “Mrs. Ralston has swallowed some of it.”

Kennedy seemed so intent on Vanderdyke that I had to repeat the remark.

Without looking up, he said: “Oh, one can swallow it﻿—it’s strange, but it is comparatively inert if swallowed even in a pretty good-sized quantity. I doubt if Mrs. Ralston ever heard of it before except by hearsay. If she had, she’d have scratched herself with it instead of swallowing it.”

===Chinese===

我们的注意力都集中在范戴克身上。谁也没有留意，拉尔斯顿夫人走到桌边，拿起了那只葫芦。不等奥康纳来得及阻拦，她已把舌头在那层黑色物质上抹了一下。好在她只沾了薄薄一点，奥康纳眼疾手快，一把从她唇边打落，随即将葫芦掷出窗外，砸碎了玻璃。

「肯尼迪！」他发狂似的喊道，「拉尔斯顿夫人吞了那东西！」

肯尼迪的心思似乎全在范戴克身上，我只得把这句话又说了一遍。

他头也不抬地说：「哦，吞下去倒不要紧——说来奇怪，这东西哪怕吞下相当大的一口，也几乎不起作用。我怀疑拉尔斯顿夫人从前对它只有耳闻。她若是真了解，就该拿它划破自己的皮肉，而不是把它吞下去。」

===Original===

If Craig had been indifferent to the emergency of Vanderdyke before, he was all action now that the confession had been made. In an instant Vanderdyke was stretched on the floor and Craig had taken out the apparatus I had seen during the afternoon.

“I am prepared for this,” he exclaimed quickly. “Here is the apparatus for artificial respiration. Nott, hold that rubber funnel over his nose, and start the oxygen from the tank. Pull his tongue forward so it won’t fall down his throat and choke him. I’ll work his arms. Walter, make a tourniquet of your handkerchief and put it tightly on the muscles of his left arm. That may keep some of the poison in his arm from spreading into the rest of his body. This is the only antidote known﻿—artificial respiration.”

Kennedy was working feverishly, going through the motions of first aid to a drowned man. Mrs. Ralston was on her knees beside Vanderdyke, kissing his hands and forehead whenever Kennedy stopped for a minute, and crying softly.

“Schuyler, poor boy, I wonder how you could have done it. I was with him that day. We rode up in his car, and as we passed through Williston he said he would stop a minute and wish Templeton luck. I didn’t think it strange, for he said he had nothing any longer against Laura Wainwright, and Templeton only did his duty as a lawyer against us. I forgave John for prosecuting us, but Schuyler didn’t, after all. Oh, my poor boy, why did you do it? We could have gone somewhere else and started all over again﻿—it wouldn’t have been the first time.”

===Chinese===

如果说刚才克雷格对范戴克的危局还无动于衷，那么如今供认已出，他立刻雷厉风行起来。眨眼之间，范戴克已被平放在地板上，克雷格取出了我下午见过的那套器具。

「我早有准备，」他急速地说。「这是做人工呼吸的器具。诺特，把那只橡胶漏斗罩在他鼻子上，打开氧气罐放氧。把他的舌头拉向前，免得坠进喉咙把他噎住。我来抡他的手臂。沃尔特，拿你的手帕做个止血带（tourniquet），紧紧扎住他左臂的肌肉。这样或许能拦住臂里的一部分毒，不让它扩散到身体其余部分。已知的解药只有这一种——人工呼吸。」

肯尼迪拼命地忙活着，一招一式如同急救溺水的人。拉尔斯顿夫人跪在范戴克身旁，每逢肯尼迪歇手的空当，便去吻他的手和额头，低声啜泣着。

「斯凯勒，可怜的孩子，你怎么下得去手啊。那天我同他在一起。我们坐他的车上来，路过威利斯敦时，他说要停一停，祝坦普尔顿好运。我没觉得有什么蹊跷，因为他说过，他对劳拉·温赖特早没了芥蒂，坦普尔顿跟我们作对，不过是尽律师的本分。约翰起诉我们，我原谅了他，斯凯勒到底没能原谅。唉，我可怜的孩子，你为什么要这么做？我们本可以去别处从头再来——那又不是头一回了。」

===Original===

At last came the flutter of an eyelid and a voluntary breath or two. Vanderdyke seemed to realise where he was. With a last supreme effort he raised his hand and drew it slowly across his face. Then he fell back, exhausted by the effort.

But he had at last put himself beyond the reach of the law. There was no tourniquet that would confine the poison now in the scratch across his face. Back of those lacklustre eyes he heard and knew, but could not move or speak. His voice was gone, his limbs, his face, his chest, and, last, his eyes. I wondered if it were possible to conceive a more dreadful torture than that endured by a mind which so witnessed the dying of one organ after another of its own body, shut up, as it were, in the fullness of life, within a corpse.

I looked in bewilderment at the scratch on his face. “How did he do it?” I asked.

Carefully Craig drew off the azure ring and examined it. In that part which surrounded the blue lapis lazuli, he indicated a hollow point, concealed. It worked with a spring and communicated with a little receptacle behind, in such a way that the murderer could give the fatal scratch while shaking hands with his victim.

I shuddered, for my hand had once been clasped by the one wearing that poison ring, which had sent Templeton, and his fiancée and now Vanderdyke himself, to their deaths.

===Chinese===

眼皮终于颤了颤，又自主地吸了一两口气。范戴克似乎认出了自己身在何处。他拼尽最后的力气抬起手，缓缓从脸上抹过。随即无力地跌了回去——这一下已耗尽了他。

可他终究把自己放到了法律再也够不着的地方。此刻他脸上那道抓痕里的毒，再没有任何止血带能够困住了。在那双失神的眼睛后面，他听得见，也明白，却动不得，说不得。先是声音没了，接着是四肢、脸、胸口，最后是眼睛。一副在生命全盛之时被封进一具尸骸里的头脑，就这样眼睁睁看着自己一个又一个器官死去——我想不出，世上还有没有比这更可怕的酷刑。

我茫然地望着他脸上的抓痕。「他是怎么下的手？」我问。

克雷格小心翼翼地褪下那枚天青色的戒指，细细察看。他指给我们看：环绕那颗青金石的一圈里，藏着一个空心的尖刺，靠弹簧发动，后面连着一个小小的储药室——如此一来，凶手只消同被害人握一握手，便能划出那致命的一痕。

我不寒而栗——戴这枚毒戒的人，也曾这样握过我的手。正是它，送了坦普尔顿和他的未婚妻的命，如今，又轮到了范戴克自己。

## The Bacteriological Detective / 细菌学侦探

===Original===

Kennedy was deeply immersed in writing a lecture on the chemical compositions of various bacterial toxins and antitoxins, a thing which was as unfamiliar to me as Kamchatka, but as familiar to Kennedy as Broadway and Forty-second Street.

“Really,” he remarked, laying down his fountain-pen and lighting his cigar for the hundredth time, “the more one thinks of how the modern criminal misses his opportunities the more astonishing it seems. Why do they stick to pistols, chloroform, and prussic acid when there is such a splendid assortment of refined methods they might employ?”

“Give it up, old man,” I replied helplessly, “unless it is because they haven’t any imagination. I hope they don’t use them. What would become of my business if they did? How would you ever get a really dramatic news feature for the *Star* out of such a thing? ‘Dotted line marks route taken by fatal germ; cross indicates spot where antitoxin attacked it’﻿—ha! ha! not much for the yellow journals in that, Craig.”

“To my mind, Walter, it would be the height of the dramatic﻿—far more dramatic than sending a bullet into a man. Any fool can shoot a pistol or cut a throat, but it takes brains to be up-to-date.”

“It may be so;” I admitted, and went on reading, while Kennedy scratched away diligently on his lecture. I mention this conversation both because it bears on my story, by a rather peculiar coincidence, and because it showed me a new side of Kennedy’s amazing researches. He was as much interested in bacteria as in chemistry, and the story is one of bacteria.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪正埋头撰写一篇讲稿，题目是各种细菌毒素与抗毒素（bacterial toxins and antitoxins）的化学组成。这门学问于我，陌生得如同堪察加（Kamchatka）；于他，却熟悉得好比百老汇和第四十二街的路口。

「说真的，」他放下自来水笔，第一百次点着雪茄，说道，「越是琢磨现代罪犯怎样坐失良机，就越叫人惊奇。明明有一大堆精巧高明的手段尽可采用，他们为什么偏要死抱着手枪、氯仿和氢氰酸不放呢？」

「我认输，老兄，」我无可奈何地答道，「除非是因为他们毫无想象力。但愿他们千万别用那些手段。真用起来，我这碗饭还吃得成吗？那样的事，你叫人怎么替《明星报》（*Star*）弄出一条像样的轰动新闻？『虚线为致命病菌行经路线；叉号标志抗毒素阻击之处』——哈！哈！这可没什么好让黄色报刊（yellow journals）大做文章的，克雷格。」

「依我看，沃尔特，那才是戏剧性的极致——比往人身上送一颗子弹戏剧性多了。随便哪个蠢货都会开枪、割喉咙，可要赶得上时代，就非得有脑子不可。」

「也许是这样吧。」我承认了，继续读我的东西，肯尼迪则奋笔疾书赶他的讲稿。我记下这段对话，一是因为一个相当离奇的巧合——它同我这个故事大有干系；二是因为它让我看到了肯尼迪那些惊人研究的又一面。他对细菌的兴趣不亚于对化学的兴趣，而这个故事，正是一个关于细菌的故事。

===Original===

It was perhaps a quarter of an hour later when the buzzer on our hall door sounded. Imagine my surprise on opening the door to discover the slight figure of what appeared to be a most fascinating young lady who was heavily veiled. She was in a state almost bordering on hysteria, as even I, in spite of my usual obtuseness, noticed.

“Is Professor Kennedy in?” she inquired anxiously.

“Yes, ma’am;” I replied, opening the door into our study.

She advanced toward him, repeating her inquiry.

“I am Professor Kennedy. Pray be seated,” he said.

The presence of a lady in our apartment was such a novelty that really I forgot to disappear, but busied myself straightening the furniture and opening a window to allow the odour of stale tobacco to escape.

===Chinese===

大约一刻钟后，门厅的门铃响了。开门一看，门外竟站着一位身姿纤秀、想来十分动人的年轻小姐，脸上蒙着厚厚的面纱——可以想见我多么惊讶。就连我这样一贯迟钝的人也看得出，她的状态几乎已到了歇斯底里的边缘。

「肯尼迪教授在家吗？」她焦急地问。

「在，夫人。」我应道，推开书房的门请她进去。

她朝他走去，又问了一遍。

「我就是肯尼迪教授。请坐。」他说。

我们这套公寓里来一位女士实在新鲜，我竟然忘了回避，只顾忙活着摆正家具、推开一扇窗，好放走满屋隔夜的烟草味。

===Original===

“My name is Eveline Bisbee,” she began. “I have heard, Professor Kennedy, that you are an adept at getting at the bottom of difficult mysteries.”

“You flatter me;” he said in acknowledgment. “Who was so foolish as to tell you that?”

“A friend who has heard of the Kerr Parker case,” she replied.

“I beg your pardon,” I interrupted, “I didn’t mean to intrude. I think I’ll go out. I’ll be back in an hour or two.”

===Chinese===

「我叫伊芙琳·比斯比（Eveline Bisbee）。」她开口道，「肯尼迪教授，听说您最擅长探到疑难谜案的底。」

「您过奖了。」他客气地领受，「是谁那么糊涂，跟您说这个的？」

「一位听说过克尔·帕克案的朋友。」她答道。

「对不住，」我插嘴道，「我无心打扰。我想我还是出去吧，一两个钟头后回来。」

===Original===

“Please, Mr. Jameson﻿—it is Mr. Jameson, is it not?”

I bowed in surprise.

“If it is possible I wish you would stay and hear my story. I am told that you and Professor Kennedy always work together.”

It was my turn to be embarrassed by the compliment.

===Chinese===

「请留步，詹姆森先生——您是詹姆森先生，对不对？」

我吃了一惊，躬身应是。

「要是方便，我想请您留下听听我的故事。听说您和肯尼迪教授一向是搭档办事的。」

这回轮到我被这句恭维弄得局促不安了。

===Original===

“Mrs. Fletcher, of Great Neck,” she explained, “has told me. I believe Professor Kennedy performed a great service for the Fletchers, though I do not know what it was. At any rate, I have come to you with my case, in which I have small hope of obtaining assistance unless you can help me. If Professor Kennedy cannot solve it, well, I’m afraid nobody can.” She paused a moment, then added, “No doubt you have read of the death of my guardian the other day.”

Of course we had. Who did not know that “Jim” Bisbee, the southern California oil-magnate, had died suddenly of typhoid fever at the private hospital of Dr. Bell, where he had been taken from his magnificent apartment on Riverside Drive? Kennedy and I had discussed it at the time. We had commented on the artificiality of the twentieth century. No longer did people have homes; they had apartments, I had said. They didn’t fall ill in the good old-fashioned way any more, either in fact, they even hired special rooms to die in. They hired halls for funeral services. It was a wonder that they didn’t hire graves. It was all part of our twentieth century breakup of tradition. Indeed we did know about the death of Jim Bisbee. But there was nothing mysterious about it. It was just typical in all its surroundings of the first decade of the twentieth century in a great, artificial city﻿—a lonely death of a great man surrounded by all that money could buy.

We had read of his ward, too, the beautiful Miss Eveline Bisbee, a distant relation. As under the heat of the room and her excitement, she raised her veil, we were very much interested in her. At least, I am sure that even Kennedy had by this time completely forgotten the lecture on toxins.

===Chinese===

「长岛大颈镇的弗莱彻夫人同我说的。」她解释道，「我相信肯尼迪教授为弗莱彻家立过大功，虽然我不知道是什么事。不管怎样，我是带着自己的难题来求二位的，这难题除了你们，我不指望从别处得到什么援手。倘若连肯尼迪教授也解不开——那恐怕就没人解得开了。」她顿了顿，又补上一句：「想必你们已经读到过我监护人前两天去世的消息。」

当然读到过。南加州的石油大亨「吉姆」比斯比（“Jim” Bisbee），从他里弗赛德大道（Riverside Drive）的豪华公寓被送进贝尔医生（Dr. Bell）的私立医院，突发伤寒，死在了那里——这还有谁不知道？当时肯尼迪同我还议论过一番，感慨二十世纪的人造做作。我说，人们如今不再有家，只有公寓；连生病也不肯按老派的好方式生了——实际上，他们连死都要租专门的房间去死；办丧事要租礼堂，没连坟地也一并租下，已算奇事。这一切，都是我们二十世纪传统分崩离析的一部分。我们确实知道吉姆·比斯比的死。但其中并无任何神秘。从周遭一切来看，那不过是二十世纪头十年里一座庞大的人造城市中最典型的一幕：一位大人物孤零零地死去，身边围着金钱所能买到的一切。

他监护的被监护人——美丽动人的伊芙琳·比斯比小姐，他的一位远亲——我们也读到过。房间里热，她又激动，抬手揭开面纱之后，我们对她的兴趣更浓了。至少我可以肯定：到这个时候，就连肯尼迪也把那篇毒素讲稿忘得一干二净了。

===Original===

“There is something about my guardian’s death,” she began in a low and tremulous voice, “that I am sure will bear investigating. It may be only a woman’s foolish fears, but﻿—I haven’t told this to a soul till now, except Mrs. Fletcher. My guardian had, as you perhaps know, spent his summer at his country place at Bisbee Hall, New Jersey, from which he returned rather suddenly about a week ago. Our friends thought it merely a strange whim that he should return to the city before the summer was fairly over, but it was not. The day before he returned, his gardener fell sick of typhoid. That decided Mr. Bisbee to return to the city on the following day. Imagine his consternation to find his valet stricken the very next morning. Of course they motored to New York immediately, then he wired to me at Newport, and together we opened his apartment at the Louis Quinze.

“But that was not to be the end of it. One after another, the servants at Bisbee Hall were taken with the disease until five of them were down. Then came the last blow﻿—Mr. Bisbee fell a victim in New York. So far I have been spared. But who knows how much longer it will last? I have been so frightened that I haven’t eaten a meal in the apartment since I came back. When I am hungry I simply steal out to a hotel﻿—a different one every time. I never drink any water except that which I have surreptitiously boiled in my own room over a gas-stove. Disinfectants and germicides have been used by the gallon, and still I don’t feel safe. Even the health authorities don’t remove my fears. With my guardian’s death I had begun to feel that possibly it was over. But no. This morning another servant who came up from the hall last week was taken sick, and the doctor pronounces that typhoid, too. Will I be the next? Is it just a foolish fear? Why does it pursue us to New York? Why didn’t it stop at Bisbee Hall?”

I don’t think I ever saw a living creature more overcome by horror, by an invisible, deadly fear. That was why it was doubly horrible in a girl so attractive as Eveline Bisbee. As I listened I felt how terrible it must be to be pursued by such a fear. What must it be to be dogged by a disease as relentlessly as the typhoid had dogged her? If it had been some great, but visible, tangible peril how gladly I could have faced it merely for the smile of a woman like this. But it was a peril that only knowledge and patience could meet. Instinctively I turned toward Kennedy, my own mind being an absolute blank.

===Chinese===

「我监护人的死，」她声音低微发颤地开了口，「总有某些地方，我确信值得查一查。也许不过是一个女人的痴人忧惧，可是——这事除了弗莱彻夫人，至今我对谁也没说过。你们或许知道，我监护人今夏一直在新泽西的乡间宅邸比斯比庄园（Bisbee Hall）避暑，约莫一星期前相当突然地回了城。朋友们都以为夏天还没正经过完他就回城，不过是一时古怪的兴头，其实不然。他回城的前一天，园丁得了伤寒。这一下比斯比先生拿定主意次日回城。不妨想想他第二天清早发现贴身男仆也病倒时是何等惊骇。他们当即乘汽车赶回纽约，他拍电报把我从纽波特（Newport）叫来，我们一起打开了他「路易十五」公寓（Louis Quinze）的房门。

「可事情并未到此为止。比斯比庄园的仆人一个接一个染上此病，前后病倒了五个。接着是最后致命的一击——比斯比先生本人在纽约也成了牺牲品。到目前为止我侥幸无恙。可谁知道还能撑多久？我吓坏了，回城以来一顿饭也没在公寓里吃过。饿了就悄悄溜出去上馆子——每次换一家旅馆。水，我只喝自己躲在房里用煤气炉偷偷烧开的那种。消毒水、杀菌剂论加仑地用，我还是不踏实。连卫生部门都宽解不了我的恐惧。监护人一死，我本以为这事也许到此为止了。可是不然。今天早晨，上星期刚从庄园上城来的又一个仆人病倒了，医生诊断还是伤寒。下一个会不会轮到我？这究竟是不是庸人自扰？它为什么追到纽约来缠着不放？为什么不在比斯比庄园就罢手？

我想我从没见过一个活人被恐怖——被一种无形的、致命的恐惧——压垮到这个地步。正因如此，这恐怖落在一个像伊芙琳·比斯比这样动人的姑娘身上，便加倍骇人。我一面听，一面体会到被这样的恐惧追着，该是多么可怕。被一种疾病像伤寒纠缠她那样死死咬住不放，又是什么滋味？倘若那是某种巨大却看得见、摸得着的危险，单为博这样一位女子的一笑，我都甘愿挺身去挡。可这一种危险，唯有知识与耐心才能应付。我本能地转向肯尼迪，自己脑子里一片空白。

===Original===

“Is there anyone you suspect of being the cause of such an epidemic?” he asked. “I may as well tell you right now that I have already formed two theories﻿—one perfectly natural, the other diabolical. Tell me everything.”

“Well, I had expected to receive a fortune of one million dollars, free and clear, by his will and this morning I am informed by his lawyer, James Denny, that a new will had been made. It is still one million. But the remainder, instead of going to a number of charities in which he was known to be interested, goes to form a trust fund for the Bisbee School of Mechanical Arts, of which Mr. Denny is the sole trustee. Of course, I do not know much about my guardian’s interests while he was alive, but it strikes me as strange that he should have changed so radically, and, besides, the new will is so worded that if I die without children my million also goes to this school﻿—location unnamed. I can’t help wondering about it all.”

“Why should you wonder﻿—at least what other reasons have you for wondering?”

“Oh, I can’t express them. Maybe after all it’s only a woman’s silly intuition. But often I have thought in the past few days about this illness of my guardian. It was so queer. He was always so careful. And you know the rich don’t often have typhoid.”

===Chinese===

「你怀疑有什么人是这场时疫的祸源吗？」他问，「不妨现在就告诉你：我已经形成了两种推想——一种全然是自然的，另一种则是恶魔般的。把一切都讲给我听吧。」

「是这样的：我原以为按他的遗嘱可以净得一笔一百万美元的遗产。可今天早晨，他的律师詹姆斯·丹尼（James Denny）通知我，另立了一份新遗嘱。给我的仍旧是一百万。但余下的财产，本该捐给他素来关心的一批慈善机构，如今却改作一笔信托基金，用来设立什么比斯比机械工艺学校（Bisbee School of Mechanical Arts），丹尼先生是唯一的受托人。当然，我监护人生前关心些什么，我并不深知，但遗嘱改得如此彻底，总让我觉得蹊跷；何况新遗嘱措辞巧妙，倘若我死时没有子女，我这一百万也要归这所学校——校址未具名。这一切我不能不犯嘀咕。」

「你有什么可嘀咕的——至少，你还有别的什么起疑的理由吗？」

「唉，我说不上来。也许到头来不过是女人可笑的直觉。可这几天我常常想起我监护人的这场病。太蹊跷了。他平日那样小心。再说您知道，阔人是不大得伤寒的。」

===Original===

“You have no reason to suppose that it was not typhoid fever of which he died?”

She hesitated. “No,” she replied, “but if you had known Mr. Bisbee you would think it strange, too. He had a horror of infectious and contagious diseases. His apartment and his country home were models. No sanitarium could have been more punctilious. He lived what one of his friends called an antiseptic life. Maybe I am foolish, but it keeps getting closer and closer to me now, and﻿—well, I wish you’d look into the case. Please set my mind at rest and assure me that nothing is wrong, that it is all natural.”

“I will help you, Miss Bisbee. Tomorrow night I want to take a trip quietly to Bisbee Hall. You will see that it is all right, that I have the proper letters so I can investigate thoroughly.”

I shall never forget the mute and eloquent thanks with which she said good night after Kennedy’s promise.

===Chinese===

「那么，你有没有什么根据，怀疑他致死的当真就是伤寒？」

她迟疑了。「没有，」她答道，「可您要是认识比斯比先生，也会觉得蹊跷。他对传染病怕得要命。他的公寓和乡间宅邸都堪称模范，再一丝不苟的疗养院也不过如此。他过的日子，用他一位朋友的话说，是一种『无菌的生活』（antiseptic life）。也许是我傻，可这病如今离我越来越近，而且——唉，我求您查一查这个案子。请让我安下心来，向我担保什么事也没有，一切都很自然。」

「我会帮你的，比斯比小姐。明天夜里，我想悄悄去一趟比斯比庄园。你要把事情安排妥当，让我拿到必要的函件，好彻底查个明白。」

肯尼迪许诺之后，她道晚安时那一声无声却胜过千言万语的感谢，我永远不会忘记。

===Original===

Kennedy sat with his eyes shaded under his hand for fully an hour after she had left. Then he suddenly jumped up. “Walter,” he said, “let us go over to Dr. Bell’s. I know the head nurse there. We may possibly learn something.”

As we sat in the waiting-room with its thick Oriental rugs and handsome mahogany furniture, I found myself going back to our conversation of the early evening. “By Jove, Kennedy, you were right,” I exclaimed. “If there is anything in this germ-plot idea of hers it is indeed the height of the dramatic﻿—it is diabolical. No ordinary mortal would ever be capable of it.”

Just then the head nurse came in, a large woman breathing of germlessness and cheerfulness in her spotless uniform. We were shown every courtesy. There was, in fact, nothing to conceal. The visit set at rest my last suspicion that perhaps Jim Bisbee had been poisoned by a drug. The charts of his temperature and the sincerity of the nurse were absolutely convincing. It had really been typhoid, and there was nothing to be gained by pursuing that inquiry further.

===Chinese===

她走后，肯尼迪手搭凉棚遮着眼睛，足足坐了一个钟头。忽然他一跃而起。「沃尔特，」他说，「咱们去贝尔医生的医院走一趟。那儿的护士长我认识。兴许能打听到点什么。」

我们坐在候诊室里，脚下是厚厚的东方地毯，手边是气派的红木家具。不知不觉，我又想起这天傍晚的那番对话。「天哪，肯尼迪，你说得对，」我失声喊道，「她这病菌阴谋的说法若真有几分影子，那真是戏剧性的极致——简直是恶魔行径。寻常凡人断然干不出来。」

正说着，护士长进来了——一位身材高大的妇人，一身纤尘不染的护士服，通身上下透着一股无菌的洁净与爽朗。我们受到了周到的礼遇。事实上，院方本来也没什么可隐瞒的。这一趟把我最后一丝疑心也打消了——我原还猜吉姆·比斯比或许是叫人下了药。他的体温记录单和护士的诚恳，让人深信不疑。他确实是伤寒，这条线再追下去也无所得了。

===Original===

Back at the apartment, Craig began packing his suitcase with the few things he would need for a journey. “I’m going out to Bisbee Hall tomorrow for a few days, Walter, and if you could find it convenient to come along I should like to have your assistance.”

“To tell you the truth, Craig, I am afraid to go,” I said.

“You needn’t be. I’m going down to the army post on Governor’s Island first to be vaccinated against typhoid. Then I am going to wait a few hours till it takes effect before going. It’s the only place in the city where one can be inoculated against it, so far as I know. While three inoculations are really best, I understand that one is sufficient for ordinary protection, and that is all we shall need, if any.”

“You’re sure of it?”

“Almost positive.”

“Very well, Craig. I’ll go.”

===Chinese===

回到公寓，克雷格动手收拾手提箱，只装旅途必需的几样东西。「明天我要去比斯比庄园待几天，沃尔特。你若方便同行，我很乐意有你帮把手。」

「说实话，克雷格，我怕去。」我说。

「用不着怕。我先去总督岛（Governor’s Island）的陆军哨所接种伤寒疫苗，然后等上几个钟头，等它生效再动身。据我所知，全城只有那一处能接种伤寒疫苗。虽说接种三针最好，但我了解，一针便足以应付寻常的防护，咱们充其量也就用得着这个——如果真用得着的话。」

「你有把握？」

「十拿九稳。」

「那好，克雷格，我去。」

===Original===

Down at the army post the next morning we had no difficulty in being inoculated against the disease. The work of immunising our army was going on at that time, and several thousands of soldiers in various parts of the country had already been vaccinated, with the best of results. “Do many civilians come over to be vaccinated?” asked Craig of Major Carroll, the surgeon in charge.

“Not many, for very few have heard of it,” he replied.

“I suppose you keep a record of them.”

“Only their names﻿—we can’t follow them up outside the army, to see how it works. Still, when they come to us as you and Mr. Jameson have done we are perfectly willing to vaccinate them. The Army Medical Corps takes the position that if it is good for the army it is good for civil life, and as long as only a few civilians apply we are perfectly willing to do it for a fee covering the cost.”

“And would you let me see the list?”

“Certainly. You may look it over in a moment.”

Kennedy glanced hurriedly through the short list of names, pulled out his notebook, made an entry, and handed the list back. “Thank you, Major.”

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，在岛上的陆军哨所，我们顺利接种了疫苗。当时我军的免疫接种工作正在推行，全国各地已有几千名士兵接种，效果极好。「来找你们接种的老百姓多吗？」克雷格问主管的军医卡罗尔少校（Major Carroll）。

「不多，听说过这回事的人本来就少。」他答道。

「我猜你们留着他们的记录。」

「只留名字——出了军队，我们没法追踪他们，看看效果如何。不过，老百姓像你和詹姆森先生这样找上门来，我们是十分乐意给接种的。陆军军医署（Army Medical Corps）的立场是：于军队有益的，于民间生活亦然；只要来接种的老百姓不多，我们很愿意代劳，收个成本费就是了。」

「那能让我看看那份名单吗？」

「当然。稍等片刻就能看。」

肯尼迪把那份不长的名单匆匆扫了一遍，掏出记事本记了一笔，把名单递了回去。「多谢，少校。」

===Original===

Bisbee Hall was a splendid place set in the heart of a great park whose area was measured by square miles rather than by acres. But Craig did not propose to stay there, for he arranged for accommodations in a nearby town, where we were to take our meals also. It was late when we arrived, and we spent a restless night, for the inoculation “took.” It wasn’t any worse than a light attack of the grippe, and in the morning we were both all right again, after the passing of what is called the “negative phase.” I, for one, felt much safer.

The town was very much excited over the epidemic at the hall, and if I had been wondering why Craig wanted me along my wonder was soon set at rest. He had me scouring the town and country looking up every case or rumour of typhoid for miles around. I made the local weekly paper my headquarters, and the editor was very obliging. He let me read all his news letters from his local correspondent at every crossroads. I waded through accounts of new calves and colts, new fences and barns, who “Sundayed” with his brother, etc., and soon had a list of all the cases in that part of the country. It was not a long one, but it was scattered. After I had traced them out, following Kennedy’s instructions, they showed nothing, except that they were unrelated to the epidemic at the hall.

Meanwhile, Kennedy was very busy there. He had a microscope and slides and test-tubes and chemicals for testing things, and I don’t know what all, for there was not time to initiate me into all the mysteries. He tested the water from the various driven wells and in the water-tank, and the milk from the cows;﻿—he tried to find out what food had come in from outside, though there was practically none, for the hall was self-supporting. There was no stone he left unturned.

===Chinese===

比斯比庄园是个气派的地方，坐落在一座大园林正中，园子的面积论平方英里而不论英亩。但克雷格并不打算下榻庄园，他在附近镇上安排了住处，饭也在镇上吃。我们到得很晚，又熬过一个不安稳的夜晚——疫苗「发作」了。那滋味不过像一场轻流感，熬过所谓「负性期」（negative phase）之后，早晨我们俩都好好的了。就我而论，心里踏实多了。

庄园的疫情让全镇沸沸扬扬。我若还纳闷克雷格为何带上我，这疑问也很快有了答案。他打发我跑遍镇上乡间，把方圆几英里内每一例伤寒、每一桩风声都查个遍。我把本地周报当作大本营，主编十分热心，把他那位守着每个路口的乡间通讯员寄来的通讯稿全数给我读。我在新添的牛犊马驹、新修的栅栏谷仓、谁谁「上礼拜天」去兄弟家做客之类的消息里趟了一遭，很快把那一带所有病例列成了一份清单。单子不长，却撒得到处都是。我遵照肯尼迪的指点把它们一一追查到底，结果什么也没查出来——它们同庄园的疫情毫无关联。

与此同时，肯尼迪在庄园里忙得不可开交。他有显微镜、载玻片、试管和化验用的各种药品，还有些我叫不上名堂的东西——没工夫领我一一参透全部奥妙。他化验了各处管井（driven wells）和水柜里的水，化验了奶牛的奶；——他还想查清有什么食物从外面运进来，虽然实际上几乎没有，因为庄园自给自足。真是把每块石头都翻遍了。

===Original===

When I rejoined him that night he was clearly perplexed. I don’t think my report decreased his perplexity, either.

“There is only one thing left as far as I have been able to discover after one day’s work,” he said, after we had gone over our activities for the day. “Jim Bisbee never drank the water from his own wells. He always drank a bottled water shipped down from a camp of his in New York State, where he had a remarkable mountain spring. I tested a number of the full bottles at the hall, but they were perfectly pure. There wasn’t a trace of the *bacillus typhosus* in any of them. Then it occurred to me that, after all, that was not the thing to do. I should test the empty ones. But there weren’t any empty ones. They told me they had all been taken down to the freight station yesterday to be shipped back to the camp. I hope they haven’t gone yet. Let’s drive around and see if they are there.”

The freight-master was just leaving, but when he learned we were from the hall he consented to let us examine the bottles. They were corked and in wooden cases, which protected them perfectly. By the light of the station lamps and the aid of a pocket-lens, Kennedy examined them on the outside and satisfied himself that after being replaced in the wooden cases the bottles themselves had not been handled.

“Will you let me borrow some of these bottles tonight?” he asked the agent. “I’ll give you my word that they will be returned safely tomorrow. If necessary, I’ll get an order for them.”

===Chinese===

那天夜里我回到他那里时，他分明一筹莫展。我想，我的报告也没能让他的困惑减轻分毫。

「干了一整天，就我查到的而言，只剩下一件事了。」我们把各自一天的作为碰过头之后，他说。「吉姆·比斯比从不喝自己井里的水。他一向只喝从他纽约州一处营地里运下来的瓶装水——那里有一眼极好的山泉。庄园里好几只满瓶的水我都验过，纯净无比，一丝伤寒杆菌（*bacillus typhosus*）的踪迹也没有。后来我恍然想到：这么验本来就不对，我该验的是空瓶。可庄园里一只空瓶也没有。他们告诉我，空瓶昨天全送到货运站去了，要装运回营地。但愿还没运走。咱们驱车过去看看还在不在。」

货运站长正要动身高开，但一听我们是庄园来的，便同意让我们验看瓶子。瓶子都塞着软木塞，装在木箱里，保护得完好无损。借着站台的灯光和一面袖珍放大镜，肯尼迪把瓶子外面检视了一遍，确信瓶子重新装回木箱之后，本身没有被人碰过。

「今晚这些瓶子能借我几只吗？」他问站务员，「我保证明天原物奉还。必要的话，我去办一张正式提取的手续。」

===Original===

The station-agent reluctantly yielded; especially as a small green banknote figured in the transaction. Craig and I tenderly lifted the big bottles in their cases into our trap and drove back to our rooms in the hotel. It quite excited the hangers-on to see us drive up with a lot of empty five-gallon bottles and carry them upstairs, but I had long ago given up having any fear of public opinion in carrying out anything Craig wanted.

In our room we worked far into the night. Craig carefully swabbed out the bottom and sides of each bottle by inserting a little piece of cotton on the end of a long wire. Then he squeezed the water out of the cotton swab on small glass slides coated with agar-agar, or Japanese seaweed, a medium in which germ-cultures multiply rapidly. He put the slides away in a little oven with an alcohol-lamp which he had brought along, leaving them to remain overnight at blood heat.

I had noticed all this time that he was very particular not to touch any of the bottles on the outside. As for me, I wouldn’t have touched them for the world. In fact, I was getting so I hesitated to touch anything. I was almost afraid to breathe, though I knew there was no harm in that. However, it was not danger of infection in touching the bottles that made Craig so careful. He had noted, in the dim light of the station lamps, what seemed to be fingermarks on the bottles, and they had interested him, in fact, had decided him on a further investigation of the bottles.

===Chinese===

站务员勉勉强强应承了——尤其是因为这笔交割里夹进了一张小小的绿色钞票。克雷格和我小心翼翼地把装在木箱里的大瓶子搬上我们那辆轻便马车，赶回旅馆房间。我们载着一堆五加仑的空瓶子回来、又一趟趟抬上楼，看得那班闲人好不兴奋。不过，只要是克雷格要办的事，对于旁人怎么说，我早就置之度外了。

在房间里，我们一直干到深夜。克雷格把一小团棉花扎在长铁丝头上，伸进每只瓶子，细细擦拭瓶底瓶壁。然后把棉签里的水挤到涂有琼脂（agar-agar，即日本海藻）的小玻璃载片上——那是一种病菌培养物繁殖极快的培养基。他把载片放进随身带来的一只用酒精灯加热的小烘箱里，让它们在血液的温度下过一夜。

这一路我都注意到，他格外小心，绝不碰任何一只瓶子的外头。至于我，天大好处也不肯碰。实际上我已经到了这步田地：碰什么都得先犹豫一下，连呼吸都发怵——虽然明知呼吸无碍。不过，克雷格如此小心，倒不是怕摸瓶子会染上病菌。他在站台昏暗的灯光下就注意到瓶子上似乎有指印，那些指印让他上了心——事实上，正是它们使他打定主意，要把这些瓶子再往深里查一查。

===Original===

“I am now going to bring out these very faint fingerprints on the bottles,” remarked Craig, proceeding with his examination in the better light of our room. “Here is some powder known to chemists as ‘grey powder’﻿—mercury and chalk. I sprinkle it over the faint markings, so, and then I brush it off with a camel’s-hair brush lightly. That brings out the imprint much more clearly, as you can see. For instance, if you place your dry thumb on a piece of white paper you leave no visible impression. If grey powder is sprinkled over the spot and then brushed off a distinct impression is seen. If the impression of the fingers is left on something soft, like wax, it is often best to use printers’ ink to bring out the ridges and patterns of the fingermarks. And so on for various materials. Quite a science has been built up around fingerprints.

“I wish I had that enlarging camera which I have in my laboratory. However, my ordinary camera will do, for all I want is to preserve a record of these marks, and I can enlarge the photographs later. In the morning I will photograph these marks and you can do the developing of the films. Tonight we’ll improvise the bathroom as a darkroom and get everything ready so that we can start in bright and early.”

===Chinese===

「现在，我要把瓶身上这些极淡的指纹显现出来。」克雷格说，在房间里较亮的灯光下继续检验。「这儿是一种化学家叫做『灰色粉末』（grey powder）的东西——汞和白垩。我把它撒在淡淡的印痕上，像这样，再用驼毛刷轻轻扫去。你瞧，印痕一下子就清楚多了。打个比方：你把干燥的拇指按在一张白纸上，看不出留下什么印子；往那处撒上灰色粉末再扫掉，一枚清清楚楚的印痕就显出来了。倘若指印留在蜡一类柔软的东西上，往往最好用印刷油墨来显出指纹的纹嵴与花样。各式材料，各有各的招数。围绕指纹，已经建立起一门不小的学问了。

「真希望我实验室里那台放大照相机在手上。不过普通照相机也够了，我要的只是把这些痕迹留存一份记录，照片日后可以放大。明早我来拍下这些痕迹，胶卷的冲洗就交给你。今晚咱们把浴室临时改成暗房，把一切备置停当，明儿一早就能开工。」

===Original===

We were, indeed, up early. One never has difficulty in getting up early in the country: it is so noisy, at least to a city-bred man. City noise at five a.m. is sepulchral silence compared with bucolic activity at that hour.

There were a dozen negatives which I set about developing after Craig had used up all our films. Meanwhile, he busied himself adjusting his microscope and test-tubes and getting the agar slides ready for examination.

Shirtsleeves rolled up, I was deeply immersed in my work when I heard a shout in the next room, and the bathroom door flew open.

===Chinese===

第二天我们果真起了个大早。在乡下早起从来不是难事：天一亮就闹得欢——至少在城里长大的人听来如此。清晨五点城里的喧嚣，同那个时辰田园间的闹腾一比，简直是坟墓里的死寂。

克雷格把胶卷统统拍完，剩下十几张底片，由我动手冲洗。他则忙着调校显微镜和试管，把琼脂载片一一备好待验。

我挽着衬衫袖子正埋头干活，忽听隔壁房间一声欢呼，浴室的门砰地敞开了。

===Original===

“Confound you, Kennedy, do you want to ruin these films?” I cried.

He shut the door with a bang. “Hurrah, Walter!” he exclaimed. “I think I have it, at last. I have just found some most promising colonies of the bacilli on one of my slides.”

I almost dropped the pan of acid I was holding, in my excitement. “Well,” I said, concealing my own surprise, “I’ve found out something, too. Every one of these fingerprints so far is from the same pair of hands.”

We scarcely ate any breakfast, and were soon on our way up to the hall. Craig had provided himself at the local stationer’s with an inking-pad, such as is used for rubber stamps. At the hall he proceeded to get the impressions of the fingers and thumbs of all the servants.

===Chinese===

「该死的，肯尼迪，你想毁了这些胶卷吗？」我大叫。

他砰地关上门。「好哇，沃尔特！」他欢声说道，「我想我终于逮着它了。刚才我在一张载片上发现了几簇长势喜人的杆菌菌落。」

我一激动，险些把手里那盘酸液打翻。「嘿，」我掩饰着惊讶说，「我也有个发现。到目前为止，这些指纹没一枚不是出自同一双手。」

早饭几乎没顾上吃，我们很快就动身上了庄园。克雷格已在本地文具店置办了一块盖橡皮图章用的油墨印台。到了庄园，他把全体仆人的十指指纹一一取了下来。

===Original===

It was quite a long and difficult piece of work to compare the fingerprints we had taken with those photographed, in spite of the fact that writers descant on the ease with which criminals are traced by this system devised by the famous Galton. However, we at last finished the job between us; or rather Craig finished it, with an occasional remark from me. His dexterity amazed me; it was more than mere book knowledge.

For a moment we sat regarding each other hopelessly. None of the fingerprints taken at the hall tallied with the photographed prints. Then Craig rang for the housekeeper, a faithful old soul whom even the typhoid scare could not budge from her post.

“Are you sure I have seen all the servants who were at the hall while Mr. Bisbee was here?” asked Craig.

“Why, no, sir﻿—you didn’t ask that. You asked to see all who are here now. There is only one who has left, the cook, Bridget Fallon. She left a couple of days ago﻿—said she was going back to New York to get another job. Glad enough I was to get rid of her, too, for she was drunk most of the time after the typhoid appeared.”

===Chinese===

把取来的指纹同拍下照的指纹一一比对，实在是件又费时又费力的活计——尽管作家们把那位大名鼎鼎的高尔顿（Galton）发明的这套体系夸得天花乱坠，说凭它追查罪犯易如反掌。不管怎样，我们总算合力把事办完了；或者说，是克雷格办完的，我偶尔插上一句。他那手熟极而流的功夫令我惊叹——那可远不止书本上的学问。

一时间，我们俩面面相觑，全没了主意。庄园里取的指纹，没有一枚对得上照片上的印痕。于是克雷格按铃唤管家婆——一位忠心耿耿的老人家，连伤寒的风声都没能把她从岗位上吓走。

「比斯比先生住庄园期间用过的仆人，你敢肯定我都见全了？」克雷格问。

「哎呀，没有，先生——您没这么问呀。您问的是眼下还在的都见一见。走的只有一个，就是厨娘布里奇特·法伦（Bridget Fallon）。她前两天走的——说是要回纽约另找东家。她走了我才巴不得呢，伤寒一出，她成天醉醺醺的。」

===Original===

“Well, Walter, I guess we shall have to go back to New York again, then,” exclaimed Kennedy. “Oh, I beg pardon, Mrs. Rawson, for interrupting. Thank you ever so much. Where did Bridget come from?”

“She came well recommended, sir. Here is the letter in my writing-desk. She had been employed by the Caswell-Joneses at Shelter Island before she came here.”

“I may keep this letter?” asked Craig, scanning it quickly.

“Yes.”

===Chinese===

「那么，沃尔特，看来咱们又得回纽约去了。」肯尼迪说道，「哦，失礼了，罗森夫人（Mrs. Rawson），打断您了。多谢多谢。布里奇特原是从哪儿雇来的？」

「她来的时候还带着挺好的荐书呢，先生。信就在我写字台里。来这儿以前，她在谢尔特岛（Shelter Island）的卡斯韦尔-琼斯（Caswell-Jones）家做过。」

「这封信可以归我吗？」克雷格一边飞快扫了一眼，一边问。

「可以。」

===Original===

“By the way, where were the bottles of spring water kept?”

“In the kitchen.”

“Did Bridget take charge of them?”

“Yes.”

“Did Mr. Bisbee have any guests during the last week that he was here?”

“Only Mr. Denny one night.”

“H’m!” exclaimed Craig. “Well, it will not be so hard for us to unravel this matter, after all, when we get back to the city. We must make that noon train, Walter. There is nothing more for us to do here.”

===Chinese===

「对了，泉水瓶一向放在哪儿？」

「厨房里。」

「归布里奇特经管吗？」

「是。」

「比斯比先生在这儿住的最后一个星期，府上收过客人没有？」

「只有丹尼先生来过一夜。」

「嗯！」克雷格轻咳一声，「好在这案子回到城里去理出头绪，到底也不算难。咱们得赶上正午那班车，沃尔特。这儿没有别的可干了。」

===Original===

Emerging from the Tube at Ninth Street, Craig hustled me into a taxicab, and in almost no time we were at police headquarters.

Fortunately, Inspector Barney O’Connor was in and in an amiable mood, too, for Kennedy had been careful that the Central Office received a large share of credit for the Kerr Parker case. Craig sketched hastily the details of this new case. O’Connor’s face was a study. His honest blue Irish eyes fairly bulged in wonder, and when Craig concluded with a request for help I think O’Connor would have given him anything in the office, just to figure in the case.

“First, I want one of your men to go to the surrogate’s office and get the original of the will. I shall return it within a couple of hours﻿—all I want to do is to make a photographic copy. Then another man must find this lawyer, James Denny, and in some way get his fingerprints﻿—you must arrange that yourself. And send another fellow up to the employment offices on Fourth Avenue and have him locate this cook, Bridget Fallon. I want her fingerprints, too. Perhaps she had better be detained, for I don’t want her to get away. Oh, and say, O’Connor, do you want to finish this case up like the crack of a whip tonight?”

===Chinese===

在第九街钻出地铁，克雷格连拖带拽把我塞进一辆出租车，转眼间就到了警察总局。

幸好巴尼·奥康纳警督在局里，兴致也正好——克尔·帕克案里，肯尼迪特意让中央办公厅分去了好大一份功劳。克雷格三言两语，把这件新案的始末勾了个大概。奥康纳的那张脸，本身就是一景：一双诚实的爱尔兰蓝眼睛惊得都快凸出来了。等克雷格末了开口求助，我想，只要能在这案子里挂上个名，奥康纳恨不得把办公室里的什么东西都搬给他。

「第一，派个人到遗嘱检验法庭（surrogate’s office），把遗嘱原件借出来。两个钟头之内我准归还——我不过要拍个照相副本。其次，再派个人去找那位律师詹姆斯·丹尼，想法子弄到他的指纹——这一节你得亲自安排。再打发一个人到第四大道的职业介绍所去，找到那个厨娘布里奇特·法伦。她的指纹我也要。说不定最好把她看住，我可不想叫她跑了。哦，还有，奥康纳，今晚你想不想把这案子办得像鞭梢一响那样干脆利落？」

===Original===

“I’m game, sir. What of it?”

“Let me see. It is now four o’clock. If you can get hold of all these people in time I think I shall be ready for the final scene tonight﻿—say, at nine. You know how to arrange it. Have them all present at my laboratory at nine, and I promise we shall have a story that will get into the morning papers with leaded type on the front page.”

“Now, Walter,” he added, as we hurried down to the taxicab again, “I want you to drop off at the Department of Health with this card to the commissioner. I believe you know Dr. Leslie. Well, ask him if he knows anything about this Bridget Fallon. I will go on uptown to the laboratory and get my apparatus ready. You needn’t come up till nine, old fellow, for I shall be busy till then, but be sure when you come that you bring the record of this Fallon woman if you have to beg, borrow, or steal it.”

===Chinese===

「我奉陪，先生。怎么讲？」

「我算算。现在四点。你若能及时把这些人统统找齐，今晚的压轴戏——这样吧，九点——我想我就能开场。怎么张罗你是行家。九点整，把他们一个不落全带到我的实验室来。我担保，有一条新闻明早准上各报头版，用加空铅的醒目铅字排出。」

「现在，沃尔特，」我们匆匆下楼再上出租车时，他又说，「你带上这张名片，半道在卫生局（Department of Health）下车去见局长。我想你认得莱斯利医生（Dr. Leslie）。对，问问他知不知道这个布里奇特·法伦的底细。我径直往北去实验室，把仪器备齐。九点以前你不用来，老伙计，在那之前我一直腾不开身。可你来的时候，务必把那个法伦女人的档案带来——求也好，借也好，偷也好。」

===Original===

I didn’t understand it, but I took the card and obeyed implicitly. It is needless to say that I was keyed up to the greatest pitch of excitement during my interview with the health commissioner, when I finally got in to see him. I hadn’t talked to him long before a great light struck me, and I began to see what Craig was driving at. The commissioner saw it first.

“If you don’t mind, Mr. Jameson,” he said, after I had told him as much of my story as I could, “will you call up Professor Kennedy and tell him I’d like very much to be present tonight myself?”

“Certainly I will,” I replied, glad to get my errand done in first-class fashion in that way.

Things must have been running smoothly, for while I was sitting in our apartment after dinner, impatiently waiting for half-past eight, when the commissioner had promised to call for me and go up to the laboratory, the telephone rang. It was Craig.

“Walter, might I ask a favour of you?” he said. “When the commissioner comes ask him to stop at the Louis Quinze and bring Miss Bisbee up, too. Tell her it is important. No more now. Things are going ahead fine.”

===Chinese===

我虽然摸不着头脑，还是接过名片，照办不误。不必说，等我终于进见卫生局长时，那一场谈话里我的神经绷紧到了极点。没谈几句，我脑中灵光一闪，开始明白克雷格的用意何在。局长比我先看穿。

「詹姆森先生，你若不介意，」我把能讲的都讲完之后，他说，「能不能给肯尼迪教授打个电话，就说今晚我也非常想亲临其境？」

「当然可以。」我答道，很高兴差事竟能这样漂漂亮亮地了结。

事情想必一概顺利。晚饭后我坐在公寓里，焦躁地捱到八点半——局长答应了那时来接我同去实验室——电话铃响了。是克雷格。

「沃尔特，能求你件事吗？」他说，「局长来时，请他顺道在路易十五公寓停一停，把比斯比小姐也一并接来。告诉她事情要紧。先不多说了。一切顺利。」

===Original===

Promptly at nine we were assembled, a curious crowd. The health commissioner and the inspector, being members of the same political party, greeted each other by their first names. Miss Bisbee was nervous, Bridget was abusive, Denny was sullen. As for Kennedy, he was, as usual, as cool as a lump of ice. And I﻿—well, I just sat on my feelings to keep myself quiet.

At one end of the room Craig had placed a large white sheet such as he used in his stereopticon lectures, while at the top of the tier of seats that made a sort of little amphitheatre out of his lecture-room his stereopticon sputtered.

“Moving pictures tonight, eh?” said Inspector O’Connor.

“Not exactly,” said Craig, “though﻿—yes, they will be moving in another sense. Now, if we are all ready, I’ll switch off the electric lights.”

===Chinese===

九点整，我们一伙人齐集一堂——一群好生古怪的看客。卫生局长同警督属于同一个党，彼此以教名相称寒暄。比斯比小姐神色紧张，布里奇特泼口骂人，丹尼阴沉着脸。至于肯尼迪，一如既往，冷静得像一块冰。而我呢——唉，只能死死按住自己的情绪，好不作声。

房间一头，克雷格张起一大块白布单，就是他作投影幻灯讲演（stereopticon）用的那种；一列列座位把他的讲演室隔成了小剧场模样，那台幻灯机正安在座位顶头，噼啪地吐着光。

「今晚放电影，嗯？」奥康纳警督说。

「不算放电影，」克雷格说，「不过——也罢，从另一种意义上讲，今晚真有东西要动起来。好了，各位若都齐备，我就关电灯了。」

===Original===

The calcium sputtered some more, and a square of light was thrown on the sheet.

Kennedy snapped a little announcer such as lecturers use. “Let me invite your attention to these enlargements of fingerprints,” he began, as a huge thumb appeared on the screen. “Here we have a series of fingerprints which I will show one after another slowly. They are all of the fingers of the same person, and they were found on some empty bottles of spring water used at Bisbee Hall during the two weeks previous to the departure of Mr. Bisbee for New York.

“Here are, in succession, the fingerprints of the various servants employed about the house﻿—and of a guest,” added Craig, with a slight change of tone. “They differ markedly from the fingerprints on the glass,” he continued, as one after another appeared, “all except this last one. That is identical. It is, Inspector, what we call a composite type of fingerprint﻿—in this case a combination of what is called the ‘loop’ and ‘whorl’ types.”

No sound broke the stillness save the sputtering of the oxygen on the calcium of the stereopticon.

===Chinese===

钙光灯（calcium light）又噼啪了一阵，一方亮光投上白布。

肯尼迪揿了一下讲演人常用的那种小信号器。「请各位把注意力放到这些放大的指纹上。」他开口说道，屏幕上现出一枚巨大的拇指印。「这里是一组指纹，我要一张接一张慢慢放给大家看。它们全属同一个人的手指，是从比斯比庄园几只空泉水瓶上取到的——正是比斯比先生动身来纽约前那两个星期里庄园用过的瓶子。

「这是府里雇用的各名仆人的指纹，依次映出——还有一位客人的。」克雷格补上一句，语气微微一变。「它们同玻璃瓶上的指纹判然有别，」指纹一张张映出来，他继续说，「唯独最后这一枚例外。它一模一样。警督，这就是我们所谓的复合型指纹——在这一例中，是『箕形』（loop）与『斗形』（whorl）两类纹型的结合。」

万籁俱寂，只有幻灯机上氧气喷烧钙光的噼啪声。

===Original===

“The owner of the fingers from which these prints were made is in this room. It was from typhoid germs on these fingers that the fever was introduced into the drinking water at Bisbee Hall.”

Kennedy paused to emphasise the statement, then continued. “I am now going to ask Dr. Leslie to give us a little talk on a recent discovery in the field of typhoid fever﻿—you understand, Commissioner, what I mean, I believe?”

“Perfectly. Shall I mention names?”

“No, not yet.”

===Chinese===

「留下这些指纹的那双手，它的主人就在这间屋里。正是从这几根手指上的伤寒病菌起，热病才被引进了比斯比庄园的饮用水。」

肯尼迪顿了顿，好让这句话落地生根，然后接着说：「下面我请莱斯利医生给我们略谈一谈伤寒领域的一项新发现——局长，我想您明白我指的是什么吧？」

「完全明白。要我指名道姓吗？」

「先不要。」

===Original===

“Well,” began Dr. Leslie, clearing his throat, “within the past year or two we have made a most weird and startling discovery in typhoid fever. We have found what we now call ‘typhoid carriers’﻿—persons who do not have the disease themselves, perhaps never have had it, but who are literally living test-tubes of the typhoid bacillus. It is positively uncanny. Everywhere they go they scatter the disease. Down at the department we have the records of a number of such instances, and our men in the research laboratories have come to the conclusion that, far from being of rare occurrence, these cases are comparatively common. I have in mind one particular case of a servant girl, who, during the past five or six years, has been employed in several families.

“In every family typhoid fever has later broken out. Experts have traced out at least thirty, cases and several deaths due to this one person. In another case we found an epidemic up in Harlem to be due to a typhoid carrier on a remote farm in Connecticut. This carrier, innocently enough, it is true, contaminated the milk-supply coming from that farm. The result was over fifty cases of typhoid here in this city.

“However, to return to the case of the servant I have mentioned. Last spring we had her under surveillance, but as there was no law by which we could restrain her permanently she is still at large. I think one of the Sunday papers at the time had an account of her﻿—they called her ‘Typhoid Bridget,’ and in red ink she was drawn across the page in gruesome fashion, frying the skulls of her victims in a frying-pan over a roaring fire. That particular typhoid carrier, I understand﻿—”

===Chinese===

「好吧。」莱斯利医生清了清嗓子，开了腔，「就在过去一两年间，我们在伤寒上取得了一个极其离奇、骇人听闻的发现。我们发现了如今所称的『伤寒带菌者』（typhoid carriers）——这种人自己不患病，也许从来没有患过，却字面不差地是一支支活的伤寒杆菌试管。这事实在邪性。他们走到哪里，就把疾病撒到哪里。我们局里存着若干这类实例的记录，研究实验室的同事们得出的结论是：这类病例绝非罕见，反倒相当常见。我脑子里就有现成的一例：一个做女仆的姑娘，过去五六年里先后受雇于好几户人家。

「每一户人家后来都闹起了伤寒。专家们追查出的病例至少有三十起，还有几条人命，全系于此人一身。另一例中，我们发现哈莱姆的一场疫情，源头是康涅狄格州一座偏远农庄上的伤寒带菌者。那位带菌者——诚然是全然无辜地——污染了那座农庄供应的牛奶。结果是本城添了五十多例伤寒。

「不过，还是回到我刚提的那个女仆吧。去年春天，我们把她置于监视之下，可没有哪条法律容许我们把她长久管束，她至今逍遥在外。我记得当时有一家星期日报纸登过她的事——称她『伤寒布里奇特』，还用红墨水把她画得横贯整版，狰狞可怖：她蹲在一炉旺火前，拿平底锅煎着她那些受害者的骷髅。据我所知，那一位伤寒带菌者——」

===Original===

“Excuse me, Commissioner, if I interrupt, but I think we have carried this part of the programme far enough to be absolutely convincing,” said Craig. “Thank you very much for the clear way in which you have put it.”

Craig snapped the announcer, and a letter appeared on the screen. He said nothing, but let us read it through.

To whom it may concern:

This is to certify that Bridget Fallon has been employed in my family at Shelter Island for the past season and that I have found her a reliable servant and an excellent cook.

A. St. John Caswell-Jones.

===Chinese===

「恕我打断，局长，」克雷格说，「我想这一节节目已经演得够透，足令人深信不疑了。多谢您讲得这样清楚。」

克雷格揿响信号器，屏幕上现出一封信。他一言不发，由着我们从头读到尾。

敬启者：

兹证明布里奇特·法伦上一季受雇于舍下，工作地点在谢尔特岛，本人认为她做事可靠，厨艺上佳。

A·圣约翰·卡斯韦尔-琼斯。

===Original===

“Before God, Mr. Kennedy, I’m innocent,” screeched Bridget. “Don’t have me arrested. I’m innocent. I’m innocent.”

Craig gently, but firmly, forced her back into her chair.

Again the announcer snapped. This time the last page of Mr. Bisbee’s will appeared on the sheet, ending with his signature and the witnesses.

“I’m now going to show these two specimens of handwriting very greatly enlarged,” he said, as the stereopticon plates were shifted again.

===Chinese===

「老天在上，肯尼迪先生，我是清白的。」布里奇特尖声叫道，「别叫人抓我。我是清白的。我是清白的。」

克雷格轻轻的、却不容分说地把她按回椅子里。

信号器又揿响。这一回，布单上映出的是比斯比先生遗嘱的末页，以他的签名和见证人的签押收尾。

「现在，我要把这两份笔迹样本放到极大，给大家看。」他说着，幻灯片又换了一批。

===Original===

“An author of many scientific works, Dr. Lindsay Johnson, of London, has recently elaborated a new theory with regard to individuality in handwriting. He maintains that in certain diseases a person’s pulse beats are individual, and that no one suffering from any such disease can control, even for a brief space of time, the frequency or peculiar irregularities of his heart’s action, as shown by a chart recording his pulsation. Such a chart is obtained for medical purposes by means of a sphygmograph, an instrument fitted to the patient’s forearm and supplied with a needle, which can be so arranged as to record automatically on a prepared sheet of paper the peculiar force and frequency of the pulsation. Or the pulsation may be simply observed in the rise and fall of a liquid in a tube. Dr. Johnson holds the opinion that a pen in the hand of a writer serves, in a modified degree, the same end as the needle in the first-named form of the sphygmograph and that in such a person’s handwriting one can see by projecting the letters, greatly magnified, on a screen, the scarcely perceptible turns and quivers made in the lines by the spontaneous action of that person’s peculiar pulsation.

“To prove this, the doctor carried out an experiment at Charing Cross Hospital. At his request a number of patients suffering from heart and kidney diseases wrote the Lord’s Prayer in their ordinary handwriting. The different manuscripts were then taken and examined microscopically. By throwing them, highly magnified, on a screen, the jerks or involuntary motions due to the patient’s peculiar pulsations were distinctly visible. The handwriting of persons in normal health, says Dr. Johnson, does not always show their pulse beats. What one can say, however, is that when a document, purporting to be written by a certain person, contains traces of pulse beats and the normal handwriting of that person does not show them, then clearly that document is a forgery.

===Chinese===

「伦敦的林赛·约翰逊医生（Dr. Lindsay Johnson）——一位著有许多科学著作的作者——最近就笔迹的个人特征阐发了一套新理论。他主张：患某些疾病的人，脉搏的搏动是个人独有；凡患这类疾病的人，哪怕短短一瞬，也无法控制自己心脏搏动的频率或特殊的节律不齐——记录脉搏的曲线图上便看得明白。这种图，医学上是借脉搏描记器（sphygmograph）来录的：仪器缚在病人前臂上，装有一根针，调节得当，便能将脉搏特有的力度与频率自动记录在一张备好的纸上。脉搏的起伏，也可以单看管中液体的升降。约翰逊医生认为：执笔者手中的笔，在相当的程度上，起的正是上述第一种脉搏描记器里那根针的作用；因而把这种人的笔迹放大许多倍投上屏幕，就能看出笔画里那些几乎察觉不到的顿挫与颤动——那是此人独有的脉搏自行搏动留下的痕迹。

「为了证实这一点，这位医生在查令十字医院（Charing Cross Hospital）做了一项实验。应他的请求，一批患心脏病与肾病的病人，各以寻常笔迹抄写了一遍主祷文。随后这些手稿被逐一收拢，置于显微镜下检验。把它们放到极大投上屏幕，病人独特脉搏所引起的一顿一顿的不随意动作，看得一清二楚。约翰逊医生说，健康人寻常状态下的笔迹，并不总会显出脉搏。但可以断言的是：一份号称出自某人之手的文书，倘含有脉搏搏动的痕迹，而此人寻常笔迹中却不见这种痕迹，那么，这份文书分明是伪造的。

===Original===

“Now, in these two specimens of handwriting which we have enlarged it is plain that the writers of both of them suffered from a certain peculiar disease of the heart. Moreover, I am prepared to show that the pulse beats exhibited in the case of certain pen-strokes in one of these documents are exhibited in similar strokes in the other. Furthermore, I have ascertained from his family physician, whose affidavit I have here, that Mr. Bisbee did not suffer from this or any other form of heart disease. Mr. Caswell-Jones, in addition to wiring me that he refused to write Bridget Fallon a recommendation after the typhoid broke out in his country house, also says he does not suffer from heart disease in any form. From the tremulous character of the letters and figures in both these documents, which when magnified is the more easily detected, I therefore conclude that both are forgeries, and I am ready to go farther and say that they are forgeries from the same hand.

“It usually takes a couple of weeks after infection for typhoid to develop, a time sufficient in itself to remove suspicion from acts which might otherwise be scrutinised very carefully if happening immediately before the disease developed. I may add, also, that it is well known that stout people do very poorly when they contract typhoid, especially if they are old. Mr. Bisbee was both stout and old. To contract typhoid was for him a virtual death-warrant. Knowing all these facts, a certain person purposely sought out a crafty means of introducing typhoid fever into Mr. Bisbee’s family. That person, furthermore, was inoculated against typhoid three times during the month before the disease was devilishly and surreptitiously introduced into Bisbee Hall, in order to protect himself or herself should it become necessary for that person to visit Bisbee Hall. That person, I believe, is the one who suffered from an aneurism of the heart, the writer, or rather the forger, of the two documents I have shown, by one of which he or she was to profit greatly by the death of Mr. Bisbee and the founding of an alleged school in a distant part of the country﻿—a subterfuge, if you recall, used in at least one famous case for which the convicted perpetrator is now under a life sentence in Sing Sing.

===Chinese===

「现在来看我们放大的这两份笔迹样本。显而易见，两份的书写者都患有某种特殊的心脏疾病。而且，我还可以证明：其中一份文书里某些笔画中显现的脉搏搏动，在另一份文书相似的笔画中同样显现。再者，我已同比斯比先生的私人医生查证——他的宣誓证词就在我手上——比斯比先生从未患过这种或任何别种心脏病。至于卡斯韦尔-琼斯先生，他除了拍电报告诉我，他乡下宅邸闹了伤寒之后已拒绝为布里奇特·法伦写荐书之外，还声明自己不患任何形式的心脏病。两份文书中的字母与数字均带颤笔的特征——一经放大，更易察见——因此我断定：两份均属伪造。我还敢更进一步说：它们出自同一只手。

「伤寒自感染至发病，通常需两个星期上下。单是这段时间，便足以把嫌疑从某些行为上摘开——那些行为若发生在发病前夕，本会给人拿放大镜细瞧的。我还可以补一句：众所周知，胖子得了伤寒，十有八九凶险，年纪大的尤其如此。比斯比先生既胖且老。染上伤寒，对他无异一纸死刑判决书。某个深知这一切的人，处心积虑觅了一条狡计，要把伤寒热送进比斯比先生家中。更有甚者，在那场病被鬼鬼祟祟、丧尽天良地引进比斯比庄园之前的一个月里，此人接连三次接种了伤寒疫苗——以防万一同庄园打交道时殃及自身。我相信，此人正是那个患心脏动脉瘤（aneurism）的人，也就是我出示的两份文书的书写者——毋宁说，伪造者。凭其中一份，比斯比先生一死，他或她便可借兴办那所所谓的学校大发其财——校址据说远在本国一隅。诸位倘若记得，这一障眼法至少在一桩名案里用过，那案子里定罪的凶犯，如今正在新新监狱（Sing Sing）服无期徒刑。

===Original===

“I will ask Dr. Leslie to take this stethoscope and examine the hearts of everyone in the room and tell me whether there is anyone here suffering from an aneurism.”

The calcium light ceased to sputter. One person after another was examined by the health commissioner. Was it merely my imagination, or did I really hear a heart beating with wild leaps as if it would burst the bonds of its prison and make its escape if possible? Perhaps it was only the engine of the commissioner’s machine out on the campus driveway. I don’t know. At any rate, he went silently from one to the other, betraying not even by his actions what he discovered with the stethoscope. The suspense was terrible. I felt Miss Bisbee’s hand involuntarily grasp my arm convulsively. Without disturbing the silence, I reached a glass of water standing near me on Craig’s lecture-table and handed it to her.

The commissioner was bending over the lawyer, trying to adjust the stethoscope better to his ears. The lawyer’s head was resting heavily on his hand, and he was heaped up in an awkward position in the cramped lecture-room seat. It seemed an age as Dr. Leslie tried to adjust the stethoscope. Even Craig felt the excitement. While the commissioner hesitated, Kennedy reached over and impatiently switched on the electric light in full force.

===Chinese===

「现在请莱斯利医生取这具听诊器，把室内每个人的心脏都听一遍，再告诉我，在场的人里有没有谁患着动脉瘤。」

钙光停止了噼啪。卫生局长一个接一个地听诊过去。那是我纯粹的想象呢，还是当真有一颗心脏在没命地狂跳，仿佛要挣破囚牢、寻机脱逃？也许那不过是外头校园车道上局长汽车的引擎罢了。我说不清。总之，他默默地在人与人之间移动，连一举一动都不肯泄露听诊器底下听出了什么。这份煎熬叫人难以忍受。我觉出比斯比小姐的手不由自主地死死抓住了我的胳膊。为了不惊动那片寂静，我伸出手，够到克雷格讲桌上立在我近旁的一杯水，递给了她。

局长正俯身向着那位律师，想把听诊器往他耳朵上贴得更服帖些。律师的头沉沉地支在自己手上，整个人蜷作一团，窝在狭促的阶梯座位里，姿势说不出的别扭。莱斯利医生调理那听诊器的工夫，漫长得像一个世纪。连克雷格也熬不住了。局长还在迟疑，肯尼迪已经伸手过去，不耐烦地一把将电灯尽数拧亮。

===Original===

As the light flooded the room, blinding us for the instant, the large form of Dr. Leslie stood between us and the lawyer.

“What does the stethoscope tell you, Doctor?” asked Craig, leaning forward expectantly. He was as unprepared for the answer as any of us.

“It tells me that a higher court than those of New York has passed judgment on this astounding criminal. The aneurism has burst.”

I felt a soft weight fall on my shoulder. The *Morning Star* did not have the story, after all. I missed the greatest scoop of my life seeing Eveline Bisbee safely to her home after she had recovered from the shock of Denny’s exposure and punishment.


===Chinese===

灯光涌入房间，晃得我们一时睁不开眼。莱斯利医生高大的身躯，正立在我们与律师之间。

「听诊器告诉了你什么，医生？」克雷格探身向前，满怀期待地问。对这个回答，他同我们大伙儿一样毫无准备。

「它告诉我：一个比纽约各法庭更高的法庭，已对这骇人听闻的罪人作出了判决。动脉瘤破了。」

我觉得肩头落下一团柔软的重量。《早间明星报》（*Morning Star*）到头来还是没登成这条新闻。我平生最大的一桩独家新闻，就这样错过了——丹尼的败露和下场令她惊魂不定，待她缓过神来，我把她平平安安送回了家。

## The Black Hand / 黑手党

===Original===
Kennedy and I had been dining rather late one evening at Luigi’s, a little Italian restaurant on the lower West Side. We had known the place well in our student days, and had made a point of visiting it once a month since, in order to keep in practice in the fine art of gracefully handling long shreds of spaghetti. Therefore we did not think it strange when the proprietor himself stopped a moment at our table to greet us. Glancing furtively around at the other diners, mostly Italians, he suddenly leaned over and whispered to Kennedy:

“I have heard of your wonderful detective work, Professor. Could you give a little advice in the case of a friend of mine?”

“Surely, Luigi. What is the case?” asked Craig, leaning back in his chair.

===Chinese===
一天晚上，我和肯尼迪在路易吉（Luigi）的餐馆吃饭，吃得相当晚。那是下西城一家小小的意大利馆子。学生时代我们就跟这地方混得烂熟，此后每月必来一回，为的是在优雅对付长长面条丝这门精妙手艺上不致荒疏。因此，当老板本人到我们桌旁驻足片刻向我们致意时，我们并不觉得奇怪。他贼忒兮兮地朝周围的食客——多半是意大利人——扫了一眼，忽然俯身凑近肯尼迪，低声道：

「我听说过您那些神乎其神的探案本领，教授。有位朋友遇上了一桩事，您能不能给点指教？」

「当然可以，路易吉。什么事？」克雷格往椅背上一靠，问道。

===Original===
Luigi glanced around again apprehensively and lowered his voice. “Not so loud, sir. When you pay your check, go out, walk around Washington Square, and come in at the private entrance. I’ll be waiting in the hall. My friend is dining privately upstairs.”

We lingered a while over our Chianti, then quietly paid the check and departed.

===Chinese===
路易吉又提心吊胆地四下看了看，压低了嗓音。「小点声，先生。二位结账出门后，绕着华盛顿广场走一圈，再从私人入口进来。我在门厅里候着。我那位朋友正在楼上单独用饭。」

我们就着基安蒂酒（Chianti）又磨蹭了一会儿，然后悄悄结账，走了出去。

===Original===
True to his word, Luigi was waiting for us in the dark hall. With a motion that indicated silence, he led us up the stairs to the second floor, and quickly opened a door into what seemed to be a fair-sized private dining-room. A man was pacing the floor nervously. On a table was some food, untouched. As the door opened I thought he started as if in fear, and I am sure his dark face blanched, if only for an instant. Imagine our surprise at seeing Gennaro, the great tenor, with whom merely to have a speaking acquaintance was to argue oneself famous.

“Oh, it is you, Luigi,” he exclaimed in perfect English, rich and mellow. “And who are these gentlemen?”

Luigi merely replied, “Friends,” in English also, and then dropped off into a voluble, low-toned explanation in Italian.

===Chinese===
路易吉果然言而有信，正在黑黢黢的门厅里等我们。他做了个示意噤声的手势，领我们上楼到二楼，迅捷地推开一扇门，里面像是一间大小不错的包间餐室。一个男人正神经质地来回踱步。桌上摆着些饭菜，纹丝未动。门开的一瞬，我觉得他像受惊似的一怔，而且我敢肯定，他那张黝黑的脸白了一下——尽管只是一刹那。想想我们有多惊讶吧：眼前竟是热纳罗（Gennaro），那位大名鼎鼎的男高音——同这样的人哪怕仅仅有面谈之交，便足以自证身价非凡了。

「哦，是你们啊，路易吉，」他用一口地道而醇厚圆润的英语招呼道。「这几位先生是？」

路易吉也只用英语答了两个字：「朋友。」接着便用意大利语低声滔滔不绝地解释起来。

===Original===
I could see, as we waited, that the same idea had flashed over Kennedy’s mind as over my own. It was now three or four days since the papers had reported the strange kidnapping of Gennaro’s five-year-old daughter Adelina, his only child, and the sending of a demand for ten thousand dollars ransom, signed, as usual, with the mystic Black Hand﻿—a name to conjure with in blackmail and extortion.

===Chinese===
等待之际我看得出来，同一个念头也在肯尼迪的脑海里一闪而过，正如在我脑海里一样。报上报道热纳罗五岁的独生女阿代利娜（Adelina）离奇遭绑架、家里被索要一万美元赎金，至今已有三四天了——勒索信照例署着那个神秘的黑手党（Black Hand）：一个在恐吓勒索行里如同咒语般灵验的名号。

===Original===
As Signor Gennaro advanced toward us, after his short talk with Luigi, almost before the introductions were over, Kennedy anticipated him by saying: “I understand, Signor, before you ask me. I have read all about it in the papers. You want someone to help you catch the criminals who are holding your little girl.”

“No, no!” exclaimed Gennaro excitedly. “Not that. I want to get my daughter first. After that, catch them if you can﻿—yes, I should like to have someone do it. But read this first and tell me what you think of it. How should I act to get my little Adelina back without harming a hair of her head?” The famous singer drew from a capacious pocketbook a dirty, crumpled, letter, scrawled on cheap paper.

Kennedy translated it quickly. It read:

===Chinese===
热纳罗先生同路易吉短短交谈几句后，便朝我们走来，介绍的话音未落，肯尼迪已先发制人地说：「您不必开口我就明白，先生。来龙去脉我在报上全读到了。您想要有人帮您抓住扣着您小女儿的罪犯。」

「不，不！」热纳罗激动地嚷道。「不是这个。我要先找回我的女儿。那之后，抓得住他们就抓——是的，我也乐得有人去抓。可您先读读这封信，告诉我您有何高见。我该怎么行事，才能不伤我小阿代利娜一根头发，把她平平安安要回来？」这位著名歌唱家从一只宽大的皮夹子里，掏出一封脏兮兮、皱巴巴的信，字迹潦草地涂在廉价纸上。

肯尼迪飞快地把信读了一遍。信文如下：

===Original===
**Honourable sir:** Your daughter is in safe hands. But, by the saints, if you give this letter to the police as you did the other, not only she but your family also, someone near to you, will suffer. We will not fail as we did Wednesday. If you want your daughter back, go yourself, alone and without telling a soul, to Enrico Albano’s Saturday night at the twelfth hour. You must provide yourself with $10,000 in bills hidden in Saturday’s *Il Progresso Italiano*. In the back room you will see a man sitting alone at a table. He will have a red flower on his coat. You are to say, “A fine opera is *I Pagliacci*.” If he answers, “Not without Gennaro,” lay the newspaper down on the table. He will pick it up, leaving his own, the *Bolletino*. On the third page you will find written the place where your daughter has been left waiting for you. Go immediately and get her. But, by the God, if you have so much as the shadow of the police near Enrico’s your daughter will be sent to you in a box that night. Do not fear to come.
