We pledge our word to deal fairly if you deal fairly. This is a last warning. Lest you shall forget we will show one other sign of our power tomorrow.

La Mano Nera.

===Chinese===
**尊贵的先生：** 令嫒在我们手中安然无恙。但是，圣徒在上，你若把这封信像上一封那样交给警察，那么遭殃的就不止她一个，还有你的家人——你身边的一个亲人。我们不会像星期三那样失手了。你若想要回女儿，就亲自去，独自一人，不告诉任何人，星期六夜里十二点整，到恩里科·阿尔巴诺（Enrico Albano）的店里。你必须自备一万美元（$10,000）钞票，藏在当天的《意大利进步报》（*Il Progresso Italiano*）里。你会在里屋看见一个人独坐桌旁。他外衣上会戴一朵红花。你就说：「《丑角》（*I Pagliacci*）是部好歌剧。」他若答「没有热纳罗可不行」，你就把报纸放在桌上。他会拿走报纸，留下他自己那份《博莱蒂诺》（*Bolletino*）。第三版上写着你女儿被留在何处等你。马上去把她接走。但是，上帝作证，恩里科店附近若是有警察的半点影子，当天夜里你女儿就会被装在一只箱子里送还给你。不要怕来。你若公平待我们，我们担保公平待你。这是最后一次警告。怕你忘了，明天我们将再显一回我们的手段。

黑手党（La Mano Nera）。

===Original===
The end of this ominous letter was gruesomely decorated with a skull and crossbones, a rough drawing of a dagger thrust through a bleeding heart, a coffin, and, under all, a huge black hand. There was no doubt about the type of letter that it was. It was such as have of late years become increasingly common in all our large cities, baffling the best detectives.

“You have not showed this to the police, I presume?” asked Kennedy.

“Naturally not.”

“Are you going Saturday night?”

“I am afraid to go and afraid to stay away,” was the reply, and the voice of the fifty-thousand-dollars-a-season tenor was as human as that of a five-dollar-a-week father, for at bottom all men, high or low, are one.

===Chinese===
这封凶信的末尾，画着叫人毛骨悚然的花样：一具骷髅加交叉的白骨，一幅信手涂抹的匕首刺穿滴血心脏的图样，一口棺材，而在这一切底下，是一只巨大的黑手。这是什么性质的信，已毫无疑义。近年来，这类信在我们所有大城市里越来越多，连最精干的侦探也一筹莫展。

「这封信您没有给警察看过吧，我猜？」肯尼迪问。

「当然没有。」

「星期六晚上您去吗？」

「去也怕，不去也怕。」他这样回答。这位一季演出费高达五万美元的男高音，嗓音听来同一个每周只挣五美元的父亲一样平常——归根到底，人不论高低贵贱，都是一样的。

===Original===
“ ‘We will not fail as we did Wednesday,’ ” reread Craig. “What does that mean?”

Gennaro fumbled in his pocketbook again, and at last drew forth a typewritten letter bearing the letterhead of the Leslie Laboratories, Incorporated.

===Chinese===
「『我们不会像星期三那样失手了』，」克雷格又读了一遍。「这话是什么意思？」

热纳罗又在皮夹子里摸索了半天，终于抽出一封打字机打的信，信头印着莱斯利实验所（Leslie Laboratories, Incorporated）的字样。

===Original===
“After I received the first threat,” explained Gennaro, “my wife and I went from our apartments at the hotel to her father’s, the banker Cesare, you know, who lives on Fifth Avenue. I gave the letter to the Italian Squad of the police. The next morning my father-in-law’s butler noticed something peculiar about the milk. He barely touched some of it to his tongue, and he has been violently ill ever since. I at once sent the milk to the laboratory of my friend Doctor Leslie to have it analysed. This letter shows what the household escaped.”

===Chinese===
「接到第一封恐吓信之后，」热纳罗解释道，「我和我妻子就从旅馆的公寓搬到了她父亲家——银行家切萨雷（Cesare），您知道的，就住在第五大道。那封信我交给了警察的意大利分队（Italian Squad）。第二天早上，我岳父的管家发觉牛奶有些异样。他只用舌尖碰了一点点，从此就一直大病不起。我立刻把牛奶送到我朋友莱斯利医生（Doctor Leslie）的实验室去化验。这封信写明了我们全家侥幸躲过了什么。」

===Original===
“My dear Gennaro,” read Kennedy. “The milk submitted to us for examination on the 10th inst. has been carefully analysed, and I beg to hand you herewith the result:

“Specific gravity 1.036 at 15 degrees Cent.
					
						
							Water
							84.60
							percent
						
						
							Casein
							3.49
							”
						
						
							Albumin
							.56
							”
						
						
							Globulin
							1.32
							”
						
						
							Lactose
							5.08
							”
						
						
							Ash
							.72
							”
						
						
							Fat
							3.42
							”
						
						
							Ricin
							1.19
							”
						
					
				
				

“Ricin is a new and little-known poison derived from the shell of the castor-oil bean. Professor Ehrlich states that one gram of the pure poison will kill 1,500,000 guinea pigs. Ricin was lately isolated by Professor Robert, of Rostock, but is seldom found except in an impure state, though still very deadly. It surpasses strychnine, prussic acid, and other commonly known drugs. I congratulate you and yours on escaping and shall of course respect your wishes absolutely regarding keeping secret this attempt on your life. Believe me,

“Very sincerely yours,

“C. W. Leslie.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的热纳罗：本月10日送交我们检验的牛奶，已经仔细化验完毕，谨将结果奉告如下：

「比重：15摄氏度时为1.036。
水分……84.60%
酪蛋白……3.49%
白蛋白…….56%
球蛋白……1.32%
乳糖……5.08%
灰分…….72%
脂肪……3.42%
蓖麻毒素（ricin）……1.19%

「蓖麻毒素是一种新颖而鲜为人知的毒药，取自蓖麻籽的外壳。埃利希教授（Professor Ehrlich）指出，一克纯品毒素足以杀死1,500,000只豚鼠。蓖麻毒素近来刚由罗斯托克（Rostock）的罗伯特教授（Professor Robert）分离出来，但除不纯状态外极少得见，虽则仍极其致命。其毒性凌驾于士的宁、氢氰酸及其他常见药物之上。恭喜您和您全家逃过此劫；至于为这次谋害您性命的行径保密一事，我当然绝对遵命。请相信我，

「您最真诚的，

「C·W·莱斯利（C. W. Leslie）。」

===Original===
As Kennedy handed the letter back, he remarked significantly: “I can see very readily why you don’t care to have the police figure in your case. It has got quite beyond ordinary police methods.”

“And tomorrow, too, they are going to give another sign of their power,” groaned Gennaro, sinking into the chair before his untasted food.

“You say you have left your hotel?” inquired Kennedy.

“Yes. My wife insisted that we would be more safely guarded at the residence of her father, the banker. But we are afraid even there since the poison attempt. So I have come here secretly to Luigi, my old friend Luigi, who is preparing food for us, and in a few minutes one of Cesare’s automobiles will be here, and I will take the food up to her﻿—sparing no expense or trouble. She is heartbroken. It will kill her, Professor Kennedy, if anything happens to our little Adelina.

“Ah, sir, I am not poor myself. A month’s salary at the opera-house, that is what they ask of me. Gladly would I give it, ten thousand dollars﻿—all, if they asked it, of my contract with Herr Schleppencour, the director. But the police﻿—bah!﻿—they are all for catching the villains. What good will it do me if they catch them and my little Adelina is returned to me dead? It is all very well for the Anglo-Saxon to talk of justice and the law, but I am﻿—what you call it?﻿—an emotional Latin. I want my little daughter﻿—and at any cost. Catch the villains afterward﻿—yes. I will pay double then to catch them so that they cannot blackmail me again. Only first I want my daughter back.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪把信递还回去，意味深长地说：「我很能明白，您为什么不愿意让警察在您的案子里插上一脚。它早已大大超出寻常警察的那一套办法了。」

「而且明天，他们还要再显一回威风呢。」热纳罗呻吟一声，颓然跌坐回那桌未动箸的饭菜前。

「您是说，你们已经离开旅馆了？」肯尼迪问。

「是的。我妻子坚持说，住到她父亲那位银行家的宅邸里更安全。可自打下毒那回，我们连那儿也怕了。所以我偷偷跑到路易吉这儿来——我的老朋友路易吉，他正在给我们备饭。过几分钟，切萨雷家的一辆汽车就到，我把饭给她带回去——不惜任何花费，不辞任何麻烦。她心都碎了。肯尼迪教授，我们的小阿代利娜要是有个三长两短，她会活不成的。

「啊，先生，我自己并不穷。他们要的，不过是我在歌剧院一个月的薪水。我乐意给，一万美元——哪怕他们要我把同总监施莱彭考尔先生（Herr Schleppencour）签的合同全数搭上，我也给。可是警察——呸！——他们一心只想抓那帮恶棍。要是人抓到了，我的小阿代利娜却死了还给我，那对我有什么好处？盎格鲁—撒克逊人大谈正义与法律，那自然好，可我是个——你们怎么说来着？——感情用事的拉丁人。我要我的小女儿——不惜任何代价。之后再抓恶棍——好啊。到那时我肯出双倍的钱去抓他们，叫他们再也没法敲诈我。只是先让我把女儿要回来。」

===Original===
“And your father-in-law?”

“My father-in-law, he has been among you long enough to be one of you. He has fought them. He has put up a sign in his banking-house, ‘No money paid on threats.’ But I say it is foolish. I do not know America as well as he, but I know this: the police never succeed﻿—the ransom is paid without their knowledge, and they very often take the credit. I say, pay first, then I will swear a righteous vendetta﻿—I will bring the dogs to justice with the money yet on them. Only show me how, show me how.”

===Chinese===
「那您的岳父呢？」

「我的岳父，他在你们中间待得够久，已经算是你们中的一员了。他同他们斗过。他在他的银行里挂出了一块牌子：『恐吓不给钱。』可我说这是愚蠢的。我不像他那么了解美国，可我知道一点：警察从来办不成事——赎金瞒着他们照付，功劳倒常常归了他们。我说，先付钱，然后我要发一场正义的仇杀（vendetta）——我要叫那帮狗东西连同身上的赃款一并捉拿归案。只求有人指点我该怎么办，该怎么办。」

===Original===
“First of all,” replied Kennedy, “I want you to answer one question, truthfully, without reservation, as to a friend. I am your friend, believe me. Is there any person, a relative or acquaintance of yourself or your wife or your father-in-law, whom you even have reason to suspect of being capable of extorting money from you in this way? I needn’t say that that is the experience of the district attorney’s office in the large majority of cases of this so-called Black Hand.”

“No,” replied the tenor without hesitation: “I know that, and I have thought about it. No, I can think of no one. I know you Americans often speak of the Black Hand as a myth coined originally by a newspaper writer. Perhaps it has no organisation. But, Professor Kennedy, to me it is no myth. What if the real Black Hand is any gang of criminals who choose to use that convenient name to extort money? Is it the less real? My daughter is gone!”

===Chinese===
「首先，」肯尼迪答道，「我要您回答一个问题，像对朋友一样，说实话，不保留。相信我，我是您的朋友。在您自己、您妻子或您岳父的亲戚熟人当中，可有任何一个人——哪怕您只是有一点点理由怀疑——干得出这种敲诈您钱财的事？不必我说，检察官办公室经办的这类所谓黑手党案子里，绝大多数都是这种情况。」

「没有。」男高音毫不迟疑地答道。「这我知道，我也琢磨过。没有，我想不出任何人。我知道你们美国人常说，黑手党本是报纸写手编造出来的神话。也许它根本没有什么组织。可是肯尼迪教授，对我而言它绝不是神话。倘若真正的黑手党就是随便哪伙借这个现成名号敲钱的罪犯，那又如何？它就不真实了吗？我的女儿没了！」

===Original===
“Exactly,” agreed Kennedy. “It is not a theory that confronts you. It is a hard, cold fact. I understand that perfectly. What is the address of this Albano’s?”

Luigi mentioned a number on Mulberry Street, and Kennedy made a note of it.

“It is a gambling saloon,” explained Luigi. “Albano is a Neapolitan, a Camorrista, one of my countrymen of whom I am thoroughly ashamed, Professor Kennedy.”

===Chinese===
「正是，」肯尼迪附和道。「眼下摆在您面前的不是什么理论，而是铁一般的冷酷事实。这一点我完全明白。这家阿尔巴诺的店，地址在哪儿？」

路易吉报了桑树街（Mulberry Street）上的一个门牌，肯尼迪记了下来。

「那是家赌场，」路易吉解释道。「阿尔巴诺是个那不勒斯人，卡莫拉党徒（Camorrista）——我的一个同胞，肯尼迪教授，我为他羞耻透了。」

===Original===
“Do you think this Albano had anything to do with the letter?”

Luigi shrugged his shoulders.

Just then a big limousine was heard outside. Luigi picked up a huge hamper that was placed in a corner of the room and, followed closely by Signor Gennaro, hurried down to it. As the tenor left us he grasped our hands in each of his.

“I have an idea in my mind,” said Craig simply. “I will try to think it out in detail tonight. Where can I find you tomorrow?”

“Come to me at the opera-house in the afternoon, or if you want me sooner at Mr. Cesare’s residence. Good night, and a thousand thanks to you, Professor Kennedy, and to you, also, Mr. Jameson. I trust you absolutely because Luigi trusts you.”

===Chinese===
「您觉得这个阿尔巴诺同这封信有关系吗？」

路易吉耸了耸肩。

正说着，外面传来一辆大型豪华轿车的声响。路易吉拎起放在屋角的一只大食盒，热纳罗先生紧随其后，匆匆下楼朝汽车走去。男高音离开我们时，用他的两只手分别握住了我们的手。

「我心里有个想法，」克雷格说得简简单单。「今晚我要把它细细想透。明天到哪儿找您？」

「下午到歌剧院来找我，急的话就到切萨雷先生府上。晚安，千恩万谢，肯尼迪教授，也谢谢您，詹姆森先生。你们二位我信得过，因为路易吉信得过你们。」

===Original===
We sat in the little dining-room until we heard the door of the limousine bang shut and the car shoot off with the rattle of the changing gears.

“One more question, Luigi,” said Craig as the door opened again. “I have never been on that block in Mulberry Street where this Albano’s is. Do you happen to know any of the shopkeepers on it or near it?”

“I have a cousin who has a drugstore on the corner below Albano’s, on the same side of the street.”

“Good! Do you think he would let me use his store for a few minutes Saturday night﻿—of course without any risk to himself!”

“I think I could arrange it.”

“Very well. Then tomorrow, say at nine in the morning, I will stop here, and we will all go over to see him. Good night, Luigi, and many, thanks for thinking of me in connection with this case. I’ve enjoyed Signor Gennaro’s singing often enough at the opera to want to render him this service, and I’m only too glad to be able to be of service to all honest Italians; that is, if I succeed in carrying out a plan I have in mind.”

===Chinese===
我们在小餐室里坐着，直到听见豪华轿车的门砰地关上，汽车换挡咔啦作响地飞驰而去。

「还有个问题，路易吉，」门再开时克雷格说。「阿尔巴诺店所在的那段桑树街，我从没去过。那街上或附近的店主，你可有认识的？」

「我有个堂兄弟，在阿尔巴诺下首的街角开了家药房，就在同一边街面上。」

「好！你看他星期六晚上能不能让我用他的店几分钟——当然，绝不叫他本人担半点风险！」

「这事我想我能安排。」

「很好。那么明天，就上午九点吧，我先到这儿，咱们一同去见他。晚安，路易吉，多谢你在这案子上想到了我。热纳罗先生的歌喉，我在歌剧院里听的次数够多了，正想报答他这一番；能替所有诚实的意大利人效点劳，我更是求之不得——当然啦，前提是我心里盘算的那个计划能办成。」

===Original===
A little before nine the following day Kennedy and I dropped into Luigi’s again. Kennedy was carrying a suitcase which he had taken over from his laboratory to our rooms the night before. Luigi was waiting for us, and without losing a minute we sallied forth.

By means of the tortuous twists of streets in old Greenwich village we came out at last on Bleecker Street and began walking east amid the hurly-burly of races of lower New York. We had not quite reached Mulberry Street when our attention was attracted by a large crowd on one of the busy corners, held back by a cordon of police who were endeavouring to keep the people moving with that burly good nature which the six-foot Irish policeman displays toward the five-foot burden-bearers of southern and eastern Europe who throng New York.

===Chinese===
第二天九点差一点，我和肯尼迪又踱进了路易吉的店。肯尼迪提着一只手提箱，是前一晚从实验室带回我们公寓去的。路易吉已等在那里，我们片刻不耽搁，当即出动。

我们在老格林尼治村曲里拐弯的街巷里穿来绕去，最后上到布利克街（Bleecker Street），便在纽约下城各族人等闹哄哄的漩涡里朝东走去。还没到桑树街，一个繁忙街角上的一大群人就把我们吸引住了：一圈警察拦着人群，连推带哄地赶他们往前走——那是六英尺高的爱尔兰警察，对待挤满纽约的南欧、东欧五英尺高的负重苦力们所特有的那种粗豪的好脾气。

===Original===
Apparently, we saw, as we edged up into the front of the crowd, here was a building whose whole front had literally been torn off and wrecked. The thick plate-glass of the windows was smashed to a mass of greenish splinters on the sidewalk, while the windows of the upper floors and for several houses down the block in either street were likewise broken. Some thick iron bars which had formerly protected the windows were now bent and twisted. A huge hole yawned in the floor inside the doorway, and peering in we could see the desks and chairs a tangled mass of kindling.

===Chinese===
我们挤到人群前排才看清：这里的整幢房子，门脸是真的被整个掀掉、炸塌了。窗上厚厚的平板玻璃，在人行道上炸成了一摊泛绿的碎片；楼上几层的窗户，连同两条街上往远处数好几户人家的窗户，也照样震碎了。原先护窗的几根粗铁条，如今弯的弯、扭的扭。进门内的地板上豁开一个大窟窿，探眼望去，桌子椅子已成了一堆纠缠不清的引火柴。

===Original===
“What’s the matter?” I inquired of an officer near me, displaying my reporter’s fire-line badge, more for its moral effect than in the hope of getting any real information in these days of enforced silence toward the press.

“Black Hand bomb,” was the laconic reply.

“Whew!” I whistled. “Anyone hurt?”

“They don’t usually kill anyone, do they?” asked the officer by way of reply to test my acquaintance with such things.

“No,” I admitted. “They destroy more property than lives. But did they get anyone this time? This must have been a thoroughly overloaded bomb, I should judge by the looks of things.”

“Came pretty close to it. The bank hadn’t any more than opened when, *bang!* went this gaspipe-and-dynamite thing. Crowd collected before the smoke had fairly cleared. Man who owns the bank was hurt, but not badly. Now come, beat it down to headquarters if you want to find out any more.﻿—You’ll find it printed on the pink slips﻿—the ‘squeal book’﻿—by this time. ’Gainst the rules for me to talk,” he added with a good-natured grin, then to the crowd: “G’wan, now. You’re blockin’ traffic. Keep movin’.”

===Chinese===
「出什么事了？」我问身旁的一名警官，一边出示我记者的火线采访证（fire-line badge）——如今这年头官方对报界讳莫如深，与其指望真能问出什么，不如说指望它那点震慑作用。

「黑手党炸弹。」对方回得干巴巴的。

「嚯！」我打了个呼哨。「伤着人没有？」

「这种炸弹一般不炸死人，对吧？」警官反问了一句，掂掂我对这类事懂多少。

「不炸死人，」我承认，「毁掉的财产比人命多。不过这回伤着人没有？看这架势，我料这颗炸弹装药装得太满了。」

「差一点儿。银行刚开门，这根煤气管子加炸药的家伙就『砰！』一声上了天。烟还没散利索，人就围上来了。银行老板伤了，不重。行了，还想知道什么，自己上总局打听去——这会儿该登在粉色单子上了，就是那本『报警簿』（squeal book）。跟我多说可坏了规矩。」他添了一句，和和气气地咧嘴一笑，又转向人群：「走吧走吧，你们堵着道了。别停下来。」

===Original===
I turned to Craig and Luigi. Their eyes were riveted on the big gilt sign, half broken, and all askew overhead. It read:

Ciro di Cesare & Co. Bankers

New York, Genoa, Naples, Rome, Palermo

“This is the reminder so that Gennaro and his father-in-law will not forget,” I gasped.

“Yes,” added Craig, pulling us away, “and Cesare himself is wounded, too. Perhaps that was for putting up the notice refusing to pay. Perhaps not. It’s a queer case﻿—they usually set the bombs off at night when no one is around. There must be more back of this than merely to scare Gennaro. It looks to me as if they were after Casare, too, first by poison, then by dynamite.”

===Chinese===
我转向克雷格和路易吉。两人的眼睛都死死钉在头顶那块断了一半、歪斜欲坠的大金字招牌上。招牌上写着：

奇罗·迪·切萨雷银行（Ciro di Cesare & Co. Bankers）

纽约、热那亚、那不勒斯、罗马、巴勒莫

「这就是那声『提醒』，好叫热纳罗和他岳父不敢忘。」我倒抽一口气说。

「是啊，」克雷格把我们拉开，补充道，「而且切萨雷本人也伤了。也许是算他挂出拒付告示的账，也许不是。这案子蹊跷——他们通常都是夜里没人时才引爆炸弹。这背后绝不止吓唬热纳罗这么简单。依我看，他们连切萨雷也要算计——先下毒，再动炸药。」

===Original===
We shouldered our way out through the crowd and went on until we came to Mulberry Street, pulsing with life. Down we went past the little shops, dodging the children, and making way for women with huge bundles of sweatshop clothing accurately balanced on their heads or hugged up under their capacious capes. Here was just one little colony of the hundreds of thousands of Italians﻿—a population larger than the Italian population of Rome﻿—of whose life the rest of New York knew and cared nothing.

At last we came to Albano’s little wine-shop, a dark, evil, malodorous place on the street level of a five-story, alleged “new-law” tenement. Without hesitation Kennedy entered, and we followed, acting the part of a slumming party. There were a few customers at this early hour, men out of employment and an inoffensive-looking lot, though of course they eyed us sharply. Albano himself proved to be a greasy, low-browed fellow who had a sort of cunning look. I could well imagine such a fellow spreading terror in the hearts of simple folk by merely pressing both temples with his thumbs and drawing his long bony forefinger under his throat﻿—the so-called Black Hand sign that has shut up many a witness in the middle of his testimony even in open court.

===Chinese===
我们从人堆里挤出去，继续走，来到生机涌动的桑树街。我们沿街下行，经过一间间小店，绕闪着孩子们，给那些头上稳稳顶着一摞摞血汗衣裳、或是把大包袱拢在宽松斗篷里的女人们让路。这只是成千上万意大利人聚居的一小块侨居地——人口比罗马城里的意大利人还多——而他们的生活，纽约其余地方的人既不了解，也漠不关心。

我们终于走到阿尔巴诺那间小酒铺——一间阴暗、邪气、气味呛人的底层铺面，开在一幢五层、号称「新法案」（new-law）的经济公寓楼下。肯尼迪毫不迟疑地走了进去，我们跟着，装成到贫民窟猎奇的一行人。这么早的时辰，店里只有寥寥几个客人，都是些失业的男人，样子倒不凶恶，不过照例拿眼睛狠狠打量我们。阿尔巴诺本人，原来是个油腻腻、额角低矮的家伙，带着一脸刁滑相。我完全可以想象，这种人只消用两个拇指按住太阳穴、再用瘦长的食指在喉咙底下一划，就足以把恐怖撒进淳朴百姓的心里——这就是所谓的黑手党暗号，在公开法庭上也叫多少证人话说到一半便闭了嘴。

===Original===
We pushed through to the low-ceilinged back room, which was empty, and sat down at a table. Over a bottle of Albano’s famous California “red ink” we sat silently. Kennedy was making a mental note of the place. In the middle of the ceiling was a single gas-burner with a big reflector over it. In the back wall of the room was a horizontal oblong window, barred, and with a sash that opened like a transom. The tables were dirty and the chairs rickety. The walls were bare and unfinished, with beams innocent of decoration. Altogether it was as unprepossessing a place as I had ever seen.

Apparently satisfied with his scrutiny, Kennedy got up to go, complimenting the proprietor on his wine. I could see that Kennedy had made up his mind as to his course of action.

===Chinese===
我们挤进那间天花板低矮的后屋——屋里空无一人——在一张桌旁坐下。就着一瓶阿尔巴诺出名的加州「红墨水」（red ink）酒，我们默默坐着。肯尼迪正把这里的一切默记在心。天花板正中是一只煤气喷口，上方罩着个大反光罩。屋子后墙上开着一扇横长的长方形窗，装着铁栏，窗扇像气窗一样开合。桌子脏兮兮，椅子晃晃悠悠。四壁光秃秃的没装修，房梁上不见一点修饰。总而言之，这是我所见过的最不起眼、最不讨人喜欢的地方。

端详得差不多了，肯尼迪似乎满意，起身要走，还恭维了老板几句酒。我看得出来，肯尼迪对自己下一步怎么走，已经拿定了主意。

===Original===
“How sordid crime really is,” he remarked as we walked on down the street. “Look at that place of Albano’s. I defy even the police news reporter on the *Star* to find any glamour in that.”

Our next stop was at the corner at the little store kept by the cousin of Luigi, who conducted us back of the partition where prescriptions were compounded, and found us chairs.

A hurried explanation from Luigi brought a cloud to the open face of the druggist, as if he hesitated to lay himself and his little fortune open to the blackmailers. Kennedy saw it and interrupted.

===Chinese===
「犯罪这东西，实际上多寒碜啊，」我们沿街走下去时他说。「瞧瞧阿尔巴诺那地方。我倒要看看，连《明星报》（*Star*）跑警察新闻的记者，谁能从那儿找出半点罗曼蒂克来。」

我们的下一站，是街角路易吉堂兄弟开的那家小店。他把我们领到配药间的隔断后面，搬来椅子。

路易吉匆匆一番解释，在那位药剂师坦率的脸上蒙起一层阴云，他像是犹豫了，怕把自己和自己那点薄产暴露在勒索者面前。肯尼迪看在眼里，插了话。

===Original===
“All that I wish to do,” he said, “is to put in a little instrument here and use it tonight for a few minutes. Indeed, there will be no risk to you, Vincenzo. Secrecy is what I desire, and no one will ever know about it.”

Vincenzo was at length convinced, and Craig opened his suitcase. There was little in it except several coils of insulated wire; some tools, a couple of packages wrapped up, and a couple of pairs of overalls. In a moment Kennedy had donned overalls and was smearing dirt and grease over his face and hands. Under his direction I did the same.

===Chinese===
「我要做的，」他说，「不过是在这儿装一个小小的器具，今晚用上几分钟。真的，文森佐（Vincenzo），你不会冒任何风险。我要的就是保密，这事永远没人会知道。」

文森佐终于被说服了。克雷格打开手提箱，里面除了几卷绝缘电线、一些工具、两个包好的小包和两条工装裤，几乎没有别的东西。转眼间肯尼迪已套上工装裤，往脸上、手上抹起污泥和油垢。在他的指点下，我也如法炮制。

===Original===
Taking the bag of tools, the wire, and one of the small packages, we went out on the street and then up through the dark and ill-ventilated hall of the tenement. Halfway up a woman stopped us suspiciously.

“Telephone company,” said Craig curtly. “Here’s permission from the owner of the house to string wires across the roof.”

He pulled an old letter out of his pocket, but as it was too dark to read even if the woman had cared to do so, we went on up as he had expected, unmolested. At last we came to the roof, where there were some children at play a couple of houses down from us.

Kennedy began by dropping two strands of wire down to the ground in the back yard behind Vincenzo’s shop. Then he proceeded to lay two wires along the edge of the roof.

===Chinese===
我们带上工具袋、电线和一个小包，走到街上，钻进经济公寓昏暗不通气的门厅往上走。上到半路，一个女人满腹狐疑地拦住了我们。

「电话公司的，」克雷格简短地说。「这是房主同意在屋顶架线的许可。」

他从口袋里掏出一封旧信，可天黑得根本没法读——就算那女人真想看也没用——于是不出他所料，我们一路畅通无阻地上了顶。最后我们到了屋顶，隔两户人家的地方有些孩子在玩耍。

肯尼迪先把两股电线从文森佐店铺后面的后院放到地上，接着沿着屋顶檐口布起两根线来。

===Original===
We had worked only a little while when the children began to collect. However, Kennedy kept right on until we reached the tenement next to that in which Albano’s shop was.

“Walter,” he whispered, “just get the children away for a minute now.”

“Look here, you kids,” I yelled, “some of you will fall off if you get so close to the edge of the roof. Keep back.”

It had no effect. Apparently they looked not a bit frightened at the dizzy mass of clotheslines below us.

“Say, is there a candy-store on this block?” I asked in desperation.

“Yes, sir,” came the chorus.

“Who’ll go down and get me a bottle of ginger ale?” I asked.

A chorus of voices and glittering eyes was the answer. They all would. I took a half-dollar from my pocket and gave it to the oldest.

“All right now, hustle along, and divide the change.”

===Chinese===
我们干了没多大工夫，孩子们就围拢过来了。肯尼迪却不管不顾，一直干到紧挨阿尔巴诺店铺的那幢公寓屋顶。

「沃尔特，」他悄声说，「现在把孩子们支开一会儿。」

「喂，你们这帮小家伙，」我大喊，「再挨着屋檐边站，有人要摔下去的。退后。」

毫无用处。显然，对我们脚下那令人晕眩的一大片晾衣绳，他们一点不怕。

「喂，这条街上有糖果店吗？」我急中生智地问。

「有，先生。」齐声应道。

「谁下去给我买瓶姜汁汽水来？」我问。

一片嚷嚷声和一双双发亮的眼睛就是回答。个个都愿意。我从口袋里掏出半美元，给了最大的那个。

「好了，快去快回，找的零钱你们分了。」

===Original===
With the scamper of many feet they were gone, and we were alone. Kennedy had now reached Albano’s, and as soon as the last head had disappeared below the scuttle of the roof he dropped two long strands down into the back yard, as he had done at Vincenzo’s.

I started to go back, but he stopped me.

“Oh, that will never do,” he said. “The kids will see that the wires end here. I must carry them on several houses farther as a blind and trust to luck that they don’t see the wires leading down below.”

===Chinese===
一阵劈劈啪啪的脚步声，他们跑没了影，只剩下我们俩。肯尼迪这时已到了阿尔巴诺那户的屋顶，等最后一颗小脑袋从天窗口一消失，他就把两股长线放进后院，同在文森佐那儿一样。

我正要往回走，他拦住了我。

「哦，那可不行，」他说。「孩子们会看见线到这儿就断了。我必须把它们再往前架过几户人家，做个幌子，再听天由命，指望没人瞧见往下引的线。」

===Original===
We were several houses down, still putting up wires when the crowd came shouting back, sticky with cheap trust-made candy and black with East Side chocolate. We opened the ginger ale and forced ourselves to drink it so as to excite no suspicion, then a few minutes later descended the stairs of the tenement, coming out just above Albano’s.

I was wondering how Kennedy was going to get into Albano’s again without exciting suspicion. He solved it neatly.

“Now, Walter, do you think you could stand another dip into that red ink of Albano’s?”

I said I might in the interests of science and justice﻿—not otherwise.

===Chinese===
我们又过去几户人家，还在架线，那群孩子嚷嚷着跑回来了，嘴里粘着廉价的托拉斯糖果，脸上黑糊糊全是东区的巧克力。我们打开姜汁汽水，硬着头皮灌了下去，免得惹人起疑，几分钟后便从公寓楼梯下来，出来的地方恰好在阿尔巴诺店的上首。

我正在琢磨，肯尼迪怎样才能不惹怀疑地再进一次阿尔巴诺的店。他利利索索地解决了。

「喂，沃尔特，你说你还能不能再受一回阿尔巴诺那『红墨水』的灌？」

我说，看在科学与正义的份上或许可以——否则免谈。

===Original===
“Well, your face is sufficiently dirty,” he commented, “so that with the overalls you don’t look very much as you did the first time you went in. I don’t think they will recognise you. Do I look pretty good?”

“You look like a coal-heaver out of a job,” I said. “I can scarcely restrain my admiration.”

“All right. Then take this little glass bottle. Go into the back room and order something cheap, in keeping with your looks. Then when you are all alone break the bottle. It is full of gas drippings. Your nose will dictate what to do next. Just tell the proprietor you saw the gas company’s wagon on the next block and come up here and tell me.”

===Chinese===
「嗯，你这脸够脏了，」他评点道，「配上工装裤，跟你头一回进去时已不大像一个人了。我想他们认不出你。我这样子还行吧？」

「你活像个失业的运煤工，」我说。「我简直抑制不住我的钦佩。」

「好。那就拿着这只小玻璃瓶。进后屋，点样便宜的东西，跟你的扮相相称。等屋里只剩你一人，就把瓶子砸碎。里面装满了煤气残液（gas drippings）。接下来怎么办，你的鼻子自会吩咐你。只管对老板说，你在下一个街区看见了煤气公司的车，然后上这儿来告诉我。」

===Original===
I entered. There was a sinister-looking man, with a sort of unscrupulous intelligence, writing at a table. As he wrote and puffed at his cigar, I noticed a scar on his face, a deep furrow running from the lobe of his ear to his mouth. That, I knew, was a brand set upon him by the Camorra. I sat and smoked and sipped slowly for several minutes, cursing him inwardly more for his presence than for his evident look of the “*mala vita*.” At last he went out to ask the barkeeper for a stamp.

Quickly I tiptoed over to another corner of the room and ground the little bottle under my heel. Then I resumed my seat. The odour that pervaded the room was sickening.

The sinister-looking man with the scar came in again and sniffed. I sniffed. Then the proprietor came in and sniffed.

===Chinese===
我走了进去。一个相貌凶险的男人正伏在桌前写字，透着一种不择手段的精明。他一边写一边喷着雪茄烟，我注意到他脸上一道疤，一道从耳垂直划到嘴边的深沟。我知道，那是卡莫拉（Camorra）烙在他身上的印记。我坐着抽烟，慢慢啜饮，有好几分钟，心里暗暗咒他——倒不为他那满脸明摆着的「*mala vita*」（意大利语：黑道生涯）相，更为他偏偏守在这儿碍事。他终于出去向酒保借邮票了。

我赶紧蹑手蹑脚走到屋子另一角，用鞋跟碾碎了小瓶，然后回到座位上。那股顷刻弥漫全屋的气味，熏得人直恶心。

那个带疤的凶险男人又进来，抽了抽鼻子。我也抽鼻子。接着老板进来，也抽鼻子。

===Original===
“Say,” I said in the toughest voice I could assume, “you got a leak. Wait. I seen the gas company wagon on the next block when I came in. I’ll get the man.”

I dashed out and hurried up the street to the place where Kennedy was waiting impatiently. Rattling his tools, he followed me with apparent reluctance.

As he entered the wine-shop he snorted, after the manner of gasmen, “Where’s de leak?”

“You find-a da leak,” grunted Albano. “What-a you get-a you pay for? You want-a me do your work?”

“Well, half a dozen o’ you wops get out o’ here, that’s all. D’youse all wanter be blown ter pieces wid dem pipes and cigarettes? Clear out,” growled Kennedy.

===Chinese===
「喂，」我拿出装得出的最冲的嗓音说，「漏气了。等等。我进来那会儿，瞧见煤气公司的车就在下一个街区。我去把人叫来。」

我一阵风冲出去，沿街跑到肯尼迪等得不耐烦的地方。他把工具弄得哗啦响，老大不情愿似的跟我走。

他一进酒铺，就摆出煤气工人的做派哼了一声：「哪儿漏的？」

「漏-a 在哪儿，你自家找，」阿尔巴诺咕哝道。「你付-a 的什么钱，得-a 什么货。还要我-a 替你干活？」

「行了，你们这帮意大利佬（wops），五六七个统统给我滚出去。是不是还想跟这些管子啊、烟卷啊一块儿给炸成碎片儿？滚。」肯尼迪粗声喝道。

===Original===
They retreated precipitately, and Craig hastily opened his bag of tools.

“Quick, Walter, shut the door and hold it,” exclaimed Craig, working rapidly. He unwrapped a little package and took out a round, flat disc-like thing of black vulcanised rubber. Jumping up on a table, he fixed it to the top of the reflector over the gas-jet.

“Can you see that from the floor, Walter?” he asked under his breath.

“No,” I replied, “not even when I know it is there.”

Then he attached a couple of wires to it and led them across the ceiling toward the window, concealing them carefully by sticking them in the shadow of a beam. At the window he quickly attached the wires to the two that were dangling down from the roof and shoved them around out of sight.

“We’ll have to trust that no one sees them,” he said. “That’s the best I can do at such short notice. I never saw a room so bare as this, anyway. There isn’t another place I could put that thing without its being seen.”

===Chinese===
众人抱头鼠窜，克雷格赶忙打开工具袋。

「快，沃尔特，关门，顶住！」克雷格一边飞快动手一边喊。他解开一个小包，取出一件圆扁盘状的黑色硫化橡胶（vulcanised rubber）玩意儿，跳上桌子，把它装到煤气喷口上方反光罩的顶端。

「从地板上瞧得见它吗，沃尔特？」他压着嗓子问。

「瞧不见，」我答道，「就算知道它在那儿也瞧不见。」

接着他往上面接了两根线，顺着天花板引向窗户，仔细把它们藏进一根房梁的阴影里。到了窗前，他飞快地把线接到从屋顶垂下来的那两股上，又拨到看不见的地方。

「只好指望没人瞧见它们了，」他说。「时间这么仓促，我能做到的就这些。反正我还从没见过这么光秃秃的屋子。换了别处，那东西搁哪儿都得露眼。」

===Original===
We gathered up the broken glass of the gas drippings bottle, and I opened the door.

“It’s all right, now,” said Craig, sauntering out before the bar. “Only de next time you has anyt’ing de matter call de company up. I ain’t supposed to do dis wit’out orders, see?”

A moment later I followed, glad to get out of the oppressive atmosphere, and joined him in the back of Vincenzo’s drugstore, where he was again at work. As there was no back window there, it was quite a job to lead the wires around the outside from the back yard and in at a side window. It was at last done, however, without exciting suspicion, and Kennedy attached them to an oblong box of weathered oak and a pair of specially constructed dry batteries.

“Now,” said Craig, as we washed off the stains of work and stowed the overalls back in the suitcase, “that is done to my satisfaction. I can tell Gennaro to go ahead safely now and meet the Black-Handers.”

===Chinese===
我们收拾起煤气残液瓶的碎玻璃，我打开了门。

「好啦，没事儿了，」克雷格说，慢悠悠踱到吧台前。「只是下回再有什么毛病，打电话给公司叫人。没上头吩咐，按说俺可不兴白干这活儿，懂吧？」

过了一刻我也跟了出来，逃出那股压抑的空气，松了口气，到文森佐药房后间与他会合——他又干上了活。那儿没有后窗，把电线从后院绕过外墙、再从一扇侧窗引进来，颇费了一番工夫。好在终于弄妥，没惹人起疑，肯尼迪把它们接上一只风蚀橡木（weathered oak）的长方盒和一对特制的干电池。

「好了，」克雷格说。我们洗掉干活留下的污迹，把工装裤塞回手提箱。「这活我干得称心。现在我可以告诉热纳罗放心去会那帮黑手党徒了。」

===Original===
From Vincenzo’s we walked over toward Centre Street, where Kennedy and I left Luigi to return to his restaurant, with instructions to be at Vincenzo’s at half-past eleven that night.

We turned into the new police headquarters and went down the long corridor to the Italian Bureau. Kennedy sent in his card to Lieutenant Giuseppe in charge, and we were quickly admitted. The lieutenant was a short, full-faced, fleshy Italian, with lightish hair and eyes that were apparently dull, until you suddenly discovered that that was merely a cover to their really restless way of taking in everything and fixing the impressions on his mind, as if on a sensitive plate.

===Chinese===
离了文森佐的店，我们朝中央街走去。到那儿后，我和肯尼迪让路易吉回他自己的餐馆去，吩咐他当晚十一点半到文森佐店里等候。

我们拐进新警察总局，沿着长长的走廊走到意大利科（Italian Bureau）。肯尼迪递进名片，求见主管的朱塞佩中尉（Lieutenant Giuseppe），很快便获准进去。这位中尉是个身材矮小、面盘宽阔、体态丰腴的意大利人，头发浅淡，那双眼睛看上去呆滞无神——直到你猛然发觉，那不过是个幌子：它们其实一刻不停地扫视着一切，把种种印象像感光底片似的铭刻在他脑海里。

===Original===
“I want to talk about the Gennaro case,” began Craig. “I may add that I have been rather closely associated with Inspector O’Connor of the Central Office on a number of cases, so that I think we can trust each other. Would you mind telling me what you know about it if I promise you that I, too, have something to reveal?”

===Chinese===
「我想谈谈热纳罗的案子，」克雷格开门见山。「我可以补一句：中央办公厅的奥康纳警督那儿，我在好几起案子里都同他打过相当多的交道，所以我想，咱们尽可以彼此信任。要是我保证自己也有一点东西要奉告，您可愿意把您知道的讲给我听？」

===Original===
The lieutenant leaned back and watched Kennedy closely without seeming to do so. “When I was in Italy last year,” he replied at length, “I did a good deal of work in tracing up some Camorra suspects. I had a tip about some of them to look up their records﻿—I needn’t say where it came from, but it was a good one. Much of the evidence against some of those fellows who are being tried at Viterbo was gathered by the Carabinieri as a result of hints that I was able to give them﻿—clues that were furnished to me here in America from the source I speak of. I suppose there is really no need to conceal it, though. The original tip came from a certain banker here in New York.”

===Chinese===
中尉往后一靠，不动声色地紧盯着肯尼迪。「去年我在意大利的时候，」他终于开口说，「为追查几名卡莫拉嫌犯做了大量工作。我得到过关于其中一些人的线报，要查他们的底——线报哪儿来的我就不必说了，总之相当可靠。眼下在维泰博（Viterbo）受审的那伙人中，有一部分罪证，就是卡宾枪骑兵（Carabinieri）根据我提供的线索搜集的——而那些线索，又是我在美洲这里从我说起的那个来源得来的。不过，我想其实也用不着隐瞒：最初的线报，出自纽约这里某位银行家。」

===Original===
“I can guess who it was,” nodded Craig.

“Then, as you know, this banker is a fighter. He is the man who organised the White Hand﻿—an organisation which is trying to rid the Italian population of the Black Hand. His society had a lot of evidence regarding former members of both the Camorra in Naples and the Mafia in Sicily, as well as the Black Hand gangs in New York, Chicago, and other cities. Well, Cesare, as you know, is Gennaro’s father-in-law.

“While I was in Naples looking up the record of a certain criminal I heard of a peculiar murder committed some years ago. There was an honest old music master who apparently lived the quietest and most harmless of lives. But it became known that he was supported by Cesare and had received handsome presents of money from him. The old man was, as you may have guessed, the first music teacher of Gennaro, the man who discovered him. One might have been at a loss to see how he could have an enemy, but there was one who coveted his small fortune. One day he was stabbed and robbed. His murderer ran out into the street, crying out that the poor man had been killed. Naturally a crowd rushed up in a moment, for it was in the middle of the day. Before the injured man could make it understood who had struck him the assassin was down the street and lost in the maze of old Naples where he well knew the houses of his friends who would hide him. The man who is known to have committed that crime﻿—Francesco Paoli﻿—escaped to New York. We are looking for him today. He is a clever man, far above the average﻿—son of a doctor in a town a few miles from Naples, went to the university, was expelled for some mad prank﻿—in short, he was the black sheep of the family. Of course over here he is too highborn to work with his hands on a railroad or in a trench, and not educated enough to work at anything else. So he has been preying on his more industrious countrymen﻿—a typical case of a man living by his wits with no visible means of support.

“Now I don’t mind telling you in strict confidence,” continued the lieutenant, “that it’s my theory that old Cesare has seen Paoli here, knew he was wanted for that murder of the old music master, and gave me the tip to look up his record. At any rate Paoli disappeared right after I returned from Italy, and we haven’t been able to locate him since. He must have found out in some way that the tip to look him up had been given by the White Hand. He had been a Camorrista, in Italy, and had many ways of getting information here in America.”

===Chinese===
「是谁我猜得到。」克雷格点了点头。

「那么，您也知道，这位银行家是个斗士。组织起『白手会』（White Hand）的就是他——那是一个力图把黑手党从意大利侨民中清除出去的团体。他那个会里掌握着大量罪证，既关乎那不勒斯的卡莫拉和西西里的玛菲亚（Mafia）的旧党徒，也牵涉纽约、芝加哥等地的黑手党团伙。嗯，您知道，切萨雷正是热纳罗的岳父。

「我在那不勒斯查一名罪犯的案底时，听说多年前发生过一桩离奇的凶案。被害人是一位正直的老音乐教师，表面上过着最安静、最与世无争的日子。可是风声走漏出来，说他是受切萨雷资助的，还收过他一笔笔可观的钱财。那老头，您大概猜到了，正是热纳罗的第一位音乐老师，是发现热纳罗的伯乐。这样的人怎么会有仇家，本叫人想不通，可偏偏有人觊觎他那点薄财。有一天，他被人捅了，遭了抢。凶手窜到街上，大喊可怜的人被杀了。眨眼间人群自然就围了上来，因为那是大白天。伤者还没来得及让人明白是谁捅的他，凶手已经跑下街去，消失在老那不勒斯蛛网似的街巷里——那里面哪家藏着肯收留他的朋友，他熟得很。已知犯了那桩罪的这个人——弗朗切斯科·保利（Francesco Paoli）——逃到了纽约。我们到今天还在通缉他。此人精明得很，远在常人之上——那不勒斯城外几英里一个小镇上医生的儿子，上过大学，因为一桩疯狂的恶作剧被开除——总之，是全家的害群之马。到了这边，他自然觉得出身高贵，不肯下力气去铁路上、沟渠里做工，可要说干别的，又没受过那份教育。于是他专吃勤俭同胞的血汗——一个靠小聪明过活、没有任何正当营生的典型。

「现在我不妨私下告诉您，」中尉接着说，「照我的推断，老切萨雷在这儿见过保利，知道他因谋杀老音乐教师被通缉，这才给了我那条查他底细的线报。不管怎样，我一从意大利回来，保利就不见了，从此我们再也没能找到他。他多半是想法子打听到了，查他的线报是白手会递的。他在意大利当过卡莫拉党徒，到了美洲这边，搞消息的门路多得很。」

===Original===
He paused, and balanced a piece of cardboard in his hand.

“It is my theory of this case that if we could locate this Paoli we could solve the kidnapping of little Adelina Gennaro very quickly. That’s his picture.”

Kennedy and I bent over to look at it, and I started in surprise. It was my evil-looking friend with the scar on his cheek.

“Well,” said Craig, quietly handing back the card, “whether or not he is the man, I know where we can catch the kidnappers tonight, Lieutenant.”

It was Giuseppe’s turn to show surprise now.

“With your assistance I’ll get this man and the whole gang tonight,” explained Craig, rapidly sketching over his plan and concealing just enough to make sure that no matter how anxious the lieutenant was to get the credit he could not spoil the affair by premature interference.

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，手里颠着一张硬纸板。

「关于本案，我的推断是：只要找到这个保利，小阿代利娜·热纳罗遭绑架一案很快便可告破。这就是他的照片。」

我和肯尼迪俯身去看，我惊得猛地一震。那正是我那位面目凶恶、脸上带疤的朋友。

「好吧，」克雷格不动声色地把卡片递回去，「不管他是不是此人，中尉，我知道今晚能在哪儿抓住那帮绑匪。」

这回轮到朱塞佩吃惊了。

「有您协助，今晚我就能拿到这个人，连同整个团伙。」克雷格解释着，飞快地把计划勾了个大概，又恰到好处地藏了几手——不管中尉抢功的心有多切，也决不至于插手过早、坏了大事。

===Original===
The final arrangement was that four of the best men of the squad were to hide in a vacant store across from Vincenzo’s early in the evening, long before anyone was watching. The signal for them to appear was to be the extinguishing of the lights behind the coloured bottles in the druggist’s window. A taxicab was to be kept waiting at headquarters at the same time with three other good men ready to start for a given address the moment the alarm was given over the telephone.

===Chinese===
最后商定：当晚早些时候，趁还没人盯梢，分队里四名最得力的人手先埋伏进文森佐店对面的一间空铺子。给他们出动的信号，是熄掉药店橱窗里彩色药瓶后面的灯。与此同时，总局里备好一辆出租车待命，车上另坐三名好手，电话里警报一响，便直奔指定的地址。

===Original===
We found Gennaro awaiting us with the greatest anxiety at the opera-house. The bomb at Cesare’s had been the last straw. Gennaro had already drawn from his bank ten crisp one-thousand-dollar bills, and already had a copy of *Il Progresso* in which he had hidden the money between the sheets.

“Mr. Kennedy,” he said, “I am going to meet them tonight. They may kill me. See, I have provided myself with a pistol﻿—I shall fight, too, if necessary for my little Adelina. But if it is only money they want, they shall have it.”

“One thing I want to say,” began Kennedy.

===Chinese===
我们在歌剧院找到热纳罗时，他正心急如焚。切萨雷银行那一炸，是压垮他的最后一根稻草。热纳罗已从银行取出了十张崭新的千元大钞，也备好了一份《意大利进步报》，钱就夹在报页中间。

「肯尼迪先生，」他说，「今晚我要去见他们。他们也许会杀了我。瞧，我给自己备了一支手枪——为了我的小阿代利娜，必要时我也要拼一拼。可要是他们要的只是钱，就让他们拿去。」

「有句话我要说。」肯尼迪开了口。

===Original===
“No, no, no!” cried the tenor. “I will go﻿—you shall not stop me.”

“I don’t wish to stop you,” Craig reassured him. “But one thing﻿—do exactly as I tell you, and I swear not a hair of the child’s head will be injured and we will get the blackmailers, too.”

“How?” eagerly asked Gennaro. “What do you want me to do?”

“All I want you to do is to go to Albano’s at the appointed time. Sit down in the back room. Get into conversation with them, and, above all, Signor, as soon as you get the copy of the *Bolletino* turn to the third page, pretend not to be able to read the address. Ask the man to read it. Then repeat it after him. Pretend to be overjoyed. Offer to set up wine for the whole crowd. Just a few minutes, that is all I ask, and I will guarantee that you will be the happiest man in New York tomorrow.”

Gennaro’s eyes filled with tears as he grasped Kennedy’s hand. “That is better than having the whole police force back of me,” he said. “I shall never forget, never forget.”

===Chinese===
「不，不，不！」男高音嚷道。「我要去——你们拦不住我。」

「我并不想拦您，」克雷格安抚他，「但有一条——一切照我说的去做。我担保，孩子一根头发都不会伤着，咱们还能把那帮勒索犯一并拿下。」

「怎么个办法？」热纳罗急切地问。「您要我做什么？」

「我要您做的只有这些：按约定时间到阿尔巴诺店里去，在后屋坐下，同他们搭上话。还有顶要紧的一条，先生，您一拿到那份《博莱蒂诺》，就翻到第三版，装作认不出上面写的地址，请那个人念给您听，再跟着他复述一遍。然后装出喜出望外的样子，提出请全场的人喝酒。只要几分钟，我只要这几分钟，我担保明天您就是全纽约最幸福的人。」

热纳罗握住肯尼迪的手，眼里噙满了泪水。「这比让全体警察给我撑腰还强，」他说。「我永远忘不了，永远忘不了。」

===Original===
As we went out Kennedy remarked: “You can’t blame them for keeping their troubles to themselves. Here we send a police officer over to Italy to look up the records of some of the worst suspects. He loses his life. Another takes his place. Then after he gets back he is set to work on the mere clerical routine of translating them. One of his associates is reduced in rank. And so what does it come to? Hundreds of records have become useless because the three years within which the criminals could be deported have elapsed with nothing done. Intelligent, isn’t it? I believe it has been established that all but about fifty of seven hundred known Italian suspects are still at large, mostly in this city. And the rest of the Italian population is guarded from them by a squad of police in number scarcely one-thirtieth of the number of known criminals. No, it’s our fault if the Black Hand thrives.”

===Chinese===
出门时肯尼迪议论道：「也怪不得他们有苦自己咽。瞧我们这边：派一名警官去意大利调几名最凶嫌犯的案底，他把命丢了。另一个人接替上去。等他回来，却又被派去干翻译案底那种抄抄写写的例行公事。他的一名同僚还被降了级。结果呢？成百上千份案底全成了废纸，因为能把罪犯递解出境的三年限期一晃而过，什么事也没办成。聪明吧？据我了解已经查明，七百名挂了号的意大利嫌犯，除了大约五十人，其余至今逍遥法外，多半就在本城。而其余的意大利侨民靠什么防着他们？一支警察分队，人数还不到已知罪犯的三十分之一。不，黑手党如今这么兴旺，全怪我们自己。」

===Original===
We had been standing on the corner of Broadway, waiting for a car.

“Now, Walter, don’t forget. Meet me at the Bleecker Street station of the subway at eleven-thirty. I’m off to the university. I have some very important experiments with phosphorescent salts that I want to finish today.”

“What has that to do with the case?” I asked, mystified.

“Nothing,” replied Craig. “I didn’t say it had. At eleven-thirty, don’t forget. By George, though, that Paoli must be a clever one﻿—think of his knowing about ricin. I only heard of it myself recently. Well, here’s my car. Goodbye.”

Craig swung aboard an Amsterdam Avenue car, leaving me to kill eight nervous hours of my weekly day of rest from the *Star*.

===Chinese===
我们一直站在百老汇的街角等电车。

「听好，沃尔特，别忘了。十一点半，地铁布利克街站碰头。我去一趟大学。有几项磷光盐（phosphorescent salts）的重要实验，我想今天赶完。」

「这跟案子有什么相干？」我摸不着头脑地问。

「不相干，」克雷格答道，「我也没说相干。十一点半，别忘了。不过，天哪，那个保利准是个厉害角色——想想看，他居然知道蓖麻毒素。这东西我自己也是最近才听说。好了，车来了。再见。」

克雷格一跃登上阿姆斯特丹大道（Amsterdam Avenue）的电车，撇下我独自去熬那难挨的八个钟头——那天本是我不去《明星报》上班的每周休息日。

===Original===
They passed at length, and at precisely the appointed time Kennedy and I met. With suppressed excitement, at least on my part, we walked over to Vincenzo’s. At night this section of the city was indeed a black enigma. The lights in the shops where olive oil, fruit, and other things were sold, were winking out one by one; here and there strains of music floated out of wine-shops, and little groups lingered on corners conversing in animated sentences. We passed Albano’s on the other side of the street, being careful not to look at it too closely, for several men were hanging idly about﻿—pickets, apparently, with some secret code that would instantly have spread far and wide the news of any alarming action.

At the corner we crossed and looked in Vincenzo’s window a moment, casting a furtive glance across the street at the dark empty store where the police must be hiding. Then we went in and casually sauntered back of the partition. Luigi was there already. There were several customers still in the store, however, and therefore we had to sit in silence while Vincenzo quickly finished a prescription and waited on the last one.

At last the doors were locked and the lights lowered, all except those in the windows which were to serve as signals.

===Chinese===
那几个钟头总算熬了过去，到了约定的时间，我和肯尼迪准时碰了头，带着按捺不住的兴奋——至少我是如此——朝文森佐的店走去。夜里，城里这一带真是一座黑黢黢的谜。卖橄榄油、水果和各色杂货的小店，灯火一盏接一盏地眨眼熄灭；酒铺里不时飘出几段音乐，街角上三三两两的人还在用抑扬顿挫的句子谈着话。我们从街对面经过阿尔巴诺的店，小心着不朝它多看，因为店门口有几个男人懒散地晃来晃去——分明是放哨的，握着某种暗号，稍有风吹草动，消息顷刻就会传得四面八方。

到了街角，我们过了马路，在文森佐的橱窗前站了片刻，偷偷朝对街那间黑黢黢的空铺子扫了一眼——警察准是埋伏在那儿。然后我们走进店，装作闲逛地踱到隔断后面。路易吉已经到了。店里却还有几位顾客，我们只好默坐着，等文森佐快快配完一张药方、打发走最后一位。

最后，门上了锁，灯也压暗了，唯独橱窗里充作信号的那几盏留着。

===Original===
“Ten minutes to twelve,” said Kennedy, placing the oblong box on the table. “Gennaro will be going in soon. Let us try this machine now and see if it works. If the wires have been cut since we put them up this morning Gennaro will have to take his chances alone.”

Kennedy reached over and with a light movement of his forefinger touched a switch.

Instantly a babel of voices filled the store, all talking at once, rapidly and loudly. Here and there we could distinguish a snatch of conversation, a word, a phrase, now and then even a whole sentence above the rest. There was the clink of glasses. I could hear the rattle of dice on a bare table, and an oath. A cork popped. Somebody scratched a match.

===Chinese===
「差十分十二点，」肯尼迪说着，把那只长方木盒摆上桌。「热纳罗快进店了。咱们现在就试试这台机器，看灵不灵。要是早上架的线被人掐断了，热纳罗就只好孤身去碰运气了。」

肯尼迪探过身去，食指轻轻一触，按下了开关。

刹那间，一片七嘴八舌的嘈杂人声灌满了店铺——全在同时说话，又快又响。断断续续地，我们能分辨出只言片语：一个词、一个短语，偶尔还有一句盖过众声的整话。有玻璃杯的碰响。我听得见骰子在光桌板上哗啦啦地滚，还有一声咒骂。一只软木塞「砰」地一响。有人划了根火柴。

===Original===
We sat bewildered, looking at Kennedy for an explanation.

“Imagine that you are sitting at a table in Albano’s back room,” was all he said. “This is what you would be hearing. This is my ‘electric ear’﻿—in other words the dictograph, used, I am told, by the Secret Service of the United States. Wait, in a moment you will hear Gennaro come in. Luigi and Vincenzo, translate what you hear. My knowledge of Italian is pretty rusty.”

“Can they hear us?” whispered Luigi in an awestruck whisper.

Craig laughed. “No, not yet. But I have only to touch this other switch, and I could produce an effect in that room that would rival the famous writing on Belshazzar’s wall﻿—only it would be a voice from the wall instead of writing.”

===Chinese===
我们坐着发懵，望着肯尼迪等他解释。

「就当你们此刻正坐在阿尔巴诺后屋的一张桌旁，」他只说了这么一句。「这就是你们会听见的动静。这是我的『电耳朵』——换句话说，侦听器（dictograph）。据说美国特勤局（Secret Service）用的就是这玩意儿。等等，热纳罗马上就要进来了。路易吉、文森佐，你们把听到的翻译出来。我那点意大利文早锈得差不多了。」

「他们听得见我们吗？」路易吉声音发颤、满脸敬畏地悄声问。

克雷格笑了。「听不见，暂时听不见。可我只要碰一下这另一个开关，就能在那间屋子里造出一桩动静来，赛得过伯沙撒（Belshazzar）王宫墙上那有名的字——只不过墙上发出来的是声音，不是字罢了。」

===Original===
“They seem to be waiting for someone,” said Vincenzo. “I heard somebody say: ‘He will be here in a few minutes. Now get out.’ ”

The babel of voices seemed to calm down as men withdrew from the room. Only one or two were left.

“One of them says the child is all right. She has been left in the back yard,” translated Luigi.

“What yard? Did he say?” asked Kennedy.

“No; they just speak of it as the ‘yard,’ ” replied Luigi.
===Chinese===
「他们像是在等什么人，」文森佐说。「我听见有人说：『他几分钟内就到。现在都出去。』」

随着人们退出那间屋子，嘈杂的人声似乎平息了下去，只剩下一两个人。

「其中一人说，孩子好好的。她被留在后院里了。」路易吉翻译道。

「哪个院子？他说了吗？」肯尼迪问。

「没有；他们只管那儿叫『院子』。」路易吉答道。

===Original===
“Jameson, go outside in the store to the telephone booth and call up headquarters. Ask them if the automobile is ready, with the men in it.”

I rang up, and after a moment the police central answered that everything was right.

“Then tell central to hold the line clear﻿—we mustn’t lose a moment. Jameson, you stay in the booth. Vincenzo, you pretend to be working around your window, but not in such a way as to attract attention, for they have men watching the street very carefully. What is it, Luigi?”

“Gennaro is coming. I just heard one of them say, ‘Here he comes.’ ”

Even from the booth I could hear the dictograph repeating the conversation in the dingy, little back room of Albano’s, down the street.
===Chinese===
「詹姆森，到外头店堂的电话亭去，给总局挂个电话。问问汽车备好没有，人上车没有。」

我拨通了电话，片刻之后，警察局总机回话说一切就绪。

「那就叫总机把线路保持畅通——一刻也不能耽搁。詹姆森，你守在电话亭里。文森佐，你在橱窗那边装作拾掇，可别惹眼，他们有人正死死盯着这条街呢。怎么了，路易吉？」

「热纳罗来了。我刚听见其中一个人说：『他来了。』」

就连身在电话亭里的我，也能听见侦听器把街那头阿尔巴诺那间昏暗小后屋里的对话，一句句传了过来。

===Original===
“He’s ordering a bottle of red wine,” murmured Luigi, dancing up and down with excitement.

Vincenzo was so nervous that he knocked a bottle down in the window, and I believe that my heartbeats were almost audible over the telephone which I was holding, for the police operator called me down for asking so many times if all was ready.

“There it is﻿—the signal,” cried Craig. “ ‘A fine opera is *I Pagliacci*.’ Now listen for the answer.”

A moment elapsed, then, “Not without Gennaro,” came a gruff voice in Italian from the dictograph.

A silence ensued. It was tense.

===Chinese===
「他在点一瓶红葡萄酒，」路易吉喃喃道，兴奋得直打转。

文森佐紧张得把橱窗里一只瓶子都碰倒了；我相信，我握着电话，连心跳声都几乎顺着话筒传了过去，因为警察局的接线生狠狠说了我一顿，嫌我一趟又一趟地问是不是都准备好了。

「来了——暗语，」克雷格喊道。「『《丑角》是部好歌剧。』现在听回话。」

片刻之后，一个粗哑的嗓音用意大利语从侦听器里传来：「没有热纳罗可不行。」

接着是一阵沉默。人人都绷紧了。

===Original===
“Wait, wait,” said a voice which I recognised instantly as Gennaro’s. “I cannot read this. What is this, 23½ Prince Street?”

“No, 33½. She has been left in the backyard,” answered the voice.

“Jameson,” called Craig, “tell them to drive straight to 33½ Prince Street. They will find the girl in the back yard﻿—quick, before the Black-Handers have a chance to go back on their word.”

I fairly shouted my orders to the police headquarters. “They’re off,” came back the answer, and I hung up the receiver.

===Chinese===
「等等，等等，」一个我立刻听出是热纳罗的声音说。「这个我认不得。这儿写的是王子街（Prince Street）23½号吗？」

「不，33½。她被留在后院了。」那声音答道。

「詹姆森，」克雷格喊道，「叫他们直奔王子街33½号。他们会在后院找到那孩子——快，赶在那帮黑手党徒来得及反悔之前。」

我几乎是扯着嗓子向警察总局下了令。「他们出发了。」听筒里回了一句，我挂上了电话。

===Original===
“What was that?” Craig was asking of Luigi. “I didn’t catch it. What did they say?”

“That other voice said to Gennaro, ‘Sit down while I count this.’ ”

“*Sh!* he’s talking again.”

“If it is a penny less than ten thousand or I find a mark on the bills I’ll call to Enrico, and your daughter will be spirited away again,” translated Luigi.
===Chinese===
「刚才是句什么？」克雷格正问路易吉。「我没听清。他们说什么了？」

「那另一个声音对热纳罗说：『你坐下，我点点数。』」

「嘘！他又开口了。」

「『要是差一个子儿，或者票子上做了记号，我就叫恩里科，你女儿马上再给你藏起来。』」路易吉翻译道。

===Original===
“Now, Gennaro is talking,” said Craig. “Good﻿—he is gaining time. He is a trump. I can distinguish that all right. He’s asking the gruff voiced fellow if he will have another bottle of wine. He says he will. Good. They must be at Prince Street now we’ll give them a few minutes more, not too much, for word will be back to Albano’s like wildfire, and they will get Gennaro after all. Ah, they are drinking again. What was that, Luigi? The money is all right, he says? Now, Vincenzo, out with the lights!”

A door banged open across the street, and four huge dark figures darted out in the direction of Albano’s.

With his finger Kennedy pulled down the other switch and shouted: “Gennaro, this is Kennedy! To the street! *Polizia! Polizia!*”

A scuffle and a cry of surprise followed. A second voice, apparently from the bar, shouted, “Out with the lights, out with the lights!”

*Bang!* went a pistol, and another.

===Chinese===
「现在热纳罗在说话了，」克雷格说。「好——他在拖时间。真是条好汉。这一段我听得明白。他在问那个粗嗓子的，还要不要再来一瓶酒。他说要。好。他们这会儿该到王子街了。再给他们几分钟，不能多给——消息传回阿尔巴诺那儿会像野火一样快，他们到头来还是会朝热纳罗下毒手。啊，又喝上了。说什么，路易吉？他说钱没毛病？好，文森佐，关灯！」

对街一扇门砰地敞开，四条高大黢黑的身影朝阿尔巴诺的店直扑过去。

肯尼迪用手指扳下另一只开关，高声喊道：「热纳罗，我是肯尼迪！到街上来！*Polizia! Polizia!*」（意大利语：警察！警察！）

接着是一阵扭打和一声惊呼。第二个嗓音，听来是从酒吧那头发出的，喊道：「把灯灭掉！把灯灭掉！」

手枪「砰」地一响，又「砰」地一响。

===Original===
The dictograph, which had been all sound a moment before, was as mute as a cigar-box.

“What’s the matter?” I asked Kennedy, as he rushed past me.

“They have shot out the lights. My receiving instrument is destroyed. Come on, Jameson; Vincenzo, stay back, if you don’t want to appear in this.”

A short figure rushed by me, faster even than I could go. It was the faithful, Luigi.

===Chinese===
刚才还满耳声响的侦听器，霎时哑得像只雪茄烟盒。

「怎么回事？」肯尼迪从我身边冲过去时，我问他。

「他们开枪把灯打灭了。我的接收器毁了。快来，詹姆森；文森佐，退后去，要是你不想在这场戏里露面的话。」

一条矮小的身影从我身边冲了过去，比我跑得还快。那是忠心耿耿的路易吉。

===Original===
In front of Albano’s an exciting fight was going on. Shots were being fired wildly in the darkness, and heads were popping out of tenement windows on all sides. As Kennedy and I flung ourselves into the crowd we caught a glimpse of Gennaro, with blood streaming from a cut on his shoulder, struggling with a policeman while Luigi vainly was trying to interpose himself between them. A man, held by another policeman, was urging the first officer on. “That’s the man,” he was crying. “That’s the kidnapper. I caught him.”

In a moment Kennedy was behind him. “Paoli, you lie. You are the kidnapper. Seize him﻿—he has the money on him. That other is Gennaro himself.”

The policeman released the tenor, and both of them seized Paoli. The others were beating at the door, which was being frantically barricaded inside.

Just then a taxicab came swinging up they street. Three men jumped out and added their strength to those who were battering down Albano’s barricade.

===Chinese===
阿尔巴诺店门前，一场激烈的混战正打得不可开交。黑暗中枪声乱飞，四面八方经济公寓的窗户里探出一颗颗脑袋。我和肯尼迪冲进人群时，瞥见热纳罗肩上一道口子血流如注，正跟一个警察扭作一团，路易吉徒劳地想把自己插进两人中间。另一个警察扭住一个男人，那男人正撺掇头一名警官动手。「就是他，」他嚷道，「就是那个绑匪。我逮住他的。」

转眼间，肯尼迪已到了他背后。「保利，你撒谎。你才是绑匪。抓住他——钱就在他身上。那一个才是热纳罗本人。」

警察放开了男高音，两人一齐扭住保利。其余的人正擂着店门，门里正疯狂地垒着障碍。

正在这时，一辆出租车沿街疾驰而至。三条汉子跳下车，加入了撞开阿尔巴诺店门障碍的人堆。

===Original===
Gennaro, with a cry, leaped into the taxicab. Over his shoulder I could see a tangled mass of dark brown curls, and a childish voice lisped “Why didn’t you come for me, papa? The bad man told me if I waited in the yard you would come for me. But if I cried he said he would shoot me. And I waited, and waited﻿—”

“There, there, Una; papa’s going to take you straight home to mother.”

A crash followed as the door yielded, and the famous Paoli gang was in the hands of the law.

===Chinese===
热纳罗大喊一声，扑进了出租车。越过他的肩头，我望见一团乱蓬蓬的深棕色卷发，一个稚嫩的声音咿咿呀呀地说：「你怎么现在才来接我呀，爸爸？坏人跟我说，只要我在院子里等，你就会来接我。可我要是一哭，他说就开枪打我。我就等呀，等呀——」

「好了，好了，乌娜（Una）；爸爸这就带你回家见妈妈。」

接着轰隆一声，店门被撞开，恶名远扬的保利匪帮落进了法网。

## The Deadly Tube / 致命的管子

===Original===

“For Heaven’s sake, Gregory, what is the matter?” asked Craig Kennedy as a tall, nervous man stalked into our apartment one evening. “Jameson, shake hands with Dr. Gregory. What’s the matter, Doctor? Surely your X-ray work hasn’t knocked you out like this?”

The doctor shook hands with me mechanically. His hand was icy. “The blow has fallen,” he exclaimed, as he sank limply into a chair and tossed an evening paper over to Kennedy.

In red ink on the first page, in the little square headed “Latest News,” Kennedy read the caption, “Society Woman Crippled for Life by X-Ray Treatment.”

“A terrible tragedy was revealed in the suit begun today,” continued the article, “by Mrs. Huntington Close against Dr. James Gregory, an X-ray specialist with offices at Madison Avenue, to recover damages for injuries which Mrs. Close alleges she received while under his care. Several months ago she began a course of X-ray treatment to remove a birthmark on her neck. In her complaint Mrs. Close alleges that Dr. Gregory has carelessly caused X-ray dermatitis, a skin disease of cancerous nature, and that she has also been rendered a nervous wreck through the effects of the rays. Simultaneously with filing the suit she left home and entered a private hospital. Mrs. Close is one of the most popular hostesses in the smart set, and her loss will be keenly felt.”

“What am I to do, Kennedy?” asked the doctor imploringly. “You remember I told you the other day about this case﻿—that there was something queer about it, that after a few treatments I was afraid to carry on any more and refused to do so? She really has dermatitis and nervous prostration, exactly as she alleges in her complaint. But, before Heaven, Kennedy, I can’t see how she could possibly have been so affected by the few treatments I gave her. And tonight, just as I was leaving the office, I received a telephone call from her husband’s attorney, Lawrence, very kindly informing me that the case would be pushed to the limit. I tell you, it looks black for me.”

===Chinese===

一天晚上，一个高个子、神色紧张的男子大步闯进我们的公寓。「看在老天的分上，格雷戈里（Gregory），这是怎么了？」克雷格·肯尼迪问道。「詹姆森，来，同格雷戈里医生握握手。您到底怎么了，医生？总不会是您的 X 射线工作把您搞垮成这样了吧？」

医生机械地同我握了握手。他的手冰凉。「祸事落下来了，」他喊道，一面浑身瘫软地跌坐进椅子，一面把一份晚报丢给肯尼迪。

头版上一个以「最新消息」为题的小方框里，肯尼迪读到一行红墨水印的标题：「社交名媛因 X 射线治疗终身致残。」

「今日提起的一场诉讼披露了一桩骇人的悲剧，」报道接着写道，「原告亨廷顿·克洛斯夫人（Mrs. Huntington Close）起诉在麦迪逊大道开业行医的 X 射线专科医生詹姆斯·格雷戈里，就她声称在后者治疗期间所受的伤害索取赔偿。几个月前，她为除去颈上一块胎记，开始接受一程 X 射线治疗。克洛斯夫人在诉状中指控格雷戈里医生粗心大意，造成了 X 射线皮炎——一种具有癌变性质的皮肤病——并称射线的影响还使她的神经系统彻底垮掉。在递交诉状的同时，她离家住进了一家私立医院。克洛斯夫人是上流交际圈里最得人心的宴客女主人之一，她的隐退将令人倍感怅然。」

「我该怎么办呢，肯尼迪？」医生苦苦哀求道。「您还记得吧，前几天我跟您提起过这桩病例——说里头有点蹊跷，治了几回之后我就不敢再治下去，便拒绝了。她确实得了皮炎和神经衰弱，同她诉状里声称的一模一样。可是老天在上，肯尼迪，我实在看不出，我给她做的那几回治疗，怎么可能把她伤成这样。就在今晚，我正要下班，她丈夫的律师劳伦斯（Lawrence）来了电话，客客气气地通知我说，这个案子要追究到底。告诉您吧，我前景不妙啊。」

===Original===

“What can they do?”

“Do? Do you suppose any jury is going to take enough expert testimony to outweigh the tragedy of a beautiful woman? Do? Why, they can ruin me, even if I get a verdict of acquittal. They can leave me with a reputation for carelessness that no mere court decision can ever overcome.”

“Gregory, you can rely on me,” said Kennedy. “Anything I can do to help you I will gladly do. Jameson and I were on the point of going out to dinner. Join us, and after that we will go down to your office and talk things over.”

“You are really too kind,” murmured the doctor. The air of relief that was written on his face was pathetically eloquent.

“Now not a word about the case till we have had dinner,” commanded Craig. “I see very plainly that you have been worrying about the blow for a long time. Well, it has fallen. The neat thing to do is to look over the situation and see where we stand.”

===Chinese===

「他们能把你怎么样？」

「怎么样？你以为随便哪个陪审团，会听够了专家证词，去压倒一位美丽妇人的悲剧吗？怎么样？哼，他们能毁了我——哪怕最后裁我无罪。他们会给我留下一顶粗心大意的名声，任何一纸法庭判决都再也洗不掉。」

「格雷戈里，我靠得住，」肯尼迪说，「凡是我能帮上忙的，我都乐意尽力。我和詹姆森正要出去吃晚饭。跟我们同去吧，饭后我们一起到你诊所，把事情商量个透。」

「您真是太好了，」医生喃喃道。他脸上写满如释重负，凄楚之意，溢于言表。

「饭吃完之前，这桩案子一个字也不许提，」克雷格发令道，「我看得分明，这场祸事你已经忧惧好久了。好了，它落下来了。眼下干净利落的做法，是把局面检视一遍，看看我们究竟站在哪里。」

===Original===

Dinner over, we rode downtown in the subway, and Gregory ushered us into an office building on Madison Avenue, where he had a very handsome suite of several rooms. We sat own in his waiting-room to discuss the affair.

“It is indeed a very tragic case,” began Kennedy, “almost more tragic than if the victim had been killed outright. Mrs. Huntington Close is﻿—or rather I suppose I should say was﻿—one of the famous beauties of the city. From what the paper says, her beauty has been hopelessly ruined by this dermatitis, which, I understand, Doctor, is practically incurable.”

Dr. Gregory nodded, and I could not help following his eyes as he looked at his own rough and scarred hands.

“Also,” continued Craig, with his eyes half closed and his fingertips together, as if, he were taking a mental inventory of the facts in the case, “her nerves are so shattered that she will be years in recovering, if she ever recovers.”

===Chinese===

吃罢晚饭，我们乘地铁进城，格雷戈里把我们领进麦迪逊大道上一幢写字楼，他在那里占着一套好几间的气派诊室。我们在他的候诊室里坐下，商量这件事。

「这实在是一桩惨痛已极的案子，」肯尼迪开腔道，「几乎比受害人当场殒命还要惨。亨廷顿·克洛斯夫人——或者不如说，我该讲曾经是——本城赫赫有名的美人之一。照报纸的说法，她的容貌已经让这皮炎毁得不可挽回。我理解，医生，这病实际上是治不好的。」

格雷戈里医生点了点头。他的目光落在自己那双粗糙结疤的手上，我不由自主地随着他望去。

「再有，」克雷格接着说，眼睛半阖，指尖抵着指尖，仿佛在心里把这案子的种种事实一一清点造册，「她的神经垮到这种地步，往后要熬好几年才能复原——倘若真能复原的话。」

===Original===

“Yes,” said the doctor simply. “I myself, for instance, am subject to the most unexpected attacks of neuritis. But, of course, I am under the influence of the rays fifty or sixty times a day, while she had only a few treatments at intervals of many days.”

“Now, on the other hand,” resumed Craig, “I know you, Gregory, very well. Only the other day, before any of this came out, you told me the whole story with your fears as to the outcome. I know that that lawyer of Close’s has been keeping this thing hanging over your head for a long time. And I also know that you are one of the most careful X-ray operators in the city. If this suit goes against you, one of the most brilliant men of science in America will be ruined. Now, having said this much, let me ask you to describe just exactly what treatments you gave Mrs. Close.”

The doctor led us into his X-ray room adjoining. A number of X-ray tubes were neatly put away in a great glass case, and at one end of the room was an operating-table with an X-ray apparatus suspended over it. A glance at the room showed that Kennedy’s praise was not exaggerated.

“How many treatments did you give Mrs. Close?” asked Kennedy.

===Chinese===

「是的，」医生简单地应道。「就拿我自己来说吧，就常常遭到最想不到的神经炎袭击。不过当然喽，我是一天五六十次暴露在射线下，而她不过是隔上许多天受寥寥几回治疗。」

「话又说回来，」克雷格继续道，「格雷戈里，我是深知你的为人的。就在前几天，这些事还没闹出来的时候，你就把全部始末连同你对结局的忧虑都对我和盘托出了。我知道，克洛斯的那位律师一直拿这件事吊在你头上。我还知道，你是全城数得着的细心 X 射线操作师之一。这场官司若是砸在你身上，美国最英才焕发的科学界人士之一就要毁于一旦。好，话说到这里，就请你原原本本地讲一讲：你究竟给克洛斯夫人做了些什么样的治疗。」

医生领我们走进隔壁的 X 射线室。好些 X 射线管整整齐齐收在一只巨大的玻璃柜里，房间一头是一张治疗台，上方悬着 X 射线设备。对这间屋子扫上一眼便知，肯尼迪的夸赞并无半点夸张。

「你给克洛斯夫人做了多少回治疗？」肯尼迪问。

===Original===

“Not over a dozen, I should say,” replied Gregory. “I have a record of them and the dates, which I will give you presently. Certainly they were not numerous enough or frequent enough to have caused a dermatitis such as she has. Besides, look here. I have an apparatus which, for safety to the patient, has few equals in the country. This big lead-glass bowl, which is placed over my X-ray tube when in use, cuts off the rays at every point except exactly where they are needed.”

He switched on the electric current, and the apparatus began to sputter. The pungent odour of ozone from the electric discharge filled the room. Through the lead-glass bowl I could see the X-ray tube inside suffused with its peculiar, yellowish-green light, divided into two hemispheres of different shades. That, I knew, was the cathode ray, not the X-ray, for the X-ray itself, which streams outside the tube, is invisible to the human eye. The doctor placed in our hands a couple of fluoroscopes, an apparatus by which X-rays can be detected. It consists simply of a closed box with an opening to which the eyes are placed. The opposite end of the box is a piece of board coated with a salt such as platino-barium cyanide. When the X-ray strikes this salt it makes it glow, or fluoresce, and objects held between the X-ray tube and the fluoroscope cast shadows according to the density of the parts which the X-rays penetrate.

With the lead-glass bowl removed, the X-ray tube sent forth its wonderful invisible radiation and made the back of the fluoroscope glow with light. I could see the bones of my fingers as I held them up between the X-ray tube and the fluoroscope. But with the lead-glass bowl in position over the tube, the fluoroscope was simply a black box into which I looked and saw nothing. So very little of the radiation escaped from the bowl that it was negligible﻿—except at one point where there was an opening in the bottom of the bowl to allow the rays to pass freely through exactly on the spot on the patient where they were to be used.

===Chinese===

「我想超不过十来回吧，」格雷戈里答道，「每回的日期我都有记录，回头就交给您。这些治疗论次数、论频度，都决计不足以造成她那样的皮炎。再说，您瞧。我这套设备，论对病人的安全，全国少有能同它媲美的。这只大铅玻璃罩，用时扣在我的 X 射线管上，四面八方的射线统统挡住，只留恰恰要用射线的那个点。」

他接通电流，设备噼噼啪啪响起来。放电生出的臭氧，那股刺鼻的气味弥漫全屋。透过铅玻璃罩，我看得见里面的 X 射线管浸着一层特有的黄绿色辉光，分成深浅不同的两半。我知道，那是阴极射线，不是 X 射线——X 射线本身流散在管外，人眼是看不见的。医生往我们手里各放了一只荧光镜（fluoroscope），一种可以探测 X 射线的器械。它构造极简单：一只密闭的匣子，一面开着孔，眼睛就贴在孔上；另一面是一块敷着铂氰化钡（platino-barium cyanide）之类盐类的板。X 射线一打在这种盐上，它便发光，也就是发出荧光；把物件举在 X 射线管和荧光镜之间，物件便投下影子，浓淡随 X 射线所穿透部位的疏密而定。

撤去铅玻璃罩，X 射线管便放送出它那奇妙而不可见的辐射，照得荧光镜的背面灼灼生光。我把手指举在管子和荧光镜之间，能看见自己的指骨。可铅玻璃罩一旦扣回管上，荧光镜便只是一只黑匣子，朝里望去，一无所见。从罩里漏出的辐射微乎其微，尽可不计——只有一处例外：罩底开着一个口，让射线畅行无阻，恰好透过病人身上要用的那一点。

===Original===

“The dermatitis, they say, has appeared all over her body, particularly on her head and shoulders,” added Dr. Gregory. “Now I have shown you my apparatus to impress on you how really impossible it would have been for her to contract it from her treatments here. I’ve made thousands of exposures with never an X-ray burn before﻿—except to myself. As for myself, I’m as careful as I can be, but you can see I am under the rays very often, while the patient is only under them once in a while.”

To illustrate his care he pointed out to us a cabinet directly back of the operating-table, lined with thick sheets of lead. From this cabinet he conducted most of his treatments as far as possible. A little peephole enabled him to see the patient and the X-ray apparatus, while an arrangement of mirrors and a fluorescent screen enabled him to see exactly what the X-rays were disclosing, without his leaving the lead-lined cabinet.

“I can think of no more perfect protection for either patient or operator,” said Kennedy admiringly. “By the way, did Mrs. Close come alone?”

“No, the first time Mr. Close came with her. After that, she came with her French maid.”

===Chinese===

「人家说，她周身上下都发了皮炎，头上肩上尤其厉害，」格雷戈里医生补充道，「我把设备演示给你们看，就是要叫你们明白，她要从我这里的治疗染上这病，实在万万不可能。我做过成千上万次照射，从没让谁受过一回 X 射线灼伤——除了我自己。至于我自己，我已经小心到家了，可你们看得出，我泡在射线底下的时辰太多，而病人不过偶尔挨一下。」

为了印证他的谨慎，他又指给我们看一张柜子，就在治疗台的正后方，内壁衬着厚厚的铅板。他的治疗，大半尽可能就是躲在这张柜子里操作的。一个小小的瞭望孔让他看得见病人和 X 射线设备，再加一套镜子和一面荧光屏的装置，他不必跨出铅柜一步，就能看清 X 射线所揭示的一切。

「我想不出对病人、对操作者还有比这更万全的防护了，」肯尼迪叹赏道。「对了，克洛斯夫人来时都是一个人吗？」

「不，头一回是克洛斯先生陪她来的。往后，她都带着她的法国女仆来。」

===Original===

The next day we paid a visit to Mrs. Close herself at the private hospital. Kennedy had been casting about in his mind for an excuse to see her, and I had suggested that we go as reporters from the *Star*. Fortunately after sending up my card on which I had written Craig’s name we were at length allowed to go up to her room.

We found the patient reclining in an easy chair, swathed in bandages, a wreck of her former self. I felt the tragedy keenly. All that social position and beauty had meant to her had been suddenly blasted.

“You will pardon my presumption,” began Craig, “but, Mrs. Close, I assure you that I am actuated by the best of motives. We represent the *New York Star*﻿—”

===Chinese===

第二天，我们到那家私立医院去拜访克洛斯夫人本人。肯尼迪一直在心里盘算着见她的借口，我提议以《明星报》（*Star*）记者的身份去。运气不错，递上名片——上面我添写了克雷格的名字——之后，我们总算获准上楼到她房间里去。

我们见到病人斜倚在安乐椅里，浑身裹着绷带，昔日的她只剩一副残躯。我痛切地感到了这场悲剧。社会地位和美貌曾经意味着的一切，霎时间被摧折殆尽。

「还请恕我冒昧，」克雷格开口道，「不过克洛斯夫人，我向您保证，我此来完全出于最善良的动机。我们是《纽约明星报》（*New York Star*）的——」

===Original===

“Isn’t it terrible enough that I should suffer so,” she interrupted, “but must the newspapers hound me, too?”

“I beg your pardon, Mrs. Close,” said Craig, “but you must be aware that the news of your suit of Dr. Gregory has now become public property. I couldn’t stop the *Star*, much less the other papers, from talking about it. But I can and will do this, Mrs. Close. I will see that justice is done to you and all others concerned. Believe me, I am not here as a yellow journalist to make newspaper copy out of your misfortune. I am here to get at the truth sympathetically. Incidentally, I may be able to render you a service, too.”

“You can render me no service except to expedite the suit against that careless doctor﻿—I hate him.”

“Perhaps,” said Craig. “But suppose someone else should be proved to have been really responsible? Would you still want to press the suit and let the guilty person escape?”

She bit her lip. “What is it you want of me?” she asked.

===Chinese===

「我受这份罪还不够惨吗，」她打断道，「连报纸也要来撵着我不放？」

「请您原谅，克洛斯夫人，」克雷格说，「可您一定明白，您起诉格雷戈里医生的消息如今已成了公开的话题。《明星报》要议论它，我拦不住，更不必说别的报纸。但这一点我能做到，也一定做到，克洛斯夫人：我担保您和一切相关的人都得到公道。请相信我，我到这里来，不是当什么黄色记者，拿您的不幸去炮制报纸素材的。我是带着同情来查个水落石出的。说不定，我还能顺便为您效一点力。」

「你帮不上我什么忙——除非催着快点把那个粗心的医生告倒。我恨他。」

「也许吧，」克雷格说。「可要是查明真正该负责的另有其人呢？您还愿意把官司打下去，放真正的罪人逍遥法外吗？」

她咬了咬嘴唇。「你想从我这里要什么？」她问。

===Original===

“I merely want permission to visit your rooms at your home and to talk with your maid. I do not mean to spy on you, far from it; but consider, Mrs. Close, if I should be able to get at the bottom of this thing, find out the real cause of your misfortune, perhaps show that you are the victim of a cruel wrong rather than of carelessness, would you not be willing to let me go ahead? I am frank to tell you that I suspect there is more to this affair than you yourself have any idea of.”

“No, you are mistaken, Mr. Kennedy. I know the cause of it. It was my love of beauty. I couldn’t resist the temptation to get rid of even a slight defect. If I had left well enough alone I should not be here now. A friend recommended Dr. Gregory to my husband, who took me there. My husband wishes me to remain at home, but I tell him I feel more comfortable here in the hospital. I shall never go to that house again﻿—the memory of the torture of sleepless nights in my room there when I felt my good looks going, going”﻿—she shuddered﻿—“is such that I can never forget it. He says I would be better off there, but no, I cannot go. Still,” she continued wearily, “there can be no harm in your talking to my maid.”

Kennedy noted attentively what she was saying. “I thank you, Mrs. Close,” he replied. “I am sure you will not regret your permission. Would you be so kind as to give me a note to her?”

She rang, dictated a short note to a nurse, signed it, and languidly dismissed us.

I don’t know that I ever felt as depressed as I did after that interview with one who had entered a living death to ambition, for while Craig had done all the talking I had absorbed nothing but depression. I vowed that if Gregory or anybody else was responsible I would do my share toward bringing on him retribution.

===Chinese===

「我只求您允许我到府上您的房间里看看，同您的女仆谈一谈。我并不是要窥探您，决无此意；可是您想想，克洛斯夫人，倘若我能把这件事追到根底，查出您这场不幸的真正原因，甚至证明您是一场残酷暗算的受害者，而不是一次粗心大意的受害者，您难道不愿让我放手进行吗？我坦白告诉您，我疑心这桩事的内情，连您自己也丝毫想不到。」

「不，您想岔了，肯尼迪先生。我知道原因在哪儿。是我爱美成性。连一点小小的瑕疵，我都禁不住要去掉——抵挡不住这个诱惑。当初要是安分知足，如今我也就不会在这里了。一位朋友向丈夫举荐了格雷戈里医生，是他带我去的。丈夫要我留在家里，可我告诉他，在医院里我反倒好受些。我再也不回那宅子了——在自己房里一夜一夜熬着无眠的酷刑，眼看着自己的美貌一分一分去了、去了」——她打了个寒噤——「那种滋味，我一辈子忘不掉。他说我回去会好些，可是不，我回不去。不过，」她倦怠地接着说，「您去同我的女仆谈谈，总归没有妨害。」

肯尼迪留意地记下她的话。「谢谢您，克洛斯夫人，」他答道，「我敢说，您决不会后悔给了这个许可。可否劳驾，给我一封给她的便条？」

她按了铃，向一位护士口授了一封短简，签了字，有气无力地打发我们走了。

我不记得有哪一回，比那次访谈之后更叫我心灰意冷——同一位抱负已陷活死境的人谈过之后。克雷格虽说包揽了全部谈话，我吸进去的却只有沮丧。我暗暗起誓：倘若格雷戈里，或者随便哪个别人，要对此负责，我必尽我一份力量，叫恶人遭报。

===Original===

The Closes lived in a splendid big house in the Murray Hill section. The presentation of the note quickly brought Mrs. Close’s maid down to us. She had not gone to the hospital because Mrs. Close had considered the services of the trained nurses quite sufficient.

Yes, the maid had noticed how her mistress had been failing, had noticed it long ago, in fact almost at the time when she had begun the X-ray treatment. She had seemed to improve once when she went away for a few days, but that was at the start, and directly after her return she grew worse again, until she was no longer herself.

“Did Dr. Gregory, the X-ray specialist, ever attend Mrs. Close at her home, in her room?” asked Craig.

“Yes, once, twice, he call, but he do no good,” she said with her French accent.

“Did Mrs. Close have other callers?”

===Chinese===

克洛斯夫妇住在默里山（Murray Hill）地段一幢堂皇的大宅里。呈上便条，很快就把克洛斯夫人的女仆唤下楼来见我们。她没有去医院，因为克洛斯夫人认为，有专业护士侍候尽够了。

是的，女仆早看出女主人一日不如一日——早就看出来了，事实上几乎就在她开始 X 射线治疗的时候。有一回她出门几天，人像是一时见好，可那是起初的事；一回来便又见沉，直到整个人都不成样子。

「格雷戈里医生，那位 X 射线专科医生，可曾到府上来，在克洛斯夫人房间里给她治过？」克雷格问。

「来过，一回，两回，他来，可治不好，」她带着法国口音说。

「克洛斯夫人还接待别的客人吗？」

===Original===

“But, m’sieur, everyone in society has many. What does m’sieur mean?”

“Frequent callers﻿—a Mr. Lawrence, for instance?”

“Oh, yes, Mr. Lawrence frequently.”

“When Mr. Close was at home?”

“Yes, on business and on business, too, when he was not at home. He is the attorney, m’sieur.”

“How did Mrs. Close receive him?”

===Chinese===

「可是，先生，社交场上的人家谁不是宾客盈门呢。先生这话是什么意思？」

「常来的客人——比方说，一位劳伦斯先生？」

「哦，是的，劳伦斯先生常来。」

「克洛斯先生在家的时候？」

「是的，是公事；他不在家的时候，也还是公事。他是律师，先生。」

「克洛斯夫人是怎么接待他的？」

===Original===

“He is the attorney, m’sieur,” Marie repeated persistently.

“And he, did he always call on business?”

“Oh, yes, always on business, but well, madame, she was a very beautiful woman. Perhaps he like beautiful women﻿—*eh bien*? That was before the Doctor Gregory treated madame. After the doctor treated madame M’sieur Lawrence do not call so often. That’s all.”

“Are you thoroughly devoted to Mrs. Close? Would you do a favour for her?” asked Craig point-blank.

“Sir, I would give my life, almost, for madame. She was always so good to me.”

===Chinese===

「他是律师，先生，」玛丽（Marie）执拗地又答了一遍。

「那么他呢，他回回都是为公事来的？」

「哦，是的，回回是公事，不过么，先生，太太她从前是位大美人呀。也许他就好美人这一口——*eh bien*（可不是）？那是在格雷戈里医生给太太治疗之前。医生给太太治了之后，劳伦斯先生就不那么常来了。就是这么回事。」

「你对克洛斯夫人是不是忠心耿耿？肯不肯为她帮个忙？」克雷格单刀直入地问。

「先生，为了太太，我连性命差不多都肯给的。她一向待我那么好。」

===Original===

“I don’t ask you to give your life for her, Marie,” said Craig, “but you can do her a great service, a very great service.”

“I will do it.”

“Tonight,” said Craig, “I want you to sleep in Mrs. Close’s room. You can do so, for I know that Mr. Close is living at the St. Francis Club until his wife returns from the sanitarium. Tomorrow morning come to my laboratory”﻿—Craig handed her his card﻿—“and I will tell you what to do next. By the way, don’t say anything to anyone in the house about it, and keep a sharp watch on the actions of any of the servants who may go into Mrs. Close’s room.”

“Well,” said Craig, “there is nothing more to be done immediately.” We had once more regained the street and were walking uptown. We walked in silence for several blocks.

“Yes,” mused Craig, “there is something you can do, after all, Walter. I would like you to look up Gregory and Close and Lawrence. I already know something about them. But you can find out a good deal with your newspaper connections. I would like to have every bit of scandal that has ever been connected with them, or with Mrs. Close, or,” he added significantly, “with any other woman. It isn’t necessary to say that not a breath of it must be published﻿—yet.”

===Chinese===

「我不要你送性命，玛丽，」克雷格说，「可你能帮她一个大忙，一个非常大的忙。」

「我一定做。」

「就在今晚，」克雷格说，「我要你睡在克洛斯夫人的房间里。这你办得到，因为我知道，在太太从疗养院回来之前，克洛斯先生一直住在圣弗朗西斯俱乐部（St. Francis Club）。明天早晨到我的实验室来」——克雷格递给她一张名片——「我告诉你下一步怎么办。还有，这件事对宅子里任何人一个字也别提；哪个仆人要是进克洛斯夫人的房间，都留神盯住他们的举动。」

「好啦，」克雷格说，「眼下没有别的什么事可立刻办的了。」我们重新回到街上，往住宅区方向走着。一连好几个街区，我们都没有说话。

「不过话得说回来，」克雷格沉吟道，「到底有一件事你能做，沃尔特。我想请你去查一查格雷戈里、克洛斯和劳伦斯这三个人的底。他们的情形我已略知一二，可凭你的报界门路，还能打听到不少。凡是同他们仨、同克洛斯夫人沾过边的风流话，我要一条不落，」他又意味深长地补了一句，「同任何别的女人沾过边的，也要。不必说，其中一丝一毫眼下都不得登报——眼下。」

===Original===

I found a good deal of gossip, but very little of it, indeed, seemed to me at the time to be of importance. Dropping in at the St. Francis Club, where I had some friends, I casually mentioned the troubles of the Huntington Closes. I was surprised to learn that Close spent little of his time at the Club, none at home, and only dropped into the hospital to make formal inquiries as to his wife’s condition. It then occurred to me to drop into the office of *Society Squibs*, whose editor I had long known. The editor told me, with that nameless look of the cynical scandalmonger, that if I wanted to learn anything about Huntington Close I had best watch Mrs. Frances Tulkington, a very wealthy Western divorcée about whom the smart set were much excited, particularly those whose wealth made it difficult to stand the pace of society as it was going at present.

“And before the tragedy,” said the editor with another nameless look, as if he were imparting a most valuable piece of gossip, “it was the talk of the town, the attention that Close’s lawyer was paying to Mrs. Close. But to her credit let me say that she never gave us a chance to hint at anything, and﻿—well, you know us; we don’t need much to make snappy society news.”

The editor then waged even more confidential, for if I am anything at all, I am a good listener, and I have found that often by sitting tight and listening I can get more than if I were a too-eager questioner.

“It really was a shame﻿—the way that man Lawrence played his game,” he went on. “I understand that it was he who introduced Close to Mrs. T. They were both his clients. Lawrence had fought her case in the courts when she sued old Tulkington for divorce, and a handsome settlement he got for her, too. They say his fee ran up into the hundred thousands﻿—contingent, you know. I don’t know what his game was”﻿—here he lowered his voice to a whisper “but they say Close owes him a good deal of money. You can figure it out for yourself as you like. Now, I’ve told you all I know. Come in again, Jameson, when you want some more scandal, and remember me to the boys down on the *Star*.”

===Chinese===

我打听到不少闲话，不过当时在我看来，其中实在少有要紧的。我到圣弗朗西斯俱乐部串了个门，那边我有几个朋友，随口提起亨廷顿·克洛斯夫妇的烦心事。我这才惊讶地得知：克洛斯在俱乐部里难得照面，家更是索性不回，医院也只偶尔进去，形式上问一声太太的病情。接着我灵机一动，顺路去了《社会轶闻》（*Society Squibs*）的编辑部——那位主编我相识多年。主编带着专播丑闻的老手那种莫可名状的眼色告诉我：要打听亨廷顿·克洛斯的行止，最好盯住弗朗西丝·塔尔金顿夫人（Mrs. Frances Tulkington）——一位极有钱的西部离异妇人，上流交际圈正为她闹得沸沸扬扬，那些家底儿已经撑不住眼下社交排场的人家，谈兴尤其浓厚。

「惨案发生之前，」主编又摆出那副莫可名状的眼色，仿佛正在吐露一件顶宝贵的秘闻，「克洛斯的律师对克洛斯夫人大献殷勤，那可是满城谈论的话题。不过替她说句公道话：她从不曾给过我们半点借题发挥的机会，而且——嗯，您知道我们这号人；材料不用多，我们就能编出有声有色的社交新闻来。」

说罢主编愈发推心置腹起来，因为我这个人别的谈不上，至少是个好听众；我早就发现，稳坐着专心听，往往比急吼吼地追问收获还大。

「那可真叫无耻——劳伦斯那个人耍的手段，」他接着说。「据我了解，把克洛斯引见给塔太太的，正是他。他们两位都是他的委托人。塔太太起诉老塔尔金顿离婚，就是劳伦斯替她在法庭上打的官司，还替她争到一笔可观的赡养费。据说他的律师费高达好几十万——按胜诉分成算的，您懂的。他葫芦里卖的什么药，我就不知道了」——说到这里，他把嗓音压成耳语——「可是据说，克洛斯欠着他一大笔钱呢。其中文章，您爱怎么琢磨就怎么琢磨。得，我知道的可都倒给您了。往后还想再听点闲话，尽管来，詹姆森；替我向《明星报》那帮伙计问好。」

===Original===

The following day the maid visited Kennedy at his laboratory while I was reporting to him on the result of my investigations.

She looked worn and haggard. She had spent a sleepless night and begged that Kennedy would not ask her to repeat the experiment.

“I can promise you, Marie,” he said, “that you will rest better tonight. But you must spend one more night in Mrs. Close’s room. By the way, can you arrange for me to go through the room this morning when you go back?”

Marie said she could, and an hour or so later Craig and I quietly slipped into the Close residence under her guidance. He was carrying something that looked like a miniature barrel, and I had another package which he had given me, both carefully wrapped up. The butler eyed us suspiciously, but Marie spoke a few words to him and I think showed him Mrs. Close’s note. Anyhow he said nothing.

Within the room that the unfortunate woman had occupied Kennedy took the coverings off the packages. It was nothing but a portable electric vacuum cleaner, which he quickly attached and set running. Up and down the floor, around and under the bed he pushed the cleaner. He used the various attachments to clean the curtains, the walls, and even the furniture. Particularly did he pay attention to the base board on the wall back of the bed. Then he carefully removed the dust from the cleaner and sealed it up in a leaden box.

===Chinese===

第二天，那女仆到实验室来见肯尼迪，我正在向他汇报调查的结果。

她一脸憔悴，形容枯槁。她一夜无眠，恳求肯尼迪别再叫她重演那场试验。

「我向你担保，玛丽，」他说，「今夜你会睡得安稳些。可你还得在克洛斯夫人的房里再过一夜。对了，今早你回去的时候，能不能安排我把那房间细细查看一遍？」

玛丽说可以。约莫一个钟头之后，克雷格和我在她的引领下悄悄进了克洛斯宅子。他提着一件东西，模样像只小小的圆桶，我又拿着他交给我的一只包裹，两样都包得严严实实。管家满腹狐疑地打量我们，可玛丽同他讲了几句，我想还给他看了克洛斯夫人的便条。反正他一声没吭。

在那不幸女人住过的房间里，肯尼迪解开了包裹。原来不过是一台便携式电吸尘器，他三下两下接好，开动起来。地板上来来回回、床底下床四周，他推着吸尘器走了一遍又一遍，又换上各式附件去清洁窗帘、墙壁，连家具也不放过。床后墙面的踢脚板，他收拾得格外仔细。然后他小心翼翼地把吸尘器里的积尘取出来，封进一只铅盒。

===Original===

He was about to detach and pack up the cleaner when another idea seemed to occur to him. “Might as well make a thorough job of it, Walter,” he said, adjusting the apparatus again. “I’ve cleaned everything but the mattress and the brass bars behind the mattress on the bed. Now I’ll tackle them. I think we ought to go into the suction-cleaning business﻿—more money in it than in being a detective, I’ll bet.”

The cleaner was run over and under the mattress and along every crack and cranny of the brass bed. This done and this dust also carefully stowed away, we departed, very much to the mystification of Marie and, I could not help feeling, of other eyes that peered in through keyholes or cracks in doors.

“At any rate,” said Kennedy exultingly, “I think we have stolen a march on them. I don’t believe they were prepared for this, not at least at this stage in the game. Don’t ask me any questions, Walter. Then you will have no secrets to keep if anyone should try to pry them loose. Only remember that this man Lawrence is a shrewd character.”

The next day Marie came, looking even more careworn than before.

“What’s the matter, mademoiselle?” asked Craig. “Didn’t you pass a better night?”

===Chinese===

他正要拆卸吸尘器装箱，似乎又想起一个念头。「索性一不做二不休，干个彻底吧，沃尔特，」他说着又摆弄起那套设备，「就是床垫和床后的铜栏杆还没吸过，这回我来收拾它们。我看咱们真该改行干吸尘清扫的营生——我敢打赌，比当侦探赚钱。」

吸尘器在床垫上上下下、铜床架每一道裂缝沟槽里都推过了一遍。这一步做完，这批灰尘也小心收拢妥当，我们便动身离去，留下满腹狐疑的玛丽，还有——我不由得觉得——那些正从锁孔门缝里向内张望的眼睛。

「不管怎么说，」肯尼迪得意洋洋地说，「我想我们抢了他们的先手。我谅他们对此毫无防备——至少在眼下这一步棋上没有。什么也别问我，沃尔特。这样，万一有人想撬开你的口风，你也无密可泄。只记住一条：这个劳伦斯是个厉害角色。」

第二天玛丽来了，神色比先前愈发忧烦。

「怎么啦，小姐？」克雷格问。「昨夜没睡得好些吗？」

===Original===

“Oh, *mon Dieu*, I rest well, yes. But this morning, while I am at breakfast, Mr. Close send for me. He say that I am discharged. Some servant tell of your visit and he verry angr‑ry. And now what is to become of me﻿—will madame his wife give a recommendation now?”

“Walter, we have been discovered,” exclaimed Craig with considerable vexation. Then he remembered the poor girl who had been an involuntary sacrifice to our investigation. Turning to her he said: “Marie, I know several very good families, and I am sure you will not suffer for what you have done by being faithful to your mistress. Only be patient a few days. Go live with some of your folks. I will see that you are placed again.”

The girl was profuse in her thanks as she dried her tears and departed.

“I hadn’t anticipated having my hand forced so soon,” said Craig after she had gone, leaving her address. “However, we are on the right track. What was it that you were going to tell me when Marie came in?”

“Something that may be very important, Craig,” I said, “though I don’t understand it myself. Pressure is being brought to bear on the *Star* to keep this thing out of the papers, or at least to minimise it.”

===Chinese===
