「哦，*mon Dieu*（我的天），我歇得好，是的。可是今天早上，我正吃着早饭，克洛斯先生打发人叫了我去。他说把我辞了。不知哪个仆人说起你们来过，他气、气得不得了。这下我可怎么办呢——他家太太这会儿还会给我写荐书吗？」

「沃尔特，咱们暴露了。」克雷格嚷道，懊恼得很。随即他想起这可怜的姑娘，她已成了我们调查的无辜牺牲品。他转过身对她说：「玛丽，我认识好几户非常好的人家；你这样忠于主人的姑娘，决计不会因这件事吃亏。只需耐心等几天，先去同你的亲友们住些日子。我保管再给你寻个好去处。」

姑娘千恩万谢，拭干眼泪走了。

「我没料到这么快就被人逼着亮了牌，」她留下地址走后，克雷格说。「不过，我们走的路子是对的。玛丽进来的时候，你本来要告诉我什么来着？」

「一件也许十分重要的事，克雷格，」我说，「虽然我自己也闹不明白。《明星报》正在承受压力，要我们把这件事从报上压下去，或者至少轻描淡写。」

===Original===

“I’m not surprised,” commented Craig. “What do you mean by pressure being brought?”

“Why, Close’s lawyer, Lawrence, called up the editor this morning﻿—I don’t suppose that you know, but he has some connection with the interests which control the *Star*﻿—and said that the activity of one of the reporters from the *Star*, Jameson by name, was very distasteful to Mr. Close and that this reporter was employing a man named Kennedy to assist him.

“I don’t understand it, Craig;” I confessed, “but here one day they give the news to the papers, and two days later they almost threaten us with suit if we don’t stop publishing it.”

“It is perplexing,” said Craig, with the air of one who was not a bit perplexed, but rather enlightened.

He pulled down the district telegraph messenger lever three times, and we sat in silence for a while.

===Chinese===

「我一点也不奇怪，」克雷格评道。「你说的承受压力，是怎么回事？」

「是这样：克洛斯的律师劳伦斯今早给编辑打了电话——想必备你也不知道，他同掌管《明星报》的那班利益集团有些瓜葛——说是《明星报》一个姓詹姆森的记者四处活动，令克洛斯先生十分反感，还说这记者雇了一个名叫肯尼迪的人做帮手。」

「我真弄不懂，克雷格，」我坦白道，「他们头一天把消息捅给报界，隔了两天，又差不多要拿官司吓唬我们，逼我们别再登。」

「是叫人纳闷，」克雷格说，那口气却毫无纳闷之意，倒像是恍然大悟。

他把地区电报信使（district telegraph messenger）的呼叫杆拉下三下，我们便默然坐了一会儿。

===Original===

“However,” he resumed, “I shall be ready for them tonight.”

I said nothing. Several minutes elapsed. Then the messenger rapped on the door.

“I want these two notes delivered right away,” said Craig to the boy; “here’s a quarter for you. Now mind you don’t get interested in a detective story and forget the notes. If you are back here quickly with the receipts I’ll give you another quarter. Now scurry along.”

Then, after the boy had gone, he said casually to me: “Two notes to Close and Gregory, asking them to be present with their attorneys tonight. Close will bring Lawrence, and Gregory will bring a young lawyer named Asche, a very clever fellow. The notes are so worded that they can hardly refuse the invitation.”

===Chinese===

「不过，」他重新开口，「今夜我会做好对付他们的准备。」

我没作声。几分钟过去了。随后，信使敲响了门。

「这两张便条要立刻送到，」克雷格对那孩子说，「给，两毛五。记着，别一头扎进什么侦探故事里，把送便条的正事给忘了。你要是带着回执麻利地回来，我再给你两毛五。快去吧。」

男孩走后，他随口对我说：「两张便条，一张给克洛斯，一张给格雷戈里，请他们今晚各带律师到场。克洛斯会带劳伦斯来，格雷戈里会带一位名叫阿舍（Asche）的年轻律师来，那人很精明。便条的措辞，叫他们想推辞都难。」

===Original===

Meanwhile I carried out an assignment for the *Star*, and telephoned my story in so as to be sure of being with Craig at the crucial moment. For I was thoroughly curious about his next move in the game. I found him still in his laboratory attaching two coils of thin wire to the connections on the outside of a queer-looking little black box.

“What’s that?” I asked, eyeing the sinister looking little box suspiciously. “An infernal machine? You’re not going to blow the culprit into eternity, I hope.”

“Never mind what it is, Walter. You’ll find that out in due time. It may or it may not be an infernal machine of a different sort than any you have probably ever heard of. The less you know now the less likely you are to give anything away by a look or an act. Come now, make yourself useful as well as ornamental. Take these wires and lay them in the cracks of the floor, and be careful not to let them show. A little dust over them will conceal them beautifully.”

Craig now placed the black box back of one of the chairs well down toward the floor, where it could hardly have been perceived unless one were suspecting something of the sort. While he was doing so I ran the wires across the floor, and around the edge of the room to the door.

“There,” he said, taking the wires from me. “Now I’ll complete the job by carrying them into the next room. And while I’m doing it, go over the wires again and make sure they are absolutely concealed.”

===Chinese===

这半天里，我替《明星报》跑了一趟差，把稿子用电话传回报社，好确保关键时刻守在克雷格身边。因为我对他这盘棋的下一步，实在好奇得很。我找到他时，他还在实验室里，正把两卷细金属丝接到一只模样古怪的小黑盒外壁的接线柱上。

「那是什么？」我问，满腹狐疑地打量着那只来者不善的小盒子。「是台暗杀炸弹（infernal machine）？您总不至于打算把真凶崩上西天去吧，我希望。」

「先别管它是什么，沃尔特。到时候你自然会明白。它也许是、也许不是一具暗杀炸弹——反正同你多半听说过的任何一种都不是一路。你现在知道得越少，泄漏的可能就越小——不论是一个眼神还是一个动作。来，别光当个摆设，也出把力。把这些电线拿去，铺进地板缝里，当心别露出痕迹。撒上一点灰，就遮得天衣无缝。」

克雷格随即把黑盒子放到一把椅子后面，紧贴着地板——若不是心里先存了这份疑心，几乎谁也察觉不到它。趁他忙着的当口，我把电线沿地板拉过去，绕着房间四边一直拉到门口。

「好了，」他说着从我手里接过电线。「现在我把它们引进隔壁房间，这活儿就算收尾。趁我干这工夫，你再把线检查一遍，务必遮得严严实实。」

===Original===

That night six men gathered in Kennedy’s laboratory. In my utter ignorance of what was about to happen I was perfectly calm, and so were all the rest, except Gregory. He was easily the most nervous of us all, though his lawyer Asche tried repeatedly to reassure him.

“Mr. Close,” began Kennedy, “if you and Mr. Lawrence will sit over here on this side of the room while Dr. Gregory and Mr. Asche sit on the opposite side with Mr. Jameson in the middle, I think both of you opposing parties will be better suited. For I apprehend that at various stages in what I am about to say both you, Mr. Close, and you, Dr. Gregory, will want to consult your attorneys. That, of course, would be embarrassing, if not impossible, should you be sitting near each other. Now, if we are ready, I shall begin.”

Kennedy placed a small leaden casket on the table of his lecture hall. “In this casket,” he commenced solemnly, “there is a certain substance which I have recovered from the dust swept up by a vacuum cleaner in the room of Mrs. Close.”

One could feel the very air of the room surcharged with excitement. Craig drew on a pair of gloves and carefully opened the casket. With his thumb and forefinger he lifted out a glass tube and held it gingerly at arm’s length. My eyes were riveted on it, for the bottom of the tube glowed with a dazzling point of light.

Both Gregory and his attorney and Close and Lawrence whispered to each other when the tube was displayed, as indeed they did throughout the whole exhibition of Kennedy’s evidence.

===Chinese===

当晚，六个人聚在肯尼迪的实验室里。我对将要发生的事一无所知，反倒镇定得很，其余各位也都如此——只有格雷戈里例外。他无疑是我们中间最最紧张的一个，尽管他的律师阿舍一再宽慰他。

「克洛斯先生，」肯尼迪开腔道，「如果您和劳伦斯先生在这边坐，格雷戈里医生和阿舍先生坐对面，詹姆森先生居中，我想你们对垒的两造都会觉得更相宜。因为我料想，在我下面这番话的各个关口，克洛斯先生您和格雷戈里医生您，都会想同各自的律师磋商。你们若坐得挨近，那自然难堪，甚至根本没法办。那么，都预备好了，我就开讲。」

肯尼迪把一只小小的铅匣放到讲堂的桌上。「这只匣子里，」他郑重其事地说，「装着一种物质，是我用吸尘器从克洛斯夫人房里扫出的灰尘中回收得到的。」

满屋的空气仿佛都充了电，紧张欲燃。克雷格戴上一副手套，小心打开铅匣。他用拇指和食指拈出一支玻璃管，小心翼翼地直臂举着。我的目光死死钉在它上面——管底正亮着一点炫目的光。

玻璃管一亮相，格雷戈里和他的律师、克洛斯和劳伦斯便交头接耳起来——其实在肯尼迪出示证据的全过程中，他们始终如此。

===Original===

“No infernal machine was ever more subtle,” said Craig, “than the tube which I hold in my hand. The imagination of the most sensational writer of fiction might well be thrilled with the mysteries of this fatal tube and its power to work fearful deeds. A larger quantity of this substance in the tube would produce on me, as I now hold it, incurable burns, just as it did on its discoverer before his death. A smaller amount, of course, would not act so quickly. The amount in this tube, if distributed about, would produce the burns inevitably, providing I remained near enough for a long-enough time.”

Craig paused a moment to emphasise his remarks.

“Here in my hand, gentlemen, I hold the price of a woman’s beauty.”

He stopped again for several moments, then resumed.

“And now, having shown it to you, for my own safety I will place it back in its leaden casket.”

===Chinese===

「再没有哪具暗杀炸弹，」克雷格说，「比我手里这支管子更阴毒的了。最爱耸人听闻的小说家，其想象力也尽可以在这致命管子的重重神秘、在它为祸的可怕伟力面前震颤一番。管中这种物质份量再多一些，此刻这样举着，便会在我的手上造成不治的灼伤——正如它在发现者生前所做的那样。份量少些，见效当然就慢些。这支管子里的量，若撒布开来，只要我贴得够近、时候够长，灼伤便注定躲不掉。」

克雷格停了停，好加重他这番话的分量。

「诸位先生，此刻我手中所握的，正是一位女子美貌的代价。」

他又停了好一会儿，才继续讲下去。

「好了，既然已给诸位看过，为自身安全计，我这就把它放回铅匣。」

===Original===

Drawing off his gloves, he proceeded.

“I have found out by a cablegram today that seven weeks ago an order for one hundred milligrams of radium bromide at thirty-five dollars a milligram from a certain person in America was filled by a corporation dealing in this substance.”

Kennedy said this with measured words, and I felt a thrill run through me as he developed his case.

“At that same time, Mrs. Close began a series of treatments with an X-ray specialist in New York,” pursued Kennedy. “Now, it is not generally known outside scientific circles, but the fact is that in their physiological effects the X-ray and radium are quite one and the same. Radium possesses this advantage, however, that no elaborate apparatus is necessary for its use. And, in addition, the emanation from radium is steady and constant, whereas the X-ray at best varies slightly with changing conditions of the current and vacuum in the X-ray tube. Still, the effects on the body are much the same.

“A few days before this order was placed I recall the following despatch which appeared in the New York papers. I will read it.

===Chinese===

他褪下手套，接着往下讲。

「今天我通过一封越洋电报查明：七个星期前，一家经营这种物质的公司，办妥了美国某君的一张订单——溴化镭（radium bromide）一百毫克，每毫克三十五美元。」

肯尼迪把这话说得字斟句酌；案情层层展开，我只觉一阵震颤流遍全身。

「正是在那前后，克洛斯夫人开始在纽约一位 X 射线专科医生那里接受一系列治疗，」肯尼迪接着说。「这件事在科学界之外知道的人不多，但事实是：就生理效应而言，X 射线同镭完全是一回事。不过镭有这么一个长处：使用它，无须任何复杂的设备。况且，镭的辐射平稳恒定，而 X 射线再好，也要随 X 射线管内电流与真空状况的起伏而略有涨落。话虽如此，二者对人体的影响大体无异。」

「订单发出前几天，我记得纽约各报登过下面这则电讯。我念给你们听。」

===Original===

“ ‘Liege, Belgium, Oct. —, 1910. What is believed to be the first criminal case in which radium figures as a death-dealing agent is engaging public attention at this university town. A wealthy old bachelor, Pailin by name, was found dead in his flat. A stroke of apoplexy was at first believed to have caused his death, but a close examination revealed a curious discolouration of his skin. A specialist called in to view the body gave as his opinion that the old man had been exposed for a long time to the emanations of X-ray or radium. The police theory is that M. Pailin was done to death by a systematic application of either X-rays or radium by a student in the university who roomed next to him. The student has disappeared.’

“Now here, I believe, was the suggestion which this American criminal followed, for I cut it out of the paper rather expecting sooner or later that some clever person would act on it. I have thoroughly examined the room of Mrs. Close. She herself told me she never wanted to return to it, that her memory of sleepless nights in it was too vivid. That served to fix the impression that I had already formed from reading this clipping. Either the X-ray or radium had caused her dermatitis and nervousness. Which was it? I wished to be sure that I would make no mistake. Of course I knew it was useless to look for an X-ray machine in or near Mrs. Close’s room. Such a thing could never have been concealed. The alternative? Radium! Ah! that was different. I determined on an experiment. Mrs. Close’s maid was prevailed on to sleep in her mistress’s room. Of course radiations of brief duration would do her no permanent harm, although they would produce their effect, nevertheless. In one night the maid became extremely nervous. If she had stayed under them several nights no doubt the beginning of a dermatitis would have affected her, if not more serious trouble. A systematic application, covering weeks and months, might in the end even have led to death.

“The next day I managed, as I have said, to go over the room thoroughly with a vacuum cleaner﻿—a new one of my own which I had bought myself. But tests of the dust which I got from the floors, curtains, and furniture showed nothing at all. As a last thought I had, however, cleaned the mattress of the bed and the cracks and crevices in the brass bars. Tests of that dust showed it to be extremely radioactive. I had the dust dissolved, by a chemist who understands that sort of thing, recrystallised, and the radium salts were extracted from the refuse. Thus I found that I had recovered all but a very few milligrams of the radium that had been originally purchased in London. Here it is in this deadly tube in the leaden casket.

“It is needless to add that the night after I had cleaned out this deadly element the maid slept the sleep of the just﻿—and would have been all right when next I saw her but for the interference of the unjust on whom I had stolen a march.”

===Chinese===

「『比利时列日（Liege），1910 年 10 月某日。镭充当夺命凶器，据信在这桩刑案中尚属首例；此案眼下正引起这座大学城公众的瞩目。一位名叫帕兰（Pailin）的富有老单身汉，被人发现死在寓所里。起初以为他死于中风，但细验尸体，发现皮肤上有一种奇怪的变色。应邀验尸的一位专家发表意见说，老人曾长期暴露在 X 射线或镭的辐射之下。警方的推测是：帕兰先生是被邻室而居的一名大学生用 X 射线或镭有系统地照射害死的。该学生已不知去向。』」

「我相信，美国这位罪犯正是从这里得的启发，因为这则剪报是我亲手从报上剪下来的，当时我就料定，迟早会有哪个聪明人照此行事。我把克洛斯夫人的房间彻底查了一遍。她亲口对我说过，她再也不愿回到那个房间；无眠之夜的记忆，对它来说太分明了。这就更坐实了我读剪报时便已形成的印象：造成她皮炎和神经衰弱的，不是 X 射线就是镭。究竟是哪一个？我务求不出差错。我当然明白，在克洛斯夫人的房间里或近旁去找一台 X 射线机是徒劳的——那种东西根本藏不住。那么另一条路呢？镭！啊！那可就不同了。我决意做一次实验。我劝动了克洛斯夫人的女仆，到女主人的房里去睡。当然，为期短暂的照射不会给她造成永久性伤害，尽管辐射照样会生效。一夜之间，女仆就变得极度神经过敏。她若一连几夜睡在其下，无疑就会初发皮炎，倘若不是更重的祸事。周复一周、月复一月的有系统照射，末了甚至可能致死。」

「第二天，我照方才说的那样，用真空吸尘器把那房间彻底清扫了一遍——一台我自己现买的新机器。可是从地板、窗帘和家具上采来的灰尘，检验下来一无所获。不过最后灵机一动，我又把床垫和铜栏的裂缝沟槽统统吸了一遍。对那批灰尘的检验表明，它的放射性极强。我请了一位精于此道的化学家，把灰尘溶解、重结晶，从渣滓中把镭盐提了出来。这才发现，当初在伦敦购进的那批镭，除寥寥几毫克外，竟尽数回收在我手里。喏，就在这铅匣里这支致命的管子中。」

「不必多说：把这件致命元素清除之后的那个夜晚，女仆睡了个正派人的酣觉——倘若不是那帮不正派的家伙从中作梗——我抢的正是他们的先手——等我再见着她时，她本该安然无恙了。」

===Original===

Craig paused while the lawyers whispered again to their clients. Then he continued: “Now three persons in this room had an opportunity to secrete the contents of this deadly tube in the crevices of the metal work of Mrs. Close’s bed. One of these persons must have placed an order through a confidential agent in London to purchase the radium from the English Radium Corporation. One of these persons had a compelling motive, something to gain by using this deadly element. The radium in this tube in the casket was secreted, as I have said, in the metal work of Mrs. Close’s bed, not in large enough quantities to be immediately fatal, but mixed with dust so as to produce the result more slowly but no less surely, and thus avoid suspicion. At the same time Mrs. Close was persuaded﻿—I will not say by whom﻿—through her natural pride, to take a course of X-ray treatment for a slight defect. That would further serve to divert suspicion. The fact is that a more horrible plot could hardly have been planned or executed. This person sought to ruin her beauty to gain a most selfish and despicable end.”

Again Craig paused to let his words sink into our minds.

“Now I wish to state that anything you gentlemen may say will be used against you. That is why I have asked you to bring your attorneys. You may consult with them, of course, while I am getting ready my next disclosure.”

As Kennedy had developed his points in the case I had been more and more amazed. But I had not failed to notice how keenly Lawrence was following him.

===Chinese===

律师们又同各自的当事人窃窃私语，克雷格便停了下来。随后他继续道：「这间屋子里，有三个人有机会把这支致命管子里的东西，藏进克洛斯夫人床架金属构件的缝隙里。这三个人当中，必有一人通过伦敦的一名心腹代办，向英国镭公司（English Radium Corporation）购下了这批镭。这三个人当中，必有一人怀着一股逼人的动机——从动用这件致命元素中有所渔利。匣子里这支管中的镭，如我所说，当时就藏在克洛斯夫人床架的金属构件里，份量不至立时毙命，而是掺在灰尘里，叫恶果来得慢些，却同样确定不移，借此躲开嫌疑。与此同时，克洛斯夫人被人劝说——我不说是被谁——出于天生的自矜爱美，为一个微不足道的瑕疵接受了一程 X 射线治疗。这就更起了转移嫌疑的作用。事实是，再难策划、再难实施出比这更狠毒的阴谋了。此人处心积虑毁掉她的美貌，为的是达成一个极端自私、极端卑鄙的目的。」

克雷格又停了停，好让这些话在我们脑子里扎根。

「现在我要声明：诸位先生无论说出什么，都将被用作对你们不利的证据。正因如此，我才请各位带上律师。当然，在我准备下一项披露的当口，你们尽可同他们磋商。」

肯尼迪步步推进案情，我越听越是惊愕。但我也没有忽略，劳伦斯正何等锋利地步步紧随着他的每一句话。

===Original===

With half a sneer on his astute face, Lawrence drawled: “I cannot see that you have accomplished anything by this rather extraordinary summoning of us to your laboratory. The evidence is just as black against Dr. Gregory as before. You may think you’re clever, Kennedy, but on the very statement of facts as you have brought them out there is plenty of circumstantial evidence against Gregory﻿—more than there was before. As for anyone else in the room, I can’t see that you have anything on us﻿—unless perhaps this new evidence you speak of may implicate Asche, or Jameson,” he added, including me in a wave of his hand, as if he were already addressing a jury. “It’s my opinion that twelve of our peers would be quite as likely to bring in a verdict of guilty against them as against anyone else even remotely connected with this case, except Gregory. No, you’ll have to do better than this in your next case, if you expect to maintain that so-called reputation of yours for being a professor of criminal science.”

As for Close, taking his cue from his attorney, he scornfully added: “I came to find out some new evidence against the wretch who wrecked the beauty of my wife. All I’ve got is a tiresome lecture on X-rays and radium. I suppose what you say is true. Well, it only bears out what I thought before. Gregory treated my wife at home, after he saw the damage his office treatments had done. I guess he was capable of making a complete job out of it﻿—covering up his carelessness by getting rid of the woman who was such a damning piece of evidence against his professional skill.”

Never a shade passed Craig’s face as he listened to this tirade. “Excuse me a moment,” was all he said, opening the door to leave the room. “I have just one more fact to disclose. I will be back directly.”

Kennedy was gone several minutes, during which Close and Lawrence fell to whispering behind their hands, with the assurance of those who believed that this was only Kennedy’s method of admitting a defeat. Gregory and Asche exchanged a few words similarly, and it was plain that Asche was endeavouring to put a better interpretation on something than Gregory himself dared hope.

As Kennedy reentered, Close was buttoning up his coat preparatory to leaving, and Lawrence was lighting a fresh cigar.

===Chinese===

劳伦斯那张精明的脸上挂着半分讥诮，拖长了声音说：「我看不出，您把我们这般稀奇地召到实验室来，究竟办成了什么。对格雷戈里医生不利的证据，还同先前一样黑得很。您也许自以为高明，肯尼迪；可单就您摆出的事实而论，对格雷戈里的间接证据有的是——比先前还多。至于屋里旁的人，我看您抓不到我们半点把柄——除非您口口声声的什么新证据，牵连的是阿舍，或者詹姆森，」他补了一句，手一挥把我扫了进去，俨然已经在向陪审团致辞。「依我看，由十二位同侪组成的陪审团，裁他们有罪的可能，同裁这案子里任何沾点边的人一样大——只有格雷戈里例外。不成的。您要想保住您那块所谓犯罪侦查学教授的招牌，下一个案子可得露一手比这高明的。」

至于克洛斯，他跟着律师的调子，轻蔑地加了一句：「我来，是要找一找新证据，对付那个毁了我妻子容貌的恶棍。结果只落得听一堂 X 射线和镭的闷课。我想您讲的都对。好啊，这不过印证了我先前的想法。格雷戈里在诊所里治坏了我妻子之后，又上家里来治。我琢磨他是存心把事做绝——把这位活证除掉，好遮掩他的粗心，因为她那副模样，本身就是砸他行医手艺的一桩铁证。」

听着这通抢白，克雷格脸上纹丝不动。「失陪片刻，」他只说了这一句，开门出了屋。「我还有最后一桩事实要披露。去去就回。」

肯尼迪去了几分钟。这工夫，克洛斯和劳伦斯遮着嘴交头接耳，一副笃定这不过是肯尼迪变相认输的神气。格雷戈里和阿舍也交谈了几句，看得出，阿舍正竭力把一件事朝好处解释——好处连格雷戈里自己都不敢奢望。

肯尼迪再进来时，克洛斯正在扣大衣扣子准备动身，劳伦斯正点一支新雪茄。

===Original===

In his hand Kennedy held a notebook. “My stenographer writes a very legible shorthand; at least I find it so﻿—from long practice, I suppose. As I glance over her notes I find many facts which will interest you later﻿—at the trial. But﻿—ah, here at the end﻿—let me read:

“ ‘Well, he’s very clever, but he has nothing against me, has he?’

“ ‘But he can’t do that. No one could ever have recognised you on your flying trip to London disguised as a diamond merchant who had just learned that he could make his faulty diamonds good by applications of radium and who wanted a good stock of the stuff.’

“ ‘Still, we’ll have to drop the suit against Gregory after all, in spite of what I said. That part is hopelessly spoiled.’

「『是啊，我也这么想。哦，好了，我如今脱身了。她这下没法不答应离婚了，外加一笔悄悄了结的赡养。这是她自找的——别的法子我们都试遍了，可她——她太贞静，不肯入彀。是她逼我们走的这一步。』

「『是啊，这下你能顺顺当当离婚了。可咱们能不能叫肯尼迪这家伙闭嘴？哪怕他拿不到证据，这种事只消走漏一点风声，传到塔尔金顿太太耳朵里，也是不妙的。』

===Original===

Close’s face was livid. “It’s a pack of lies!” he shouted, advancing toward Kennedy, “a pack of lies! You are a fakir and a blackmailer. I’ll have you in jail for this, by God﻿—and you too, Gregory.”

“One moment, please,” said Kennedy calmly. “Mr. Lawrence, will you be so kind as to reach behind your chair? What do you find?”

Lawrence lifted up the plain black box and with it he pulled up the wires which I had so carefully concealed in the cracks of the floor.

“That,” said Kennedy, “is a little instrument called the microphone. Its chief merit lies in the fact that it will magnify a sound sixteen hundred times, and carry it to any given point where you wish to place the receiver. Originally this device was invented for the aid of the deaf, but I see no reason why it should not be used to aid the law. One needn’t eavesdrop at the keyhole with this little instrument about. Inside that box there is nothing but a series of plugs from which wires, much finer than a thread, are stretched taut. Yet a fly walking near it will make a noise as loud as a draft-horse. If the microphone is placed in any part of the room, especially if near the persons talking﻿—even if they are talking in a whisper﻿—a whisper such as occurred several times during the evening and particularly while I was in the next room getting the notes made by my stenographer﻿—a whisper, I say, is like shouting your guilt from the housetops.

“You two men, Close and Lawrence, may consider yourselves under arrest for conspiracy and whatever other indictments will lie against such creatures as you. The police will be here in a moment. No, Close, violence won’t do now. The doors are locked﻿—and see, we are four to two.”

===Chinese===

克洛斯的脸涨成了猪肝色。「一派胡言！」他吼着，朝肯尼迪逼过去，「一派胡言！你是个骗子，是个敲诈勒索的。老天在上，我要叫你为这个蹲大牢——还有你，格雷戈里。」

「请稍安片刻，」肯尼迪平静地说。「劳伦斯先生，劳驾您往椅子背后伸手摸一摸，摸着了什么？」

劳伦斯提起那只其貌不扬的黑盒子，连带着拽出一束电线——正是我方才千小心万小心藏进地板缝里的那些。

「这件东西，」肯尼迪说，「是一样小小的仪器，名叫麦克风（microphone）。它最要紧的长处，在于能把声音放大一千六百倍，并送到你愿意安放接收器的任何指定地点。这装置当初是发明来帮助聋人的，可我看不出，它为什么不能用来帮助法律。有了这件小仪器，就用不着趴在锁孔上偷听了。那盒子里面空无一物，只有一排小柱，柱间绷得笔直的金属丝，比一根线还要细得多。然而一只苍蝇在近旁爬动，响动便大得像一匹拉车的大马。把麦克风放在屋里任何地方，尤其放在说话人近旁——哪怕他们讲的是悄悄话——今晚就有过好几回这样的悄悄话，尤其是我在隔壁房间取速记员的记录的那阵子——我说，一句悄悄话，就等于把你们的罪状嚷到了房顶上。」

「你们两位，克洛斯和劳伦斯，可以视自己已因阴谋罪被捕，此外还有什么罪名能安到你们这等人头上，一并照算。警察转眼就到。不，克洛斯，如今动蛮是没有用的。门都锁上了——而且你瞧，我们四对二。」

## The Diamond Maker / 钻石制造者

===Original===

“I’ve called, Professor Kennedy, to see if we can retain you in a case which I am sure will tax even your resources. Heaven knows it has taxed ours.”

The visitor was a large, well-built man. He placed his hat on the table and, without taking off his gloves, sat down in an easy chair which he completely filled.

“Andrews is my name﻿—third vice-president of the Great Eastern Life Insurance Company. I am the nominal head of the company’s private detective force, and though I have some pretty clever fellows on my staff we’ve got a case that, so far, none of us has been able to unravel. I’d like to consult you about it.”

Kennedy expressed his entire willingness to be consulted, and after the usual formalities were over, Mr. Andrews proceeded.

===Chinese===

「肯尼迪教授，我来拜访，是想看看能不能聘请您承办一件案子——我敢说，这案子即便动用您的全部本事，怕也要大费周章。天知道，它已经把我们折腾得够呛了。」

来客是个身材魁伟的壮硕男子。他把帽子往桌上一搁，手套也没摘，就在一把安乐椅里落了座——整个人把那椅子填得满满当当。

「我姓安德鲁斯（Andrews）——大东方人寿保险公司的第三副总裁。公司自设的私家侦探班子，名义上归我统领。虽说手下也有几个相当精明干练的人，可眼下这桩案子，到现在谁也没能理出个头绪来。我想向您讨教一二。」

肯尼迪表示极愿效劳；寻常客套一过，安德鲁斯先生便讲入了正题。

===Original===

“I suppose you are aware that the large insurance companies maintain quite elaborate detective forces and follow very keenly such of the cases of their policyholders as look at all suspicious. This case which I wish to put in your hands is that of Mr. Solomon Morowitch, a wealthy Maiden Lane jeweller. I suppose you have read something in the papers about his sudden death and the strange robbery of his safe?”

“Very little,” replied Craig. “There hasn’t been much to read.”

“Of course not, of course not,” said Mr. Andrews with some show of gratification. “I flatter myself that we have pulled the wires so as to keep the thing out of the papers as much as possible. We don’t want to frighten the quarry till the net is spread. The point is, though, to find out who is the quarry. It’s most baffling.”

===Chinese===

「想必您知道，那些大保险公司都养着相当完备的侦探班子，凡投保人的案子有半点可疑之处，他们无不紧盯不放。我想交到您手上的这桩案子，事关所罗门·莫罗维奇（Solomon Morowitch）先生——梅登巷一位富有的珠宝商。他猝然离世、保险箱又遭离奇劫掠的事，报上想必您多少读到过一点吧？」

「读到得很少，」克雷格答道，「本来报上也没登多少。」

「当然没有，当然没有，」安德鲁斯先生面有得色地说，「不客气地讲，是我们暗中疏通，才尽量把这事摁在了报章之外。网还没有张好，我们不想惊走猎物。只是问题在于，猎物究竟是谁。这事实在扑朔迷离。」

===Original===

“I am at your service,” interposed Craig quietly, “but you will have to enlighten me as to the facts in the case. As to that, I know no more than the newspapers.”

“Oh, certainly, certainly. That is to say, you know nothing at all and can approach it without bias.” He paused and then, seeming to notice something in Craig’s manner, added hastily: “I’ll be perfectly frank with you. The policy in question is for one hundred thousand dollars, and is incontestable. His wife is the beneficiary. The company is perfectly willing to pay, but we want to be sure that it is all straight first. There are certain suspicious circumstances that in justice to ourselves we think should be cleared up. That is all﻿—believe me. We are not seeking to avoid an honest liability.”

“What are these suspicious circumstances?” asked Craig, apparently satisfied with the explanation.

===Chinese===

「愿听差遣，」克雷格不动声色地插话道，「不过案情内情，还得请您给我指点指点。这方面，我知道的不比报纸多。」

「哦，当然，当然。这么说吧，您对此一无所知，正好可以不带先入之见地着手查办。」他顿了顿，似乎觉察到克雷格神色有异，忙又补上一句：「我愿对您坦诚相告。这张保单保额十万美元，且已过争议期。受益人是他的妻子。公司完全愿意赔付，只是想先把事情弄个确实。有几处可疑的情节，为了对公司自己有个交代，我们认为理应查个清楚。仅此而已——请相信我。我们绝无逃避一笔正当赔付责任的意思。」

「可疑的情节指什么？」克雷格问，看来对这番解释已经满意。

===Original===

“This is in strict confidence, gentlemen,” began Mr. Andrews. “Mr. Morowitch, according to the story as it comes to us, returned home late one night last week, apparently from his office, in a very weakened, a semiconscious, condition. His family physician, Doctor Thornton, was summoned, not at once, but shortly. He pronounced Mr. Morowitch to be suffering from a congestion of the lungs that was very like a sudden attack of pneumonia.

“Mr. Morowitch had at once gone to bed, or at least was in bed, when the doctor arrived, but his condition grew worse so rapidly that the doctor hastily resorted to oxygen, under which treatment he seemed to revive. The doctor had just stepped out to see another patient when a hurry call was sent to him that Mr. Morowitch was rapidly sinking. He died before the doctor could return. No statement whatever concerning the cause of his sudden illness was made by Mr. Morowitch, and the death-certificate, a copy of which I have, gives pneumonia as the cause of death. One of our men has seen Doctor Thornton, but has been able to get nothing out of him. Mrs. Morowitch was the only person with her, husband at the time.”

There was something in his tone that made me take particular note of this last fact, especially as he paused for an instant.

===Chinese===

「以下的话，二位务须严守秘密。」安德鲁斯先生开了腔。「据我们听到的说法，上礼拜一天深夜，莫罗维奇先生回到了家——看样子是从铺子里回来——人已虚弱不堪，处于半昏迷状态。他的家庭医生桑顿（Doctor Thornton）被请了去，不是立刻，但时隔不久。医生诊断莫罗维奇先生患的是肺充血，极像肺炎急性发作。

「医生赶到时，莫罗维奇先生已经上了床——或者至少已经躺在了床上——但病情恶化得极快，医生赶紧用上氧气，经这一治，他似乎有了起色。医生刚出门去看另一位病人，便有人急报：莫罗维奇先生眼看不行了。还没等医生赶回来，人就去了。莫罗维奇先生对自己突然发病的缘由，只字未曾留下；而死亡证明书——副本就在我手里——写的死因是肺炎。我们的人已经见过桑顿医生，却没能从他嘴里掏出半句话。当时守在丈夫身边的，只有莫罗维奇夫人一个人。」

他话音里有种什么东西，令我对最后这一节格外留了心，何况说到此处他还特意顿了一顿。

===Original===

“Now, perhaps there would be nothing surprising about it all, so far at least, were it not for the fact that the following morning, when his junior partner, Mr. Kahan, opened the place of business, or rather went to it, for it was to remain closed, of course, he found that during the night someone had visited it. The lock on the great safe, which contained thousands of dollars’ worth of diamonds, was intact; but in the top of the safe a huge hole was found﻿—an irregular, round hole, big enough to put your foot through. Imagine it, Professor Kennedy, a great hole in a safe that is made of chrome steel, a safe that, short of a safety-deposit vault, ought to be about the strongest thing on earth.

“Why, that steel would dull and splinter even the finest diamond-drill before it made an impression. The mere taking out and refitting of drills into the brace would be a most lengthy process. Eighteen or twenty hours is the time by actual test which it would take to bore such a hole through those laminated plates, even if there were means of exerting artificial pressure. As for the police, they haven’t even a theory yet.”

“And the diamonds?”

===Chinese===

「喏，至少到此为止，这一切原本也许没什么可怪的——坏就坏在第二天早晨。他的小合伙人卡恩先生（Mr. Kahan）第二天早晨到铺子里去——不如说是走到铺子跟前，因为铺子当然一直关着没有营业——却发现夜里有人进去过。那座大保险箱里存放着价值数千美元的钻石，箱门上的锁完好无损；可保险箱顶上却给人开出一个大窟窿——一个不规则的圆洞，大得能塞进一只脚去。您想想吧，肯尼迪教授，一座铬钢打造的保险箱上开出一个大洞！除了银行保管库，这种保险箱该算是世上顶坚固的东西了。

「嘿，那种钢，就算用最好的金刚石钻头去钻，不等它留下印痕，钻头自己先钝了、裂了。光是把钻头从钻柄里退出来再换上新的，就是旷日持久的功夫。按实际测试，要在那些叠层钢板上钻出这样一个洞，就算有办法施加人工压力，也得十八到二十个钟头。至于警方，他们到如今连个像样的推测都还没立起来。」

「那钻石呢？」

===Original===

“All gone﻿—everything of any value was gone. Even the letter-files were ransacked. His desk was broken open, and papers of some nature had been taken out of it. Thorough is no name for the job. Isn’t that enough to arouse suspicion?”

“I should like to see that safe,” was all Kennedy said.

“So you shall, so you shall,” said Mr. Andrews. “Then we may retain you in our service? My car is waiting downstairs. We can go right down to Maiden Lane if you wish.”

===Chinese===

「全没了——凡是值点钱的东西，一件不剩。连信函夹都给翻了个底朝天。写字台被撬开，若干文件被人取走。那桩活儿干净彻底得没法形容。这还不足以引人起疑吗？」

「我倒想亲眼看看那座保险箱。」肯尼迪只说了这一句。

「一定让您看，一定让您看，」安德鲁斯先生说，「这么说，我们算是聘定您了？我的车就在楼下候着。您若愿意，咱们这就去梅登巷。」

===Original===

“You may retain me on one condition,” said Craig without moving. “I am to be free to get at the truth whether it benefits or hurts the company, and the case is to be entirely in my hands.”

“Hats on,” agreed Mr. Andrews, reaching in his vest pocket and pulling out three or four brevas. “My chauffeur is quite a driver. He can almost beat the subway down.”

“First, to my laboratory,” interposed Craig. “It will take only a few minutes.”

===Chinese===

「聘我可以，但有一个条件，」克雷格坐着没动，「无论真相于公司有利还是有损，我都必须放手去查；而且案子要全权交到我手里。」

「一言为定，」安德鲁斯先生应道，一面把手伸进马甲口袋，掏出三四支布雷瓦雪茄（brevas）。「我的司机开车有两下子，去下城差不多能赛过地铁。」

「先去一趟我的实验室，」克雷格插话道，「用不了几分钟。」

===Original===

We drove up to the university and stopped on the campus while Craig hurried into the Chemistry Building to get something.

“I like your professor of criminal science,” said Andrews to me, blowing a huge fragrant cloud of smoke.

I, for my part, liked the vice-president. He was a man who seemed thoroughly to enjoy life, to have most of the good things, and a capacity for getting out of them all that was humanly possible. He seemed to be particularly enjoying this Morowitch case.

===Chinese===

车开到大学，停在校园里，克雷格匆匆跑进化学楼去取点东西。

「我喜欢你们这位犯罪侦查学教授，」安德鲁斯对我说，喷出一大团芬芳的雪茄烟雾。

就我而言，我也喜欢这位副总裁。此人似乎把生活咀嚼得有滋有味，世上的好处大半占尽，还有一副能从这些好处里榨出人间一切乐趣的本事。眼下这桩莫罗维奇案，他看起来就经营得格外起劲。

===Original===

“He has solved some knotty cases,” was all I said. “I’ve come to believe there is no limit to his resourcefulness.”

“I hope not. He’s up against a tough one this trip, though, my boy.”

I did not even resent the “my boy.” Andrews was one of those men in whom we newspaper writers instinctively believe. I knew that it would be “pens lifted” only so long as the case was incomplete. When the time comes with such men they are ready to furnish us the best copy in the world.

===Chinese===

「他破过不少难解的案子，」我只说了这么一句，「我已经开始相信，他的急智没有穷尽。」

「但愿如此。不过这一趟，他可是碰上了块硬骨头，我的老弟。」

连那一声「老弟」我也没觉得刺耳。安德鲁斯属于我们报人本能信得过的那种人。我知道，只要案子未了，他们便会「搁笔不写」；而一旦时机到来，这种人随时肯把天下最好的报道素材拱手奉上。

===Original===

Kennedy quickly rejoined us, carrying a couple of little glass bottles with ground-glass stoppers.

Morowitch & Co. was, of course, closed when we arrived, but we had no trouble in being admitted by the Central Office man who had been detailed to lock the barn door after the horse was stolen. It was precisely as Mr. Andrews had said. Mr. Kahan showed us the safe. Through the top a great hole had been made﻿—I say made, for at the moment I was at a loss to know whether it had been cut, drilled, burned, blown out, or whatnot.

Kennedy examined the edges of the hole carefully, and just the trace of a smile of satisfaction flitted over his face as he did so. Without saying a word he took the glass stopper out of the larger bottle which he had brought and poured the contents on the top of the safe near the hole. There it lay, a little mound of reddish powder.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪很快回到我们身边，手里拿着两个带磨口玻璃塞的小玻璃瓶。

我们到时，莫罗维奇公司（Morowitch & Co.）自然大门紧闭，不过守在那里的中央办公厅探员让我们进去没费半点周折——派他来，本就是贼去关门的意思。情形同安德鲁斯先生说的分毫不差。卡恩先生领我们看了那座保险箱。箱顶给弄出了一个大洞——我说「弄出」，是因为当时我实在说不上来，那洞究竟是切出来的、钻出来的、烧穿的、炸开的，还是别的什么手法弄出来的。

肯尼迪仔细察看洞口边缘；察看之际，一丝满意的笑意从他脸上一掠而过。他一言不发，拧开随身带来的那个大些的瓶子的玻璃塞，把里面的东西倒在洞口近旁的箱顶上。那是小小一堆微微发红的粉末。

===Original===

Kennedy took a little powder of another kind from the other bottle and lighted it with a match.

“Stand back﻿—close to the wall,” he called as he dropped the burning mass on the red powder. In two or three leaps he joined us at the far end of the room.

Almost instantly a dazzling, intense flame broke out, and sizzled and crackled. With bated breath we watched. It was almost incredible, but that glowing mass of powder seemed literally to be sinking, sinking right down into the cold steel. In tense silence we waited. On the ceiling we could still see the reflection of the molten mass in the cup which it had burned for itself in the top of the safe.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪又从另一个瓶子里取了少许别样的粉末，用火柴点着。

「退后——贴墙站好！」他喊着，一面把那团燃着的粉末丢进红粉堆里，一面三步并作两步蹿到房间另一头，同我们站在一处。

几乎就在一瞬之间，一团炫目的烈焰腾起，嘶嘶作响，噼啪爆裂。我们屏住呼吸看着。那情景几乎叫人难以置信：那团炽亮的粉末仿佛真的在往下沉，径直沉进冰冷的钢铁里去。我们屏息静气地等着。天花板上，仍能映出那摊熔融之物的反光——它已在保险箱顶上给自己烧出了一个凹坑。

===Original===

At last it fell through into the safe﻿—fell as the burning roof of a frame building would fall into the building. No one spoke a word, but as we cautiously peered over the top of the safe we instinctively turned to Kennedy for an explanation. The Central Office man, with eyes as big as half-dollars, acted almost as if he would have liked to clap the irons on Kennedy. For there in the top of the safe was another hole, smaller but identical in nature with the first one.

“Thermite,” was all Kennedy said.

“Thermite?” echoed Andrews, shifting the cigar which he had allowed to go out in the excitement.

===Chinese===

末了，它塌了进去，落进保险箱里——就像木架房屋烧塌的屋顶落进屋子里那样。没有一个人出声，可当我们小心翼翼探头去看箱顶时，却都本能地转向肯尼迪，等他解释。那位中央办公厅探员瞪着半美元硬币般大的眼睛，那副神情，几乎恨不得当场就把手铐给肯尼迪铐上。因为保险箱顶上又多了一个洞，比头一个小些，性质却一模一样。

「铝热剂（thermite）。」肯尼迪只说了这一句。

「铝热剂？」安德鲁斯跟着念了一遍，挪了挪嘴里的雪茄——方才一激动，雪茄早就灭了。

===Original===

“Yes, an invention of a chemist named Goldschmidt, of Essen, Germany. It is a compound of iron oxide, such as comes off a blacksmith’s anvil or the rolls of a rolling-mill, and powdered metallic aluminum. You could thrust a red-hot bar into it without setting it off, but when you light a little magnesium powder and drop it on thermite, a combustion is started that quickly reaches fifty-four hundred degrees Fahrenheit. It has the peculiar property of concentrating its heat to the immediate spot on which it is placed. It is one of the most powerful oxidising agents known, and it doesn’t even melt the rest of the steel surface. You see how it ate its way through the steel. Either black or red thermite will do the trick equally well.”

No one said anything. There was nothing to say.

===Chinese===

「不错。这是德国埃森一位名叫戈德施密特（Goldschmidt）的化学家的发明。它由氧化铁——比如铁匠砧子上、轧钢机轧辊上崩落下来的那种——和金属铝粉混合而成。把一根烧红的铁条捅进去，它也不会起火；可只要点着一小撮镁粉，往铝热剂上一丢，便会引发一场燃烧，温度顷刻高达华氏五千四百度。它有一种奇特的性质：热量能全然集中到它所放置的那一小点上。它是已知最强的氧化剂之一，连四周的钢面都不曾熔化。您都看见它是怎样把钢铁『啃』穿的了。黑色或红色铝热剂，干这桩事同样在行。」

谁都没有作声。也没什么可说的。

===Original===

“Someone uncommonly clever, or instructed by someone uncommonly clever, must have done that job,” added Craig. “Well, there is nothing more to be done here,” he added, after a cursory look about the office. “Mr. Andrews, may I have a word with you? Come on, Jameson. Good day, Mr. Kahan. Good day, Officer.”

Outside we stopped for a moment at the door of Andrews’s car.

“I shall want to see Mr. Morowitch’s papers at home,” said Craig, “and also to call on Doctor Thornton. Do you think I shall have any difficulty?”

===Chinese===

「干这活儿的，必是个绝顶聪明的人，或是受了高人指点，」克雷格补了一句，随后在办公室里草草环视了一周，又道：「好了，这里没什么可做的了。安德鲁斯先生，借一步说话？走吧，詹姆森。卡恩先生，再见。警官，再见。」

到了外面，我们在安德鲁斯的车门前略停了片刻。

「我要看看莫罗维奇先生留在家里的文件，」克雷格说，「还要去拜访桑顿医生。您看会有什么为难吗？」

===Original===

“Not at all,” replied Mr. Andrews, “not at all. I will go with you myself and see that you have none. Say, Professor Kennedy,” he broke out, “that was marvellous. I never dreamed such a thing was possible. But don’t you think you could have learned something more up there in the office by looking around?”

“I did learn it,” answered Kennedy. “The lock on the door was intact﻿—whoever did the job let himself in by a key. There is no other way to get in.”

Andrews gave a low whistle and glanced involuntarily up at the window with the sign of Morowitch & Co. in gold letters several floors above.

===Chinese===

「一点也不，」安德鲁斯先生答道，「一点也不。我亲自陪您去，保管叫您处处畅通。哎，我说肯尼迪教授，」他忽然嚷起来，「方才那一手真叫绝了。我做梦也想不到世上竟有这种事。不过您不觉得，刚才在楼上办公室里多转一转、多看一看，还能再多查出点什么来吗？」

「我查出来了，」肯尼迪答道，「大门上的锁完好无损——干这活儿的人是拿钥匙自己进来的。此外再无别的进路。」

安德鲁斯低低吹了声口哨，不由自主地抬头瞥了一眼几层楼上方那扇窗——莫罗维奇公司的鎏金字号就悬在那里。

===Original===

“Don’t look up. I think that was Kahan looking out at us,” he said, fixing his eyes on his cigar. “I wonder if he knows more about this than he has told! He was the ‘company,’ you know, but his interest in the business was only very slight. By George﻿—”

“Not too fast, Mr. Andrews,” interrupted Craig. “We have still to see Mrs. Morowitch and the doctor before we form any theories.”

“A very handsome woman, too,” said Andrews, as we seated ourselves in the car: “A good deal younger than Morowitch. Say, Kahan isn’t a bad-looking chap, either, is he? I hear he was a very frequent visitor at his partner’s house. Well, which first, Mrs. M. or the doctor?”

===Chinese===

「别抬头。我看方才那是卡恩在朝下瞧咱们，」他眼睛盯着自己手里的雪茄说。「不知道他是不是还有所隐瞒！要知道，『莫罗维奇公司』里那个『公司』，挂的就是他的名，可他在这份产业里的利益却轻得很。老天——」

「先别急着下断语，安德鲁斯先生，」克雷格打断他，「见过莫罗维奇夫人和医生，咱们再立理论不迟。」

「那还是位非常标致的女人呢，」我们在车里坐定时，安德鲁斯说道，「比莫罗维奇年轻一大截。哎，卡恩那小子长相也不赖，对不对？听说他是合伙人府上的常客。那么，先去哪一头——莫罗维奇夫人，还是医生？」

===Original===

“The house,” answered Craig.

Mr. Andrews introduced us to Mrs. Morowitch, who was in very deep mourning, which served, as I could not help noticing, rather to heighten than lessen her beauty. By contrast it brought out the rich deep colour of her face and the graceful lines of her figure. She was altogether a very attractive young widow.

She seemed to have a sort of fear of Andrews, whether merely because he represented the insurance company on which so much depended or because there were other reasons for fear, I could not, of course, make out. Andrews was very courteous and polite, yet I caught myself asking if it was not a professional rather than a personal politeness. Remembering his stress on the fact that she was alone with her husband when he died, it suddenly flashed across my mind that somewhere I had read of a detective who, as his net was being woven about a victim, always grew more and more ominously polite toward the victim. I know that Andrews suspected her of a close connection with the case. As for myself, I don’t know what I suspected as yet.

===Chinese===

「去她家。」克雷格答道。

安德鲁斯先生把我们引见给莫罗维奇夫人。她一身重孝，而正如我不由自主留意到的，这孝服非但不减其姿色，反倒更添了几分。相衬之下，她脸庞上浓丽深沉的颜色、身段婀娜的线条，都愈发显豁。总而言之，是位极有魅力的年轻寡妇。

她似乎对安德鲁斯有几分惧意——究竟仅仅因为他代表着那家干系重大的保险公司，还是别有害怕的理由，我自然无从断定。安德鲁斯客气周到、彬彬有礼，可我不禁暗自掂量：这份礼貌，究竟是出于职业，还是出于私人情谊？想起他曾特意强调，她丈夫咽气时只有她一人在场，我脑中蓦地闪过从前在哪里读到过的一位侦探——那张网渐渐罩向猎物时，他对猎物总是越来越客气，客气得叫人心头发毛。我知道，安德鲁斯疑心她与本案干系非浅。至于我自己——眼下还谈不上疑心什么。

===Original===

No objection was offered to our request to examine Mr. Morowitch’s personal effects in the library, and accordingly Craig ransacked the desk and the letter-file. There was practically nothing to be discovered.

“Had Mr. Morowitch ever received any threats of robbery?” asked Craig, as he stood before the desk.

“Not that I know of,” replied Mrs. Morowitch. “Of course every jeweller who carries a large stock of diamonds must be careful. But I don’t think my husband had any special reason to fear robbery. At least he never said anything about it. Why do you ask?”

===Chinese===

我们要查看书房中莫罗维奇先生遗物的请求，没有受到任何阻拦；于是克雷格把写字台和信函夹翻了个遍。几乎一无所获。

「莫罗维奇先生生前可曾收到过什么抢劫的恐吓？」克雷格站在写字台前问。

「据我所知没有，」莫罗维奇夫人答道，「当然，凡是囤着大宗钻石的珠宝商，都得处处小心。可我不觉得我丈夫有什么特别的理由怕人抢劫。至少他从不曾提起过。您为什么这样问？」

===Original===

“Oh, nothing. I merely thought there might be some hint as to the motives of the robbery,” said Craig. He was fingering one of those desk-calendars which have separate leaves for each day with blank spaces for appointments.

“ ‘Close deal Poissan,’ ” he read slowly from one of the entries, as if to himself. “That’s strange. It was the correspondence under the letter *P* that was destroyed at the office, and there is nothing in the letter-file here, either. Who was Poissan?”

Mrs. Morowitch hesitated, either from ignorance or from a desire to evade the question. “A chemist, I think,” she said doubtfully. “My husband had some dealings with him﻿—some discovery he was going to buy. I don’t know anything about it. I thought the deal was off.”

===Chinese===

「哦，没什么。我只是想，那桩劫案的动机方面，或许能寻出些端倪，」克雷格说。他手里正摩挲着一本活页台历——一天一页、页上留有记事空白的那种。

「『普瓦松交易谈成（Close deal Poissan）』，」他缓缓地念着其中一条记事，仿佛在自言自语。「这就怪了。办公室里被销毁的，正是字母 *P* 底下的那部分来往信件，这里的信函夹里同样一无所有。普瓦松（Poissan）是谁？」

莫罗维奇夫人迟疑了，不知是因为不知情，还是有意回避这个问题。「记得是位化学家吧，」她迟疑地说，「我丈夫同他有些生意往来——好像要买他一项什么发明。内情我一概不知。我还当那笔买卖早告吹了呢。」

===Original===

“The deal?”

“Really, Mr. Kennedy, you had better ask Mr. Kahan. My husband talked very, little to me about business affairs.”

“But what was the discovery?”

===Chinese===

「那笔买卖？」

「真的，肯尼迪先生，这些您最好去问卡恩先生。生意上的事，我丈夫向来很少同我讲。」

「可那项发明究竟是什么？」

===Original===

“I don’t know. I only heard Mr. Morowitch and Mr. Kahan refer to some deal about a discovery regarding diamonds.”

“Then Mr. Kahan knows about it?”

“I presume so.”

“Thank you, Mrs. Morowitch,” said Kennedy, when it was evident that she either could not or would not add anything to what she had said. “Pardon us for causing all this trouble.”

===Chinese===

「我不知道。我只听莫罗维奇先生和卡恩先生提起过，有一桩关于钻石发明的买卖。」

「那么卡恩先生知道内情？」

「想必是的。」

「谢谢您，莫罗维奇夫人。」见她显然要么说不出、要么不愿再多说一句，肯尼迪便道，「多有搅扰，还望包涵。」

===Original===

“No trouble at all,” she replied graciously, though I could see she was intent on every word and motion of Kennedy and Andrews.

Kennedy stopped the car at a drugstore a few blocks away and asked for the business telephone directory. In an instant, under chemists, he put his finger on the name of Poissan﻿—“Henri Poissan, electric furnaces﻿—William St.,” he read.

“I shall visit him tomorrow morning. Now for the doctor.”

===Chinese===

「谈不上搅扰。」她和颜悦色地答道，虽然我看得出，肯尼迪和安德鲁斯的一言一动，她都在屏息留意。

肯尼迪让车在几个街区外的一家药房门前停下，要来行业电话簿。转眼之间，他的手指便按在了化学家一栏里普瓦松的名字上——「亨利·普瓦松（Henri Poissan），电炉（electric furnace）——威廉街（William St.）」，他念道。

「明天上午我去拜访他。现在去见医生。」

===Original===

Doctor Thornton was an excellent specimen of the genus physician to the wealthy﻿—polished, cool, suave. One of Mr. Andrews’s men, as I have said, had seen him already, but the interview had been very unsatisfactory. Evidently, however, the doctor had been turning something over in his mind since then and had thought better of it. At any rate, his manner was cordial enough now.

As he closed the doors to his office, he began to pace the floor. “Mr. Andrews,” he said, “I am in some doubt whether I had better tell you or the coroner what I know. There are certain professional secrets that a doctor must, as a duty to his patients, conceal. That is professional ethics. But there are also cases when, as a matter of public policy, a doctor should speak out.”

He stopped and faced us.

===Chinese===

桑顿医生是「富人的医生」这一属里的上好标本——圆熟、冷静、和婉。前面说过，安德鲁斯先生的一名手下已经见过他，但那番晤谈很不尽如人意。不过显而易见，自那以后，医生心里又反复掂量过什么，终于改了主意。至少，他此刻的态度已够热络了。

他掩上诊室的门，开始来回踱步。「安德鲁斯先生，」他说，「我正拿不定主意，该把我知道的讲给您，还是讲给验尸官。有些职业上的秘密，出于对病人的责任，医生必须保守。这是职业道德。但也有些时候，出于公益，医生理应直言。」

他停下来，面对着我们。

===Original===

“I don’t mind telling you that I dislike the publicity that would attend any statement I might make to the coroner.”

“Exactly,” said Andrews. “I appreciate your position exactly. Your other patients would not care to see you involved in a scandal﻿—or at least you would not care to have them see you so involved, with all the newspaper notoriety such a thing brings.”

Doctor Thornton shot a quick glance at Andrews, as if he would like to know just how much his visitor knew or suspected.

===Chinese===

「不瞒各位说，我不喜欢向验尸官作证必然招来的那阵轰动。」

「正是，」安德鲁斯说，「您的处境我一清二楚。您那些主顾绝不愿意看见您卷进丑闻——或者至少，您绝不愿意让他们看见您卷进去，还闹得满城报章、尽人皆知。」

桑顿医生飞快地瞥了安德鲁斯一眼，仿佛很想知道这位来客究竟知道多少、又疑心到了几分。

===Original===

Andrews drew a paper from his pocket. “This is a copy of the death-certificate,” he said. “The Board of Health has furnished it to us. Our physicians at the insurance company tell me it is rather extraordinarily vague. A word from us calling the attention of the proper authorities to it would be sufficient, I think. But, Doctor, that is just the point. We do not desire publicity any more than you do. We could have the body of Mr. Morowitch exhumed and examined, but I prefer to get the facts in the case without resorting to such extreme measures.”

“It would do no good,” interrupted the doctor hastily. “And if you’ll save me the publicity, I’ll tell you why.”

Andrews nodded, but still held the death-certificate where the doctor was constantly reminded of it.

===Chinese===

安德鲁斯从口袋里抽出一张纸。「这是死亡证明书的副本，」他说，「卫生局提供给我们的。我们保险公司的医师告诉我，这份证明含糊得出奇。只要我们向主管当局递上一句话，提请他们注意此事，我想就尽够了。可是，医生，问题恰恰在这里。我们同您一样不愿声张。我们本可以申请把莫罗维奇先生的遗体开棺检验，但我宁可不动这种极端手段，就把案子的真相弄个明白。」

「开棺也无济于事，」医生急忙打断，「只要你们肯替我免去那场轰动，我就告诉你们缘由。」

安德鲁斯点了点头，却仍把那份死亡证明书举在一旁，教医生时时想得起它的存在。

===Original===

“In that certificate I have put down the cause of death as congestion of the lungs due to an acute attack of pneumonia. That is substantially correct, as far as it goes. When I was summoned to see Mr. Morowitch I found him in a semiconscious state and scarcely breathing. Mrs. Morowitch told me that he had been brought home in a taxicab by a man who had picked him up on William Street. I’m frank to say that at first sight I thought it was a case of plain intoxication, for Mr. Morowitch sometimes indulged a little freely when he made a splendid deal. I smelled his breath, which was very feeble. It had a sickish sweet odour, but that did not impress me at the time. I applied my stethoscope to his lungs. There was a very marked congestion, and I made as my working diagnosis pneumonia. It was a case for quick and heroic action. In a very few minutes I had a tank of oxygen from the hospital.

“In the meantime I had thought over that sweetish odour, and it flashed on my mind that it might, after all, be a case of poisoning. When the oxygen arrived I administered it at once. As it happens, the Rockefeller Institute has just published a report of experiments with a new antidote for various poisons, which consists simply in a new method of enforced breathing and throwing off the poison by oxidising it in that way. In either case﻿—the pneumonia theory or the poison theory﻿—this line of action was the best that I could have adopted on the spur of the moment. I gave him some strychnine to strengthen his heart and by hard work I had him resting apparently a little easier. A nurse had been sent for, but had not arrived when a messenger came to me telling of a very sudden illness of Mrs. Morey, the wife of the steel-magnate. As the Morey home is only a half-block away, I left Mr. Morowitch, with very particular instructions to his wife as to what to do.

===Chinese===

「证明书上，我填的死因是急性肺炎引起的肺充血。就其本身而言，大体不差。我被请去看莫罗维奇先生时，他已处于半昏迷状态，呼吸微乎其微。莫罗维奇夫人告诉我，是一位先生在威廉街上发现了他，用出租车把他送回家的。坦白说，乍一看我以为不过是寻常的醉酒——莫罗维奇先生谈成一注好买卖之后，偶尔也会放纵几杯。我闻了闻他的气息，气息极弱，带着一股甜腻腻的怪味，但当时并没有上心。我把听诊器贴上他的胸腔：肺部充血的迹象十分显著，我便以肺炎作为临诊判断。这病势需要果断有力的处置。没几分钟，我就从医院弄来了一罐氧气。

「其间我把那股甜味又琢磨了一遍，脑中电光一闪：这说不定终究是一桩中毒。氧气一到，我立刻给他用上。碰巧，洛克菲勒研究所（Rockefeller Institute）刚发表了一份报告，讲的是一种对付诸般毒物的新解毒法——说穿了，就是一种强制呼吸、借氧化把毒质驱出体外的新法子。无论按肺炎的设想还是按中毒的设想——仓促之间，这条处置路数都是我能采取的上策。我给他注射了一点士的宁强心，经过一番努力，他看似安顿得略平稳了些。护士已经打发人去请，还没有到，又有信差来报，说钢铁大亨莫雷（Morey）夫人突发急病。莫雷府上就在半条街外，我便离开了莫罗维奇先生，对他夫人该如何照料，千叮万嘱了一番。

===Original===

“I had intended to return immediately, but before I got back Mr. Morowitch was dead. Now I think I’ve told you all. You see, it was nothing but a suspicion﻿—hardly enough to warrant making a fuss about. I made out the death-certificate, as you see. Probably that would have been all there was to it if I hadn’t heard of this incomprehensible robbery. That set me thinking again. There, I’m glad I’ve got it out of my system. I’ve thought about it a good deal since your man was here to see me.”

“What do you suspect was the cause of that sweetish odour?” asked Kennedy.

===Chinese===

「我本想立刻回去，可还没等我返回，莫罗维奇先生已经故去。现在，我想该说的都说了。你们看，那不过是一点疑心——还远不足以小题大做。我开了死亡证明书，正如你们所见。倘若不是后来听说那场莫名其妙的劫案，事情大概也就到此为止了。可那桩案子又叫我重新琢磨起来。好了，这块石头总算吐了出来，我痛快多了。自贵方那位手下来过之后，这事我在心里翻来覆去想了不知多少遍。」

「您疑心那股甜味的来由是什么？」肯尼迪问。

===Original===

The doctor hesitated. “Mind, it is only a suspicion. Cyanide of potassium or cyanogen gas; either would give such an odour.”

“Your treatment would have been just the same had you been certain?”

“Practically the same, the Rockefeller treatment.”

“Could it have been suicide?” asked Andrews.

===Chinese===

医生迟疑了一下。「请注意，这只是疑心。氰化钾，或者氰气（cyanogen）——两者都能发出那种气味。」

「倘若当时您便能确定，处置也是一样？」

「实质上一样——洛克菲勒疗法。」

「会不会是自杀？」安德鲁斯问。

===Original===

“There was no motive for it, I believe,” replied the doctor.

“But was there any such poison in the Morowitch house?”

“I know that they were much interested in photography. Cyanide of potassium is used in certain processes in photography.”

“Who was interested in photography, Mr. or Mrs. Morowitch?”

===Chinese===

「我想，他没有自杀的动机，」医生答道。

「可莫罗维奇家里有这类毒药吗？」

「我知道他们夫妇对摄影都极感兴趣。氰化钾在摄影的某些工序里就用得到。」

「谁对摄影感兴趣——莫罗维奇先生，还是夫人？」

===Original===

“Both of them.”

“Was Mrs. Morowitch?”

“Both of them,” repeated the doctor hastily. It was evident how Andrews’s questions were tending, and it was also evident that the doctor did not wish to commit himself or even to be misunderstood.

Kennedy had sat silently for some minutes, turning the thing over in his mind. Apparently disregarding Andrews entirely, he now asked, “Doctor, supposing it had been cyanogen gas which caused the congestion of the lungs, and supposing it had not been inhaled in quantities large enough to kill outright, do you nevertheless feel that Mr. Morowitch was in a weak enough condition to die as a result of the congestion produced by the gas after the traces of the cyanogen had been perhaps thrown off?”

===Chinese===

「两位都感兴趣。」

「莫罗维奇夫人也？」

「两位都是。」医生急忙重复了一遍。安德鲁斯的问话往哪个方向去，一望即知；同样一望即知的是，医生不愿把话说死，甚至连被人误解也不愿意。

肯尼迪默然坐了几分钟，在心里把这事掂量来掂量去。此刻他仿佛全然没把安德鲁斯放在眼里，开口问道：「医生，假如造成肺部充血的正是氰气，又假使吸入的量还不至于当场致命，您是否仍然认为，莫罗维奇先生当时的底子已虚弱到这般地步——即便氰气的残迹或已排出体外，由毒气引发的那场充血本身，也足以要了他的命？」

===Original===

“That is precisely the impression which I wished to convey.”

“Might I ask whether in his semiconscious state he said anything that might at all serve as a clue?”

“He talked ramblingly, incoherently. As near as I can remember it, he seemed to believe himself to have become a millionaire, a billionaire. He talked of diamonds, diamonds, diamonds. He seemed to be picking them up, running his fingers through them, and once I remember he seemed to want to send for Mr. Kahan and tell him something. ‘I can make them, Kahan,’ he said, ‘the finest, the largest, the whitest﻿—I can make them.’ ”

===Chinese===

「我正想让各位听明白的，就是这个意思。」

「可否请问，他在半昏迷之中，可曾说过什么哪怕稍稍能充作线索的话？」

「他说得颠三倒四、语无伦次。凭我的记忆，他仿佛深信自己成了百万富翁、亿万富翁。他念叨着钻石，钻石，钻石。他像是在一颗一颗地拾捡，手指在钻堆里穿来穿去；有一回——我记得——他好像要人去请卡恩先生，有话要对他说。『我造得出来，卡恩，』他说，『最纯净的，最大颗的，最白亮的——我造得出来。』」

===Original===

Kennedy was all attention as Dr. Thornton added this new evidence.

“You know,” concluded the doctor, “that in cyanogen poisoning there might be hallucinations of the wildest kind. But then, too, in the delirium of pneumonia it might be the same.”

I could see by the way Kennedy acted that for the first time a ray of light had dawned upon him in tracing out the case. As we rose to go, the doctor shook hands with us. His last words were said with an air of great relief, “Gentlemen, I have eased my conscience considerably.”

===Chinese===

桑顿医生补充这段新证词时，肯尼迪凝神细听，须臾不分。

「你们知道，」医生归结道，「氰气中毒会引起最狂乱的幻觉。不过话又说回来，肺炎的谵妄之中，也未尝不会如此。」

从肯尼迪的神情看得出，追索这桩案子以来，他心中头一次透进了一线光亮。我们起身告辞，医生同我们一一握手。他最后的话，说得如释重负：「二位，我的良心松快多了。」

===Original===

As we parted for the night Kennedy faced Andrews. “You recall that you promised me one thing when I took up this case?” he asked.

Andrews nodded.

“Then take no steps until I tell you. Shadow Mrs. Morowitch and Mr. Kahan, but do not let them know you suspect them of anything. Let me run down this Poissan clue. In other words, leave the case entirely in my hands in other respects. Let me know any new facts you may unearth, and some time tomorrow I shall call on you, and we will determine what the next step is to be. Good night. I want to thank you for putting me in the way of this case. I think we shall all be surprised at the outcome.”

===Chinese===

当晚分手时，肯尼迪面向安德鲁斯。「您还记得，我接办这案子时，您答应过我一件事？」他问。

安德鲁斯点了点头。

「那么，在我发话之前，切勿轻举妄动。盯住莫罗维奇夫人和卡恩先生的梢，但别让他们察觉你们疑心他们什么。普瓦松这条线索，交给我去追。换句话说，案子的其余方面，也全权留在我手里。你们挖到什么新事实，随时知会我；明天某个时候我会去见您，咱们再定下一步怎么走。晚安。多谢您把我引到这桩案子上来。我想，结局出来，咱们全都要吃一惊。」

===Original===

It was late the following afternoon before I saw Kennedy again. He was in his laboratory winding two strands of platinum wire carefully about a piece of porcelain and smearing on it some peculiar black glassy granular substance that came in a sort of pencil, like a stick of sealing-wax. I noticed that he was very particular to keep the two wires exactly the same distance from each other throughout the entire length of the piece of porcelain, but I said nothing to distract his attention, though a thousand questions about the progress of the case were at my tongue’s end.

Instead I watched him intently. The black substance formed a sort of bridge connecting and covering the wires. When he had finished he said: “Now you can ask me your questions, while I heat and anneal this little contrivance. I see you are bursting with curiosity.”

“Well, did you see Poissan?” I asked.

===Chinese===

第二天下午很晚，我才又见到肯尼迪。他在实验室里，正把两股铂丝仔细地绕在一片瓷件上，再往上面涂一种奇特的黑色玻璃状颗粒物——那东西成笔状，跟一根火漆棒似的。我注意到，他格外讲究让两股丝在整个瓷件的长度上始终保持等距；虽说一千个关于案情进展的问题已经涌到舌尖，我还是一言不发，免得乱了他的心神。

我只是目不转睛地看着。那黑色物质连起并覆盖住两股丝，成了一道小桥。他做完手头的活，说道：「现在你可以问你的问题了，正好我给这小玩意儿加热退火。我看你都好奇得憋不住了。」

「怎么样，见到普瓦松了吗？」我问。

===Original===

Kennedy continued to heat the wire-covered porcelain. “I did, and he is going to give me a demonstration of his discovery tonight.”

“His discovery!”

“You remember Morowitch’s ‘hallucination,’ as the doctor called it? That was no hallucination; that was a reality. This man Poissan says he has discovered a way to make diamonds artificially out of pure carbon in an electric furnace. Morowitch, I believe, was to buy his secret. His dream of millions was a reality﻿—at least to him.”

“And did Kahan and Mrs. Morowitch know it?” I asked quickly.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪继续给缠着丝的瓷件加热。「见到了。今晚，他要给我演示他的发明。」

「他的发明！」

「你还记得医生所说的莫罗维奇的『幻觉』吧？那不是幻觉，是实情。这位普瓦松宣称，他发现了一种办法，能用电炉把纯碳人工制成钻石。我相信，莫罗维奇正是要买下他的秘密。他那百万家财之梦，本是实有其事——至少对他自己而言。」

「那卡恩和莫罗维奇夫人知道吗？」我赶紧问。

===Original===

“I don’t know yet,” replied Craig, finishing the annealing.

The black glassy substance was now a dull grey.

“What’s that stuff you were putting on the wire?” I asked.

“Oh, just a byproduct made in the manufacture of sulphuric acid,” answered Kennedy airily, adding, as if to change the subject: “I want you to go with me tonight. I told Poissan I was a professor in the university and that I would bring one of our younger trustees, the son of the banker, T. Pierpont Spencer, who might put some capital into his scheme. Now, Jameson, while I’m finishing up my work here, run over to the apartment and get my automatic revolver. I may need it tonight. I have communicated with Andrews, and he will be ready. The demonstration will take place at half-past eight at Poissan’s laboratory. I tried to get him to give it here, but he absolutely refused.”

===Chinese===

「还不知道，」克雷格答道，退火也正好做完。

那黑色玻璃状物质，如今变成了暗灰色。

「你往丝上涂的是什么东西？」我问。

「哦，不过是硫酸制造业里的一种副产品。」肯尼迪轻描淡写地答道，接着又像有意岔开话头似的说：「今晚我要你陪我同去。我告诉普瓦松，我是大学教授，还要带一位我们学校年轻的董事来——银行家 T·皮尔庞特·斯宾塞（T. Pierpont Spencer）的公子，说不定会往他的计划里投些资本。好了，詹姆森，趁我在这里收尾，你跑一趟公寓，把我的自动手枪取来。今晚兴许用得着。我已知会了安德鲁斯，他自会准备妥当。演示八点半在普瓦松的实验室进行。我本想让他把演示挪到这儿来做，他断然回绝了。」

===Original===

Half an hour later I rejoined Craig at his laboratory, and we rode down to the Great Eastern Life Building.

Andrews was waiting for us in his solidly furnished office. Outside I noted a couple of husky men, who seemed to be waiting for orders from their chief.

From the manner in which the vice-president greeted us it was evident that he was keenly interested in what Kennedy was about to do. “So you think Morowitch’s deal was a deal to purchase the secret of diamond-making?” he mused.

“I feel sure of it,” replied Craig. “I felt sure of it the moment I looked up Poissan and found that he was a manufacturer of electric furnaces. Don’t you remember the famous Lemoine case in London and Paris?”

===Chinese===

半小时后，我回到克雷格的实验室同他会合，两人乘车下城，直抵大东方人寿大楼。

安德鲁斯在他那间陈设敦实的办公室里等着我们。门外我瞥见两个彪形大汉，看似正候着上司的号令。

从这位副总裁迎接我们的那副架势看，肯尼迪将要做的事让他兴味十足。「这么说，你认为莫罗维奇那笔买卖，是要买下制造钻石的秘密？」他沉吟道。

「我确信如此，」克雷格答道，「一查到普瓦松其人是电炉制造商，我就确信了。伦敦、巴黎那桩有名的勒穆瓦纳案（Lemoine case），您不记得了吗？」

===Original===

“Yes, but Lemoine was a fakir of the first water;” said Andrews. “Do you think this man is, too?”

“That’s what I’m going to find out tonight before I take another step,” said Craig. “Of course there can be no doubt that by proper use the electric furnace will make small, almost microscopic diamonds. It is not unreasonable to suppose that some day someone will be able to make large diamonds synthetically by the same process.”

“Maybe this man has done it,” agreed Andrews. “Who knows? I’ll wager that if he has and that if Morowitch had bought an interest in his process Kahan knew of it. He’s a sharp one. And Mrs. Morowitch doesn’t let grass grow under her feet, when it comes to seeing the main chance as to money. Now just supposing Mr. Morowitch had bought an interest in a secret like that and supposing Kahan was in love with Mrs. Morowitch and that they﻿—”

“Let us suppose nothing, Mr. Andrews,” interrupted Kennedy. “At least not yet. Let me see; it is now ten minutes after eight. Poissan’s place is only a few blocks from here. I’d like to get there a few minutes early. Let’s start.”

===Chinese===

「记得，可勒穆瓦纳是个头等的大骗子，」安德鲁斯说，「你觉得这个人也是吗？」

「今晚我要把它弄个水落石出，才走下一步，」克雷格说，「电炉用得其法，确能造出细小的、几乎要在显微镜下才看得见的钻石，这无可置疑。有朝一日，有人能用同样的工艺造出大颗的钻石，这设想也不为无理。」

「也许这个人当真做到了，」安德鲁斯附和道，「谁说得准呢？我敢打赌，倘若他做到了，而莫罗维奇又买下了他工艺的一份权益，卡恩一定知情。那家伙精得很。至于莫罗维奇夫人，一碰上钱财上的大好机会，她可是半点工夫也不肯耽误的人。喏，假设莫罗维奇先生当真买了那样一门秘密的权益，再假设卡恩爱上了莫罗维奇夫人，而他们俩——」

「咱们什么也别假设，安德鲁斯先生，」肯尼迪打断他，「至少现在不要。我看看——八点过十分了。普瓦松的地方离这儿只隔几个街区。我想提前几分钟到。走吧。」

===Original===

As we left the office, Andrews signalled to the two men outside, and they quietly followed a few feet in the rear, but without seeming to be with us.

Poissan’s laboratory was at the top of a sort of loft building a dozen stories or so high. It was a peculiar building, with several entrances besides a freight elevator at the rear and fire-escapes that led to adjoining lower roofs.

We stopped around the corner in the shadow, and Kennedy and Andrews talked earnestly. As near as I could make out Kennedy was insisting that it would be best for Andrews and his men not to enter the building at all, but wait downstairs while he and I went up. At last the arrangement was agreed on.

“Here,” said Kennedy, undoing a package he had carried, “is a little electric bell with a couple of fresh dry batteries attached to it, and wires that will reach at least four hundred feet. You and the men wait in the shadow here by this side entrance for five minutes after Jameson and I go up. Then you must engage the night watchman in some way. While he is away you will find two wires dangling down the elevator shaft. Attach them to these wires from the bell and the batteries﻿—these two﻿—you know how to do that. The wires will be hanging in the third shaft﻿—only one elevator is running at night, the first. The moment you hear the bell begin to ring; jump into the elevator and come up to the twelfth floor﻿—we’ll need you.”

===Chinese===

我们离开办公室时，安德鲁斯向外面的两个人递了个眼色，他们便悄没声儿地缀在后面几步远处，看上去却不像是与我们同行的。

普瓦松的实验室在一座十二层上下的仓库式高楼顶上。这楼颇有些特别：出入口有好几个，楼后另有一部货梯，防火梯接着邻近较低的几处屋顶。

我们在拐角的阴影里停下，肯尼迪和安德鲁斯低声商量了好一阵。据我大致听来，肯尼迪力主安德鲁斯和他的人根本不要进楼，只在一楼守候，由他和我两人上去。最后总算议定了这个安排。

「给，」肯尼迪一面解开随身带来的一个包裹，一面说，「这是一只小电铃，接两节新干电池，配的电线至少能拉四百英尺。詹姆森和我上去之后，你带着两位在这道侧门旁的阴影里等上五分钟，然后务必想个法子把夜班门卫缠住。趁他不在，你们会在电梯井里发现垂下来的两根电线。把它们接到电铃和电池的引线上——就这两根——怎么接，你们是内行。线会垂在第三口井道里——夜间只有第一部电梯运行。铃声一响，立刻进电梯直上十二层——我们会用得着你们的。」

===Original===

As Kennedy and I rode up in the elevator I could not help thinking what an ideal place a downtown office building is for committing a crime, even at this early hour of the evening. If the streets were deserted, the office buildings were positively uncanny in their grim, black silence with only here and there a light.

The elevator in the first shaft shot down again to the ground floor, and as it disappeared Kennedy took two spools of wire from his pocket and hastily shoved them through the lattice work the third elevator shaft. They quickly unrolled, and I could hear them strike the top of the empty car below in the basement. That meant that Andrews on the ground floor could reach the wires and attach them to the bell.

Quickly in the darkness Kennedy attached the ends of the wires to the curious little coil I had seen him working on in the laboratory, and we proceeded down the hall to the rooms occupied by Poissan, Kennedy had allowed for the wire to reach from the elevator-shaft up this hall, also, and as he walked he paid it out in such a manner that it fell on the floor close to the wall, where, in the darkness, it would never be noticed or stumbled over.

Around an “L” in the hall I could see a ground-glass window with a light shining through it. Kennedy stopped at the window and quickly placed the little coil on the ledge, close up against the glass, with the wires running from it down the hall. Then we entered.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪和我乘电梯上楼的当儿，我不禁暗想：下城的写字楼真是作案的理想去处，哪怕才入夜不久。街面上人迹已稀，写字楼更是在阴冷漆黑的静默里透着说不出的森然，只是东一点西一点地亮着灯。

第一口井道里的电梯又直坠底层；等它一消失，肯尼迪从口袋里掏出两轴细线，急急地从格栅缝里塞进了第三口电梯井。线轴飞快地散开，我听得见它们砸在底下地下室里空电梯轿厢顶上的声音。这就是说，一楼的安德鲁斯够得着那两根线，能把它们接到电铃上。

黑暗里，肯尼迪飞快地把线头接到那个我在实验室见他摆弄过的古怪小线圈上，然后我们顺着走廊朝普瓦松占用的房间走去。从电梯井到这条走廊尽头这段线的余量，肯尼迪也早留足了；他一边走一边放线，手法让线贴着墙根落在地板上——黑地里谁也不会留意，更不会绊脚。

走廊拐过一道「L」形弯，我望见一扇磨砂玻璃窗透出灯光。肯尼迪在窗前停住，迅速把那只小线圈搁上窗台，紧贴着玻璃，引线从线圈一路顺着走廊铺了回去。然后我们进了门。

===Original===

“On time to the minute, Professor,” exclaimed Poissan, snapping his watch. “And this, I presume, is the banker who is interested in my great discovery of making artificial diamonds of any size or colour?” he added, indicating me.

“Yes,” answered Craig, “as I told you, a son of Mr. T. Pierpont Spencer.”

I shook hands with as much dignity as I could assume, for the role of impersonation was a new one to me.

Kennedy carelessly laid his coat and hat on the inside ledge of the ground-glass window, just opposite the spot where he had placed the little coil on the other side of the glass. I noted that the window was simply a large pane of wire-glass set in the wall for the purpose of admitting light in the daytime from the hall outside.

===Chinese===

「分秒不差，教授。」普瓦松啪的一声合上怀表，扬声说道。「那么这位，想必就是对我不论大小、不论颜色都能人工造钻的伟大发明深感兴趣的银行家了？」他补了一句，朝我一指。

「正是，」克雷格答道，「我先前说过的，T·皮尔庞特·斯宾塞先生的公子。」

我竭力端着架子同他握手——冒名顶角，这对我还是头一遭。

肯尼迪随随便便地把外套和帽子搁在磨砂玻璃窗内侧的窗台上，正对着玻璃另一面他放小线圈的地方。我看出来了：那扇窗不过是墙上安的一大幅夹丝玻璃，为的是白天从外头走廊里借光。

===Original===

The whole thing seemed eerie to me﻿—especially as Poissan’s assistant was a huge fellow and had an evil look such as I had seen in pictures of the inhabitants of quarters of Paris which one does not frequent except in the company of a safe guide. I was glad Kennedy had brought his revolver, and rather vexed that he had not told me to do likewise. However, I trusted that Craig knew what he was about.

We seated ourselves some distance from a table on which was a huge, plain, oblong contrivance that reminded me of the diagram of a parallelopiped which had caused so much trouble in my solid geometry at college.

“That’s the electric furnace, sir,” said Craig to me with an assumed deference, becoming a college professor explaining things to the son of a great financier. “You see the electrodes at either end? When the current is turned on and led through them into the furnace you can get the most amazing temperatures in the crucible. The most refractory of chemical compounds can be broken up by that heat. What is the highest temperature you have attained, Professor?”

“Something over three thousand degrees Centigrade,” replied Poissan, as he and his assistant busied themselves about the furnace.

===Chinese===

这一切在我眼里透着阴森——尤其普瓦松的助手是个彪形大汉，一脸凶相，像我在画片上见过的巴黎某些街区居民的模样——那种地方，若没有稳妥的向导引路，是不便去的。肯尼迪带了枪，我很庆幸；他没吩咐我也带一支，我又有点恼。不过我相信，克雷格自有主张。

我们在离一张桌子不远的地方坐下。桌上架着一台硕大、浑朴的长方家伙，叫我想起大学立体几何课上那个害我不浅的平行六面体图来。

「先生，这就是电炉。」克雷格对我摆出一副恭谨的口吻，俨然一位向大金融家之子讲解学问的大学教授。「看到两端的电极了吗？电流一通，顺着电极引进炉内，坩埚里就能达到惊人的温度。最难熔的化合物，那份高热也能把它们分解。教授，您达到过的最高温度是多少？」

「摄氏三千度出头。」普瓦松答道，一面和助手在炉边忙活。

===Original===

We sat watching him in silence.

“Ah, gentlemen, now I am ready,” he exclaimed at length, when everything was arranged to his satisfaction. “You see, here is a lump of sugar carbon﻿—pure amorphous carbon: Diamonds, as you know, are composed of pure carbon crystallised under enormous pressure. Now, my theory is that if we can combine an enormous pressure and an enormous heat we can make diamonds artificially. The problem of pressure is the thing, for here in the furnace we have the necessary heat. It occurred to me that when molten cast iron cools it exerts a tremendous pressure. That pressure is what I use.”

“You know, Spencer, solid iron floats on molten iron like solid water﻿—ice﻿—floats on liquid water,” explained Craig to me.

Poissan nodded. “I take this sugar carbon and place it in this soft iron cup. Then I screw on this cap over the cup, so. Now I place this mass of iron scraps in the crucible of the furnace and start the furnace.”

===Chinese===

我们坐在一旁，默默看着他操作。

「啊，二位，现在一切就绪。」等样样都调排得称心如意，他终于扬声说道。「你们看，这是一块糖碳（sugar carbon）——纯净的无定形碳。诸位知道，钻石本是纯碳在莫大压力下结晶而成。我的理论是：只要把莫大的压力与莫大的高热合到一处，便能人工造出钻石。难就难在压力——因为这炉子里，高热已经有了。我想到，铸铁由熔融状态冷却时，会施出惊人的压力。我用的，正是这份压力。」

「斯宾塞先生，您知道，固态铁浮在铁水上，就像固态的水——冰——浮在液态的水上一样。」克雷格向我解释道。

普瓦松点了点头。「我把这块糖碳放进这只软铁杯，再把盖子旋上杯口——这样。如今，我把这一堆铁屑放进炉膛的坩埚，开炉。」

===Original===

He turned a switch, and long yellowish-blue sheets of flame spurted out from the electrodes on either side. It was weird, gruesome. One could feel the heat of the tremendous electric discharge.

As I looked at the bluish-yellow flames they gradually changed to a beautiful purple, and a sickish sweet odour filled the room. The furnace roared at first, but as the vapors increased it became a better conductor of the electricity, and the roaring ceased.

In almost no time the mass of iron scraps became molten. Suddenly Poissan plunged the cast-iron cup into the seething mass. The cup floated and quickly began to melt. As it did so he waited attentively until the proper moment. Then with a deft motion he seized the whole thing with a long pair of tongs and plunged it into a vat of running water. A huge cloud of steam filled the room.

I felt a drowsy sensation stealing over me as the sickish sweet smell from the furnace increased. Gripping the chair, I roused myself and watched Poissan attentively. He was working rapidly. As the molten mass cooled and solidified he took it out of the water and laid it on an anvil.

===Chinese===

他合上开关，两侧电极顿时喷出长长的黄蓝色火舌。景象诡异骇人。那股巨大放电的热力，人隔着老远都感觉得到。

我盯着那些蓝黄色火焰看时，它们渐渐变成一种美艳的紫色，一股甜腻腻的怪味弥漫全屋。起初炉子吼声隆隆，但随着气体越积越多，炉内成了更佳的导电体，吼声便止息了。

转眼之间，那堆铁屑已成铁水。普瓦松猛地把铸铁杯按进滚滚铁水。杯子浮在铁水面上，很快开始熔化。他凝神守着，静候火候一到，手腕一翻，用一柄长钳把整个东西夹住，一下浸进一槽流水里。一大团蒸汽腾起，罩满了屋子。

炉子那边甜腻的怪味一阵浓似一阵，我觉出一种昏昏欲睡的感觉爬上头来。我攥住椅背，振作精神，盯住普瓦松的一举一动。他动作飞快。等那团铁水冷却凝固，他把它从水里捞出，搁上了一座铁砧。

===Original===

Then his assistant began to hammer it with careful, sharp blows, chipping off the outside.

“You see, we have to get down to the core of carbon gently,” he said, as he picked up the little pieces of iron and threw them into a scrap-box. “First rather brittle cast iron, then hard iron, then iron and carbon, then some black diamonds, and in the very centre the diamonds.

“Ah! we are getting to them. Here is a small diamond. See, Mr. Spencer﻿—gently François﻿—we shall come to the large ones presently.”

“One moment, Professor Poissan,” interrupted Craig; “let your assistant break them out while I stand over him.”

===Chinese===

接着，他的助手抡锤敲打，锤锤又准又脆，把外皮一片片剥落。

「瞧见没有，得轻轻巧巧地触到碳芯，」他一面拾起碎铁块丢进废料箱，一面说。「先是相当脆的铸铁，再是硬铁，再是铁和碳，再是一些黑金刚石，最当中的，便是钻石。

「啊！到了。这是颗小钻石。看呀，斯宾塞先生——轻着点，弗朗索瓦（François）——大颗的马上就到。」

「且慢，普瓦松教授，」克雷格插话道，「让您的助手把它们敲出来，我站在他旁边看着。」

===Original===

“Impossible. You would not know when you saw them. They are just rough stones.”

“Oh, yes, I would.”

“No, stay where you are. Unless I attend to it the diamonds might be ruined.”

There was something peculiar about his insistence, but after he picked out the next diamond I was hardly prepared for Kennedy’s next remark.

===Chinese===

「不行。你们见了也认不出。它们不过是些未经琢磨的原石。」

「哦，我认得出。」

「不，待在原地别动。不经我的手，钻石兴许就毁了。」

他这般坚持，其中透着蹊跷；可等他拣出第二颗钻石，肯尼迪接下来说的话，还是叫我猝不及防。

===Original===

“Let me see the palms of your hands.”

Poissan shot an angry glance at Kennedy, but he did not open his hands.

“I merely wish to convince you, ‘Mr. Spencer,’ ” said Kennedy to me, “that it is no sleight-of-hand trick and that the professor has not several uncut stones palmed in his hand like a prestidigitator.”

The Frenchman faced us, his face livid with rage. “You call me a prestidigitator, a fraud﻿—you shall suffer for that! *Sacrebleu! Ventre du Saint Gris!* No man ever insults the honour of Poissan. François, water on the electrodes!”

===Chinese===

「请把两只手掌摊开，给我看看。」

普瓦松狠狠瞪了肯尼迪一眼，双手却迟迟不摊开。

「我不过想让您这位『斯宾塞先生』信服，」肯尼迪对我说，「这不是耍手法玩的戏法，教授也并没有像变戏法的（prestidigitator）那样，把几颗未加工的石头扣在掌心里。」

那法国人转过脸来对着我们，气得面色铁青。「你骂我是变戏法的、是骗子——你要为此付出代价！*Sacrebleu! Ventre du Saint Gris!*（法语咒骂：天杀的！）从来没有人敢侮辱普瓦松的名誉。弗朗索瓦，往电极上浇水！」

===Original===

The assistant dashed a few drops of water on the electrodes. The sickish odour increased tremendously. I felt myself almost going, but with an effort I again roused myself. I wondered how Craig stood the fumes, for I suffered an intense headache and nausea.

“Stop!” Craig thundered. “There’s enough cyanogen in this room already. I know your game﻿—the water forms acetylene with the carbon, and that uniting with the nitrogen of the air under the terrific heat of the electric arc forms hydrocyanic acid. Would you poison us, too? Do you think you can put me unconscious out on the street and have a society doctor diagnose my case as pneumonia? Or do you think we shall die quietly in some hospital as a certain New York banker did last year after he had watched an alchemist make silver out of apparently nothing?”

The effect on Poissan was terrible. He advanced toward Kennedy, the veins in his face fairly standing out. Shaking his forefinger, he shouted: “You know that, do you? You are no professor, and this is no banker. You are spies, spies. You come from the friends of Morowitch, do you? You have gone too far with me.”

Kennedy said nothing, but retreated and took his coat and hat off the window ledge. The hideous penetrating light of the tongues of flame from the furnace played on the ground-glass window.

===Chinese===

助手往电极上泼了几滴水。那股甜腻的怪味陡然大盛。我觉得自己几乎要栽过去，可一咬牙，又把自己振作了起来。我不明白克雷格是怎么顶住这股烟气的——我已头痛欲裂，恶心翻涌。

「住手！」克雷格厉声喝道，「这屋里的氰气已经够多了。你的把戏我清楚——水同碳化合成乙炔，乙炔再在电弧的奇热之下同空气中的氮化合，便成了氢氰酸。你还想连我们一起毒倒吗？你以为，把我昏死过去往街上一扔，再请位上流社会的医生把我的病诊断成肺炎，就万事大吉了？还是指望我们像去年纽约那位银行家一样——看完炼金术士无中生有地变出银子，便悄没声儿地死在某家医院里？」

这番话对普瓦松的打击非同小可。他朝肯尼迪逼过来，脸上的青筋根根绽起，抖着食指叫道：「这个你也知道？你不是教授，这位也不是银行家。你们是奸细，奸细！你们是莫罗维奇的朋友们差来的，是不是？你们欺人太甚！」

肯尼迪不作声，只退后一步，从窗台上取下外套和帽子。炉中火焰吐出的那股刺目骇人的光，正照在磨砂玻璃窗上。

===Original===

Poissan laughed a hollow laugh.

“Put down your hat and coat, Mistair Kennedy,” he hissed. “The door has been locked ever since you have been here. Those windows are barred, the telephone wire is cut, and it is three hundred feet to the street. We shall leave you here when the fumes have overcome you. François and I can stand them up to a point, and when we reach that point we are going.”

Instead of being cowed Kennedy grew bolder, though I, for my part, felt so weakened that I feared the outcome of a hand-to-hand encounter with either Poissan or François, who appeared as fresh as if nothing had happened. They were hurriedly preparing to leave us.

“That would do you no good,” Kennedy rejoined, “for we have no safe full of jewels for you to rob. There are no keys to offices to be stolen from our pockets. And let me tell you﻿—you are not the only man in New York who knows the secret of thermite. I have told the secret to the police, and they are only waiting to find who destroyed Morowitch’s correspondence under the letter *P* to apprehend the robber of his safe. Your secret is out.”

===Chinese===

普瓦松干笑了一声。

「把帽子外套放下吧，密斯特尔·肯尼迪。」他咝咝地说，「打你们进来，门就锁上了。窗户装着铁栅，电话线也铰断了，从这儿到街面，足有三百英尺。等烟气放倒了你们，我们就把二位留在这儿。弗朗索瓦和我支撑得住一阵子，撑到头了，我们就走。」

肯尼迪非但没有被吓住，气概反而更壮；我却只觉浑身发虚——真要同普瓦松或弗朗索瓦徒手相搏，只怕凶多吉少，何况那两位看着精神抖擞，仿佛什么事也不曾发生。他们正手忙脚乱地收拾，要把我们撂在这儿。

「那对你也没什么好处，」肯尼迪回敬道，「我们身上又没有装满珠宝的保险箱好抢，口袋里也没有写字间的钥匙好偷。再告诉你一句——纽约城里晓得铝热剂秘密的，可不止你一个。我已经把这秘密告诉了警方，他们只等查出是谁销毁了莫罗维奇字母 *P* 底下的信件，便去捉拿那劫保险箱的人。你的秘密败露了。」

===Original===

“Revenge! revenge!” Poissan cried. “I will have revenge. François, bring out the jewels﻿—ha! ha!﻿—here in this bag are the jewels of Mr. Morowitch. Tonight François and I will go down by the back elevator to a secret exit. In two hours all your police in New York cannot find us. But in two hours you two impostors will be suffocated﻿—perhaps you will die of cyanogen, like Morowitch, whose jewels I have at last.”

He went to the door into the hall and stood there with a mocking laugh. I moved to make a rush toward them, but Kennedy raised his hand.

“You will suffocate,” Poissan hissed again.

Just then we heard the elevator door clang, and hurried steps came down the long hall.

===Chinese===

「报复！报复！」普瓦松喊道，「我要报仇。弗朗索瓦，把珠宝拿出来——哈！哈！——莫罗维奇先生的珠宝，就在这只袋里。今夜，弗朗索瓦和我从后头的货梯下到一处秘密出口。两个钟头之内，你们纽约全城的警察也休想找到我们。而两个钟头之内，你们这两个骗子就要窒息——说不定还要死于氰气，像莫罗维奇一样——他的珠宝，终究到了我手里。」

他走到通往走廊的门前，站在那里发出嘲弄的笑。我正要向他们扑过去，肯尼迪却抬手拦住了。

「你们会窒息的。」普瓦松又咝咝地说。

正在这时，只听电梯门哐当一响，急促的脚步声顺着长长的走廊奔来。

===Original===

Craig whipped out his automatic and began pumping the bullets out in rapid succession. As the smoke cleared I expected to see Poissan and François lying on the floor. Instead, Craig had fired at the lock of the door. He had shattered it into a thousand bits. Andrews and his men were running down the hall.

“Curse you!” muttered Poissan as he banged the now useless lock, “who let those fellows in? Are you a wizard?”

Craig smiled coolly as the ventilation cleared the room of the deadly cyanogen.

“On the windowsill outside is a selenium cell. Selenium is a bad conductor of electricity in the dark, and an excellent conductor when exposed to light. I merely moved my coat and hat, and the light from the furnace which was going to suffocate us played through the glass on the cell, the circuit was completed without your suspecting that I could communicate with friends outside, a bell was rung on the street, and here they are. Andrews, there is the murderer of Morowitch, and there in his hands are the Morowitch﻿—”

===Chinese===

克雷格刷地拔出自动手枪，子弹一发接一发地连连射出。硝烟散去，我原以为会看到普瓦松和弗朗索瓦倒在地板上；谁知克雷格打的是门上的锁。那锁已被打得粉碎。安德鲁斯和他的人正顺着走廊奔来。

「该死的！」普瓦松一面捶打那把已经废了的锁，一面咬牙道，「是谁放进来的？你莫非是个巫师？」

通风换气把屋中致命的氰气渐渐排净，克雷格神色自若地微微一笑。

「外面窗台上有一只硒光电池（selenium cell）。硒这种东西，黑暗里是电的不良导体，一见了光，却是极好的导体。我不过把外套和帽子挪开，那本要闷死我们的炉火便透过玻璃照到了电池上，线路立时接通——你们做梦也想不到我还能同外头的朋友通声气——街面上铃一响，他们就到了。安德鲁斯，杀死莫罗维奇的凶手就在那儿，他手里拿着的，正是莫罗维奇家的——」

===Original===

Poissan had moved toward the furnace. With a quick motion he seized the long tongs. There was a cloud of choking vapour. Kennedy leaped to the switch and shut off the current. With the tongs he lifted out a shapeless piece of valueless black graphite.

“All that is left of the priceless Morowitch jewels,” he exclaimed ruefully. “But we have the murderer.”

“And tomorrow a certified check for one hundred thousand dollars goes to Mrs. Morowitch with my humblest apologies and sympathy,” added Andrews. “Professor Kennedy, you have earned your retainer.”


===Chinese===

普瓦松已经朝炉子那边挪了过去。他手疾眼快，抢过了那柄长钳。一团呛人的烟气腾起。肯尼迪抢步奔向开关，切断了电流。再用钳子夹出来的，是一块不成形状、一文不值的黑色石墨（graphite）。

「价值连城的莫罗维奇珠宝，剩下的就只有这个了。」他懊然叹道，「不过，凶手总算到了手。」

「还有——明天，一张十万美元的保付支票，连同我十二万分的歉意与慰问，就送到莫罗维奇夫人手里。」安德鲁斯补上一句，「肯尼迪教授，您这份聘金，花得值。」

## The Scientific Cracksman / 科学窃贼

===Original===

“I’m willing to wager you a box of cigars that you don’t know the most fascinating story in your own paper tonight,” remarked Kennedy, as I came in one evening with the four or five newspapers I was in the habit of reading to see whether they had beaten the *Star* in getting any news of importance.

“I’ll bet I do,” I said, “or I was one of about a dozen who worked it up. It’s the Shaw murder trial. There isn’t another that’s even a bad second.”

===Chinese===

「我敢拿一盒雪茄打赌，今晚你自己报上最精彩的新闻是什么，你根本说不上来。」一天晚上，我拿着四五份报纸进门时，肯尼迪说道。这几份报纸我每晚必读，为的是看看有没有哪家报馆抢在《明星报》前头，登出什么要紧的新闻。

「这我倒要赌上一赌，」我说，「再不济，经手编发这条新闻的十几个人里也有我一个。就是肖氏凶案庭审（Shaw murder trial）呗。别的新闻，连个勉强的第二名都算不上。」

===Original===

“I am afraid the cigars will be on you, Walter. Crowded over on the second page by a lot of stale sensation that everyone has read for the fiftieth time, now, you will find what promises to be a real sensation, a curious half-column account of the sudden death of John G. Fletcher.”

===Chinese===

「沃尔特，恐怕这盒雪茄要你破费了。就在被一堆人人读过五十遍的陈年耸闻挤到第二版的地方，你会找到一条有望成为真正爆炸性新闻的稿子——一则蹊跷的半栏报道，讲的是约翰·G·弗莱彻（John G. Fletcher）的猝死。」

===Original===

I laughed. “Craig,” I said, “when you put up a simple death from apoplexy against a murder trial, and *such* a murder trial; well, you disappoint me﻿—that’s all.”

“Is it a simple case of apoplexy?” he asked, pacing up and down the room, while I wondered why he should grow excited over what seemed a very ordinary news item, after all. Then he picked up the paper and read the account slowly aloud.

===Chinese===

我笑了。「克雷格，」我说，「拿一桩普普通通的中风暴毙，去跟一场凶案庭审打擂台——还是那样一场庭审；唉，你可真让我失望——就这么回事。」

「这当真是一桩普通的中风吗？」他反问，在屋里来回踱步。我心里纳闷，这么条寻常社会新闻，说到底，他究竟激动个什么劲。说着他拿起报纸，缓缓地把那则报道念出声来。

===Original===

John G. Fletcher, Steel Magnate, Dies Suddenly

Safe Open but Large Sum of Cash Untouched

===Chinese===

钢铁大亨约翰·G·弗莱彻溘然长逝

保险箱洞开，巨额现金分文未动

===Original===

John Graham Fletcher, the aged philanthropist and steelmaker, was found dead in his library this morning at his home at Fletcherwood, Great Neck, Long Island. Strangely, the safe in the library in which he kept his papers and a large sum of cash was found opened, but as far as could be learned nothing is missing.

It had always been Mr. Fletcher’s custom to rise at seven o’clock. This morning his housekeeper became alarmed when he had not appeared by nine o’clock. Listening at the door, she heard no sound. It was not locked, and on entering she found the former steel-magnate lying lifeless on the floor between his bedroom and the library adjoining. His personal physician, Dr. W. C. Bryant, was immediately notified.

Close examination of the body revealed that his face was slightly discoloured, and the cause of death was given by the physician as apoplexy. He had evidently been dead about eight or nine hours when discovered.

Mr. Fletcher is survived by a nephew, John G. Fletcher, II, who is the Blake professor of bacteriology at the University, and by a grandniece, Miss Helen Bond. Professor Fletcher was informed of the sad occurrence shortly after leaving a class this morning and hurried out to Fletcherwood. He would make no statement other than that he was inexpressibly shocked. Miss Bond, who has for several years resided with relatives, Mr. and Mrs. Francis Greene of Little Neck, is prostrated by the shock.

===Chinese===

年迈的慈善家、钢铁制造商约翰·格雷厄姆·弗莱彻（John Graham Fletcher），今晨被人发现死于长岛大颈镇弗莱彻庄园（Fletcherwood）宅邸的书房中。蹊跷的是，书房里那存放文件与大笔现金的保险箱被人打开过，但据目前所知，分文未失。

弗莱彻先生素来七点起身。今天早上到了九点还不见人影，女管家慌了神。她在门外侧耳细听，毫无声息。房门没有上锁，推门进去，只见这位昔日的钢铁大亨直挺挺躺在卧室与相邻书房之间的地板上，早已没了气息。他的私人医生W·C·布莱恩特（Dr. W. C. Bryant）当即被人请去。

细察遗体，可见面部略有淤色；医生给出的死因是中风。据判断，被发现时他已死了大约八九个钟头。

弗莱彻先生身后，有侄子小约翰·G·弗莱彻（John G. Fletcher, II）——大学布莱克讲席细菌学教授（Blake professor of bacteriology）——以及侄孙女海伦·邦德小姐（Miss Helen Bond）。弗莱彻教授今晨一下课便得到噩耗，急赴弗莱彻庄园。除表示震惊得无以言表外，他未作任何置评。海伦·邦德小姐数年来寄住在亲戚——小颈镇（Little Neck）的弗朗西斯·格林夫妇（Mr. and Mrs. Francis Greene）——家中，目前已因这场打击卧床不起。

===Original===

“Walter,” added Kennedy, as he laid down the paper and, without any more sparring, came directly to the point, “there was something missing from that safe.”

I had no need to express the interest I now really felt, and Kennedy hastened to take advantage of it.

===Chinese===

「沃尔特，」肯尼迪放下报纸，不再跟我兜圈子，直截了当点破，「那保险箱里丢了东西。」

这下我的兴趣可是当真的，无须再佯装；肯尼迪抓紧趁热打铁。

===Original===
