“Just before you came in,” he continued, “Jack Fletcher called me up from Great Neck. You probably don’t know it, but it has been privately reported in the inner circle of the University that old Fletcher was to leave the bulk of his fortune to found a great school of preventive medicine, and that the only proviso was that his nephew should be dean of the school. The professor told me over the wire that the will was missing from the safe, and that it was the only thing missing. From his excitement I judge that there is more to the story than he cared to tell over the phone. He said his car was on the way to the city, and he asked if I wouldn’t come and help him﻿—he wouldn’t say how. Now, I know him pretty well, and I’m going to ask you to come along, Walter, for the express purpose of keeping this thing out of the newspapers﻿—understand?﻿—until we get to the bottom of it.”

===Chinese===

「就在你进门之前，」他接着说，「杰克·弗莱彻从大颈镇给我来了电话。这事你多半不知道：大学内圈子里早有私下传言，说老弗莱彻要把大笔遗产捐出来，创办一所规模宏大的预防医学院，唯一的附带条件是他侄子出任院长。教授在电话里告诉我，保险箱里的遗嘱不见了，而且丢的只有这一样东西。听他那焦急的口气，我料想其中另有隐情，只是不便在电话里明说。他说他的车已经在进城路上，问我能不能去帮他一把——怎么帮，他不说。这个人我是相当熟的。沃尔特，我要你也一起去，专为一件差事：别让这事捅到报上去——明白吗？——至少在咱们查个水落石出之前。」

===Original===

A few minutes later the telephone rang and the hall-boy announced that the car was waiting. We hurried down to it; the chauffeur lounged down carelessly into his seat and we were off across the city and river and out on the road to Great Neck with amazing speed.

Already I began to feel something of Kennedy’s zest for the adventure. I found myself half a dozen times on the point of hazarding a suspicion, only to relapse again into silence at the inscrutable look on Kennedy’s face. What was the mystery that awaited us in the great lonely house on Long Island?

===Chinese===

几分钟后电话铃响，门厅小厮通报车已到了。我们匆匆下楼。司机懒洋洋往座位上一靠，车子便载着我们飞驰而去——穿过市区，越过河去，上了通往大颈镇的公路，速度快得惊人。

不知不觉间，我也染上了几分肯尼迪对这场奇遇的兴致。有好几次我都险些要贸然说出心中的猜测，可一看到他脸上那高深莫测的神情，话到嘴边又咽了回去。长岛上那座空落落的大宅里，等着我们的究竟是怎样的谜？

===Original===

We found Fletcherwood a splendid estate directly on the bay, with a long driveway leading up to the door. Professor Fletcher met us at the porte-cochère, and I was glad to note that, far from taking me as an intruder, he seemed rather relieved that someone who understood the ways of the newspapers could stand between him and any reporters who might possibly drop in.

He ushered us directly into the library and closed the door. It seemed as if he could scarcely wait to tell his story.

===Chinese===

弗莱彻庄园就坐落在海湾边上，气派堂皇，一条长长的车道直通宅门。弗莱彻教授在车廊（porte-cochère）前迎候我们。让我高兴的是，他非但不把我当作碍事的外人，反倒像是松了口气——总算有个深谙报馆行事的人，可以替他挡一挡可能登门的记者了。

他把我们径直领进书房，随手掩上门。看那样子，他简直一刻也等不及了，恨不得立刻把事情和盘托出。

===Original===

“Kennedy,” he began, almost trembling with excitement, “look at that safe door.”

We looked. It had been drilled through in such a way as to break the combination. It was a heavy door, closely fitting, and it was the best kind of small safe that the state of the art had produced. Yet clearly it had been tampered with, and successfully. Who was this scientific cracksman who had apparently accomplished the impossible? It was no ordinary hand and brain which had executed this job.

===Chinese===

「肯尼迪，」他开口道，激动得几乎发颤，「你看看那扇保险箱门。」

我们凑上去看。箱门被钻了孔，钻的位置恰好废掉了转字密码锁。那是一扇厚重严实的门，代表当时工艺所能造出的小型保险箱中的上品。可它分明被人动了手脚，而且得手了。这个显然完成了不可能之事的科学窃贼（cracksman），究竟是何方神圣？干下这活的，绝非寻常的手和脑。

===Original===

Fletcher swung the door wide, and pointed to a little compartment inside, whose steel door had been jimmied open. Then out of it he carefully lifted a steel box and deposited it on the library table.

“I suppose everybody has been handling that box?” asked Craig quickly.

===Chinese===

弗莱彻把箱门完全敞开，指着里面一个小隔间——隔间的钢门是被人用撬棍（jimmy）撬开的。接着他小心翼翼地从里面捧出一只钢盒，搁在书房桌上。

「这盒子怕是人人都上手摸过了吧？」克雷格脱口问道。

===Original===

A smile flitted across Fletcher’s features. “I thought of that, Kennedy,” he said. “I remembered what you once told me about fingerprints. Only myself has touched it, and I was careful to take hold of it only on the sides. The will was placed in this box, and the key to the box was usually in the lock. Well, the will is gone. That’s all; nothing else was touched. But for the life of me I can’t find a mark on the box, not a fingermark. Now on a hot and humid summer night like last night I should say it was pretty likely that anyone touching this metal box would have left fingermarks. Shouldn’t you think so, Kennedy?”

===Chinese===

弗莱彻脸上掠过一丝笑意。「这我早想到了，肯尼迪，」他说，「你从前给我讲过指纹的事，我记得呢。摸过这盒子的只有我一个人，而且我特意只捏住盒子两侧。遗嘱原先就存放在这盒子里，钥匙平常就插在锁眼上。喏，如今遗嘱没了。就这么一回事；旁的东西谁也没动过。可是怪了，这盒子上我怎么也找不出一点痕迹，连半个指纹都没有。像昨晚那样又闷又热的夏夜，依我说，谁碰了这只金属盒子，十有八九总该留下指纹才是。你说呢，肯尼迪？」

===Original===

Kennedy nodded and continued to examine the place where the compartment had been jimmied. A low whistle aroused us: coming over to the table, Craig tore a white sheet of paper off a pad lying there and deposited a couple of small particles on it.

“I found them sticking on the jagged edges of the steel where it had been forced,” he said. Then he whipped out a pocket magnifying-glass. “Not from a rubber glove,” he commented half to himself. “By Jove, one side of them shows lines that look as if they were the lines on a person’s fingers, and the other side is perfectly smooth. There’s not a chance of using them as a clue, except﻿—well, I didn’t know criminals in America knew that stunt.”

===Chinese===

肯尼迪点点头，继续察看隔间被撬的地方。一声低低的呼哨把我们惊动：克雷格走到桌边，从桌上的拍纸簿撕下一页白纸，往上面拈了两粒细小的东西。

「这是从钢门被撬开的锯齿边上粘下来的。」他说。随即他抽出一只袖珍放大镜。「不是橡胶手套上的，」他半自语地评道，「好家伙，这一面有纹路，看着正像人手指上的螺纹，另一面却光溜平滑。想拿它当线索是没指望了——只是——唔，我没想到美国的罪犯也懂这一手。」

===Original===

“What stunt?”

“Why, you know how keen the new detectives are on the fingerprint system? Well, the first thing some of the up-to-date criminals in Europe did was to wear rubber gloves so that they would leave no prints. But you can’t work very well with rubber gloves. Last fall in Paris I heard of a fellow who had given the police a lot of trouble. He never left a mark, or at least it was no good if he did. He painted his hands lightly with a liquid rubber which he had invented himself. It did all that rubber gloves would do and yet left him the free use of his fingers with practically the same keenness of touch. Fletcher, whatever is at the bottom of this affair, I feel sure right now that you have to deal with no ordinary criminal.”

===Chinese===

「哪一手？」

「嘿，如今的新式侦探不是把指纹法盯得紧吗？欧洲那些赶时髦的罪犯呢，头一件学会的事就是戴上橡胶手套，免得留下指印。可戴着橡胶手套，活儿就干不利索了。去年秋天在巴黎，我就听说有那么一位，把警方折腾得够呛。他从不留下痕迹，就算留下了也派不上用场。他往手上薄薄涂一层自己发明的液态橡胶（liquid rubber），既顶了橡胶手套的全部用处，又不妨碍手指活动自如，触觉照样灵敏。弗莱彻，不管这案子根子上是怎么回事，眼下我就可以断定：你要对付的，绝不是寻常罪犯。」

===Original===

“Do you suppose there are any relatives besides those we know of?” I asked Kennedy when Fletcher had left to summon the servants.

“No,” he replied, “I think not. Fletcher and Helen Bond, his second cousin, to whom he is engaged, are the only two.”

===Chinese===

「你说，会不会还有咱们不知道的亲戚？」等弗莱彻出去唤仆人时，我问肯尼迪。

「没有了，」他答道，「我看没有。算得上亲眷的，只有弗莱彻和海伦·邦德两个——她是他的远房堂妹，两人已经订了婚。」

===Original===

Kennedy continued to study the library. He walked in and out of the doors and examined the windows and viewed the safe from all angles.

“The old gentleman’s bedroom is here,” he said, indicating a door. “Now a good smart noise or perhaps even a light shining through the transom from the library might arouse him. Suppose he woke up suddenly and entered by this door. He would see the thief at work on the safe. Yes, that part of reconstructing the story is simple. But who was the intruder?”

===Chinese===

肯尼迪继续研究这间书房。他从各道门进进出出，检查了窗户，又从各个角度端详那保险箱。

「老先生的卧室在这儿。」他指着一扇门说。「夜里只要响动够大，说不定书房的灯光从门楣气窗透进去，就能把他惊醒。设想他猛然醒来，从这扇门进来，正好撞见窃贼在撬保险箱。对，故事的这一段不难复原。可闯进来的究竟是谁？」

===Original===

Just then Fletcher returned with the servants. The questioning was long and tedious, and developed nothing except that the butler admitted that he was uncertain whether the windows in the library were locked. The gardener was very obtuse, but finally contributed one possibly important fact. He had noted in the morning that the back gate, leading into a disused road closer to the bay than the main highway in front of the house, was open. It was rarely used, and was kept closed only by an ordinary hook. Whoever had opened it had evidently forgotten to hook it. He had thought it strange that it was unhooked, and in closing it he had noticed in the mud of the roadway marks that seemed to indicate that an automobile had stood there.

===Chinese===

正说着，弗莱彻领着仆人回来了。盘问冗长而乏味，一无所获，只有管家承认拿不准书房的窗户究竟锁了没有。园丁木讷得很，最后倒供出一条可能要紧的线索：他早上发觉后门开着——那道门通向一条废弃的老路，比宅前大路更近海湾。那道门平日少有人走，只拿一根普通搭扣搭着。开门的人显然忘了扣上。园丁本就觉得门没扣上蹊跷，关门时又留意到路面泥地上有一道道印痕，像是有辆汽车在那里停过。

===Original===

After the servants had gone, Fletcher asked us to excuse him for a while, as he wished to run over to the Greenes’, who lived across the bay. Miss Bond was completely prostrated by the death of her uncle, he said, and was in an extremely nervous condition. Meanwhile if we found any need of a machine we might use his uncle’s, or in fact anything around the place.

===Chinese===

仆人退下后，弗莱彻请我们稍坐片刻，他要过海湾对岸的格林家去一趟。他说，邦德小姐遭逢叔父变故，整个人垮了，神经紧张到了极点。我们若要用车，尽可以用他叔叔那辆，宅子里任何东西也任凭我们取用。

===Original===

“Walter,” said Craig, when Fletcher had gone, “I want to run back to town tonight, and I have something I’d like to have you do, too.”

We were soon speeding back along the splendid road to Long Island City, while he laid out our programme.

===Chinese===

「沃尔特，」弗莱彻走后克雷格说，「今晚我要赶回城里去，还有件事想让你办。」

我们很快便沿着那条漂亮的大路朝长岛市（Long Island City）疾驰而去，一路上他把行动计划布置停当。

===Original===

“You go down to the *Star* office,” he said, “and look through all the clippings on the whole Fletcher family. Get a complete story of the life of Helen Bond, too﻿—what she has done in society, with whom she has been seen mostly, whether she has made any trips abroad, and whether she has ever been engaged﻿—you know, anything likely to be significant. I’m going up to the apartment to get my camera and then to the laboratory to get some rather bulky paraphernalia I want to take out to Fletcherwood. Meet me at the Columbus Circle station at, say half-past ten.”

===Chinese===

「你到《明星报》报馆去，」他说，「把弗莱彻全家的剪报资料翻个遍。海伦·邦德的生平也要查个底朝天——她在社交场上做过什么，平日和谁来往最多，出没出过洋，订没订过婚——总之，凡是可能要紧的都要。我回公寓取相机，再去实验室拿几件挺占地方的仪器，要带到弗莱彻庄园去。十点半吧，哥伦布圆环站（Columbus Circle）见。」

===Original===

So we separated. My search revealed the fact that Miss Bond had always been intimate with the ultra-fashionable set, had spent last summer in Europe, a good part of the time in Switzerland and Paris with the Greenes. As far as I could find out she had never been reported engaged, but plenty of fortunes as well as foreign titles had been flitting about the ward of the steel-magnate.

Craig and I met at the appointed time. He had a lot of paraphernalia with him, and it did not add to our comfort as we sped back, but it wasn’t much over half an hour before we again found ourselves nearing Great Neck.

===Chinese===

于是我们分头行事。我这一查，查明邦德小姐素来混迹于最时髦的社交圈，去年夏天是在欧洲度过的，大半时间同格林一家待在瑞士和巴黎。就我所能查到的而言，她从未见报订婚，不过围着这位钢铁大亨的受监护姑娘转的，巨万家财也好，外国贵族的头衔也好，从来不少。

我和克雷格如约会合。他带了一大堆家什，回程车上挤得我们好不自在；不过半个多钟头，我们已再度临近大颈镇。

===Original===

Instead of going directly back to Fletcherwood, however, Craig had told the chauffeur to stop at the plant of the local electric light and power company, where he asked if he might see the record of the amount of current used the night before.

The curve sprawled across the ruled surface of the sheet by the automatic registering-needle was irregular, showing the ups and downs of the current, rising sharply from sundown and gradually declining after nine o’clock, as the lights went out. Somewhere between eleven and twelve o’clock, however, the irregular fall of the curve was broken by a quite noticeable upward twist.

===Chinese===

克雷格没有让司机径直回庄园，而是先在本地电气照明与电力公司的电厂前停了车，问能不能查一查前一晚的用电记录。

自动记录针在印着格线的记录纸上拖出一条起伏不定的曲线，显出电流的消长：日落之后骤然攀升，九点以后随着灯火渐次熄灭而缓缓回落。然而在十一点到十二点之间的某处，这条曲线不规则的下坠之势，被一个相当显眼的向上扭折打断了。

===Original===

Craig asked the men if that usually happened. They were quite sure that the curve as a rule went gradually down until twelve o’clock, when the power was shut off. But they did not see anything remarkable in it. “Oh, I suppose some of the big houses had guests,” volunteered the foreman, “and just to show off the place perhaps they turned on all the lights. I don’t know, sir, what it was, but it couldn’t have been a heavy drain, or we would have noticed it at the time, and the lights would all have been dim.”

“Well,” said Craig, “just watch and see if it occurs again tonight about the same time.”

“All right, sir.”

===Chinese===

克雷格问厂里的人，这种情形平时常不常见。他们一口咬定，按规矩曲线该一路缓降，直到十二点断电为止。但他们没觉出这里头有什么名堂。「哦，我琢磨是哪家大户来了客，」领班主动搭话，「兴许是为了显摆宅子，把灯全点上了。到底是什么事儿我说不上，先生，可那负载绝不会重——不然当时我们就会发现，各处的灯也都要发暗了。」

「好吧，」克雷格说，「今晚麻烦盯着点，看看同一时间还会不会出现。」

「好的，先生。」

===Original===

“And when you close down the plant for the night, will you bring the record card up to Fletcherwood?” asked Craig, slipping a bill into the pocket of the foreman’s shirt.

“I will, and thank you, sir.”

===Chinese===

「晚上停机之后，劳驾把记录卡送到弗莱彻庄园来，行吗？」克雷格问着，顺手把一张钞票塞进领班的衬衣口袋。

「一定送到，谢谢您，先生。」

===Original===

It was nearly half-past eleven when Craig had got his apparatus set up in the library at Fletcherwood. Then he unscrewed all the bulbs from the chandelier in the library and attached in their places connections with the usual green silk-covered flexible wire rope. These were then joined up to a little instrument which to me looked like a drill. Next he muffed the drill with a wad of felt and applied it to the safe door.

I could hear the dull *tat-tat* of the drill. Going into the bedroom and closing the door, I found that it was still audible to me, but an old man, inclined to deafness and asleep, would scarcely have been awakened by it. In about ten minutes Craig displayed a neat little hole in the safe door opposite the one made by the cracksman in the combination.

===Chinese===

等克雷格把设备在庄园书房里安装就绪，已快十一点半了。他拧下书房枝形吊灯上的全部灯泡，在原位接上接头，引出那种常见的绿丝包软电线，再统统连到一件小仪器上——在我眼里，那就是一支钻。随后他用一团毡子把钻裹住，对准了保险箱门。

钻头沉闷的「嗒嗒」声听得见。我走进卧室，掩上门，发现那声音仍隐约可闻；可要是位耳背贪睡的老人，多半不会被它吵醒。约莫十分钟工夫，克雷格便在箱门上钻出一个利落的小孔，位置正对着窃贼废掉转字锁时钻的那个。

===Original===

“I’m glad you’re honest,” I said, “or else we might be afraid of you﻿—perhaps even make you prove an alibi for last night’s job!”

He ignored my bantering and said in a tone such as he might have used before a class of students in the gentle art of scientific safe-cracking: “Now if the power company’s curve is just the same tonight as last night, that will show how the thing was done. I wanted to be sure of it, so I thought I’d try this apparatus which I smuggled in from Paris last year. I believe the old man happened to be wakeful and heard it.”

===Chinese===

「幸亏你为人诚实，」我说，「不然我们可得怕了你——说不定还要你为昨晚那桩活儿拿出不在场的证明呢！」

他不理会我的打趣，换上一副授课的腔调，仿佛正给学生讲解「科学撬保险箱之雅艺」：「要是电力公司今晚的曲线同昨晚一模一样，事情的来龙去脉就清楚了。我要的就是个确凿，所以打算试试这件去年我从巴黎私带回来的仪器。我相信，老人家那晚恰好没睡沉，听见了动静。」

===Original===

Then he pried off the door of the interior compartment which had been jimmied open. “Perhaps we may learn something by looking at this door and studying the marks left by the jimmy, by means of this new instrument of mine,” he said.

On the library table he fastened an arrangement with two upright posts supporting a dial which he called a “dynamometer.” The uprights were braced in the back, and the whole thing reminded me of a miniature guillotine.

===Chinese===

接着他撬下那个被撬开过的内层隔间的门。「看看这扇门，用我这件新仪器研究一下撬棍留下的痕迹，说不定能查出点东西。」他说。

他在书房桌上固定起一套装置：两根立柱，架着一只表盘，他管它叫「测力计（dynamometer）」。立柱背面加了斜撑，整件东西让我想起一座缩微的断头台。

===Original===

“This is my mechanical detective,” said Craig proudly. “It was devised by Bertillon himself, and he personally gave me permission to copy his own machine. You see, it is devised to measure pressure. Now let’s take an ordinary jimmy and see just how much pressure it takes to duplicate those marks on this door.”

Craig laid the piece of steel on the dynamometer in the position it had occupied in the safe, and braced it tightly. Then he took a jimmy and pressed on it with all his strength. The steel door was connected with the indicator, and the needle spun around until it indicated a pressure such as only a strong man could have exerted. Comparing the marks made in the steel in the experiment and by the safecracker, it was evident that no such pressure had been necessary. Apparently the lock on the door was only a trifling affair, and the steel itself was not very, tough. The safe-makers had relied on the first line of defence to repel attack.

Craig tried again and again, each time using less force. At last he got a mark just about similar to the original marks on the steel.

“Well, well, what do you think of that?” he exclaimed reflectively. “A child could have done that part of the job.”

===Chinese===

「这便是我的机械侦探，」克雷格得意地说。「出自贝蒂永（Bertillon）本人的设计，他还亲口准许我仿制他那台机器。你瞧，它是用来量压力的。来，咱们拿一根普通撬棍试试，看要在门上复现这些痕迹，得用多大的力。」

克雷格把那块钢门按它在保险箱里的原位卡到测力计上，牢牢固定。然后他抄起一根撬棍，倾尽全力往下压。钢门连着指示器，指针飞转，最后指出的压力，只有大力士才使得出来。可把实验压出的痕迹同撬保险箱贼留下的两相对照，明摆着根本用不着这么大的力气。看样子，门上的锁不过是件稀松平常的东西，那钢本身也不算很韧。造箱的人把御敌的希望，全寄托在了第一道防线上。

克雷格试了一回又一回，一次比一次用力小。最后他压出的痕迹，已同钢门上原有的痕迹大致无二。

「哎呀呀，这你怎么看？」他若有所思地叹道。「这一部分的活儿，小孩子也干得来。」

===Original===

Just then the lights went off for the night. Craig lighted the oil-lamp, and sat in silence until the electric light plant foreman appeared with; the card-record, which showed a curve practically identical with that of the night before.

===Chinese===

正在这时，夜间断电的时刻到了，灯全灭了。克雷格点起油灯，默默坐着，直到电厂领班送来记录卡——上面的曲线，同前一晚的几乎分毫不差。

===Original===

A few moments later Professor Fletcher’s machine came up the driveway, and he joined us with a worried and preoccupied look on his face that he could not conceal. “She’s terribly broken up by the suddenness of it all,” he murmured as he sank into an armchair. “The shock has been too much for her. In fact, I hadn’t the heart to tell her anything about the robbery, poor girl.” Then in a moment he asked, “Any more clues yet, Kennedy?”

“Well, nothing of first importance. I have only been trying to reconstruct the story of the robbery so that I can reason out a motive and a few details; then when the real clues come along we won’t have so much ground to cover. The cracksman was certainly clever. He used an electric drill to break the combination and ran it by the electric light current.”

===Chinese===

片刻之后，弗莱彻教授的汽车驶上车道。他来到我们中间，满脸掩饰不住的忧惶失神。「这一切来得太突然，她整个人都垮了。」他跌坐进安乐椅，喃喃道。「这打击对她太重了。说实话，失窃的事我根本没忍心对她讲，可怜的姑娘。」过了一会儿他问：「肯尼迪，又有新线索了吗？」

「头等的还没有。我一直在设法复原失窃的经过，好推断出动机和几处细节；等真正的线索冒出来，咱们也就不必大海捞针了。那窃贼确实高明。他用电钻钻坏转字锁，电力就取自电灯的电流。」

===Original===

“Whew!” exclaimed the professor, “is that so? He must have been above the average. That’s interesting.”

“By the way, Fletcher,” said Kennedy, “I wish you would introduce me to your fiancée tomorrow. I would like to know her.”

===Chinese===

「嚯！」教授失声道，「竟是这样？此人绝非等闲之辈。有意思。」

「对了，弗莱彻，」肯尼迪说，「明天我想请你引见一下你的未婚妻。我很想认识认识她。」

===Original===

“Gladly,” Fletcher replied, “only you must be careful what you talk about. Remember, the death of uncle has been quite a shock to her﻿—he was her only relative besides myself.”

“I will,” promised Kennedy, “and by the way, she may think it strange that I’m out here at a time like this. Perhaps you had better tell her I’m a nerve specialist or something of that sort﻿—anything not to connect me with the robbery, which you say you haven’t told her about.”

===Chinese===

「乐意之至，」弗莱彻答道，「只是说话务必留意分寸。别忘了，叔父的死对她打击不小——除了我，他是她在这世上唯一的亲人了。」

「我省得，」肯尼迪应承道，「另外，这种时候我跑到这儿来，她怕会犯疑。你不如对她说，我是个神经科专家，或者诸如此类——总之别让她把我跟失窃的事联想到一块儿，这事你不是还没告诉她吗。」

===Original===

The next morning found Kennedy out bright and early, for he had not had a very good chance to do anything during the night except reconstruct the details. He was now down by the back gate with his camera, where I found him turning it end-down and photographing the road. Together we made a thorough search of the woods and the road about the gate, but could discover absolutely nothing.

After breakfast I improvised a dark room and developed the films, while Craig went down the back lane along the shore “looking for clues,” as he said briefly. Toward noon he returned, and I could see that he was in a brown study. So I said nothing, but handed him the photographs of the road. He took them and laid them down in a long line on the library floor. They seemed to consist of little ridges of dirt on either side of a series of regular round spots, some of the spots very clear and distinct on the sides, others quite obscure in the centre. Now and then where you would expect to see one of the spots, just for the symmetry of the thing, it was missing. As I looked at the line of photographs on the floor I saw that they were a photograph of the track made by the tire of an automobile, and I suddenly recalled what the gardener had said.

===Chinese===

第二天一早，肯尼迪天一亮就出了门——夜里除了复原细节，他实在没机会做别的。此刻他正带着相机守在后门边，我去找他时，见他正把相机倒转朝下，给路面拍照。我们俩又把后门四周的树林和道路彻底搜了一遍，却一无所获。

早饭后我临时布置了间暗房，冲洗底片；克雷格则沿着岸边的后巷下去「找线索」——他只简短地说了这么一句。近中午他才回来，一副心事重重的样子。我便什么也不说，只把路面照片递给他。他接过去，一张挨一张在书房地板上排成长长一列。照片上似乎是一串规整的圆斑，两侧夹着细细的泥棱；有的圆斑边缘十分清晰，有的中心却相当模糊。时不时地，单为对称起见本该有个圆斑的地方，却偏偏空着。我望着地板上这列照片，忽然看明白了：这是一条汽车轮胎辙印的照片——我猛地想起园丁说的话。

===Original===

Next Craig produced the results of his morning’s work, which consisted of several dozen sheets of white paper, carefully separated into three bundles. These he also laid down in long lines on the floor, each package in a separate line. Then I began to realise what he was doing, and became fascinated in watching him on his hands and knees eagerly scanning the papers and comparing them with the photographs. At last he gathered up two of the sets of papers very decisively and threw them away. Then he shifted the third set a bit, and laid it closely parallel to the photographs.

“Look at these, Walter,” he said. “Now take this deep and sharp indentation. Well, there’s a corresponding one in the photograph. So you can pick them out one for another. Now here’s one missing altogether on the paper. So it is in the photograph.”

===Chinese===

接着克雷格亮出他一上午的成果：几十张白纸，仔细分成三沓。他也把这些在地板上排成长列，一沓一列。我这才咂摸出他在干什么，看得入了迷：只见他趴在地上，急切地逐一检视纸片，同照片比对。最后他极干脆地收起其中两沓，扔到一旁，把第三沓略略挪动，紧紧并排贴着照片放下。

「看这些，沃尔特，」他说。「喏，取这道又深又利的压痕，照片上正有一道对应的，这么着就能一一配对。再看这张纸上独独缺了一处——照片里同样缺着。」

===Original===

Almost like a schoolboy in his glee, he was comparing the little round circles made by the metal insertions in an “anti-skid” automobile tire. Time and again I had seen imprints like that left in the dust and grease of an asphalted street or the mud of a road. It had never occurred to me that they might be used in any way. Yet here Craig was, calmly tracing out the similarity before my very eyes, identifying the marks made in the photograph with the prints left on the bits of paper.

As I followed him, I had a most curious feeling of admiration for his genius. “Craig,” I cried, “that’s the thumbprint of an automobile.”

===Chinese===

他兴致勃勃，简直像个学童，比对的正是一根「防滑」（anti-skid）汽车轮胎里金属嵌钉压出的小圆圈。这类印子，我在柏油街面的尘土油垢里、在道路的泥泞里见过不知多少回，却从未想过它们竟有半点用处。可此时此刻，克雷格就在我眼皮底下，从容不迫地把相似之处一一理出，把照片上的痕迹同纸片上的印子一一认对。

跟着他的思路走，我心里对他的天才涌起一股说不出的钦佩。「克雷格，」我叫出声来，「这是汽车的指纹啊。」

===Original===

“There speaks the yellow journalist,” he answered merrily. “ ‘Thumb Print System Applied to Motor Cars’﻿—I can see the Sunday feature story you have in your mind with that headline already. Yes, Walter, that’s precisely what this is. The Berlin police have used it a number of times with the most startling results.”

“But, Craig,” I exclaimed suddenly, “the paper prints, where did you get them? What machine is it?”

===Chinese===

「听听，这可是黄色新闻记者的口吻，」他笑呵呵地答道。「『指纹法应用于汽车』——你心里盘算的周日特稿，连标题我都替你想好了。没错，沃尔特，就是这么回事。柏林警方用这一手查过好几回，成效惊人。」

「可是，克雷格，」我突然叫道，「那些纸上的印子，你从哪儿弄来的？是哪辆车？」

===Original===

“It’s one not very far from here,” he answered sententiously, and I saw he would say nothing more that might fix a false suspicion on anyone. Still, my curiosity was so great that if there had been an opportunity I certainly should have tried out his plan on all the cars in the Fletcher garage.

Kennedy would say nothing more, and we ate our luncheon in silence. Fletcher, who had decided to lunch with the Greenes, called Kennedy up on the telephone to tell him it would be all right for him to call on Miss Bond later in the afternoon.

===Chinese===

「就在离这儿不远的一处。」他言简意赅地答道。我明白，他不会再多说什么，免得平白让什么人蒙上不白之冤。话虽如此，我的好奇心实在按捺不住——但凡有个机会，我肯定要把弗莱彻家车库里的汽车挨个儿照他的法子验一遍。

肯尼迪再不肯多说一个字，我们便默默用了午餐。弗莱彻决定在格林家吃饭，打电话告诉肯尼迪，下午晚些时候去拜望邦德小姐没有问题。

===Original===

“And I may bring over the apparatus I once described to you to determine just what her nervous condition is?” he asked. Apparently the answer was yes, for Kennedy hung up the receiver with a satisfied, “Goodbye.”

“Walter, I want you to come along with me this afternoon as my assistant. Remember I’m now Dr. Kennedy, the nerve specialist, and you are Dr. Jameson, my colleague, and we are to be in consultation on a most important case.”

===Chinese===

「我还可以带上我曾跟您说过的那套仪器，去测定她的神经状况究竟如何？」他问。答案显然是肯定的——肯尼迪心满意足地道了声「再见」，挂上了听筒。

「沃尔特，今天下午我要你以助手身份随我同行。记住了：我现在是神经科专家肯尼迪医生，你是我同事詹姆森医生，咱们要会诊一个极其重要的病例。」

===Original===

“Do you think that’s fair?” I asked hotly, “to take that girl off her guard, to insinuate yourself into her confidence as a medical adviser, and worm out of her some kind of fact incriminating someone? I suppose that’s your plan, and I don’t like the ethics, or rather the lack of ethics, of the thing.”

“Now think a minute, Walter. Perhaps I am wrong; I don’t know. Certainly I feel that the end will justify the means. I have an idea that I can get from Miss Bond the only clue that I need, one that will lead straight to the criminal. Who knows? I have a suspicion that the thing I’m going to do is the highest form of your so-called ethics. If what Fletcher tells us is true that girl is going insane over this thing. Why should she be so shocked over the death of an uncle she did not live with? I tell you she knows something about this case that it is necessary for us to know, too. If she doesn’t tell someone, it will eat her mind out. I’ll add a dinner to the box of cigars we have already bet on this case that what I’m going to do is for the best﻿—for her best.”

===Chinese===

「你觉得这公平吗？」我愤然质问，「趁人家姑娘不设防，打着医学顾问的幌子骗取信任，再从她嘴里套出什么牵连旁人的事实？我猜你就是这么盘算的。这种手段的道德——不，这种没道德的勾当——我不敢恭维。」

「先想想再说，沃尔特。也许是我错了；我不知道。但我的确相信，目的正当，手段亦无愧。我有预感，我要的那条线索——那条直通罪犯的线索——只有邦德小姐能给。谁说得准呢？我倒疑心，我将要做的，恰恰是你那套所谓道德的最高形式。倘若弗莱彻所言不虚，那姑娘正被这件事逼疯。一个不同住的叔父死了，她何苦震惊至此？告诉你，她知道这案子的内情，而这内情咱们也非知道不可。再不找人倾诉，这事会把她的心啃空的。咱们在这案子上已经赌了一盒雪茄，我再添一顿晚饭：我下面要做的事，是为了她好——千真万确为她好。」

===Original===

Again I yielded, for I was coming to have more and more faith in the old Kennedy I had seen made over into a first-class detective, and together we started for the Greenes’, Craig carrying something in one of those long black handbags which physicians use.

Fletcher met us on the driveway. He seemed to be very much affected, for his face was drawn, and he shifted from one position to another nervously, from which we inferred that Miss Bond was feeling worse. It was late afternoon, almost verging on twilight, as he led us through the reception-hall and thence onto a long porch overlooking the bay and redolent with honeysuckle.

===Chinese===

我又一次让了步——眼见这位老克雷格脱胎换骨成了一流侦探，我对他的信心一天深过一天。于是我们一道动身去格林家；克雷格提着医生用的那种细长黑色手提包，里面装着家伙。

弗莱彻到车道上来迎我们。他像是大受煎熬，面容揪紧，局促不安地一会儿换个姿势；我们由此推断，邦德小姐的情形更糟了。时已向晚，天色将暝，他领我们穿过接待厅，步上一道长长的游廊——廊下俯临海湾，满架金银花香气袭人。

===Original===

Miss Bond was half reclining in a wicker chair us we entered. She started to rise to greet us, but Fletcher gently restrained her, saying, as he introduced us, that he guessed the doctors would pardon any informality from an invalid.

Fletcher was a pretty fine fellow, and I had come to like him; but I soon found myself wondering what he had ever done to deserve winning such a girl as Helen Bond. She was what I should describe as the ideal type of “new” woman﻿—tall and athletic, yet without any affectation of mannishness. The very first thought that struck me was the incongruousness of a girl of her type suffering from an attack of “nerves,” and I felt sure it must be as Craig had said, that she was concealing a secret that was having a terrible effect on her. A casual glance might not have betrayed the true state of her feelings, for her dark hair and large brown eyes and the tan of many suns on her face and arms betokened anything but the neurasthenic. One felt instinctively that she was, with all her athletic grace, primarily a womanly woman.

===Chinese===

我们进去时，邦德小姐半倚在一张藤椅里。她想起身相迎，却被弗莱彻轻轻按住；他一边为我们引见，一边说，病人失礼之处，想必医生们不会见怪。

弗莱彻人是挺不错的，我也渐渐喜欢上了他；可我不免纳闷，他究竟修了什么福，能赢得海伦·邦德这样的姑娘。要我说，她正是「新」女性的理想典型——高挑健美，却不带半点故作须眉的习气。我脑子里冒出的头一个念头便是：她这等人竟会犯「神经衰弱」，实在不伦不类；随即我便认定，事情必如克雷格所言——她藏着一桩秘密，那秘密正可怕地噬咬着她。乍一看，她的真实心境未必露馅：深色的头发，棕色的大眼睛，那张脸和两条手臂被无数骄阳晒出的棕色，怎么看也不像个神经衰弱患者。可你本能地感觉到：纵有一身运动的飒爽，她骨子里首先是个十足的女人。

===Original===

The sun sinking toward the hills across the bay softened the brown of her skin and, as I observed by watching her closely, served partially to conceal the nervousness which was wholly unnatural in a girl of such poise. When she smiled there was a false note in it; it was forced and it was sufficiently evident to me that she was going through a mental hell of conflicting emotions that would have killed a woman of less self-control.

I felt that I would like to be in Fletcher’s shoes﻿—doubly so when, at Kennedy’s request, he withdrew, leaving me to witness the torture of a woman of such fine sensibilities, already hunted remorselessly by her own thoughts.

===Chinese===

落日斜沉向海湾对岸的山峦，把她皮肤上的棕色映得柔和了，也——我贴近观察她时看得分明——多少遮掩了那份紧张；以她这般沉静自持的姑娘，那份紧张本不该有。她微笑时，笑里透着一丝假音；那笑是硬挤出来的，而在我看来越发清楚：她正经受着一场矛盾情感交织的内心炼狱——换一个自控力稍差的女人，早已被折磨死了。

我心想，恨不得自己就是弗莱彻——待到应肯尼迪之请退出去、撇下我来眼睁睁看着这样一位敏感的女子受煎熬时，这念头更强烈了一倍；她早已被自己的思绪追猎得无处可逃。

===Original===

Still, I will give Kennedy credit for a tactfulness that I didn’t know the old fellow possessed. He carried through the preliminary questions very well for a pseudo-doctor, appealing to me as his assistant on inconsequential things that enabled me to “save my face” perfectly. When he came to the critical moment of opening the black bag, he made a very appropriate and easy remark about not having brought any sharp shiny instruments or nasty black drugs.

“All I wish to do, Miss Bond, is to make a few, simple little tests of your nervous condition. One of them we specialists call reaction time, and another is a test of heart action. Neither is of any seriousness at all, so I beg of you not to become excited, for the chief value consists in having the patient perfectly quiet and normal. After they are over I think I’ll know whether to prescribe absolute rest or a visit to Newport.”

===Chinese===

不过说句公道话，肯尼迪这份老练周全，倒是我先前不知道这位老兄也有的。他冒牌医生的身份，把开场问诊应付得有模有样，还时不时在无关紧要处请教我这个「助手」，让我把面子撑得十足。待到开黑提包的关键一刻，他又恰到好处、轻描淡写地来了一句：没带什么锋光闪闪的锐利器械，也没带那些黑乎乎的苦药。

「邦德小姐，我想做的，不过是几样简简单单的小测验，测测您的神经状况。其中一样，我们行内人叫反应时间（reaction time）；另一样，是测心脏的活动。两样都毫无严重性可言，所以千万请您别激动——测验要紧的，是病人完全安静、如常。做完之后，我想我就能开出方子了：是绝对静养呢，还是去纽波特（Newport）走一遭。」

===Original===

She smiled languidly, as he adjusted a long, tightly fitting rubber glove on her shapely forearm and then encased it in a larger, absolutely inflexible covering of leather. Between the rubber glove and the leather covering was a liquid communicating by a glass tube with a sort of dial. Craig had often explained to me how the pressure of the blood was registered most minutely on the dial, showing the varied emotions as keenly as if you had taken a peep into the very mind of the subject. I think the experimental psychologists called the thing a “plethysmograph.”

Then he had an apparatus which measured association time. The essential part of this instrument was the operation of a very delicate stopwatch, and this duty was given to me. It was nothing more nor less than measuring the time that elapsed between his questions to her and her answers, while he recorded the actual questions and answers and noted the results which I worked out. Neither of us was unfamiliar with the process, for when we were in college these instruments were just coming into use in America. Kennedy had never let his particular branch of science narrow him, but had made a practice of keeping abreast of all the important discoveries and methods in other fields. Besides, I had read articles about the chronoscope, the plethysmograph, the sphygmograph, and others of the new psychological instruments. Craig carried it off, however, as if he did that sort of thing as an everyday employment.

===Chinese===

他往她线条优美的小臂上套进一只长而贴身的橡胶手套，外面再罩上一只更大的、绝无弹性的皮质套筒，她有气无力地笑了笑。橡胶手套与皮套之间充着液体，液体经一根玻璃管连通着一只类似表盘的装置。克雷格从前常对我讲解，血压如何在表盘上纤毫毕现地记录下来，把种种情绪的波动显示得历历分明，就像窥见了受测者的心底。我想，实验心理学家们管这玩意儿叫「体积描记器（plethysmograph）」。

接着他又取出一套测联想时间（association time）的装置。这仪器的关键在于操作一只极灵敏的秒表，这差事派给了我。说白了，就是计量他向她发问和她作答之间隔去的时间；他则把问答原原本本记下，再把我算出的结果一一标注。这套流程我们俩都不生疏——当年读大学那会儿，这类仪器才刚刚传进美国。肯尼迪从不让自己那门专科框住眼界，一向留意跟紧其他领域一切重要的发现与方法。何况，计时镜（chronoscope）、体积描记器、脉搏描记器（sphygmograph）这些新式心理学仪器，我都读过有关的文章。可克雷格那一副做派，倒仿佛这是他的日常营生似的。

===Original===

“Now, Miss Bond,” he said, and his voice was so reassuring and persuasive that I could see she was not made even a shade more nervous by our simple preparations, “the game﻿—it is just like a children’s parlour game﻿—is just this: I will say a word﻿—take ‘dog,’ for instance. You are to answer back immediately the first word that comes into your mind suggested by it﻿—say ‘cat.’ I will say ‘chain,’ for example, and probably you will answer ‘collar,’ and so on. Do you catch my meaning? It may seem ridiculous, no doubt, but before we are through I feel sure you’ll see how valuable such a test is, particularly in a simple case of nervousness such as yours.”

===Chinese===

「好了，邦德小姐，」他说，嗓音是那样宽慰人心、循循善诱，我看得出，我们这点简单的准备丝毫没有令她多一分紧张，「这游戏——就跟孩子们的室内游戏一个样——规矩是这样：我说一个词——比如『狗』。您立刻回答脑海里被它勾起的第一个词——比如『猫』。比方我说『链子』，您多半会答『项圈』，如此等等。明白我的意思吗？这游戏看着是有点可笑，不过我担保，玩到后来您就会明白这种测验多么管用——尤其是对您这样单纯的神经紧张。」

===Original===

I don’t think she found any sinister interpretation in his words, but I did, and if ever I wanted to protest it was then, but my voice seemed to stick in my throat.

He was beginning. It was clearly up to me to give in and not interfere. As closely as I was able I kept my eyes riveted on the watch and other apparatus, while my ears and heart followed with mingled emotions the low, musical voice of the girl.

===Chinese===

我想她并没听出他话里暗藏的机关，我可听出来了。若说有哪个时刻我想抗辩，就是那一刻了——可我的嗓音仿佛卡在了喉咙里。

他开始了。我显然只能认命，不再从中作梗。我竭力把目光钉在秒表和其他仪器上，耳朵和心却怀着百般滋味，追随着姑娘那低柔悦耳的声音。

===Original===

I will not give all the test, for there was much of it, particularly at the start, that was in reality valueless, since it was merely leading up to the “surprise tests.” From the colourless questions Kennedy suddenly changed. It was done in an instant, when Miss Bond had been completely disarmed and put off her guard.

“Night,” said Kennedy. “Day,” came back the reply from Miss Bond.

===Chinese===

测验的全过程我不必尽述，其中许多——尤其是开头部分——其实毫无价值，不过是为后面的「突击测验」做铺垫而已。就在邦德小姐完全卸下心防、毫无戒备之际，肯尼迪陡然变了，撇开那些寡淡无味的问题换了打法。一切只在转瞬之间。

「夜。」肯尼迪说。「昼。」邦德小姐应声而答。

===Original===

“Automobile.” “Horse.”

“Bay.” “Beach.”

“Road.” “Forest.”

“Gate.” “Fence.”

“Path.” “Shrubs.”

“Porch.” “House.”

===Chinese===

「汽车。」「马。」

「海湾。」「沙滩。」

「路。」「森林。」

「门。」「栅栏。」

「小径。」「灌木。」

「游廊。」「房子。」

===Original===

Did I detect or imagine a faint hesitation?

“Window.” “Curtain.”

===Chinese===

那一瞬的迟疑，是我察觉到了，还是错觉？

「窗。」「窗帘。」

===Original===

Yes, it was plain that time. But the words followed one another in quick succession. There was no rest. She had no chance to collect herself. I noted the marked difference in the reaction time and, in my sympathy, damned this cold, scientific third degree.

“Paris.” “France.”

===Chinese===

没错，这一回明明白白。可词一个接一个，密得让人喘不过气。没有停顿。她毫无定神的空当。我留意到反应时间的显著变化，出于同情，心里咒骂起这场冷酷的科学逼供。

「巴黎。」「法国。」

===Original===

“Quartier Latin.” “Students.”

“Apaches.” Craig gave it its Gallicised pronunciation, “Apash.” “Really, Dr. Kennedy,” she said, “there is nothing I can associate with them﻿—well, yes, *les vaches*, I believe. You had better count that question out. I’ve wasted a good many seconds.”

===Chinese===

「拉丁区。」「学生。」

「阿帕奇（Apache）。」克雷格按法语的腔调念成了「阿帕什」（Apash）。「说真的，肯尼迪医生，」她说，「这个词我实在联想不出什么——哦，有了，*les vaches*，我想是的。这一问您最好作废，我耽搁了好几秒钟。」

===Original===

“Very well, let us try again,” he replied with a forced unconcern, though the answer seemed to interest him, for “*les vaches*” meant “the cows,” otherwise known as the police.

No lawyer could have revelled in an opportunity for putting leading questions more ruthlessly than did Kennedy. He snapped out his words sharply and unexpectedly.

“Chandelier.” “Light.”

===Chinese===

「很好，咱们再来。」他答道，装出一副若无其事的样子，可这个答案显然叫他上了心——因为「*les vaches*」的意思是「母牛」，也就是对警察的谑称。

再没有哪个律师，能比肯尼迪更狠地抓住机会连番诱导盘问。他吐出的词，短促、尖锐、出其不意。

「枝形吊灯。」「灯。」

===Original===

“Electric light,” he emphasised. “Broadway,” she answered, endeavouring to force a new association of ideas to replace one which she strove to conceal.

===Chinese===

「电灯。」他加重了语气。「百老汇。」她答道，竭力造出一段新的联想，来顶替她拼命想遮掩的那一段。

===Original===

“Safe.” “Vaults.” Out of the corner of my eye I could see that the indicator showed a tremendously increased heart action. As for the reaction time, I noted that it was growing longer and more significant. Remorselessly he pressed his words home. Mentally I cursed him.

“Rubber.” “Tire.”

===Chinese===

「保险箱。」「地下金库。」我眼角的余光瞥见，指示器上的心跳骤然大增。至于反应时间，我注意到它越拖越长，意味也越来越深。他毫不留情，词词逼到要害。我在心里咒骂他。

「橡胶。」「轮胎。」

===Original===

“Steel.” “Pittsburg,” she cried at random.

“Strongbox,” No answer.

===Chinese===

「钢。」「匹兹堡。」她随口喊出。

「铁柜。」没有回答。

===Original===

“Lock.” Again no answer. He hurried his words. I was leaning forward, tense with excitement and sympathy.

“Key.” Silence and a fluttering of the blood pressure indicator.

“Will.”

===Chinese===

「锁。」还是没有回答。他加快了节奏。我身子前倾，紧张与怜悯交织，绷得紧紧的。

「钥匙。」沉默，只有血压指示器上一阵急颤。

「遗嘱。」

===Original===

As the last word was uttered her air of frightened defiance was swept away. With a cry of anguish, she swayed to her feet. “No, no, doctor, you must not, you must not,” she cried with outstretched arms. “Why do you pick out those words of all others? Can it be﻿—” If I had not caught her I believe she would have fainted.

===Chinese===

最后一个词一出口，她那惊惶抗拒的神气霎时崩溃。她哀呼一声，摇摇晃晃地站起身来。「不，不，医生，您不能这样，您不能这样，」她张开双臂喊道。「您为什么偏偏挑那些词？难道——」若不是我一把扶住，我相信她当场就要晕倒。

===Original===

The indicator showed a heart alternately throbbing with feverish excitement and almost stopping with fear. What would Kennedy do next, I wondered, determined to shut him off as soon as I possibly could. From the moment I had seen her I had been under her spell. Mine should have been Fletcher’s place, I knew, though I cannot but say that I felt a certain grim pleasure in supporting even momentarily such a woman in her time of need.

“Can it be that you have guessed what no one in the world, no, not even dear old Jack, dreams? Oh, I shall go mad, mad, mad!”

===Chinese===

指示器上，那颗心时而狂热地悸动，时而惊惧得几乎停跳。我在想，肯尼迪下一步还要怎样，打定主意一有机会立刻叫停。从见到她的那一刻起，我就被她摄住了。我心里明白，此刻本该站在那里的是弗莱彻——可我仍不能不说，在这样一位女子危难之际，哪怕只扶她片刻，我心里也掠过一丝近乎残忍的快意。

「难道说，您猜到了这世上无人知晓的事——不，连亲爱的老杰克都做梦也想不到的事？哦，我要疯了，疯了，疯了！」

===Original===

Kennedy was on his feet in an instant, advancing toward her. The look in his eyes was answer enough for her. She knew that he knew, and she paled and shuddered, shrinking away from him.

“Miss Bond,” he said in a voice that forced attention﻿—it was low and vibrating with feeling﻿—“Miss Bond, have you ever told a lie to shield a friend?”

“Yes,” she said, her eyes meeting his.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪霍然起身，向她逼近。他眼中的神色，对她已是足够的回答。她知道他知道了；她面无人色，瑟瑟发抖，直往后缩。

「邦德小姐，」他开口了，那嗓音令人不能不听——低沉，因情感而震颤——「邦德小姐，您可曾为了庇护一个朋友而说过谎？」

「说过。」她说，目光直直迎着他。

===Original===

“So can I,” came back the same tense voice, “when I know the truth about that friend.”

Then for the first time tears came in a storm. Her breath was quick and feverish. “No one will ever believe, no one will understand. They will say that I killed him, that I murdered him.”

Through it all I stood almost speechless, puzzled. What did it all mean?

===Chinese===

「我也能。」同样紧绷的嗓音答道，「只要我知道那朋友的真相。」

这时，眼泪终于决堤。她的呼吸急促而滚烫。「没有人会相信，没有人会明白。他们会说是我害死了他，是我谋杀了他。」

我自始至终几乎说不出话，一头雾水。这一切究竟是怎么回事？

===Original===

“No,” said Kennedy, “no, for they will never know of it.”

“Never know?”

“Never﻿—if in the end justice is done. Have you the will? Or did you destroy it?”

===Chinese===

「不，」肯尼迪说，「不，因为他们永远不会知道。」

「永远不知道？」

「永远——只要最终正义得以伸张。遗嘱在您手上吗？还是已经毁了？」

===Original===

It was a bold stroke.

“Yes. No. Here it is. How could I destroy it, even though it was burning out my very soul?”

She literally tore the paper from the bosom of her dress and cast it from her in horror and terror.

===Chinese===

这是一记大胆的狠招。

「在。不。在这儿。我怎么能毁了它——哪怕它烧灼得我魂灵俱焦？」

她当真从连衣裙的怀里扯出那张纸，惊恐万状地扔了出去。

===Original===

Kennedy picked it up, opened it, and glanced hurriedly through it. “Miss Bond,” he said, “Jack shall never know a word of this. I shall tell him that the will has been found unexpectedly in John Fletcher’s desk among some other papers. Walter, swear on your honour as a gentleman that this will was found in old Fletcher’s desk.”

“Dr. Kennedy, how can I ever thank you?” she exclaimed, sinking wearily down into a chair and pressing her hands to her throbbing forehead.

“By telling me just how you came by this will, so that when you and Fletcher are married I may be as good a friend, without suspicion, to you as I am to him. I think a full confession would do you good, Miss Bond. Would you prefer to have Dr. Jameson not hear it?”

===Chinese===

肯尼迪拾起纸来，展开，匆匆扫了一遍。「邦德小姐，」他说，「这件事，杰克一个字也不会知道。我会告诉他，遗嘱是在约翰·弗莱彻的书桌里夹杂在其他文件中意外找到的。沃尔特，以你绅士的名誉起誓：这份遗嘱是在老弗莱彻的书桌里找到的。」

「肯尼迪医生，叫我怎么谢您才好？」她喊道，颓然跌坐进椅子里，双手按住突突直跳的额头。

「就把这遗嘱的来历原原本本告诉我——这样，等您和弗莱彻结了婚，我对您也能像对他一样，做个不惹猜疑的挚友。我想，痛痛快快吐个干净，对您有好处，邦德小姐。您愿意让詹姆森医生避开不听吗？」

===Original===

“No, he may stay.”

“This much I know, Miss Bond. Last summer in Paris with the Greenes you must have chanced to hear, of Pillard, the Apache, one of the most noted cracksmen the world has ever produced. You sought him out. He taught you how to paint your fingers with a rubber composition, how to use an electric drill, how to use the old-fashioned jimmy. You went down to Fletcherwood by the back road about a quarter after eleven the night of the robbery in the Greenes’ little electric runabout. You entered the library by an unlocked window, you coupled your drill to the electric light connections of the chandelier. You had to work quickly, for the power would go off at midnight, yet you could not do the job later, when they were sleeping more soundly, for the very same reason.”

===Chinese===

「不必，他可以留下。」

「以下这些，我已经知道，邦德小姐。去年夏天，您同格林一家在巴黎，想必有机会听说了皮拉尔（Pillard）——阿帕奇，世人见过最有名的保险箱大盗之一。您寻到了他门下。他教会您往手指上涂橡胶涂料，教会您用电钻，教会您使老式撬棍。案发当晚十一点一刻前后，您开着格林家那辆小小的电动轻便车（electric runabout），取道后路去了弗莱彻庄园。您从一扇没锁的窗进了书房，把电钻接上了枝形吊灯的电灯线路。您必须抢时间，因为午夜一到就断电；可您又不能更晚动手——等一家人睡得更沉的时候——原因还是这同一个。」

===Original===

It was uncanny as Kennedy rushed along in his reconstruction of the scene, almost unbelievable. The girl watched him, fascinated.

“John Fletcher was wakeful that night. Somehow or other he heard you at work. He entered the library and, by the light streaming from his bedroom, he saw who it was. In anger he must have addressed you, and his passion got the better of his age﻿—he fell suddenly on the floor with a stroke of apoplexy. As you bent over him he died. But why did you ever attempt so foolish an undertaking? Didn’t you know that other people knew of the will and its terms, that you were sure to be traced out in the end, if not by friends, by foes? How did you suppose you could profit by destroying the will, of which others knew the provisions?”

===Chinese===

肯尼迪一泻千里地复原着现场，听着的人只觉毛骨悚然，几乎不敢相信。姑娘怔怔地望着他，像被定住了一样。

「那天夜里约翰·弗莱彻没有睡沉。不知怎么，他听见了您在干活。他走进书房，借着卧室里泻出的灯光，看清了来人是谁。他准是在盛怒之下呵斥了您，怒火压垮了他的年纪——他中风猝然倒地。您俯身去看他时，他已经死了。可您为什么要干这等蠢事？您难道不知道，遗嘱及其条款还有旁人知情？到最后您必然被查出来——查您的不是朋友，就是仇家。条款的底细人家一清二楚，您毁了遗嘱，又能落得什么好处？」

===Original===

Any other woman than Helen Bond would have been hysterical long before Kennedy had finished pressing home remorselessly one fact after another of her story. But, with her, the relief now after the tension of many hours of concealment seemed to nerve her to go to the end and tell the truth.

What was it? Had she some secret lover for whom she had dared all to secure the family fortune? Or was she shielding someone dearer to her than her own reputation? Why had Kennedy made Fletcher withdraw?

===Chinese===

换作任何一个别的女子，不等肯尼迪这样毫不留情地把她的故事一桩桩一件件逼到眼前，早就歇斯底里了。可她不一样——藏了这许多个钟头，此刻紧绷一松，反倒像是给了她勇气，索性走到头，把真话说完。

这是怎么回事？她有个秘密情人，为着他才豁出去要攫住这份家产？还是她在庇护某个比她自己的名声更亲的人？肯尼迪又为什么支开弗莱彻？

===Original===

Her eyes dropped and her breast rose and fell with suppressed emotion. Yet I was hardly prepared for her reply when at last she slowly raised her head and looked us calmly in the face.

“I did it because I loved Jack.”

Neither of us spoke. I, at least, had fallen completely under the spell of this masterful woman. Right or wrong, I could not restrain a feeling of admiration and amazement.

===Chinese===

她垂下眼帘，胸口随着强抑的情感起伏。可当她终于缓缓抬起头，平静地直视我们时，她的回答仍让我措手不及。

「我这么做，是因为我爱杰克。」

我们俩都没有说话。至少是我，已经彻底拜倒在这位气魄夺人的女子的魅惑之下。无论对错，我抑不住心头的惊叹与钦佩。

===Original===

“Yes,” she said as her voice thrilled with emotion, “strange as it may sound to you, it was not love of self that made me do it. I was, I am madly in love with Jack. No other man has ever inspired such respect and love as he has. His work in the university I have fairly gloated over. And yet﻿—and yet, Dr. Kennedy, can you not see that I am different from Jack? What would I do with the income of the wife of even the dean of the new school? The annuity provided for me in that will is paltry. I need millions. From the tiniest baby I have been reared that way. I have always expected this fortune. I have been given everything I wanted. But it is different when one is married﻿—you must have your own money. I need a fortune, for then I could have the town house, the country house, the yacht, the motors, the clothes, the servants that I need﻿—they are as much a part of my life as your profession is of yours. I must have them.

“And now it was all to slip from my hands. True, it was to go in such a way by this last will as to make Jack happy in his new school. I could have let that go, if that was all. There are other fortunes that have been laid at my feet. But I wanted Jack, and I knew Jack wanted me. Dear boy, he never could realise how utterly unhappy intellectual poverty would have made me and how my unhappiness would have reacted on him in the end. In reality this great and beneficent philanthropy was finally to blight both our love and our lives.

===Chinese===

「是的，」她说，声音激动得发颤，「在你们听来也许不可思议，可逼我这么做的，并不是自私自爱。我从前、如今都疯狂地爱着杰克。从来没有哪个男人，能像他这样唤起我如此的敬与爱。他在大学里的事业，我简直是心醉神迷、与有荣焉。然而——然而，肯尼迪医生，您难道看不出，我和杰克不是一路人？哪怕做新学院院长的太太，那份进项于我有什么用？那份遗嘱里给我安排的年金，少得可怜。我要的是几百万。从襁褓之中，我受的就是这种教养。这份家产，我从来就是等着到手的。我要的东西，从来就没有得不到的。可人一旦结了婚，情形就不同了——你必须有自己的钱。我需要一大笔财产，有了它，我才有城里的宅子、乡间的别墅、游艇、汽车、衣裳、仆役——我要的这一切，如同您的职业之于您，本就是我生命的一部分。我非有不可。

「可如今，这一切都要从我手里溜走了。不错，按这最后一份遗嘱的安排，这笔钱将成就杰克的新学院，让他得偿所愿。倘若仅止于此，我本可以撒手。送到我脚边来的家财，又不是独此一份。可我要的是杰克，我也知道杰克要的是我。这傻孩子，他永远不会明白，智识上的清贫会把我折磨到何等地步，而我的不快，到头来又会怎样反噬他自己。说到底，这桩盛大的、悲天悯人的善举，终究要毁掉我们的爱情，也毁掉我们俩的一生。

===Original===

“What was I to do? Stand by and see my life and my love ruined or refuse Jack for the fortune of a man I did not love? Helen Bond is not that kind of a woman, I said to myself. I consulted the greatest lawyer I knew. I put a hypothetical case to him, and asked his opinion in such a way as to make him believe he was advising me how to make an unbreakable will. He told me of provisions and clauses to avoid, particularly in making benefactions. That was what I wanted to know. I would put one of those clauses in my uncle’s will. I practised uncle’s writing till I was as good a forger of that clause as anyone could have become. I had picked out the very words in his own handwriting to practise from.

“Then I went to Paris and, as you have guessed, learned how to get things out of a safe like that of uncle’s. Before God, all I planned to do was to get that will, change it, replace it, and trust that uncle would never notice the change. Then when he was gone, I would have contested the will. I would have got my full share either by court proceedings or by settlement out of court. You see, I had planned it all out. The school would have been founded﻿—I, we would have founded it. What difference, I said, did thirty millions or fifty millions make to an impersonal school, a school not yet even in existence? The twenty million dollars or so difference, or even half of it, meant life and love to me.

===Chinese===

「叫我怎么办？眼睁睁看着我的性命和爱情一同葬送，还是为了一个我并不爱的男人的家产，回绝杰克？海伦·邦德不是那种女人，我对自己说。我去请教了我所知道的最了不得的律师。我给他出了一桩假设的案子，问法上做了手脚，让他以为是在指点我怎样立一份无懈可击的遗嘱。他给我讲了哪些条款要避讳，尤其是行捐赠之时。这正是我要打听的东西。我要在叔叔的遗嘱里塞进这样一条。我照着叔叔的笔迹反复练习，练到伪造那一条款的本事臻于化境，无人能出其右。练习的底本，就是从他亲笔文书中挑出来的字。

「后来我去了巴黎，如您所猜，学会了怎样从叔叔那样一只保险箱里取东西。皇天在上，我原本的打算，不过是拿到那份遗嘱，改掉，再放回去，只求叔叔永不起疑。等他百年之后，我会去争议这份遗嘱。不论对簿公堂，还是庭外和解，我那一份总归拿得回来。您瞧，这一切我都盘算好了。学院照样办得起来——是我，是我们，把它办起来。三千万也好，五千万也好，对一所非亲非故的学院——一所连影子都还没有的学院——又能有多大分别？可那两千万美元上下的差额，哪怕只拿到一半，于我就是性命，就是爱情。

===Original===

“I had planned to steal the cash in the safe, anything to divert attention from the will and make it look like a plain robbery. I would have done the altering of the will that night and have returned it to the safe before morning. But it was not to be. I had almost opened the safe when my uncle entered the room. His anger completely unnerved me, and from the moment I saw him on the floor to this I haven’t had a sane thought. I forgot to take the cash, I forgot everything but that will. My only thought was that I must get it and destroy it. I doubt if I could have altered it with my nerves so upset. There, now you have my whole story. I am at your mercy.”

===Chinese===

「我原还打算把箱里的现金一并偷走——只要能把注意从遗嘱上引开，教一切看着像一场寻常的盗窃。当晚我就把遗嘱改好，天亮前再放回保险箱。可天不遂人愿。箱子几乎就要打开，叔叔进了屋。他的暴怒把我彻底吓破了胆；从看见他倒在地上那一刻起，直到此刻，我再没有一个清醒的念头。现金忘了拿，什么都忘了，只记得那份遗嘱。我心里只剩一个念头：拿到它，毁掉它。神经乱成那样，只怕想改也改不成了。好了，我的全部经过都在这里了，我听凭二位发落。」

===Original===

“No,” said Kennedy, “believe me, there is a mental statute of limitations that as far as Jameson and myself are concerned has already erased this affair. Walter, will you find Fletcher?”

I found the professor pacing up and down the gravel walk impatiently.

“Fletcher,” said Kennedy, “a night’s rest is all Miss Bond really needs. It is simply a case of overwrought nerves, and it will pass off of itself. Still, I would advise a change of scene as soon as possible. Good afternoon, Miss Bond, and my best wishes for your health.”

===Chinese===

「不，」肯尼迪说，「请相信，心理上也有一条追诉时效——就詹姆森和我而言，这桩事早已一笔勾销。沃尔特，去把弗莱彻找来好吗？」

我在砾石小径上找到教授，他正等得来回踱步。

「弗莱彻，」肯尼迪说，「邦德小姐需要的，不过是好好睡上一夜。只是神经过度疲劳罢了，自然会过去的。不过我仍建议尽早换个环境。午安，邦德小姐，祝您玉体康健。」

===Original===

“Good afternoon, Dr. Kennedy. Good afternoon, Dr. Jameson.”

I for one was glad to make my escape.

A half-hour later, Kennedy, with well-simulated excitement, was racing me in the car up to the Greenes’ again. We literally burst unannounced into the tête-à-tête on the porch.

===Chinese===

「午安，肯尼迪医生。午安，詹姆森医生。」

反正我是巴不得脱身走人。

半小时后，肯尼迪装出一副兴奋莫名的样子，载着我乘车再度风驰电掣赶去格林家。我们简直就是不速而至，一头闯进了游廊上那场促膝密谈（tête-à-tête）。

===Original===

“Fletcher, Fletcher,” cried Kennedy, “look what Walter and I have just discovered in a tin strongbox poked off in the back of your uncle’s desk!”

Fletcher seized the will and by the dim light that shone through from the hall read it hastily. “Thank God,” he cried; “the school is provided for as I thought.”

“Isn’t it glorious!” murmured Helen.

True to my instinct I muttered, “Another good newspaper yarn killed.”

===Chinese===

「弗莱彻，弗莱彻，」肯尼迪喊道，「看看我和沃尔特刚刚在你叔叔书桌最里头翻出什么——一只白铁保险匣！」

弗莱彻一把抓过遗嘱，就着门厅透进来的微光匆匆读罢。「感谢上帝，」他喊道，「学院的着落果然如我所料，分毫未变。」

「这不是太好了吗！」海伦喃喃道。

我不改新闻记者的本色，咕哝了一句：「又一条好新闻，就这么给断送了。」

## The Seismograph Adventure / 地震仪奇案

===Original===

“Dr. James Hanson, Coroner’s Physician, Criminal Courts Building,” read Craig Kennedy, as he held a visitor’s card in his hand. Then to the visitor he added, “Take a chair, Doctor.”

The physician thanked him and sat down. “Professor Kennedy,” he began, “I have been referred to you by Inspector O’Connor of the Detective Bureau. It may seem an impertinence for a city official to call on you for assistance, but﻿—well, you see, I’m completely floored. I think, too, that the case will interest you. It’s the Vandam case.”

If Dr. Hanson had suddenly turned on the current of an induction coil and I had been holding the handles I don’t think the thrill I received could have been any more sudden. The Vandam case was the sensation of the moment, a triple puzzle, as both Kennedy and myself had agreed. Was it suicide, murder, or sudden death? Every theory, so far, had proved unsatisfactory.

“I have read only what the newspapers have published,” replied Craig to the doctor’s look of inquiry. “You see, my friend Jameson here is on the staff of the *Star*, and we are in the habit of discussing these cases.”

“Very glad to meet you, Mr. Jameson,” exclaimed Dr. Hanson at the implied introduction. “The relations between my office and your paper have always been very satisfactory, I can assure you.”

“Thank you, Doctor. Depend on me to keep them so,” I replied, shaking his proffered hand.

===Chinese===

「詹姆斯·汉森医生（Dr. James Hanson），刑事法院大楼，法医官（Coroner’s Physician）。」克雷格·肯尼迪手里捏着一张来客的名片，念道。接着又对来客说：「请坐，医生。」

医生道了谢，坐下。「肯尼迪教授，」他开口道，「是侦探局的奥康纳警督介绍我来找您的。一个市政官员登门求援，也许显得冒失，可是——唉，您瞧，我是一筹莫展了。我想，这桩案子您也会感兴趣的。是范达姆案。」

就算汉森医生突然给感应圈（induction coil）通上电流，而我又恰好握着电极柄，我想我所受的震动也不会比这更突然。范达姆案正是眼下最轰动的新闻，用肯尼迪和我一致的说法，是个三重谜：是自杀，是谋杀，还是暴病身亡？迄今为止，每一种理论都难以自圆其说。

「报纸上登过的，我都读过了，」克雷格答道，回应医生探询的目光。「您瞧，我这位朋友詹姆森在《明星报》（*Star*）任职，我们习惯在一起讨论这类案子。」

「认识您很高兴，詹姆森先生，」经这一间接引见，汉森医生朗声说道。「敞署同贵报的关系一向十分融洽，这一点我可以向您保证。」

「谢谢您，医生。此事包在我身上，」我握住他伸来的手，答道。

===Original===

“Now, as to the case,” continued the doctor slowly. “Here is a beautiful woman in the prime of life, the wife of a very wealthy retired banker considerably older than herself﻿—perhaps nearly seventy﻿—of very fine family. Of course you have read it all, but let me sketch it so you will look at it from my point of view. This woman, apparently in good health, with every luxury money can buy, is certain within a very few years, from her dower rights, to be numbered among the richest women in America. Yet she is discovered in the middle of the night by her maid, seated at the table in the library of her home, unconscious. She never regains consciousness, but dies the following morning.

“The coroner is called in, and, as his physician, I must advise him. The family physician has pronounced it due to natural causes, the uremic coma of latent kidney trouble. Some of the newspapers, I think the *Star* among them, have hinted at suicide. And then there are others, who have flatly asserted it was murder.”

===Chinese===

「好吧，先说案子，」医生缓缓接着说。「有这么一位正当盛年的美貌妇人，丈夫是一位极其富有的退休银行家，年纪比她大得多——恐怕快七十了——出身极为高贵。这些你们当然都在报上读过了，不过请容我勾个轮廓，好让你们从我的角度看它。这位妇人表面上身体健康，享尽金钱所能买到的一切奢华，而且凭着她的寡妇产（dower rights），不出几年，准会跻身美国最富有的妇人之列。然而，一天深夜，她的贴身女仆却发现她坐在自家书房的桌前，不省人事。她再没有恢复知觉，第二天早晨就死了。

「验尸官请了来，我作为他的法医，必须向他提出意见。家庭医生已宣布这是自然死亡——潜伏的肾病引起的尿毒症昏迷。有几家报纸——我想《明星报》也在其内——暗示是自杀。另有一些报纸，则断然宣称是谋杀。」

===Original===

The coroner’s physician paused to see if we were following him. Needless to say Kennedy was ahead of him.

“Have you any facts in your possession which have not been given to the public yet?” asked Craig.

“I’m coming to that in a moment,” replied Dr. Hanson. “Let me sketch the case first. Henry Vandam had become﻿—well, very eccentric in his old age, we will say. Among his eccentricities none seems to have impressed the newspapers more than his devotion to a medium and her manager, Mrs. May Popper and Mr. Howard Farrington. Now, of course, the case does not go into the truth or falsity of spiritualism, you understand. You have your opinion, and I have mine. What this aspect of the case involves is merely the character of the medium and her manager. You know, of course, that Henry Vandam is completely under their control.”

He paused again, to emphasise the point.

===Chinese===

法医官停了停，看我们是否跟得上他的思路。不必说，肯尼迪已经跑到他前头去了。

「您手里有没有什么还没向公众透露的事实？」克雷格问。

「这就说到正题了，」汉森医生答道。「容我先把案情勾完。亨利·范达姆（Henry Vandam）变得——唔，我们不妨说，晚年非常古怪。在他的种种怪癖里，最叫报纸津津乐道的，莫过于他对一位灵媒及其经纪人的虔信——梅·波珀夫人（Mrs. May Popper）和霍华德·法林顿先生（Mr. Howard Farrington）。当然，您明白，本案并不追究唯灵论（spiritualism）本身是真是假。您有您的看法，我有我的。这方面的案情牵涉的只是灵媒及其经纪人的品行。您当然知道，亨利·范达姆已经完全落在他们两人的掌握之中了。」

他又停了停，好加重这一点的分量。

===Original===

“You asked me if I was in possession of any facts which have not been given to the press. Yes, I am. And just there lies the trouble. They are so very conflicting as to be almost worse than useless, as far as I can see. We found near the unfortunate woman a small pillbox with three capsules still in it. It was labelled ‘One before retiring’ and bore the name of a certain druggist and the initials ‘Dr. C. W. H.’ Now, I am convinced that the initials are merely a blind and do not give any clue. The druggist says that a maid from the Vandam house brought in the prescription, which of course he filled. It is a harmless enough prescription﻿—contains, among other things, four and a half grains of quinine and one-sixth of a grain of morphine. Six capsules were prepared altogether.

===Chinese===

「您方才问我，有没有掌握什么尚未见报的事实。有。麻烦恰恰出在这里。在我看来，这些事实相互矛盾得太厉害，几乎比没有还糟。我们在那位不幸的妇人身边找到一只小药盒，里面还剩三粒胶囊。标签上写着「睡前服一粒」，还印着一家药房的名字和缩写签名「C. W. H. 医生」。至于那个缩写，我确信不过是幌子，提供不了任何线索。药剂师说，是范达姆家的一个女仆送处方来配的药，他照方配了就是。那是个再无害不过的方子——所含成分之中，有四格令半奎宁、六分之一格令吗啡。一共配了六粒胶囊。

===Original===

“Now, of course my first thought was that she might have taken several capsules at once and that it was a case of accidental morphine poisoning, or it might even be suicide. But it cannot be either, to my mind, for only three of the six capsules are gone. No doubt, also, you are acquainted with the fact that the one invariable symptom of morphine poisoning is the contraction of the pupils of the eyes to a pinpoint﻿—often so that they are unrecognisable. Moreover, the pupils are symmetrically contracted, and this symptom is the one invariably present in coma from morphine poisoning and distinguishes it from all other forms of death.

===Chinese===

「当然，我起初的想法是：她也许一口气服了好几粒，是误服吗啡中毒，甚至可能是自杀。但依我看，两者都不能成立，因为六粒胶囊只少了三粒。毫无疑问，您也知道，吗啡中毒有一个一成不变的症状，就是瞳孔收缩成针尖大小——往往收缩到几乎辨认不出。而且两眼瞳孔是对称收缩的；这一症状在吗啡中毒昏迷中必然出现，是它区别于其他一切死亡形式的地方。

===Original===

“On the other hand, in the coma of kidney disease one pupil is dilated and the other contracted﻿—they are unsymmetrical. But in this case both the pupils are normal, or only a very little dilated, and they are symmetrical. So far we have been able to find no other poison than the slight traces of morphine remaining in the stomach after so many hours. I think you are enough of a chemist to know that no doctor would dare go on the stand and swear to death from morphine poisoning in the face of such evidence against him. The veriest tyro of an expert toxicologist could too easily confute him.”

===Chinese===

「另一方面，肾病患者昏迷时，一只瞳孔放大，一只收缩——两眼不对称。可本案死者两眼瞳孔都正常，或者只是略微放大，而且对称。到目前为止，除了时隔这么多小时之后胃里残留的微量吗啡，我们再也找不出别的毒物。我想，您是十足的化学家，自然明白：面对这样的反证，没有哪个医生敢上证人席，宣誓断定死者死于吗啡中毒。哪怕最蹩脚的毒物学专家，也能轻易把他驳倒。」

===Original===

Kennedy nodded. “Have you the pillbox and the prescription?”

“I have,” replied Dr. Hanson, placing them on the table.

Kennedy scrutinised them sharply. “I shall need these,” he said. “Of course you understand I will take very good care of them. Is there anything else of importance?”

“Really, I don’t know,” said the physician dubiously. “It’s rather out of my province, but perhaps you would think it important. It’s mighty uncanny anyhow. Henry Vandam, as you doubtless know, was much more deeply interested in the work of this medium than was his wife. Perhaps Mrs. Vandam was a bit jealous﻿—I don’t know. But she, too, had an interest in spiritualism, though he was much more deeply influenced by Mrs. Popper than she.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪点了点头。「药盒和处方在您手里吗？」

「在这儿，」汉森医生答着，把它们放到桌上。

肯尼迪锐利地细看了一番。「这两样我要借用，」他说，「当然，您放心，我会好好保管。还有别的要紧东西吗？」

「实在说，我不知道，」医生将信将疑地说。「这多少出了我的本行，不过您或许会觉得要紧。反正这事儿邪门得很。亨利·范达姆对这位灵媒的活动，您必定知道，兴趣比他太太深得多。也许范达姆夫人（Mrs. Vandam）还有点妒意——这我不知道。不过她对唯灵论也有兴趣，只是受波珀夫人的影响，远不如她丈夫受得深罢了。

===Original===

“Here’s the strange part of it. The old man believes so thoroughly in rappings and materialisations that he constantly keeps a notebook in his pocket in which he records all the materialisations he thinks he sees and the rappings he hears, along with the time and place. Now it so happened that on the night Mrs. Vandam was taken ill, he had retired﻿—I believe in another part of the house, where he has a regular séance-room. According to his story, he was awakened from a profound sleep by a series of rappings. As was his custom, he noted the time at which they occurred. Something made him uneasy, and he said to his ‘control’﻿—at least this is his story:

“ ‘John, is it about Mary?’

===Chinese===

「怪就怪在这里。老头儿对通灵敲击（rappings）和显形（materialisations）信得透彻，口袋里常年揣着一个笔记本，凡是他自以为看见的显形、听到的敲击，连同时间地点，一并记录在案。偏偏在范达姆夫人发病的那天夜里，他已经睡下了——我想是在宅子的另一头，那里有他一间正式的降神室（séance-room）。照他自己的说法，一连串敲击声把他从酣睡中惊醒。他照例记下了敲击发生的时间。有什么东西令他不安起来，他便问他的「控制灵」（control）——至少他是这么说的：

「『约翰，是为玛丽的事吗？』

===Original===

“Three raps answered ‘yes,’ the usual code.

“ ‘What is the matter? Is she ill?’

“The three answering raps were so vigorous that he sprang out of bed and called for his wife’s maid. The maid replied that Mrs. Vandam had not gone to bed yet, but that there was a light in the library and she would go to her mistress immediately. The next moment the house was awakened by the screams of the maid calling for help, that Mrs. Vandam was dying.

===Chinese===

「三下敲击回答『是』——这是他们惯用的暗码。

「『出什么事了？她病了吗？』

「回答的三下敲得那么急促有力，他一跃而起，去喊太太的女仆。女仆回说，范达姆夫人还没有上床，书房里亮着灯，她这就到主人那儿去。紧接着，全宅都被女仆的呼救尖叫声惊醒了——范达姆夫人要不行了。

===Original===

“That was three nights ago. On each of the two succeeding nights Henry Vandam says he has been awakened at precisely the same hour by a rapping, and on each night his ‘control’ has given him a message from his dead wife. As a man of science, I attribute the whole thing to an overwrought imagination. The original rappings may have been a mere coincidence with the fact of the condition of Mrs. Vandam. However, I give this to you for what it is worth.”

===Chinese===

「那是三夜以前的事了。此后接连两夜，亨利·范达姆都说他在分毫不差的同一时刻被敲击声惊醒，每夜他的「控制灵」都给他捎来亡妻的一个口信。作为科学界的人，我把这一切归之于过度紧张的想象。最初那阵敲击，说不定只是碰巧赶上范达姆夫人发病罢了。不过，这些话有多大用处，就由您掂量吧。」

===Original===

Craig said nothing, but, as was his habit, shaded his eyes with the tips of his fingers, resting his elbows on the arms of his chair: “I suppose,” he said, “you can give me the necessary authority to enter the Vandam house and look at the scene of these happenings?”

“Certainly,” assented the physician, “but you will find it a queer place. There are spirit paintings and spirit photographs in every room, and Vandam’s own part of the house﻿—well, it’s creepy, that’s all I can say.”

“And also I suppose you have performed an autopsy on the body and will allow me to drop into your laboratory tomorrow morning and satisfy myself on this morphine point?”

“Certainly,” replied the coroner’s physician, “at any time you say.”

===Chinese===

克雷格没作声，只是照他的习惯，用指尖遮住眼睛，肘部搁在椅子扶手上。「我想，」他说，「您可以给我必要的授权，让我进范达姆宅看看这些事情发生的现场吧？」

「当然可以，」医生一口应承，「不过您会发现那是个古怪地方。每个房间里都挂着灵异绘画和灵异照片，至于范达姆自己住的那半边宅子——唉，阴森森的，我只能这么说。」

「另外我想，您已经解剖过遗体了，可否容我明早到您的实验室去一趟，就吗啡这一点亲自求证一下？」

「当然，」法医官答道，「您说什么时候就什么时候。」

===Original===

“At ten sharp, then, tomorrow I shall be there,” said Craig. “It is now eight-thirty. Do you think I can see Vandam tonight? What time do these rappings occur?”

“Why, yes, you surely will be able to see him tonight. He hasn’t stirred from the house since his wife died. He told me he momentarily expected messages from her direct when she had got strong enough in her new world. I believe they had some kind of a compact to that effect. The rappings come at twelve-thirty.”

“Ah, then I shall have plenty of time to run over to my laboratory before seeing Mr. Vandam and get some apparatus I have in mind. No, Doctor, you needn’t bother to go with me. Just give me a card of introduction. I’ll see you tomorrow at ten. Good night﻿—oh, by the way, don’t give out any of the facts you have told me.”

===Chinese===

「那就明早十点整，我准时到，」克雷格说。「现在八点半。您看今晚我能见到范达姆吗？这些敲击声都是几点来？」

「哦，能，今晚您准能见到他。自太太去世，他就没迈出过宅门一步。他告诉我，只等她在新世界里养足气力，他便时时刻刻盼着她直接捎话来。我想，他们两口子生前大概有过这么个约定。敲击声是十二点半来。」

「啊，那么见范达姆先生之前，时间绰绰有余，我可以先跑一趟实验室，取几件我心里的仪器。不用了，医生，您不必陪我跑。给我一张引见名片就行。明天十点见。晚安——哦，对了，您刚才告诉我的这些情况，一条也别透露出去。」

===Original===

“Jameson,” said Craig, when we were walking rapidly over toward the university, “this promises to be an uncommonly difficult case.”

“As I view it now,” I said, “I have suspicions of everybody concerned in it. Even the view of the *Star*, that it is a case of suicide due to overwrought nerves, may explain it.”

“It might even be a natural death,” Craig added. “And that would make it a greater mystery than ever﻿—a case for psychical research. One thing that I am going to do tonight will tell me much, however.”

===Chinese===

「詹姆森，」我们快步朝大学走去时，克雷格说，「看样子，这将是一桩格外棘手的案子。」

「照我现在的看法，」我说，「凡是沾着这案子边的人，我一个都怀疑。就连《明星报》的说法——说这是神经过度紧张导致的自杀——说不定也说得通。」

「甚至可能是自然死亡，」克雷格补充道。「那可就比什么都更玄了——成了一桩该交给心灵研究（psychical research）的案子。不过，今晚我要做的一件事，会告诉我很多东西。」

===Original===

At the laboratory he unlocked a glass case and took out a little instrument which looked like two horizontal pendulums suspended by fine wires. There was a large magnet near each pendulum, and the end of each pendulum bore a needle which touched a circular drum driven by clockwork. Craig fussed with and adjusted the apparatus, while I said nothing, for I had long ago learned that in applying a new apparatus to doing old things Craig was as dumb as an oyster, until his work was crowned with success.

===Chinese===

在实验室里，他打开一只玻璃柜，取出一件小仪器，看去像两只悬在细丝上的水平摆。每只摆的近旁各有一块大磁铁，摆的末端各装着一根细针，针尖触在由钟表机构带动的圆鼓上。克雷格摆弄着、调试着那套装置，我则一言不发，因为我很早就领教过了：拿新家伙做老事情的时候，克雷格嘴严得像只牡蛎，不到大功告成，绝不吐半个字。

===Original===

We had no trouble in getting in to see Mr. Vandam in his séance-room. His face was familiar to me, for I had seen him in public a number of times, but it looked strangely altered. He was nervous, and showed his age very perceptibly.

It was as the coroner’s physician had said. The house was littered with reminders of the cult, books, papers, curious daubs of paintings handsomely framed, and photographs; hazy overexposures, I should have called them, but Mr. Vandam took great pride in them, and Kennedy quite won him over by his admiration for them.

They talked about the rappings, and the old man explained where and when they occurred. They proceeded from a little cabinet or closet at one end of the room. It was evident that he was a thorough believer in them and in the messages they conveyed.

Craig carefully noted everything about the room and then fell to admiring the spirit photographs, if such they might be called.

===Chinese===

我们毫不费事地就被引进降神室，见到了范达姆先生。他的脸我是熟识的，公开场合我见过他好几次，可如今却变得几乎认不出来了。他神经过敏，分明见老。

一切正如法医官所言。宅子里到处堆着这门秘教的遗物：书籍、文件、装了考究画框的怪诞涂抹画，还有照片——要叫我说，尽是些晕糊糊的曝光过度——可范达姆先生偏偏引以为豪，而肯尼迪对它们一番赞赏，一下子就赢得了他的欢心。

他们谈起敲击的事，老头儿说明了敲击发生的地点和时间。声音发自房间一头的一只小柜橱，或者说小间。显而易见，他对敲击本身、对敲击传达的讯息，都信得不折不扣。

克雷格把房间里的一切细细记在心里，然后转而去欣赏那些灵异照片——如果它们配得上这个称呼的话。

===Original===

“The best of all I do not display, they are too precious,” said the old man. “Would you like to see them?”

Craig assented eagerly, and Vandam left us for a moment to get them. In an instant Craig had entered the cabinet, and in a dark corner on the floor he deposited the mechanism he had brought from the laboratory. Then he resumed his seat, shutting the box in which he had brought the mechanism, so that it would not appear that he had left anything about the room.

Artfully he led the conversation along lines that interested the old man until he seemed to forget the hour. Not so, Craig. He knew it was nearing half-past twelve. The more they talked the more uncanny did this house and room of spirits seem to me. In fact, I was rapidly reaching the point where I could have sworn that once or twice something incorporeal brushed by me. I know now that it was purely imagination, but it shows what tricks the imagination can play on us.

===Chinese===

「最好的那几张我从不陈列，太珍贵了，」老头儿说。「二位想看看吗？」

克雷格热切地应允了，范达姆丢下我们片刻，去取照片。说时迟那时快，克雷格已经钻进柜橱，把从实验室带来的机件安置在地面一个暗角里。随后他回到座位上，把装过机件的箱子合好，免得露出他在房里留下东西的痕迹。

他巧妙地把谈话引到老头儿感兴趣的话题上，引得对方似乎把时辰都忘了。克雷格可没忘。他知道快到十二点半了。他们谈得越久，这幢宅子和这间精灵之室在我眼里就越发阴森。说实话，我很快就到了这般地步：简直可以发誓，有一两回，什么无形无质的东西擦身掠过我。如今我知道那纯粹是想象，可这也足见想象能跟我们耍些什么花招。

===Original===

*Rap! rap! rap! rap! rap!*

Five times came a curiously hollow noise from the cabinet. If it had been possible I should certainly have fled, it was so sudden and unexpected. The hall clock downstairs struck the half-hour in those chimes written by Handel for St. Paul’s.

Craig leaned over to me and whispered hoarsely, “Keep perfectly still﻿—don’t move a hand or foot.”

===Chinese===

*咚！咚！咚！咚！咚！*

柜橱里传来五下空得出奇的声响。倘若做得到，我准会夺门而逃——实在太突然、太出人意料了。楼下门厅的大钟敲响了半点，奏的正是亨德尔（Handel）为圣保罗座堂写的那套钟乐。

克雷格俯过身来，哑着嗓子低语：「一动也别动——手脚都别动。」

===Original===

The old man seemed utterly to have forgotten us. “Is that you, John?” he asked expectantly.

*Rap! rap! rap!* came the reply.

“Is Mary strong enough to speak to me tonight?”

*Rap! rap!*

“Is she happy?”

*Rap! rap!*

“What makes her unhappy? What does she want? Will you spell it out?”

*Rap! rap! rap!*

===Chinese===

老头儿似乎把我们忘得一干二净。「是你在吗，约翰？」他满心期待地问。

*咚！咚！咚！*传来回答。

「玛丽今夜气力够不够跟我说话？」

*咚！咚！*

「她幸福吗？」

*咚！咚！*

「什么事叫她不快活？她想要什么？你可肯拼出来？」

*咚！咚！咚！*

===Original===

Then, after a pause, the rapping started slowly, and distinctly to spell out words. It was so weird and uncanny that I scarcely breathed. Letter after letter the message came, nineteen raps for *s*, eight for *h*, five for *e*, according to the place in the alphabet, numerically, of the required letter. At last it was complete.

“She thinks you are not well. She asks you to have that prescription filled again.”

“Tell her I will do it tomorrow morning. Is there anything else?”

*Rap! rap!* came back faintly:

===Chinese===

接着，停顿片刻之后，敲击声缓缓响起，清清楚楚地拼起字来。那情形如此诡异，我几乎不敢喘气。讯息一个字母一个字母地传来：按所需字母在字母表中的序数，*s* 敲十九下，*h* 敲八下，*e* 敲五下。最后，讯息完整了。

「她觉得你身子不适。她请你把那个处方再配一服。」

「告诉她，明早我就去配。还有别的吗？」

*咚！咚！*幽幽地传回来：

===Original===

“John, John, don’t go yet,” pleaded the old man earnestly. It was easy to see how thoroughly he believed in “John,” as perhaps well he might after the warning of his wife’s death three nights before. “Won’t you answer one other question?”

Fainter, almost imperceptibly, came a *rap! rap!*

For several minutes the old man sat absorbed in thought, trance-like. Then, gradually, he seemed to realise that we were in the room with him. With difficulty he took up the thread of the conversation where the rappings had broken it.

“We were talking about the photographs,” he said slowly. “I hope soon to get one of my wife as she is now that she is transfigured. John has promised me one soon.”

===Chinese===

「约翰，约翰，先别走，」老头儿恳切地哀告道。他对「约翰」信得多么透彻，一望便知——三夜之前先报了太太的死讯，他这样信也难怪。「再答我一个问题，好不好？」

*咚！咚！*又轻轻响起，轻得几乎察觉不到。

好几分钟，老头儿坐在那里出神，恍如入了定。渐渐地，他似乎才意识到我们还在房里。他费了好大力气，才把被敲击声打断的话头重新接起来。

「我们方才说到照片，」他缓缓地说。「我希望不久就能得到一张我太太如今圣化之后的模样。约翰答应我，快了。」

===Original===

He was gathering up his treasures preparatory to putting them back in their places of safekeeping. The moment he was out of the room Craig darted into the cabinet and replaced his mechanism in the box. Then he began softly to tap the walls. At last he found the side that gave a noise similar to that which we had heard, and he seemed pleased to have found it, for he hastily sketched on an old envelope a plan of that part of the house, noting on it the location of the side of the cabinet.

===Chinese===

他收拾起他的珍宝，准备放回妥藏的地方。他一出房门，克雷格立刻闪进柜橱，把机件装回箱里。接着他开始轻轻叩击墙壁。终于，他找到了能发出同我们刚才听到的那种声响的一面，找到之后他似乎很满意，赶忙在一只旧信封上勾出宅子那一部分的草图，标明了柜橱那一面所在的位置。

===Original===

Kennedy almost dragged me back to our apartment, he was in such a hurry to examine the apparatus at his leisure. He turned on all the lights, took the thing out of its case, and stripped off the two sheets of ruled paper wound around the two revolving drums. He laid them flat on the table and studied them for some minutes with evidently growing satisfaction.

At last he turned to me and said, “Walter, here is a ghost caught in the act.”

I looked dubiously at the irregular up-and-down scrawl on the paper, while he rang up the Homicide Bureau of the Central Office and left word for O’Connor to call him up the first thing in the morning.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪几乎是拖着我赶回公寓——他急不可待，要从容检视那套装置。他把灯全部拧亮，取出机件，把缠绕在两只旋转圆鼓上的两页格纸揭了下来。他将纸平摊在桌上，端详了好几分钟，脸上的满意神色越来越浓。

末了他转向我说：「沃尔特，这儿有一个当场拿获的鬼。」

我对着纸上那阵起起伏伏的乱曲线将信将疑，他则给中央办公厅的凶案科（Homicide Bureau）挂了电话，留言叫奥康纳明天一早先给他回电话。

===Original===

Still eyeing with satisfaction the record traced on the sheets of paper, he lighted a cigarette in a matter-of-fact way and added: “It proves to be a very much flesh-and-blood ghost, this ‘John.’ It walked up to the wall back of that cabinet, rapped, listened to old Vandam, rapped some more, got the answer it wanted, and walked deliberately away. The cabinet, as you may have noticed, is in a corner of the room with one side along the hallway. The ghost must have been in the hall.”

===Chinese===

他仍旧心满意足地端详着纸页上描下的记录，若无其事地点上一支烟，又说道：「这个『约翰』，原来是个血肉之躯十足的鬼。它走到柜橱后面的墙边，敲了几下，听老范达姆说话，再敲几下，得到想要的答复，然后从容不迫地走开了。那柜橱，你也许注意到了，在房间的角落里，一面贴着过道。鬼当时必定就在过道里。」

===Original===

“But who was it?”

“Not so fast, Walter,” laughed Craig. “Isn’t it enough for one night that we have found out that much?”

Fortunately I was tired, or I certainly should have dreamed of rappings and of “John” that night. I was awakened early by Kennedy talking with someone over the telephone. It was Inspector O’Connor.

===Chinese===

「可那到底是谁？」

「别急，沃尔特，」克雷格笑道。「一夜工夫查明了这么多，还不够吗？」

幸亏我累了，不然那一夜我准会梦见敲击声和「约翰」。一大早，肯尼迪同什么人打电话的声音把我吵醒。是奥康纳警督。

===Original===

Of course I heard only one side of the conversation, but as near as I could gather Kennedy was asking the inspector to obtain several samples of ink for him. I had not heard the first part of the conversation, and was considerably surprised when Kennedy hung up the receiver and said:

“Vandam had the prescription filled again early this morning, and it will soon be in the hands of O’Connor. I hope I haven’t spoiled things by acting too soon, but I don’t want to run the risk of a double tragedy.”

===Chinese===

电话我当然只听得见一头，不过约莫听得出，肯尼迪是在请警督替他弄几份墨水样品。开头那段我没听着，所以肯尼迪挂上听筒说出下面这句话时，我吃惊不小：

「范达姆今早一早就把处方又配了一服，很快就到奥康纳手里了。但愿我没把事情办糟——动手太快了——可我不想冒闹出第二场悲剧的风险。」

===Original===

“Well,” I said, “it is incomprehensible to me. First I suspected suicide. Then I suspected murder. Now I almost suspect a murder and a suicide. The fact is, I don’t know just what I suspect. I’m like Dr. Hanson﻿—floored. I wonder if Vandam would voluntarily take all the capsules at once in order to be with his wife?”

===Chinese===

「唉，」我说，「我算是摸不着头脑了。起初我疑是自杀。后来又疑是谋杀。如今我几乎疑心是一桩谋杀加一桩自杀。说实话，我连自己到底疑心什么都不知道。我同汉森医生一样——一筹莫展。我在想，范达姆会不会为了去陪太太，心甘情愿一口气服光所有胶囊呢？」

===Original===

“One of them alone would be quite sufficient if the ‘ghost’ should take a notion, as I think it will, to walk in the daytime,” replied Craig enigmatically. “I don’t want to run any chances, as I have said. I may be wrong in my theory of the case, Walter, so let us not discuss this phase of it until I have gone a step farther and am sure of my ground. O’Connor’s man will get the capsules before Vandam has a chance to take the first one, anyhow. The ‘ghost’ had a purpose in that message, for O’Connor tells me that Vandam’s lawyer visited him yesterday and in all probability a new will is being made, perhaps has already been made.”

===Chinese===

「倘若那『鬼』起意——我料它会的——改在大白天现身，单单一粒就绰绰有余了，」克雷格答得莫测高深。「我说过了，我不想冒任何风险。沃尔特，我对案子的推断也可能有错，所以在再迈出一步、站稳脚跟之前，咱们先不谈这一层。无论如何，奥康纳的人会在范达姆来得及服下头一粒之前把胶囊拿到手。那『鬼』在那条讯息里是存着目的的——奥康纳告诉我，范达姆的律师昨天去见过他，十之八九正在立新遗嘱，也许已经立好了。」

===Original===

We breakfasted in silence and later rode down to the office of Dr. Hanson, who greeted us enthusiastically.

“I’ve solved it at last,” he cried, “and it’s easy.”

Kennedy looked gravely over the analysis which Dr. Hanson shoved into his hand, and seemed very much interested in the probable quantity of morphine that must have been taken to yield such an analysis. The physician had a textbook open on his desk.

“Our old ideas of the infallible test of morphine poisoning are all exploded,” he said, excitedly beginning to read a passage he had marked in the book.

===Chinese===

我们默默用了早餐，随后坐车到汉森医生的办公室，他兴冲冲地迎接我们。

「我到底解开了！」他嚷道，「容易得很。」

汉森医生把一份化验单塞到他手里，肯尼迪神色凝重地看了一遍，对要服下多大剂量的吗啡才能得出这样的化验结果，似乎大感兴趣。医生的桌上摊着一本教科书。

「吗啡中毒万无一失的检验法，我们的老观念全都过时啦，」他说着，兴奋地念起书上一段他画了记号的文字。

===Original===

“ ‘I have thought that inequality of the pupils, that is to say, where they are not symmetrically contracted, is proof that a case is not one of narcotism, or morphine poisoning. But Professor Taylor has recorded a case of morphine poisoning in which the unsymmetrical contraction occurred.’

“There, now, until I happened to run across that in one of the authorities I had supposed the symmetrical contraction of the pupils of the eyes to be the distinguishing symptom of morphine poisoning Professor Kennedy, in my opinion we can, after all, make out our case as one of morphine poisoning.”

===Chinese===

「『我原以为，瞳孔不相等——也就是说，两眼不是对称收缩时——便可证明该病例并非麻醉中毒，即吗啡中毒。但泰勒教授（Professor Taylor）记录过一例吗啡中毒，其中恰恰出现了不对称收缩。』

「瞧，在那以前——直到我碰巧在一部权威著作里读到这一条为止——我一直以为，两眼瞳孔对称收缩才是吗啡中毒的鉴别症状肯尼迪教授，依我之见，我们终究能把本案定成吗啡中毒。」

===Original===

“Is that case in the book all you base your opinion on?” asked Craig with excessive politeness.

“Yes, sir,” replied the doctor reluctantly.

“Well,” said Kennedy quietly, “if you will investigate that case quoted from Professor Taylor, you will find that it has been proved that the patient had one glass eye.”

“Then my contention collapses and she was not poisoned?”

“No, I do not say that. All I say is that expert testimony would refute us as far as we have gone. But if you will let me make a few tests of my own I can readily clear up that end of the case, I now feel sure. Let me take these samples to my laboratory.”

===Chinese===

「您的全部依据，就是书上那一例吗？」克雷格问道，客气得过了头。

「是的，先生，」医生勉勉强强地答道。

「那么，」肯尼迪不动声色地说，「您若去查一查泰勒教授所引的那一例，就会发现那病人有一只眼睛是玻璃的，这一点早已查明。」

「那我的论断就垮了，她不是被毒死的？」

「不，我没这么说。我只是说，照我们目前的进展，专家证词会把我们驳倒。不过，倘若您容我自己做几项检验，我如今确信，案子这一头我能轻而易举地理清。请让我把这些样品带回实验室。」

===Original===

I was surprised when we ran into Inspector O’Connor waiting for us in the corridor of the Criminal Courts Building as we left the office of the coroner’s physician. He rushed up to Kennedy and shoved into his hand a pillbox in which six capsules rattled. Kennedy narrowly inspected the box, opened it, and looked thoughtfully at the six white capsules lying so innocently within.

===Chinese===

离开法医办公室时，我们竟在刑事法院大楼的走廊里撞见等候我们的奥康纳警督，我不由一怔。他抢步上前，把一只药盒塞进肯尼迪手里，盒里六粒胶囊哗啦作响。肯尼迪细细察看盒子，打开来，若有所思地望着躺在里面那六粒显得无辜之极的白色胶囊。

===Original===

“One of these capsules would have been worth hundreds of thousands of dollars to ‘John,’ ” said Craig contemplatively, as he shut the box and deposited it carefully in his inside vest pocket. “I don’t believe I even said good morning to you, O’Connor,” he continued. “I hope I haven’t kept you waiting here long. Have you obtained the samples of ink?”

“Yes, Professor. Here they are. As soon as you telephoned this morning I sent my men out separately to get them. There’s the ink from the druggist, this is from the Vandam library, this is from Farrington’s room, and this is from Mrs. Popper’s apartment.”

“Thank you, Inspector. I don’t know what I’d do without your help,” said Kennedy, eagerly taking four small vials from him. “Science is all right, but organisation enables science to work quickly. And quickness is the essence of this case.”

===Chinese===

「这六粒里随便哪一粒，对『约翰』来说都值几十万美元，」克雷格沉吟着说，一面合上药盒，小心地放进背心内袋。「我看我连早安都忘了同你道了，奥康纳，」他接着说，「没叫你在这儿久等吧？墨水样品弄到了？」

「弄到了，教授。喏，都在这儿。今早您电话一挂，我就分头派人去取了。这瓶是药房的墨水，这瓶取自范达姆家的书房，这瓶来自法林顿的房间，这瓶来自波珀夫人的公寓。」

「多谢，警督。没有您帮忙，我真不知该怎么办，」肯尼迪说着，急切地从他手里接过四只小瓶。「科学固然好，可组织才能让科学跑得快。而这桩案子，要的就是一个快字。」

===Original===

During the afternoon Kennedy was very busy in his laboratory, where I found him that night after my hurried dinner, from which he was absent.

“What, is it after dinnertime?” he exclaimed, holding up a glass beaker and watching the reaction of something he poured into it from a test-tube.

“Craig, I believe that when you are absorbed in a case, you would rather work than eat. Did you have any lunch after I left you?”

===Chinese===

整个下午，肯尼迪都在实验室里忙。那晚我匆匆用过晚饭——他没有露面——便到实验室去找他。

「怎么，已经过了晚饭时候？」他嚷道，手里举着一只玻璃烧杯，正把试管里的什么东西倒进去，观察反应。

「克雷格，我看你一扎进案子，就宁可干活不要吃饭了。我走了以后，你午饭吃了没有？」

===Original===
