“I don’t think so,” he replied, regarding the beaker and not his answer. “Now, Walter, old fellow, I don’t want you to be offended with me, but really I can work better if you don’t constantly remind me of such things as eating and sleeping. Say, do you want to help me﻿—really?”

“Certainly. I am as interested in the case as you are, but I can’t make heads or tails of it,” I replied.

===Chinese===

「好像没有，」他答道，眼睛盯着烧杯，而不是他的回答。「嗳，沃尔特，老伙计，你别见怪，可你若别老拿吃饭睡觉这类事来提醒我，我的确干得更好。哎，你愿不愿意帮我的忙——真心愿意？」

「当然愿意。这案子我同你一样上心，只是我理不出个头绪，」我答道。

===Original===

“Then, I wish you would look up Mrs. Popper tonight and have a private séance with her. What I want you to do particularly is to get a good idea of the looks of the room in which she is accustomed to work. I’m going to duplicate it here in my laboratory as nearly as possible. Then I want you to arrange with her for a private ‘circle’ here tomorrow night. Tell her it is with a few professors at the university who are interested in psychical research and that Mr. Vandam will be present. I’d rather have her come willingly than to force her to come. Incidentally watch that manager of hers, Farrington. By all means he must accompany her.”

===Chinese===

「那么，今晚我想劳你去拜访波珀夫人，同她私下搞一场降神会（séance）。我要你特别留心的，是把她惯常作法的那间屋子的样子看个清楚。我要在这实验室里把它尽可能原样复制出来。然后，你再同她约定明晚来这里搞一场私人「降神圈」（circle）。你就说，到场的是大学里几位关心心灵研究的教授，范达姆先生也会在场。她肯自己来，总比我逼她来强。顺便盯一盯她那位经纪人法林顿。无论如何，他必须陪她同来。」

===Original===

That evening I dropped casually in on Mrs. Popper. She was a woman of great brilliance and delicacy, both in her physical and mental perceptions, of exceptional vivacity and cleverness. She must have studied me more closely than I was aware of, for I believe she relied on diverting my attention whenever she desired to produce one of her really wonderful results. Needless to say, I was completely mystified by her performance. She did spirit writing that would have done credit to the immortal Slade, told me a lot of things that were true, and many more that were unverifiable or hopelessly vague. It was really worth much more than the price, and I did not need to feign the interest necessary to get her terms for a circle in the laboratory.

===Chinese===

当晚，我装作顺路，拜访了波珀夫人。这位妇人无论体感还是心识，都敏锐精微得出奇，才思敏捷，机变过人。她对我的揣摩，必定比我察觉的深得多——我相信，每逢她要拿出一样真正惊人的绝活，靠的就是引开我的注意。不必说，她的表演把我彻底镇住了。她那手灵界书写（spirit writing），连不朽的斯莱德（Slade）见了也要增色；她对我说了许多应验的事，更多的事则无从查证，或是含糊得没边儿。这一场实在值回票价还有余，要探她开降神圈的价码，我连兴致都不必假装。

===Original===

Of course I had to make the terms with Farrington. The first glance aroused my suspicions of him. He was shifty-eyed, and his face had a hard and mercenary look. In spite of, perhaps rather because of, my repugnance we quickly came to an agreement, and as I left the apartment I mentally resolved to keep my eye on him.

Craig came in late, having been engaged in his chemical analyses all the evening. From his manner I inferred that they had been satisfactory, and he seemed much gratified when I told him that I had arranged successfully for the séance and that Farrington would accompany the medium.

As we were talking over the case a messenger arrived with a note from O’Connor. It was written with his usual brevity: “Have just found from servants that Farrington and Mrs. P. have key to Vandam house. Wish I had known it before. House shadowed. No one has entered or left it tonight.”

===Chinese===

价钱当然得同法林顿谈。第一眼我就对他起了疑心。他眼神闪烁不定，一脸精刮逐利的狠相。尽管我满心厌恶——或者正因为厌恶——我们三言两语就谈妥了。离开公寓时，我暗暗打定主意，要盯住这个人。

克雷格回来得很晚，一整晚都埋头做他的化学分析。从他的神色看，分析结果令人满意；听我说降神会已经安排妥当、法林顿也将陪灵媒同来，他似乎大为高兴。

我们正谈论着案子，一个信差送来奥康纳的短简。还是他惯常的简洁笔法：「刚从仆人处查明：法林顿与波珀夫人持有范达姆宅钥匙。早知道就好了。宅子已派人盯守。今夜无人进出。」

===Original===

Craig looked at his watch. It was a quarter after one. “The ghost won’t walk tonight, Walter,” he said as he entered his bedroom for a much-needed rest. “I guess I was right after all in getting the capsules as soon as possible. The ghost must have flitted unobserved in there this morning directly after the maid brought them back from the druggist.”

===Chinese===

克雷格看了看表。一点一刻了。「今晚鬼是不出来走动了，沃尔特，」他说着进了卧室，去补一场急需的觉。「看来我把胶囊尽早拿到手，到底是做对了。今早女仆刚把胶囊从药房取回来，那鬼必定就是趁人不备，闪身溜进去做了手脚。」

===Original===

Again, the next morning, he had me out of bed bright and early. As we descended from the Sixth Avenue L, he led me into a peculiar little shop in the shadow of the L structure. He entered as though he knew the place well; but, then, that air of assurance was Kennedy’s stock in trade and sat very well on him.

Few people, I suppose, have ever had a glimpse of this workshop of magic and deception. This little shop of Marina’s was the headquarters of the magicians of the country. Levitation and ghostly disappearing hands were on every side. The shelves in the back of the shop were full of nickel, brass, wire, wood, and papier-mâché contrivances, new and strange to the eye of the uninitiated. Yet it was all as systematic as a hardware shop.

===Chinese===

第二天一早，他又把我从床上薅了起来。我们从第六大道高架铁道（Sixth Avenue L）上下来，他领我钻进高架桥影子里一家古怪的小铺子。他进门那副熟门熟路的样子——不过话说回来，那副从容笃定的气派本是肯尼迪的看家本钱，安在他身上再合适不过。

我想，见识过这座魔法与戏法作坊的人寥寥无几。玛里纳（Marina）这家小铺子，是全国魔术师的大本营。四下里到处是悬浮术道具和幽灵般消失的手。铺子后头的货架上层层码满镍的、黄铜的、铁丝的、木头的、纸浆做的机巧玩意儿，外行看着既新奇又古怪。然而一切都井井有条，同五金铺子一样。

===Original===

“Is Signor Marina in?” asked Craig of a girl in the first room, given up to picture postcards. The room was as deceptive as the trade, for it was only an anteroom to the storeroom I have described above. This storeroom was also a factory, and half a dozen artisans were hard at work in it.

Yes, the signor was in, the girl replied, leading us back into the workshop. He proved to be a short man with a bland, open face and frank eyes, the very antithesis of his trade.

“I have arranged for a circle with Mrs. May Popper,” began Kennedy, handing the man his card. “I suppose you know her?”

===Chinese===

「玛里纳先生（Signor Marina）在吗？」克雷格问头一间屋里的一个姑娘，那间屋子专卖风景明信片。这屋子同这行当一样具有欺骗性——它不过是通往我方才描述的那间库房的前厅。库房同时也是工场，六七个工匠正在里面埋头干活。

在，先生在，姑娘答道，领我们往里走进工场。玛里纳先生原来是个矮个子，面庞温和开朗，眼神坦率，同他的行当恰恰相反。

「我同梅·波珀夫人约了一场降神圈，」肯尼迪递上名片，开门见山。「您想必认识她吧？」

===Original===

“Indeed yes,” he answered. “I furnished her séance room.”

“Well, I want to hire for tonight just the same sort of tables, cabinets, carpets, everything that she has﻿—only hire, you understand, but I am willing to pay you well for them. It is the best way to get a good sitting, I believe. Can you do it?”

The little man thought a moment, then replied: “*Si, signor* yes﻿—very nearly, near enough. I would do anything for Mrs. Popper. She is a good customer. But her manager﻿—”

“My friend here, Mr. Jameson, has had séances with her in her own apartment,” interposed Craig. “Perhaps he can help you to recollect just what is necessary.”

===Chinese===

「当然认识，」他答道。「她的降神室就是我布置的。」

「那好，今晚我想租一套同她一模一样的桌子、柜橱、地毯——她有什么，我要什么，一切照旧——只是租，您明白，不过价钱我愿意出足。我相信，这是办一场圆满降神会的最好办法。您能办到吗？」

小个子想了想，答道：「*Si, signor*（是，先生）是的——差不多了，够接近了。为波珀夫人，什么事我都肯办。她是位好主顾。可她那位经纪人——」

「我这位朋友詹姆森先生，在她自己的公寓里同她降过神，」克雷格插话道。「必要的东西该有哪些，有他帮您回忆，错不了。」

===Original===

“I know very well, signor. I have the duplicate bill, the bill which was paid by that Farrington with a check from the banker Vandam. Leave it to me.”

“Then you will get the stuff together this morning and have it up to my place this afternoon.”

“Yes, Professor, yes. It is a bargain. I would do anything for Mrs. Popper﻿—she is a fine woman.”

===Chinese===

「我清楚得很，先生。我有那笔账的存根——那张账单，就是那个法林顿开的、范达姆银行家的支票付的。包在我身上。」

「那就请您上午把东西备齐，下午送到我那里去。」

「好，教授，好。一言为定。为波珀夫人，什么事我都肯办——她是位好妇人。」

===Original===

Late that afternoon I rejoined Craig at his laboratory. Signor Marina had already arrived with a truck and was disposing the paraphernalia about the laboratory. He had first laid a thick black rug. Mrs. Popper very much affected black carpets, and I had noticed that Vandam’s room was carpeted in black, too. I suppose black conceals everything that one oughtn’t to see at a séance.

A cabinet with a black curtain, several chairs, a light deal table, several banjos, horns, and other instruments were disposed about the room. With a few suggestions from me we made a fair duplication of the hangings on the walls. Kennedy was manifestly anxious to finish, and at last it was done.

After Marina had gone, Kennedy stretched a curtain over the end of the room farthest from the cabinet. Behind it he placed on a shelf the apparatus composed of the pendulums and magnets. The beakers and test-tubes were also on this shelf.

===Chinese===

那天傍晚，我回到实验室同克雷格会合。玛里纳先生已经随一辆卡车到了，正把各样行头往实验室里四处安置。他先铺上了一块厚厚的黑地毯。波珀夫人偏爱黑地毯，我也注意到范达姆那间屋子铺的同样是黑地毯。想必黑色能藏住降神会上不该叫人看见的一切吧。

挂黑帷幔的柜橱、几把椅子、一张轻便松木桌、几把班卓琴、号角和其他乐器，一一安置在屋里各处。经我从旁提了几句，墙上的帷幔也仿得像模像样。肯尼迪明摆着巴不得早点完工，而终于也完工了。

玛里纳走后，肯尼迪在离柜橱最远的房间一头张起一道帷幔。帷幔后面的架子上，安放着那套由摆锤和磁铁组成的仪器。烧杯和试管也放在这架子上。

===Original===

He had also arranged that the cabinet should be so situated that it was next a hallway that ran past his laboratory.

“Tonight, Jameson,” he said, indicating a spot on the hall wall just back of the cabinet, “I shall want you to bring my guests out here and do a little spirit rapping﻿—I’ll tell you just what to do when the time comes.”

That night, when we gathered in the transformed laboratory, there were Henry Vandam, Dr. Hanson, Inspector O’Connor, Kennedy, and myself. At last the sound of wheels was heard, and Mrs. Popper drove up in a hansom, accompanied by Farrington. They both inspected the room narrowly and seemed satisfied. I had, as I have said, taken a serious dislike to the man, and watched him closely. I did not like his air of calm assurance.

===Chinese===

他还把柜橱安置在紧挨一条过道的位置，那过道正好从他的实验室旁边穿过。

「今晚，詹姆森，」他指着过道墙上恰在柜橱背后的一个位置说，「我要你把我的客人们带到这里来，敲那么几通通灵敲击——到时候我自会告诉你怎么做。」

那天夜里，聚在这间改头换面的实验室里的，有亨利·范达姆、汉森医生、奥康纳警督、肯尼迪和我。终于，车轮声响了，波珀夫人由法林顿陪着，乘一辆双轮马车（hansom）赶到。两人把房间仔仔细细打量了一番，似乎颇为满意。我说过，我对那男人怀着深深的反感，便紧紧盯着他。我不喜欢他那副处变不惊的笃定神气。

===Original===

The lights were switched off, all except one sixteen-candle-power lamp in the farthest corner, shaded by a deep-red globe. It was just light enough to see to read very, large print with difficulty.

Mrs. Popper began immediately with the table. Kennedy and I sat on her right and left respectively, in the circle, and held her hands and feet. I confess to a real thrill when I felt the light table rise first on two legs, then on one, and finally remain suspended in the air, whence it dropped with a thud, as if someone had suddenly withdrawn his support.

The medium sat with her back to the curtain of the cabinet, and several times I could have sworn that a hand reached out and passed close to my head. At least it seemed so. The curtain bulged at times, and a breeze seemed to sweep out from the cabinet.

===Chinese===

灯全灭了，只留最远角落里一盏十六烛光的灯，罩着深红色灯罩。那光亮，勉强够人费劲地读特大号铅字。

波珀夫人立刻从桌子开始。我和肯尼迪一右一左坐在圈子里，按住她的双手双脚。我承认，当那张轻巧的桌子先两条腿翘起、继而一条腿、最终整个悬在空中时，我实实在在地打了个激灵；跟着它砰的一声砸下来，仿佛有人突然撤走了托着它的手。

灵媒背朝柜橱的帷幔坐着，有好几回，我简直可以发誓，一只手伸出来，贴着我的头掠过。至少当时像是这样。帷幔时不时鼓起来，一阵风似乎从柜橱里刮出来。

===Original===

After some time of this sort of work Craig led gradually up to a request for a materialisation of the control of Vandam, but Mrs. Popper refused. She said she did not feel strong enough, and Farrington put in a hasty word that he, too, could feel that “there was something working against them.” But Kennedy was importunate and at last she consented to see if “John” would do some rapping, even if he could not materialise.

Kennedy asked to be permitted to put the questions.

===Chinese===

这类功夫做了半天，克雷格渐渐把话引到请求显出范达姆那位控制灵的真身，可波珀夫人推辞了，说自己气力不够；法林顿也忙不迭插话，说他也觉得「今夜有股力量在同他们作对」。但肯尼迪缠磨再三，她终于答应试试「约翰」肯不肯敲几通——哪怕不能显形。

肯尼迪请求由他来发问。

===Original===

“Are you the ‘John’ who appears to Mr. Vandam every night at twelve-thirty?”

*Rap! rap! rap!* came the faint reply from the cabinet. Or rather it seemed to me to come from the floor near the cabinet, and perhaps to be a trifle muffled by the black carpet.

“Are you in communication with Mrs. Vandam?”

*Rap! rap! rap!*

===Chinese===

「你可是那位每晚十二点半现身于范达姆先生面前的『约翰』？」

*咚！咚！咚！*柜橱里传来微弱的回答。或者说，在我看来，声音倒是发自柜橱旁的地板，兴许还被黑地毯闷得有点喑哑。

「你同范达姆夫人有联络吗？」

*咚！咚！咚！*

===Original===

“Can she be made to rap for us?”

*Rap! rap!*

“Will you ask her a question and spell out her answer?”

*Rap! rap! rap!*

===Chinese===

「能叫她也来给我们敲几通吗？」

*咚！咚！*

「可肯替我问她一个问题，把她的回答拼出来？」

*咚！咚！咚！*

===Original===

Craig paused a moment to frame the question, then shot it out point-blank: “Does Mrs. Vandam know now in the other world whether anyone in this room substituted a morphine capsule for one of those ordered by her three days before she died? Does she know whether the same person has done the same thing with those later ordered by Mr. Vandam?”

“John” seemed considerably perturbed at the mention of capsules. It was a long time before any answer was forthcoming. Kennedy was about to repeat the question when a faint sound was heard.

*Rap!*﻿—

===Chinese===

克雷格顿了顿，斟酌好问句，随即开门见山地脱口而出：「范达姆夫人如今在另一个世界，可知道她死前三天叫人配的那几粒胶囊，是不是被这屋里的什么人把其中一粒换成了吗啡？她可知道，范达姆先生后来叫人配的那些，是不是也叫同一个人照方炮制了？」

「约翰」听到胶囊二字，似乎大大地慌了神。半晌没有回音。肯尼迪正要再问一遍，一声轻响起了。

*咚！*——

===Original===

Suddenly came a wild scream. It was such a scream as I had never heard before in my life. It came as though a dagger had been thrust into the heart of Mrs. Popper. The lights flashed up as Kennedy turned the switch.

A man was lying flat on the floor﻿—it was Inspector O’Connor. He had succeeded in slipping noiselessly, like a snake, below the curtain into the cabinet. Craig had told him to look out for wires or threads stretched from Mrs. Popper’s clothing to the bulging curtain of the cabinet. Imagine his surprise when he saw that she had simply freed her foot from the shoe, which I was carefully holding down, and with a backward movement of the leg was reaching out into the cabinet behind her chair and was doing the rapping with her toes.

Lying on the floor he had grasped her foot and caught her heel with a firm hand. She had responded with a wild yell that showed she knew she was trapped. Her secret was out.

===Chinese===

陡然间，一声狂叫划破寂静。我平生从未听过那样的尖叫。那叫声直如一柄匕首扎进了波珀夫人的心口。肯尼迪一拧开关，灯刷地全亮了。

一个男人平趴在地板上——是奥康纳警督。他成功地像条蛇一样悄无声息地从帷幔底下钻进了柜橱。克雷格原是吩咐他提防从波珀夫人衣服连到鼓起帷幔上的细线丝线之类。可你想想他那时的惊讶：他看见她不过是把那只脚从我死死按住的鞋里褪了出来，腿向后一伸，探进椅子背后的柜橱，正用脚趾敲得欢呢。

他卧在地板上，一把攥住她的脚，牢牢扣住她的脚跟。她报以一声狂叫——这一叫，等于承认自己落网了。她的秘密败露了。

===Original===

Hysterically Mrs. Popper began to upbraid the inspector as he rose to his feet, but Farrington quickly interposed.

“Something was working against us tonight, gentlemen. Yet you demanded results. And when the spirits will not come, what is she to do? She forgets herself in her trance; she produces, herself, the things that you all could see supernaturally if you were in sympathy.”

The mere sound of Farrington’s voice seemed to rouse in me all the animosity of my nature. I felt that a man who could trump up an excuse like that when a person was caught with the goods was capable of almost anything.

===Chinese===

波珀夫人歇斯底里地开始斥骂站起身来的警督，法林顿赶紧横身插了进来。

「今夜确有股力量在同我们作对，先生们。可诸位偏要结果。待到精灵们不肯降临，她还能怎样？她在恍惚中忘乎所以；那些东西，是她自己变化出来的——诸位若心存契合，本可以超自然地亲眼看见。」

单是法林顿的嗓音，就仿佛把我天性里所有的敌意都激了起来。我想，一个人在人赃俱获的时候还能编出这样一套说辞，那就还有什么事干不出来。

===Original===

“Enough of this fake séance,” exclaimed Craig. “I have let it go on merely for the purpose of opening the eyes of a certain deluded gentleman in this room. Now, if you will all be seated I shall have something to say that will finally establish whether Mary Vandam was the victim of accident, suicide, or murder.”

With hearts beating rapidly we sat in silence.

Craig took the beakers and test-tubes from the shelf behind the curtain and placed them on the little deal table that had been so merrily dancing about the room.

===Chinese===

「这场假降神会到此为止，」克雷格朗声道。「我听任它演下去，无非是要睁开这屋里某位执迷不悟的先生的一双眼睛。现在，请诸位各就各位，我有话要说——它将最终判定，玛丽·范达姆（Mary Vandam）究竟死于意外、自杀，还是谋杀。」

我们心口怦怦直跳，默默坐定。

克雷格从帷幔后面的架子上取下烧杯和试管，摆在那张方才还在满屋欢腾乱跳的小松木桌上。

===Original===

“The increasing frequency with which tales of murder by poison appear in the newspapers,” he began formally, “is proof of how rapidly this new civilisation of ours is taking on the aspects of the older civilisations across the seas. Human life is cheap in this country; but the ways in which human life has been taken among us have usually been direct, simple, aboveboard, in keeping with our democratic and pioneer traditions. The pistol and the bowie-knife for the individual, the rope and the torch for the mob, have been the usual instruments of sudden death. But when we begin to use poisons most artfully compounded in order to hasten an expected bequest and remove obstacles in its way﻿—well, we are practising an art that calls up all the memories of sixteenth century Italy.

===Chinese===

「毒杀案见诸报端，一日多似一日，」他一本正经地开了场，「这证明我们这个新文明，正何等迅速地染上大洋彼岸那些古老文明的气象。在这个国家，人命本不值钱；但我们这儿取人性命的路数，向来直截、简单、光明正大，同我们民主与拓荒的传统相称。单干的人用手枪和猎刀，聚众的人用绳索和火把，素来是暴死的老伙计。可等到我们开始用调配得最精巧的毒药，去催促一笔等候到手的遗产、扫清挡道的障碍——唉，我们操演的这门技艺，便把十六世纪意大利的全部旧事都召回来了。

===Original===

“In this beaker,” he continued, “I have some of the contents of the stomach of the unfortunate woman. The coroner’s physician has found that they show traces of morphine. Was the morphine in such quantities as to be fatal? Without doubt. But equally without doubt analysis could not discover and prove it in the face of one inconsistency. The usual test which shows morphine poisoning failed in this case. The pupils of her eyes were not symmetrically contracted. In fact they were normal.

===Chinese===

「这只烧杯里，」他接着说，「装的是那位不幸妇人胃里的一部分内容物。法医官已经查明，其中显示有吗啡的痕迹。吗啡的剂量是否达到了致命的程度？毫无疑问。可同样毫无疑问的是，在一处矛盾面前，化验既发现不了、也证明不了这一点。指示吗啡中毒的常规检验，在本案中失灵了。死者的两眼瞳孔并非对称收缩。事实上，它们完全正常。

===Original===

“Now, the murderer must have known of this test. This clever criminal also knew that to be successful in the use of this drug where others had failed, the drug must be skilfully mixed with something else. In that first box of capsules there were six. The druggist compounded them correctly according to the prescription. But between the time when they came into the house from the druggist’s and the time when she took the first capsule, that night, someone who had access to the house emptied one capsule of its harmless contents and refilled it with a deadly dose of morphine﻿—a white powder which looks just like the powder already in the capsules.

===Chinese===

「这么说，凶手必定知道这项检验。这个聪明的罪犯还知道，要用这味药成事——前人都失手的地方——就得把它巧妙地同别的东西掺在一起。头一盒胶囊共六粒。药剂师依照处方配得毫厘不差。可就在药盒从药房进宅、到她当晚服下头一粒之间，某个进出宅子无阻的人，倒空了其中一粒的无害药粉，重新灌进一份致命剂量的吗啡——一种白色粉末，模样同胶囊里原有的药粉一模一样。

===Original===

“Why, then, the normal pupils of the eyes? Simply because the criminal put a little atropine, or belladonna, with the morphine. My tests show absolutely the presence of atropine, Dr. Hanson,” said Craig, bowing to the physician.

“The best evidence, however, is yet to come. A second box of six capsules, all intact, was discovered yesterday in the possession of Henry Vandam. I have analysed the capsules. One contains no quinine at all﻿—it is all morphine and atropine. It is, without doubt, precisely similar to the capsule which killed Mrs. Vandam. Another night or so, and Henry Vandam would have died the same death.”

===Chinese===

「那么，瞳孔为什么正常呢？只因为罪犯在吗啡里掺了少许阿托品（atropine），也就是颠茄（belladonna）。汉森医生，我的检验确凿无误地显示含有阿托品，」克雷格说着，向医生欠了欠身。

「不过，最有力的证据还在后头。昨天，在亨利·范达姆手里又发现了一盒六粒装胶囊，全都原封未动。我把胶囊一一化验了。其中一粒根本不含奎宁——全是吗啡和阿托品。毫无疑问，它同杀死范达姆夫人的那一粒一模一样。再过一两夜，亨利·范达姆便会步她后尘，死得一模一样。」

===Original===

The old man groaned. Two such exposures had shaken him. He looked from one of us to another as if not knowing in whom he could trust. But Kennedy hurried on to his next point.

“Who was it that gave the prescription to Mrs. Vandam originally? She is dead and cannot tell. The others won’t tell, for the person who gave her that prescription was the person who later substituted the fatal capsule in place of the harmless. The original prescription is here. I have been able to discover from it nothing at all by examining the handwriting. Nor does the texture of the paper indicate anything to me. But the ink﻿—ah, the ink.

===Chinese===

老头儿呻吟了一声。接连两番揭底，把他击垮了。他挨个儿望望我们，仿佛不知道还能信谁。肯尼迪却马不停蹄，转入下一层。

「当初，是谁把那张处方交给范达姆夫人的？她死了，说不出。别人不肯说，因为给她处方的人，正是后来用致命胶囊换掉无害胶囊的人。原始处方就在这里。从笔迹上，我什么也没查出来。纸质也向我透露不出半点消息。可这墨水——啊，墨水。

===Original===

“Most inks seem very similar, I suppose, but to a person who has made a study of the chemical composition of ink they are very different. Ink is composed of iron tannate, which on exposure to air gives the black of writing. The original pigment﻿—say blue or blue-black ink﻿—is placed in the ink, to make the writing visible at first, and gradually fades, giving place to the black of the tannate which is formed. The dyestuffs employed in the commercial inks of today vary in colour from pale greenish blue to indigo and deep violet. No two give identical reactions﻿—at all events not when mixed with the iron tannate to form the pigment in writing.

===Chinese===

「在常人看来，大半墨水都一个模样，可对钻研过墨水化学成分的人来说，它们千差万别。墨水由鞣酸铁（iron tannate）构成，一接触空气，便生出字迹的黑色。原有的色料——比如蓝墨水或蓝黑墨水——掺在墨水里，使落笔之初字迹可见，随后渐渐褪去，让位给生成的鞣酸铁之黑。今日市售墨水所用的染料，颜色从淡绿蓝到靛蓝、深紫不等。没有两种反应全然相同——至少，在同鞣酸铁化合成字迹色料之后，绝无相同。

===Original===

“It is owing to the difference in these provisional colouring matters that it is possible to distinguish between writing written with different kinds of ink. I was able easily to obtain samples of the inks used by the Vandams, by Mrs. Popper, by Mr. Farrington, and by the druggist. I have compared the writing of the original prescription with a colour scale of my own construction, and I have made chemical tests. The druggist’s ink conforms exactly to the writing on the two pillboxes, but not to the prescription. One of the other three inks conforms by test absolutely to the ink in that prescription signed ‘Dr. C. W. H.’ as a blind. In a moment my chain of evidence against the owner of that bottle of ink will be complete.”

===Chinese===

「正是靠这些临时色料的差异，才能分辨出用不同墨水写下的字迹。范达姆夫妇、波珀夫人、法林顿先生和药剂师各自用的墨水，我都不费吹灰之力弄到了样品。我把原始处方上的字迹同我自制的一套色阶比对，又做了化学检验。药剂师的墨水，同两只药盒上的字迹完全吻合，却与处方不合。其余三种墨水里，有一种经检验同那张处方上的墨水绝对吻合——就是那签署「C. W. H. 医生」作幌子的墨水。用不了一会儿，指向那瓶墨水主人的证据链，就大功告成了。」

===Original===

I could not help but think of the two pendulums on the shelf behind the curtain, but Craig said nothing for a moment to indicate that he referred to that apparatus. We sat dazed. Farrington seemed nervous and ill at ease. Mrs. Popper, who had not recovered from the hysterical condition of her exposure, with difficulty controlled her emotion. Vandam was crushed.

===Chinese===

我不由想起帷幔后架子上的那两只摆，可克雷格一时只字不提，没露出半点口风说他指的就是那套仪器。我们全都呆坐当场。法林顿显得紧张不安。波珀夫人被揭底时的歇斯底里还没缓过来，勉强按捺着情绪。范达姆则彻底垮了。

===Original===

“I have not only arranged this laboratory so as to reproduce Mrs. Popper’s séance-room,” began Craig afresh, “but I have had the cabinet placed in relatively the same position a similar cabinet occupies in Mr. Vandam’s private séance-room in the Vandam mansion.

“One night, Mr. Jameson and myself were visiting Mr. Vandam. At precisely twelve-thirty we heard most unaccountable rappings from that cabinet. I particularly noted the position of the cabinet. Back of it ran a hallway. That is duplicated here. Back of this cabinet is a hallway. I had heard of these rappings before we went, but was afraid that it would be impossible for me to catch the ghost red handed. There is a limit to what you can do the first time you enter a man’s house, and, besides, that was no time to arouse suspicion in the mind of anyone. But science has a way out of every dilemma. I determined to learn something of these rappings.”

===Chinese===

「我不但把这间实验室布置成波珀夫人降神室的样子，」克雷格重新开了头，「还把柜橱安放在大体相同的位置上——同范达姆宅里范达姆先生私人降神室那只柜橱的位置相仿。

「一天夜里，我和詹姆森先生拜访范达姆先生。恰在十二点半，我们听见那柜橱里传出最不可索解的敲击。我特别留意了柜橱的位置。它背后是一条过道。这里也照此复制了。这只柜橱背后同样是过道。行前我已听说过这些敲击，只是担心无法当场拿住这鬼。头一回登人家的门，行事总有个限度；何况那时节，万万不能叫任何人生疑。可科学对每一道难题都有出路。我打定主意，要摸一摸这些敲击的底。」

===Original===

Craig paused and glanced first at Farrington, then at Mrs. Popper, and then at Mr. Vandam.

“Mr. Jameson,” he resumed, “will escort the doctor, the inspector, Mr. Farrington, Mrs. Popper, and Mr. Vandam into my imitation hall of the Vandam mansion. I want each of you in turn to tiptoe up that hall to a spot indicated on the wall, back of the cabinet, and strike that spot several sharp blows with your knuckles.”

I did as Craig instructed tiptoeing up myself first so that they could not mistake his meaning. The rest followed separately, and after a moment we returned silently in suppressed excitement to the room.

===Chinese===

克雷格顿了顿，目光先落在法林顿身上，再移向波珀夫人，又转向范达姆先生。

「詹姆森先生，」他接着说，「将把医生、警督、法林顿先生、波珀夫人和范达姆先生领进我这仿造的范达姆宅过道。请诸位依次踮起脚尖，沿过道走到柜橱背后墙上标出的那个位置，用指节朝那儿重重敲几下。」

我照克雷格的嘱咐，自己先蹑手蹑脚走了一趟，免得他们误会他的意思。其余的人挨个跟上，片刻之后，我们按捺着兴奋，悄无声息地回到房中。

===Original===

Craig was still standing by the table, but now the pendulums with the magnets and needles and the drums worked by clockwork were before him.

“Another person outside the Vandam family had a key to the Vandam mansion,” he began gravely. “That person, by the way, was the one who waited, night by night, until Mrs. Vandam took the fatal capsule, and then when she had taken it apprised the old man of the fact and strengthened an already blind faith in the shadow world.”

You could have heard a pin drop. In fact you could almost have felt it drop.

===Chinese===

克雷格仍旧立在桌旁，只是此刻，摆锤、磁铁、细针连同钟表机构带动的圆鼓，已摆在他面前。

「范达姆家之外，另有一人握有范达姆宅的钥匙，」他神情凝重地开口。「顺便说一句，正是此人，一夜一夜地守着，守到范达姆夫人服下那粒致命的胶囊，然后一得手便报知老头儿，给他那本已盲目的幽冥信仰又添了一把柴。」

静得连一根针落地都听得见。说真的，那针落地几乎都摸得着。

===Original===

“That other person who, unobserved, had free access to the house,” he continued in the breathless stillness, “is in this room now.”

He was looking at O’Connor as if for corroboration. O’Connor nodded. “Information derived from the butler,” he muttered.

“I did not know this until yesterday,” Kennedy continued, “but I suspected that something of the sort existed when I was first told by Dr. Hanson of the rappings. I determined to hear those rappings, and make a record of them. So, the night Mr. Jameson and I visited Mr. Vandam, I carried this little instrument with me.”

===Chinese===

「那个无人察觉、自由出入宅邸的人，」他在屏息的寂静中继续说，「此刻就在这间屋里。」

他望着奥康纳，仿佛请他作证。奥康纳点了点头。「从管家嘴里问出来的，」他低声说。

「这一点我直到昨天才知道，」肯尼迪接着说，「不过，汉森医生初次向我讲起敲击时，我就疑心有这一类名堂。我打定主意要亲耳听听那些敲击，并录下一个记录。于是，詹姆森先生和我拜访范达姆先生那夜，我便随身带上了这件小仪器。」

===Original===

Almost lovingly he touched the pendulums on the table. They were now at rest and kept so by means of a lever that prevented all vibration whatever.

“See, I release this lever﻿—now, let no one in the room move. Watch the needles on the paper as the clockwork revolves the drums. I take a step﻿—ever so lightly. The pendulums vibrate, and the needles trace a broken line on the paper on each drum. I stop; the lines are practically straight. I take another step and another, ever so lightly. See the delicate pendulums vibrate? See, the lines they trace are jagged lines.”

He stripped the paper off the drums and laid it flat on the table before him, with two other similar pieces of paper.

===Chinese===

他近乎爱怜地抚了抚桌上的摆锤。此刻它们静止不动，由一根杠杆固定着，杜绝一切震动。

「瞧，我松开这根杠杆——现在，屋里谁也别动。看钟表机构带动圆鼓时，细针在纸上画出的线。我迈一步——轻得不能再轻。摆锤颤动，细针便在每只圆鼓的纸上描出一条断续的线。我停住；线便几乎是直的。我再迈一步，又一步，还是轻得不能再轻。看见那灵敏的摆锤颤动了吗？瞧，它们描出的线，是锯齿状的线。」

他把纸从圆鼓上揭下，同另外两张同样的纸一起，平摊在面前的桌上。

===Original===

“Just before the time of the rapping I placed this instrument in the corner of the Vandam cabinet, just as I placed it in this cabinet after Mr. Jameson conducted you from the room. In neither case were suspicions aroused. Everything in both cases was perfectly normal﻿—I mean the ‘ghost’ was in ignorance of the presence, if not the very existence, of this instrument.

===Chinese===

「敲击将至之前，我把这件仪器安放在范达姆家柜橱的角落里，正如今晚詹姆森先生引诸位出房后，我把它安进这只柜橱一样。两回都没有引起丝毫疑心。两回一切照常——我是说，那『鬼』全然不知有这件仪器在场，甚至不知世上有这件仪器。

===Original===

“This is an improved seismograph,” he explained, “one after a very recent model by Prince Galitzin of the Imperial Academy of St. Petersburg. The seismograph, as you know, was devised to register earthquakes at a distance. This one not only measures the size of a distant earthquake, but the actual direction from which the earth-tremors come. That is why there are two pendulums and two drums.

“The magnetic arrangement is to cut short the vibrations set up in the pendulums, to prevent them from continuing to vibrate after the first shock. Thus they are ready in an instant to record another tremor. Other seismographs continue to vibrate for a long time as a result of one tremor only. Besides, they give little indication of the direction from which the tremors come.

“I think you must all appreciate that your tiptoeing up the hall must cause a far greater disturbance in this delicate seismograph than even a very severe earthquake thousands of miles away, which it was built to record.”

===Chinese===

「这是一台改良的地震仪（seismograph），」他解释道，「仿的是圣彼得堡帝国科学院（Imperial Academy of St. Petersburg）加利津亲王（Prince Galitzin）不久前推出的最新式样。诸位知道，地震仪本为遥测地震而造。这一台不但能量出远方地震的大小，还能测出地动传来的实际方向。所以它有两只摆锤、两只圆鼓。

「那套磁铁装置，为的是掐断摆锤里激起的颤动，免得初次一震之后摆个不停。如此一来，它们瞬息之间便能待命，记录下一次震动。别的地震仪只经一次震动，便要久久荡个不住。况且，它们几乎指不出震动来自何方。

「诸位想必都明白，你们踮着脚尖走过道，在这台灵敏的地震仪上激起的扰动，必定远胜于几千里外一场剧烈的地震——尽管造它出来，原是为记录那种地震的。」

===Original===

He paused and examined the papers sharply.

“This is the record made by the ‘ghost’s’ walk the other night,” he said, holding up two of them in his left hand. “Here on the table, on two other longer sheets, I have records of the vibrations set up by those in this room walking tonight.

“Here is Mr. Jameson’s﻿—his is not a bit like the ghost’s. Nor is Mr. Vandam’s. Least of all are Dr. Hanson’s and Inspector O’Connor’s, for they are heavy men.

“Now here is Mr. Farrington’s”﻿—he bent down closely, “he is a light man, and the ghost was light.”

===Chinese===

他停了停，锐利地检视那几张纸。

「这是那夜『鬼』走路留下的记录，」他说着，左手举起其中两张。「桌上这两张长一些的纸上，是今晚这屋里各位走动时激起的震动记录。

「这张是詹姆森先生的——同鬼的一点不像。范达姆先生的也不像。最不像的是汉森医生和奥康纳警督的，他们两位身量沉。

「喏，这张是法林顿先生的」——他俯身凑近细看，「他是个身子轻的人，而那鬼也轻。」

===Original===

Craig was playing with his victim like a cat with a mouse.

Suddenly I felt something brush by me, and with a swish of air and of garments I saw Mrs. Popper fling herself wildly at the table that bore the incriminating records. In another instant Farrington was on his feet and had made a wild leap in the same direction.

It was done so quickly that I must have acted first and thought afterward. I found myself in the midst of a melee with my hand at his throat and his at mine. O’Connor with a jiujitsu movement bent Farrington’s other arm until he released me with a cry of pain.

===Chinese===

克雷格戏弄起他的猎物来，活像猫戏老鼠。

冷不防，我觉得什么东西擦身而过，只见波珀夫人裹着一阵风、一阵衣袂，疯了似的朝摆着那些定罪记录的桌子扑去。转瞬间，法林顿也一跃而起，朝同一方向猛扑。

这一切快得我来不及先想后动，只能先动后想。我发觉自己已经卷进一场混战，我的手掐着他的喉咙，他的手掐着我的。奥康纳使了个柔术（jiujitsu）招数，扳住法林顿的另一条胳膊反拧，疼得他一声惨叫松开了我。

===Original===

In front of me I saw Craig grasping Mrs. Popper’s wrists as in a vise. She was glaring at him like a tigress.

“Do you suppose for a moment that that toy is going to convince the world that Henry Vandam has been deceived and that the spirit which visited him was a fraud? Is that why you have lured me here under false pretences, to play on my feelings, to insult me, to take advantage of a lone, defenceless woman, surrounded by hostile men? Shame on you,” she added contemptuously. “You call yourself a gentleman, but I call you a coward.”

===Chinese===

我面前，克雷格正像老虎钳似的钳着波珀夫人的双腕。她虎视眈眈地瞪着他，活像一头母虎。

「你以为，那么个玩意儿就能叫全世界相信，亨利·范达姆受了骗，来访他的精灵是个骗局？你把诓到这儿来，就为的这个吗——玩弄我的感情，羞辱我，欺负一个孤身无依、被一群虎视眈眈的男人围住的妇人？你不要脸，」她轻蔑地补了一句。「你自称绅士，我看你是懦夫。」

===Original===

Kennedy, always calm and collected, ignored the tirade. His voice was as cold as steel as he said: “It would do little good, Mrs. Popper, to destroy this one link in the chain I have forged. The other links are too heavy for you. Don’t forget the evidence of the ink. It was your ink. Don’t forget that Henry Vandam will not any longer conceal that he has altered his will in favour of you. Tonight he goes from here to his lawyer’s to draw up a new will altogether. Don’t forget that you have caused the Vandams separately to have the prescription filled, and that you are now caught in the act of a double murder. Don’t forget that you had access to the Vandam mansion, that you substituted the deadly for the harmless capsules. Don’t forget that your rappings announced the death of one of your victims and urged the other, a cruelly wronged and credulous old man, to leave millions to you who had deceived and would have killed him.

===Chinese===

肯尼迪永远冷静沉着，对这番痛骂置若罔闻。他开口时，嗓音冷得像钢：「波珀夫人，毁掉我锻成的链条上这一环，于事无补。其余各环，你扛不动。别忘了墨水的证据——那是你的墨水。别忘了亨利·范达姆已不再隐瞒：他改立过遗嘱，把你立为受惠人。今晚他就从这里去律师那里，另立一份全新的遗嘱。别忘了，是你先后撺掇范达姆夫妇二人分别去配那服处方，而你现在已是双重谋杀、当场拿获。别忘了，你出入范达姆宅畅通无阻，是你用致命的胶囊换掉了无害的。别忘了，你的敲击声宣告了你一名受害人的死讯，又催促另一名受害人——一位受尽欺凌、轻信不疑的老人——把几百万家财留给你这个骗了他、还差点害死他的人。

===Original===

“No, the record of the ghost on the seismograph was not Mr. Farrington’s, as I implied at the moment when you so kindly furnished this additional proof of your guilt by trying to destroy the evidence. The ghost was you, Mrs. Popper, and you are at liberty to examine the markings as minutely as you please, but you must not destroy them. You are an astute criminal, Mrs. Popper, but tonight you are under arrest for the murder of Mary Vandam and the attempted murder of Henry Vandam.”


===Chinese===

「不，地震仪上那份鬼的记录，并不是法林顿先生的——我方才那样暗示，正是在你企图销毁证据、如此好心地补上这份新罪证的时刻。那鬼就是你，波珀夫人。那些线痕，你尽可以随意细细查验，只是不许毁坏。你是个精明的罪犯，波珀夫人，但今晚，你因谋杀玛丽·范达姆、谋杀亨利·范达姆未遂，被捕了。」

## The Silent Bullet / 无声的子弹

===Original===

“Detectives in fiction nearly always make a great mistake,” said Kennedy one evening after our first conversation on crime and science. “They almost invariably antagonize the regular detective force. Now in real life that’s impossible﻿—it’s fatal.”

“Yes,” I agreed, looking up from reading an account of the failure of a large Wall Street brokerage house, Kerr Parker & Co., and the peculiar suicide of Kerr Parker. “Yes, it’s impossible, just as it is impossible for the regular detectives to antagonize the newspapers. Scotland Yard found that out in the Crippen case.”

“My idea of the thing, Jameson,” continued Kennedy, “is that the professor of criminal science ought to work with, not against, the regular detectives. They’re all right. They’re indispensable, of course. Half the secret of success nowadays is organisation. The professor of criminal science should be merely what the professor in a technical school often is﻿—a sort of consulting engineer. For instance, I believe that organisation plus science would go far toward clearing up that Wall Street case I see you are reading.”

I expressed some doubt as to whether the regular police were enlightened enough to take that view of it.

===Chinese===

「小说里的侦探几乎总要犯一个大错，」在我们第一次谈起犯罪与科学之后的一个晚上，克雷格·肯尼迪（Craig Kennedy）说道。「他们几乎无一例外，总要同正规的侦探队伍作对。可在现实生活中这是行不通的——那是致命的。」

「是啊，」我应道，一面从手头的报纸上抬起眼来——报上正登着华尔街一家大经纪行克尔·帕克公司（Kerr Parker & Co.）倒闭，以及克尔·帕克（Kerr Parker）离奇自杀的新闻。「是啊，这是行不通的，正如正规的侦探不可能同报纸作对一样。苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）在克里平案（Crippen case）里就领教过这一点。」

「詹姆森，我对这件事的想法，」肯尼迪接着说，「是犯罪侦查学教授应当同正规侦探协同行事，而不是同他们作对。他们没有问题，当然不可或缺。如今，成功的一半秘诀在于组织。犯罪侦查学教授该做的，不过是技术学校里的教授常常充当的角色——一位顾问工程师。比方说，我相信组织加上科学，对于侦破我看到你正在读的那桩华尔街案子，会大有帮助。」

我表示怀疑，正规警察界是否开明到能接受这种看法。

===Original===

“Some of them are,” he replied. “Yesterday the chief of police in a Western city sent a man East to see me about the Price murder: you know the case?”

Indeed I did. A wealthy banker of the town had been murdered on the road to the golf club, no one knew why or by whom. Every clue had proved fruitless, and the list of suspects was itself so long and so impossible as to seem most discouraging.

“He sent me a piece of a torn handkerchief with a deep bloodstain on it,” pursued Kennedy. “He said it clearly didn’t belong to the murdered man, that it indicated that the murderer had himself been wounded in the tussle, but as yet it had proved utterly valueless as a clue. Would I see what I could make of it?

“After his man had told me the story I had a feeling that the murder was committed by either a Sicilian labourer on the links or a negro waiter at the club. Well, to make a short story shorter, I decided to test the bloodstain. Probably you didn’t know it, but the Carnegie Institution has just published a minute, careful, and dry study of the blood of human beings and of animals.

“In fact, they have been able to reclassify the whole animal kingdom on this basis, and have made some most surprising additions to our knowledge of evolution. Now I don’t propose to bore you with the details of the tests, but one of the things they showed was that the blood of a certain branch of the human race gives a reaction much like the blood of a certain group of monkeys, the chimpanzees, while the blood of another branch gives a reaction like that of the gorilla. Of course there’s lots more to it, but this is all that need concern us now.

“I tried the tests. The blood on the handkerchief conformed strictly to the latter test. Now the gorilla was, of course, out of the question﻿—this was no Rue Morgue murder. Therefore it was the negro waiter.”

===Chinese===

「他们有些人是的，」他答道。「昨天，西部一座城市的警察局长派了一个人到东部来见我，谈的是普莱斯（Price）谋杀案——你知道这个案子吧？」

我何止知道。城里有位富有的银行家在通往高尔夫俱乐部的路上遇害，谁也不知道是何缘故、死于谁手。每一条线索都落了空，嫌疑人的名单本身又长又不着边际，瞧着就叫人灰心。

「他寄给我一块撕破的手帕，上头有一大片深深的血迹，」肯尼迪继续道。「他说，这手帕显然不是被害人的，它说明凶手本人在扭打中也挂了彩；只是作为线索，它至今毫无价值。我愿不愿意看看，能从它上面研究出点什么？

「听他派来的人讲完案情，我心里有了一种感觉：干下这桩谋杀的，不是球场上的西西里劳工，就是俱乐部里的黑人侍者。好吧，长话短说，我决定检验那块血迹。你多半还不知道，卡内基研究所（Carnegie Institution）刚刚发表了一份细密、审慎而枯燥的研究，研究的对象是人类和动物的血液。

「事实上，他们已经能够在这个基础上给整个动物界重新分类，还为我们关于进化的知识添上了几笔惊人的新内容。我不打算拿试验的细节来烦你，只想说其中表明的一点：人类某一支系的血液，其反应同一类猴子——黑猩猩——的血液十分相近；而另一支系的血液，反应则近似大猩猩。当然，其中门道还多得很，不过眼下与我们有关的，只有这一点。

「我做了那些试验。手帕上的血完全符合后一种反应。大猩猩当然无从谈起——这可不是莫格街凶杀案（Rue Morgue）。因此，凶手就是那个黑人侍者。」

===Original===

“But,” I interrupted, “the negro offered a perfect alibi at the start, and﻿—”

“No buts, Walter. Here’s a telegram I received at dinner: ‘Congratulations. Confronted Jackson your evidence as wired. Confessed.’ ”

“Well, Craig, I take off my hat to you,” I exclaimed. “Next you’ll be solving this Kerr Parker case for sure.”

“I would take a hand in it if they’d let me,” said he simply.

===Chinese===

「可是，」我插嘴道，「那黑人一开头就拿出了无懈可击的不在场证明，而且——」

「没有什么『可是』，沃尔特。这是我晚餐时收到的一份电报：『祝贺。已凭电告证据与杰克逊（Jackson）对质。已供认。』」

「好哇，克雷格，我向你脱帽致敬，」我失声喊道。「下一步，你准会把这桩克尔·帕克案破了吧。」

「要是他们肯让我插手，我是愿意出一把力的。」他只是淡淡地说。

===Original===

That night, without saying anything, I sauntered down to the imposing new police building amid the squalor of Center Street. They were very busy at headquarters, but, having once had that assignment for the *Star*, I had no trouble in getting in. Inspector Barney O’Connor of the Central Office carefully shifted a cigar from corner to corner of his mouth as I poured forth my suggestion to him.

“Well, Jameson,” he said at length, “do you think this professor fellow is the goods?”

I didn’t mince matters in my opinion of Kennedy. I told him of the Price case and showed him a copy of the telegram. That settled it.

“Can you bring him down here tonight?” he asked quickly.

I reached for the telephone, found Craig in his laboratory finally, and in less than an hour he was in the office.

===Chinese===

当天夜里，我没同任何人打招呼，独自溜达到中央街（Center Street）一片脏乱之中那座气派的新警察大楼。总局里上上下下忙得不可开交，不过我从前替《明星报》（*Star*）跑过这条线，进去全不费事。我向中央办公厅的巴尼·奥康纳（Barney O'Connor）警督和盘托出我的建议时，他叼着一支雪茄，不紧不慢地把它从嘴角这一头挪到那一头。

「这个嘛，詹姆森，」他终于开了口，「你觉得这位教授当真有两下子吗？」

谈到我对肯尼迪的看法，我毫不拐弯抹角。我把普莱斯案讲给他听，又把那份电报的抄件给他过目。这一下敲定了。

「你今晚能把他请到这里来吗？」他马上问。

我抓起电话，最后总算在实验室里找到了克雷格，不出一个钟头，他就坐进了办公室。

===Original===

“This is a most baffling case, Professor Kennedy, this case of Kerr Parker,” said the inspector, launching at once into his subject. “Here is a broker heavily interested in Mexican rubber. It looks like a good thing﻿—plantations right in the same territory as those of the Rubber Trust. Now in addition to that he is branching out into coastwise steamship lines; another man associated with him is heavily engaged in a railway scheme from the United States down into Mexico. Altogether the steamships and railroads are tapping rubber, oil, copper, and I don’t know what other regions. Here in New York they have been pyramiding stocks, borrowing money from two trust companies which they control. It’s a lovely scheme﻿—you’ve read about it, I suppose. Also you’ve read that it comes into competition with a certain group of capitalists whom we will call ‘the System.’

“Well, this depression in the market comes along. At once rumours are spread about the weakness of the trust companies; runs start on both of them. The System﻿—you know them﻿—make a great show of supporting the market. Yet the runs continue. God knows whether they will spread or the trust companies stand up under it tomorrow after what happened today. It was a good thing the market was closed when it happened.

“Kerr Parker was surrounded by a group of people who were in his schemes with him. They are holding a council of war in the directors’ room. Suddenly Parker rises, staggers toward the window, falls, and is dead before a doctor can get to him. Every effort is made to keep the thing quiet. It is given out that he committed suicide. The papers don’t seem to accept the suicide theory, however. Neither do we. The coroner, who is working with us, has kept his mouth shut so far, and will say nothing till the inquest. For, Professor Kennedy, my first man on the spot found that﻿—Kerr Parker﻿—was﻿—murdered.

“Now here comes the amazing part of the story. The doors to the offices on both sides were open at the time. There were lots of people in each office. There was the usual click of typewriters, and the buzz of the ticker, and the hum of conversation. We have any number of witnesses of the whole affair, but as far as any of them knows no shot was fired, no smoke was seen, no noise was heard, nor was any weapon found. Yet here on my desk is a thirty-two-calibre bullet. The coroner’s physician probed it out of Parker’s neck this afternoon and turned it over to us.”

===Chinese===

「这实在是一桩最伤脑筋的案子，肯尼迪教授，这桩克尔·帕克案。」警督开门见山，一下子就切入正题。「有这么一位经纪人，在墨西哥橡胶上重仓持有。这买卖看着挺美——种植园恰好同橡胶托拉斯（Rubber Trust）的园址在同一片地界。不止如此，他还在把摊子铺向沿海轮船航线；与他搭伙的另一个人则倾力投入一条从美国直通墨西哥的铁路计划。总而言之，这些轮船和铁路一路伸向橡胶、石油、铜矿，还有天知道些什么地方。在纽约这边，他们一直在金字塔式地炒股，向两家受他们控制的信托公司借钱。这盘棋下得可真漂亮——这些你大概都在报上读到了。你也读到了，这桩买卖同某一群资本家撞了车，我们不妨管他们叫『那个集团』（the System）。

「这不，市场萧条来了。信托公司不稳的谣言立刻传开，两家公司双双遭到挤兑。那个集团——你是知道他们的——大张旗鼓地做出支撑市场的姿态。可是挤兑照旧进行。经历了今天这一场，明天挤兑会不会蔓延、两家公司撑不撑得住，只有天知道。幸亏事情发生时交易所已经收市。

「当时，克尔·帕克身边围着一群同他一起做这些买卖的人，正在董事会议室里开作战会议。帕克忽然站起身，踉跄着扑向窗口，倒了下去，医生还没赶到就断了气。各方拼命想把事情压下去，对外宣称他是自杀。可报纸似乎并不买自杀说的账，我们也不买。同我们配合的验尸官至今守口如瓶，死因审讯之前一个字也不肯说。因为，肯尼迪教授，我最先赶到现场的手下发现——克尔·帕克——是——被人谋杀的。

「接下来才是这桩案子惊人的一幕。出事的时候，两侧办公室的门都敞开着，每间屋里都有许多人。打字机照常嗒嗒作响，行情收报机嗡嗡叫，人语嘈嘈。整件事我们有的是证人，可据他们所知，没有人开枪，没有人看见硝烟，没有人听见响声，也没有找到任何凶器。然而，我桌上这颗点三二口径（thirty-two-calibre）的子弹，是法医官今天下午从帕克颈子里探出来，交给我们的。」

===Original===

Kennedy reached for the bullet, and turned it thoughtfully in his fingers for a moment. One side of it had apparently struck a bone in the neck of the murdered man, and was flattened. The other side was still perfectly smooth. With his inevitable magnifying-glass he scrutinised the bullet on every side. I watched his face anxiously, and I could see that he was very intent and very excited.

“Extraordinary, most extraordinary,” he said to himself as he turned it over and over. “Where did you say this bullet struck?”

“In the fleshy part of the neck, quite a little back of and below his ear and just above his collar. There wasn’t much bleeding. I think it must have struck the base of his brain.”

“It didn’t strike his collar or hair?”

“No,” replied the inspector.

“Inspector, I think we shall be able to put our hands on the murderer﻿—I think we can get a conviction, sir, on the evidence that I shall get from this bullet in my laboratory.”

===Chinese===

肯尼迪伸手接过子弹，若有所思地在指间捻了片刻。它的一面显然撞上了死者颈中的骨头，已经压扁；另一面依旧光滑如初。他掏出那块从不离身的放大镜，把子弹四面八方端详了个遍。我急切地望着他的脸，看得出他极为专注，也极为兴奋。

「不寻常，太不寻常了。」他把子弹翻来覆去，自言自语道。「你刚才说，这子弹打在哪儿？」

「打在脖颈肉厚的地方，位置在耳后偏下不浅的一段，正好在衣领上方。出血不多。我想它必定打中了脑底。」

「没碰着衣领，也没碰着头发？」

「没有。」警督答道。

「警督，我想我们准能把凶手捉拿归案——先生，我敢说，凭我将从这颗子弹上在实验室里取得的证据，就能给他定罪。」

===Original===

“That’s pretty much like a storybook,” drawled the inspector incredulously, shaking his head.

“Perhaps,” smiled Kennedy. “But there will still be plenty of work for the police to do, too. I’ve only got a clue to the murderer. It will take the whole organisation to follow it up, believe me. Now, Inspector, can you spare the time to go down to Parker’s office and take me over the ground? No doubt we can develop something else there.”

“Sure,” answered O’Connor, and within five minutes we were hurrying down town in one of the department automobiles.

We found the office under guard of one of the Central Office men, while in the outside office Parker’s confidential clerk and a few assistants were still at work in a subdued and awed manner. Men were working in many other Wall Street offices that night during the panic, but in none was there more reason for it than here. Later I learned that it was the quiet tenacity of this confidential clerk that saved even as much of Parker’s estate as was saved for his widow﻿—little enough it was, too. What he saved for the clients of the firm no one will ever know. Somehow or other I liked John Downey, the clerk, from the moment I was introduced to him. He seemed to me, at least, to be the typical confidential clerk who would carry a secret worth millions and keep it.

The officer in charge touched his hat to the inspector, and Downey hastened to put himself at our service. It was plain that the murder had completely mystified him, and that he was as anxious as we were to get at the bottom of it.

===Chinese===

「这未免太像传奇小说了。」警督将信将疑，拖着调子摇头道。

「也许吧。」肯尼迪微微一笑。「不过，警察照样有的是活儿要干。我手里不过是一条指向凶手的线索。相信我，要顺着它追查下去，非动用整个组织不可。这样吧，警督，您能不能抽空陪我跑一趟帕克的办公室，带我到现场走一遍？没准儿我们还能在那里摸出点别的名堂。」

「没问题。」奥康纳应道。不出五分钟，我们就坐上局里的汽车，向城里疾驰而去。

办公室由中央办公厅的一名探员看守着；外面的写字间里，帕克的机要文书和几名助手还在压着嗓子、战战兢兢地干活。恐慌期间，那一晚华尔街许多别的商行也都有人加班，但没有一家比这里更有加班的理由。后来我才听说，多亏这位机要文书沉着的坚韧，帕克的遗产才算为他的遗孀保住了那么一点——尽管少得可怜。他为商行的客户又保住了多少，则永远不会有人知道。不知怎么，从被引见的那一刻起，我就喜欢上了这位文书——约翰·唐尼（John Downey）。至少在我看来，他正是那种典型的机要文书：怀里揣着价值百万的秘密，也守得住。

负责看守的警员向警督抬手碰了碰帽檐，唐尼连忙过来听候差遣。看得出，这桩凶案把他完全弄懵了，他同我们一样急切地想刨到根底。

===Original===

“Mr. Downey,” began Kennedy, “I understand you were present when this sad event took place.”

“Yes, sir, sitting right here at the directors’ table,” he replied, taking a chair, “like this.”

“Now can you recollect just how Mr. Parker acted when he was shot? Could you﻿—er﻿—could you take his place and show us just how it happened?”

“Yes, sir,” said Downey. “He was sitting here at the head of the table. Mr. Bruce, who is the C.O. of the firm, had been sitting here at his right; I was at the left. The inspector has a list of all the others present. That door to the right was open, and Mrs. Parker and some other ladies were in the room﻿—”

“Mrs. Parker?” broke in Kennedy.

“Yes: Like a good many brokerage firms we have a ladies’ room. Many ladies are among our clients. We make a point of catering to them. At that time I recollect the door was open﻿—all the doors were open. It was not a secret meeting. Mr. Bruce had just gone into the ladies’ department; I think to ask some of them to stand by the firm﻿—he was an artist at smoothing over the fears of customers, particularly women. Just before he went in I had seen the ladies go in a group toward the far end of the room﻿—to look down at the line of depositors on the street, which reached around the corner from one of the trust companies, I thought. I was making a note of an order to send into the outside office there on the left, and had just pushed this button here under the table to call a boy to carry it. Mr. Parker had just received a letter by special delivery, and seemed considerably puzzled over it. No, I don’t know what it was about. Of a sudden I saw him start in his chair, rise up unsteadily, clap his hand on the back of his head, stagger across the floor﻿—like this﻿—and fall here.”

===Chinese===

「唐尼先生，」肯尼迪开口道，「据我所知，这件不幸的事发生时，您就在现场。」

「是的，先生，就坐在董事会议桌这边。」他答着，拉过一把椅子，「像这样。」

「那么，您能确切回忆起帕克先生中枪时的举止吗？您能不能——呃——能不能坐到他的位置上，把当时的情形给我们演示一遍？」

「好的，先生。」唐尼说。「他当时坐在桌子这头的主位上。公司的 C.O.——布鲁斯（Bruce）先生——原先坐在他右手边；我在左手。在座其余各位的名单，警督手里有一份。右边那扇门开着，帕克夫人（Mrs. Parker）和另外几位女士就在那间屋里——」

「帕克夫人？」肯尼迪插话道。

「是的：同许多经纪行一样，我们设有一间女宾室（ladies' room）。我们的客户里有不少女士，我们也特意把她们招待得周到妥帖。我记得那时门开着——所有的门都开着，那不是一次秘密会议。布鲁斯先生刚进女宾部（ladies' department）去，我想是去请她们当中几位支持商行——安抚顾客的恐慌，尤其是女顾客的恐慌，他是行家里手。就在他进去之前，我看见女士们成群结队走向屋子那头——去看楼下街上排队取款的储户，我记得那队伍从一家信托公司绕过街角排了过来。我正在写一张单子，要送到左边那边的外间办公室去，刚按过桌子底下这个按钮，叫个当差的孩子来跑腿。帕克先生刚收到一封专递的信（special delivery），看神情对它颇为困惑。不，信里写的是什么，我不知道。突然间，我看见他在椅子里猛地一惊，晃晃悠悠地站起来，一只手拍到后脑上，踉踉跄跄地横过地板——像这样——就倒在了这里。」

===Original===

“Then what happened?”

“Why, I rushed to pick him up. Everything was confusion. I recall someone behind me saying, ‘Here, boy, take all these papers off the table and carry them into my office before they get lost in the excitement.’ I think it was Bruce’s voice. The next moment I heard someone say, ‘Stand back, Mrs. Parker has fainted.’ But I didn’t pay much attention, for I was calling to someone not to get a doctor over the telephone, but to go down to the fifth floor where one has an office. I made Mr. Parker as comfortable as I could. There wasn’t much I could do. He seemed to want to say something to me, but he couldn’t talk. He was paralysed, at least his throat was. But I did manage to make out finally what sounded to me like, ‘Tell her I don’t believe the scandal, I don’t believe it.’ But before he could say whom to tell he had again become unconscious, and by the time the doctor arrived he was dead. I guess you know everything else as well as I do.”

“You didn’t hear the shot fired from any particular direction?” asked Kennedy.

“No, sir.”

“Well, where do you think it came from?”

“That’s what puzzles me, sir. The only thing I can figure out is that it was fired from the outside office﻿—perhaps by some customer who had lost money and sought revenge. But no one out there heard it either, any more than they did in the directors’ room or the ladies’ department.”

===Chinese===

「接下来呢？」

「唉，我冲过去扶他。四下里乱成一团。我记得背后有人说：『喂，孩子，把桌上的文件统统拿走，送到我办公室去，别在忙乱里弄丢了。』我想那是布鲁斯的声音。紧跟着，又听有人说：『让开让开，帕克夫人晕过去了。』可我没大留心，因为我正朝一个人喊：别打电话请医生，干脆下五楼去，那里有位医生开业。我尽力把帕克先生安放得舒服些，别的什么也做不了。他像有话要对我说，却说不出来。他瘫痪了——至少喉咙是瘫的。不过最后我总算勉强听出，他像是在说：『告诉她，我不信那些流言，我不信。』可他还没来得及说要告诉谁，就又昏了过去；等医生赶到，人已经死了。其余的事，我估摸你们知道的和我一样多。」

「您没有听见枪声是从哪个方向来的吗？」肯尼迪问。

「没有，先生。」

「那么，您觉得它是从哪儿打来的？」

「这正是叫我想不通的地方，先生。我唯一想得出的，是枪从外间办公室打的——兴许是哪个亏了本、来寻仇的客户。可那边也没有人听见枪响，同董事会议室和女宾部里的人一样。」

===Original===

“About that message,” asked Kennedy, ignoring what to me seemed to be the most important feature of the case, the mystery of the silent bullet. “Didn’t you see it after all was over?”

“No, sir; in fact I had forgotten about it till this moment when you asked me to reconstruct the circumstances exactly. No, sir, I don’t know a thing about it. I can’t say it impressed itself on my mind at the time, either.”

“What did Mrs. Parker do when she came to?”

“Oh, she cried as I have never seen a woman cry before. He was dead by that time, of course.”

“Bruce and I saw her down in the elevator to her car. In fact, the doctor, who had arrived; said that the sooner she was taken home the better she would be. She was quite hysterical.”

“Did she say anything that you remember?”

===Chinese===

「说说那封信，」肯尼迪问道，全不理会在我看来本案最要紧的关节——无声子弹之谜。「一切过去之后，您没有再见过它吗？」

「没有，先生；说实话，直到您刚才要我原原本本复述情形，我才又想起它来。没有，先生，我对它一无所知。当时它也没在我脑子里留下什么印象。」

「帕克夫人苏醒过来以后，做了些什么？」

「噢，她哭哇，我从没见过哪个女人那样哭。到那时他当然已经死了。

「布鲁斯和我送她下楼，上了她的汽车。其实，那位赶到的医生说，越早送她回家越好。她歇斯底里得不轻。」

「您记不记得她说过什么话？」

===Original===

Downey hesitated.

“Out with it Downey,” said the inspector. “What did she say as she was going down in the elevator?”

“Nothing.”

“Tell us. I’ll arrest you if you don’t.”

“Nothing about the murder, on my honour,” protested Downey.

Kennedy leaned over suddenly and shot a remark at him, “Then it was about the note.”

Downey was surprised, but not quickly enough. Still he seemed to be considering something, and in a moment he said:

“I don’t know what it was about, but I feel it is my duty, after all, to tell you. I heard her say, ‘I wonder if he knew.’ ”

===Chinese===

唐尼犹豫了。

「有话直说，唐尼。」警督道。「她乘电梯下楼的时候说了什么？」

「没什么。」

「说出来。不说我就逮捕你。」

「跟谋杀不相干的话，我以名誉起誓一句也没有。」唐尼抗辩道。

肯尼迪忽然俯过身去，劈头给他一句：「那就是关于那封信的了。」

唐尼吃了一惊——可惜慢了半拍。他似乎还在掂量什么，片刻之后便说：

「我不知道那话是什么意思，可我到底觉得，有责任告诉你们。我听见她说：『不知道他知道了没有。』」

===Original===

“Nothing else?”

“Nothing else.”

“What happened after you came back?”

“We entered the ladies’ department. No one was there. A woman’s automobile-coat was thrown over a chair in a heap. Mr. Bruce picked it up. ‘It’s Mrs. Parker’s,’ he said. He wrapped it up hastily, and rang for a messenger.”

“Where did he send it?”

“To Mrs. Parker, I suppose. I didn’t hear the address.”

===Chinese===

「再没有别的了？」

「没有了。」

「您回来之后又发生了什么？」

「我们走进女宾部。里面空无一人。一件女式汽车外套（automobile-coat）胡乱堆在一把椅子上。布鲁斯先生把它拾了起来。『这是帕克夫人的。』他说。他匆匆把它裹好，按铃叫了名信差。」

「他把它送到哪儿去了？」

「我想是送给帕克夫人了吧。地址我没听清。」

===Original===

We next went over the whole suite of offices, conducted by Mr. Downey. I noted how carefully Kennedy looked into the directors’ room through the open door from the ladies’ department. He stood at such an angle that had he been the assassin he could scarcely have been seen except by those sitting immediately next Mr. Parker at the directors’ table. The street windows were directly in front of him, and back of him was the chair on which the motorcoat had been found.

In Parker’s own office we spent some time, as well as in Bruce’s. Kennedy made a search for the note, but finding nothing in either office, turned out the contents of Bruce’s scrap-basket. There didn’t seem to be anything in it to interest him, however, even after he had pieced several torn bits of scraps together with much difficulty, and he was about to turn the papers back again, when he noticed something sticking to the side of the basket. It looked like a mass of wet paper, and that was precisely what it was.

“That’s queer,” said Kennedy, picking it loose. Then he wrapped it up carefully and put it in his pocket. “Inspector, can you lend me one of your men for a couple of days?” he asked, as we were preparing to leave. “I shall want to send him out of town tonight, and shall probably need his services when he gets back.”

“Very well. Riley will be just the fellow. We’ll go back to headquarters, and I’ll put him under your orders.”

===Chinese===

随后，由唐尼先生引领，我们把整套办公室巡查了一遍。我留意到，肯尼迪从女宾部敞开的门口朝董事会议室里察看得何等仔细。他站的角度恰到好处：倘若他就是刺客，那么除了紧挨着帕克先生坐在董事会议桌旁的人，几乎谁也瞧不见他。临街的窗子正对着他的前方，他背后则是那把搭过汽车外套的椅子。

帕克自己的办公室，同布鲁斯的办公室一样，我们都耽搁了一阵。肯尼迪搜寻那封信，两间办公室都一无所获，他便把布鲁斯字纸篓里的东西倒了出来。里面似乎没什么让他上心的东西——连几片撕碎的纸屑，他费了好大劲拼对起来，也瞧不出名堂。他正要把纸放回去，忽然注意到有什么东西粘在纸篓一侧。那看上去像一团湿纸——事实上也正是湿纸。

「这就怪了。」肯尼迪说着，把它揭了下来。随后他仔细包好，放进口袋。「警督，能借我您手下一个人用两天吗？」我们正准备离开时，他问道。「今晚我要派他出城一趟，等他回来，多半还用得着他。」

「行。赖利（Riley）正合适。咱们回总局去，我把他交给您调遣。」

===Original===

It was not until late in the following day that I saw Kennedy again. It had been a busy day at the *Star*. We had gone to work that morning expecting to see the very financial heavens fall. But just about five minutes to ten, before the Stock Exchange opened, the news came in over the wire from our financial man on Broad Street: “ ‘The System’ has forced James Bruce, partner of Kerr Parker, the dead banker; to sell his railroad, steamship, and rubber holdings to it. On this condition it promises unlimited support to the market.”

“Forced!” muttered the managing editor, as he waited on the office phone to get the composing-room, so as to hurry up the few lines in red ink on the first page and beat our rivals on the streets with the first extras. “Why, he’s been working to bring that about for the past two weeks. What that System doesn’t control isn’t worth having﻿—it edits the news before our men get it, and as for grist for the divorce courts, and tragedies, well﻿—Hello, Jenkins, yes, a special extra. Change the big heads﻿—copy is on the way up﻿—rush it.”

“So you think this Parker case is a mess?” I asked.

“I know it. That’s a pretty swift bunch of females that have been speculating at Kerr Parker & Co.’s. I understand there’s one Titian-haired young lady﻿—who, by the way, has at least one husband who hasn’t yet been divorced﻿—who is a sort of ringleader, though she rarely goes personally to her brokers’ offices. She’s one of those uptown plungers, and the story is that she has a whole string of scalps of alleged Sunday-school superintendents at her belt. She can make Bruce do pretty nearly anything, they say. He’s the latest conquest. I got the story on pretty good authority, but until I verified the names, dates, and places, of course I wouldn’t dare print a line of it. The story goes that her husband is a hanger-on of the System, and that she’s been working in their interest, too. That was why he was so complacent over the whole affair. They put her up to capturing Bruce, and after she had acquired an influence over him they worked it so that she made him make love to Mrs. Parker. It’s a long story, but that isn’t all of it. The point was, you see, that by this devious route they hoped to worm out of Mrs. Parker some inside information about Parker’s rubber schemes, which he hadn’t divulged even to his partners in business. It was a deep and carefully planned plot, and some of the conspirators were pretty deeply in the mire, I guess. I wish I’d had all the facts about who this red-haired female Machiavelli was﻿—what a piece of muckraking it would have made! Oh, here comes the rest of the news story over the wire. By Jove, it is said on good authority that Bruce will be taken in as one of the board of directors. What do you think of that?”

===Chinese===

直到第二天很晚，我才再见到肯尼迪。那天《明星报》里忙得团团转。早晨上班时，我们都等着看金融界的青天塌下来。可就在十点差五分左右、证券交易所开盘之前，我们驻宽街（Broad Street）的金融记者从电报线上传来了消息：「『那个集团』已迫使已故银行家克尔·帕克的合伙人詹姆斯·布鲁斯（James Bruce），将他的铁路、轮船和橡胶持股出售给它；作为条件，它许诺对市场给予无限支持。」

「迫使！」主编对着办公室电话咕哝，他正等着接通排字房，好催印头版那几行红字，抢在同行前头把第一份号外撒上市面。「哼，为促成这个局面，他都忙了两个礼拜了。那个集团控制不到的东西，根本不配有——新闻还没送到我们的人手里，就先经它删改过了；至于说给离婚法庭和悲剧新闻添的料嘛，嘿——喂，詹金斯，对，出一期特别号外。换大字标题——稿子这就送上去——加急。」

「这么说，你觉得这桩帕克案是笔烂账？」我问。

「岂止是烂账，我心里有数。在克尔·帕克公司投机的，是一帮手脚麻利的女人。我听说其中有位提香色头发（Titian-haired）的年轻小姐——顺带一提，她至少有一位至今还没离掉的丈夫——算是个领头人物，尽管她极少亲自光顾经纪人的办公室。她是上城区那种一掷千金的女赌客，据说腰带上挂着一整串据称是主日学校督学的『头皮』。人们说，她能让布鲁斯干几乎任何事情——他不过是她最新的战利品。这故事我打听来的来源相当可靠，不过姓名、日期、地点没核实之前，我自然一个字也不敢见报。据说她丈夫是那个集团的一名食客，她自己也在为他们效力——正因为如此，她那位丈夫对整件事才那么泰然。是他们撺掇她去拿住布鲁斯；等她对他有了左右之力，他们又从中运作，让她驱使布鲁斯去追求帕克夫人。说来话长，这还不是全部。你瞧，要害在于：他们想借这条迂回的路子，从帕克夫人嘴里套出帕克橡胶计划的内幕——那些底细，他连生意上的合伙人都没吐露过。这是个处心积虑、谋划周密的圈套，我猜有些同谋陷得相当深。我真想把这位红发女马基雅维利（Machiavelli）的来龙去脉查个底朝天——那可是一篇多么精彩的揭黑报道（muckraking）！哦，这条新闻的后半段又从电报线上过来了。天哪，据可靠消息，布鲁斯将被吸收进董事会。你作何感想？」

===Original===

So that was how the wind lay﻿—Bruce making love to Mrs. Parker and she presumably betraying her husband’s secrets. I thought I saw it all: the note from somebody exposing the scheme, Parker’s incredulity, Bruce sitting by him and catching sight of the note, his hurrying out into the ladies’ department, and then the shot. But who fired it? After all, I had only picked up another clue.

Kennedy was not at the apartment at dinner, and an inquiry at the laboratory was fruitless also. So I sat down to fidget for a while. Pretty soon the buzzer on the door sounded, and I opened it to find a messenger-boy with a large brown paper parcel.

“Is Mr. Bruce here?” he asked.

“Why, no, he doesn’t﻿—” then I checked myself and added: “He will be here presently. You can leave the bundle.”

“Well, this is the parcel he telephoned for. His valet told me to tell him that they had a hard time to find it, but he guesses it’s all right. The charges are forty cents. Sign here.”

I signed the book, feeling like a thief, and the boy departed. What it all meant I could not guess.

===Chinese===

原来风是这么个吹法——布鲁斯追求着帕克夫人，而她多半正在出卖丈夫的秘密。我自以为把一切都看明白了：某人写来揭发这个圈套的信，帕克的不肯相信，坐在一旁的布鲁斯瞥见了那封信，他匆匆躲进女宾部，然后便是那一枪。可开枪的是谁呢？说到底，我不过又拾到一条线索罢了。

吃晚饭时肯尼迪不在公寓，到实验室去问也毫无结果。我只好坐下来干着急。不多时，门上蜂鸣器响了，我开门一看，是个送信少年（messenger-boy），抱着一只棕色大纸包。

「布鲁斯先生在吗？」他问。

「咦，不，他不住——」我话到嘴边又咽了回去，改口道：「他一会儿就回来。包裹可以留下。」

「是这样，这就是他打电话要的包裹。他的贴身男仆要我对他说，他们费了好大劲才找到这东西，不过他想不会有错。运费四毛钱。在这儿签个字。」

我在签收簿上签了字，自觉像个贼。少年走了。这一切究竟是什么意思，我猜不透。

===Original===

Just then I heard a key in the lock, and Kennedy came in.

“Is your name Bruce?” I asked.

“Why?” he replied eagerly. “Has anything come?”

I pointed to the package. Kennedy made a dive for it and unwrapped it. It was a woman’s pongee automobile-coat. He held it up to the light. The pocket on the right-hand side was scorched and burned, and a hole was torn clean through it. I gasped when the full significance of it dawned on me.

“How did you get it?” I exclaimed at last in surprise.

“That’s where organisation comes in,” said Kennedy. “The police at my request went over every messenger call from Parker’s office that afternoon, and traced every one of them up. At last they found one that led to Bruce’s apartment. None of them led to Mrs. Parker’s home. The rest were all business calls and satisfactorily accounted for. I reasoned that this was the one that involved the disappearance of the automobile-coat. It was a chance worth taking, so I got Downey to call up Bruce’s valet. The valet of course recognised Downey’s voice and suspected nothing. Downey assumed to know all about the coat in the package received yesterday. He asked to have it sent up here. I see the scheme worked.”

===Chinese===

正在这时，我听见锁眼里钥匙一响，肯尼迪回来了。

「你叫布鲁斯吗？」我问。

「怎么？」他急切地应道。「有什么东西到了？」

我指了指包裹。肯尼迪一个箭步扑上去，把它拆开。那是一件女式茧绸（pongee）汽车外套。他对着灯光举起来：右侧的口袋烧得焦黑，被干干净净地穿了一个洞。等我明白过来这其中的全部含义，不由得倒抽一口冷气。

「这是怎么弄到手的？」我终于惊讶地喊出声来。

「这正是组织显身手的地方。」肯尼迪说。「应我的请求，警方把那天下午帕克办公室打出的每一次信差传唤电话都核了一遍，并逐一追根溯源。最后他们发现其中一通打到布鲁斯的公寓；没有一通通向帕克夫人的家，其余全是业务传唤，笔笔有着落。我推断，与那件汽车外套失踪有牵连的正是这一通。这个险值得冒，于是我请唐尼打电话给布鲁斯的男仆。男仆自然听出是唐尼的声音，毫无戒心。唐尼装作对前一天收到的那一包里的外套一清二楚，请对方把它送到这里来。我看，这计策奏效了。」

===Original===

“But, Kennedy, do you think she﻿—” I stopped, speechless, looking at the scorched coat.

“Nothing to say﻿—yet,” he replied laconically. “But if you could tell me anything about that note Parker received I’d thank you.”

I related what our managing editor had said that morning. Kennedy only raised his eyebrows a fraction of an inch.

“I had guessed something of that sort,” he said merely. “I’m glad to find it confirmed even by hearsay evidence. This red-haired young lady interests me. Not a very definite description, but better than nothing at all. I wonder who she is. Ah, well, what do you say to a stroll down the White Way before I go to my laboratory? I’d like a breath of air to relax my mind.”

We had got no further than the first theatre when Kennedy slapped me on the back. “By George, Jameson, she’s an actress, of course.”

===Chinese===

「可是，肯尼迪，难道你认为她——」我望着那件烧焦的外套，说不出话来。

「无可奉告——暂时。」他言简意赅地答道。「不过，你要是能告诉我些帕克收到的那封信的来历，我就要谢你了。」

我把那天早晨我们主编的话讲了一遍。肯尼迪听了，眉毛只抬起了一线。

「这一类情况，我也大致猜到了几分。」他只应了这一句。「我很高兴看到它得到印证，哪怕是传闻证据也好。这位红发小姐引起了我的兴趣。描述算不得确切，总比两手空空强。不知道她究竟是谁。啊，这样吧，趁我去实验室之前，到白光大道（White Way）上去散散步，如何？我想透口气，让脑子松快松快。」

我们刚走到第一家剧院门口，肯尼迪就拍了拍我的背。「老天爷，詹姆森，她当然是个演员！」

===Original===

“Who is? What’s the matter with you, Kennedy? Are you crazy?”

“The red-haired person﻿—she must be an actress. Don’t you remember the auburn-haired leading lady in the ‘Follies’﻿—the girl who sings that song about ‘Mary, Mary, quite contrary’? Her stage name, you know, is Phoebe La Neige. Well, if it’s she who is concerned in this case I don’t think she’ll be playing tonight. Let’s inquire at the box-office.”

She wasn’t playing, but just what it had to do with anything in particular I couldn’t see, and I said as much.

“Why, Walter, you’d never do as a detective. You lack intuition. Sometimes I think I haven’t quite enough of it, either. Why didn’t I think of that sooner? Don’t you know she is the wife of Adolphus Hesse, the most inveterate gambler in stocks in the System? Why, I had only to put two and two together and the whole thing flashed on me in an instant. Isn’t it a good hypothesis that she is the red-haired woman in the case, the tool of the System in which her husband is so heavily involved? I’ll have to add her to my list of suspects.”

“Why, you don’t think she did the shooting?” I asked, half hoping, I must admit, for an assenting nod from him.

“Well,” he answered dryly, “one shouldn’t let any preconceived hypothesis stand between him and the truth. I’ve made a guess at the whole thing already. It may or it may not be right. Anyhow she will fit into it. And if it’s not right, I’ve got to be prepared to make a new guess, that’s all.”

===Chinese===

「谁？你这是怎么了，肯尼迪？疯了不成？」

「那个红头发的女人——她必定是个演员。你记不记得《富丽秀》（Follies）里那位赤褐色头发的女主角——唱那支《玛丽·玛丽，真别扭》（Mary, Mary, quite contrary）的歌子的姑娘？她的艺名，你是知道的，叫菲比·拉·内日（Phoebe La Neige）。嘿，倘若同本案有牵连的正是她，我看她今晚是登不了台的。咱们到售票处问问去。」

她果然没在演出。可这件事到底同什么特别的名堂相干，我看不出来，便也照直说了。

「哎呀，沃尔特，你当侦探是万万不成的。你缺直觉。有时候，我觉得自己的直觉也不太够使。我怎么没早些想到？你不知道么，她就是阿道弗斯·赫斯（Adolphus Hesse）的妻子——那个集团里在股票上赌性最重、最不可救药的赌棍。嗐，我只消把两件事实一并，整桩事情刹那间就在我眼前豁然开朗。她就是本案里那个红发女人，是她丈夫深深陷足其中的那个集团的工具——这难道不是一个漂亮的假设么？我得把她添进我的嫌疑人名单了。」

「怎么，你不至于认为枪是她开的吧？」我问。我得承认，我半心半意地巴望他点头称是。

「唔，」他淡然答道，「谁也不该让先入为主的假设横在自己与真相之间。整件事情我已经做过一番猜测，或许对，或许不对。无论如何，她都安插得进去。倘若不对，我也得准备另猜一番，仅此而已。」

===Original===

When we reached the laboratory on our return, the inspector’s man Riley was there, waiting impatiently for Kennedy.

“What luck?” asked Kennedy.

“I’ve got a list of purchasers of that kind of revolver,” he said. “We have been to every sporting-goods and arms-store in the city which bought them from the factory, and I could lay my hands on pretty nearly every one of those weapons in twenty-four hours﻿—provided, of course, they haven’t been secreted or destroyed.”

“Pretty nearly all isn’t good enough,” said Kennedy. “It will have to be all, unless﻿—”

“*That* name is in the list,” whispered Riley hoarsely.

“Oh, then it’s all right,” answered Kennedy, brightening up. “Riley, I will say that you’re a wonder at using the organisation in ferreting out such things. There’s just one more thing I want you to do. I want a sample of the notepaper in the private desks of every one of these people.” He handed the policeman a list of his nine “suspects,” as he called them. It included nearly everyone mentioned in the case.

===Chinese===

我们回去走进实验室时，警督派来的赖利已经在那儿，正等肯尼迪，等得心焦。

「手气如何？」肯尼迪问。

「我弄到了一份那种左轮手枪买主的名单。」他说。「凡是直接从厂家进货的体育用品行和枪械行，全城我们都跑遍了。二十四小时之内，那些枪我差不多支支都拿得到手——当然喽，前提是它们没有被藏起来或者毁掉。」

「差不多支支都拿到，可不够。」肯尼迪说。「必须支支不落，除非——」

「名单上就有那个名字。」赖利哑着嗓子悄声道。

「哦，那就妥了。」肯尼迪答道，神色一振。「赖利，不得不说，调动组织去刨这类东西，你真是个奇才。还有最后一件事要你办：我要这几位每个人私用书桌里信纸的样品。」他递给这名警察一份名单——他自己称作九名「嫌疑人」的名单——本案里数得着的人，差不多全在上面。

===Original===

Riley studied it dubiously and scratched his chin thoughtfully. “That’s a hard one, Mr. Kennedy, sir. You see, it means getting into so many different houses and apartments. Now you don’t want to do it by means of a warrant, do you, sir? Of course not. Well, then, how can we get in?”

“You’re a pretty good-looking chap yourself, Riley,” said Kennedy. “I should think you could jolly a housemaid, if necessary. Anyhow, you can get the fellow on the beat to do it﻿—if he isn’t already to be found in the kitchen. Why, I see a dozen ways of getting the notepaper.”

“Oh, it’s me that’s the lady-killer, sir,” grinned Riley. “I’m a regular Blarney stone when I’m out on a job of that sort. Sure, I’ll have some of them for you in the morning.”

“Bring me what you get, the first thing in the morning, even if you’ve landed only a few samples,” said Kennedy, as Riley departed, straightening his tie and brushing his hat on his sleeve.

“And now, Walter, you too must excuse me tonight,” said Craig. “I’ve got a lot to do, and shan’t be up to our apartment till very late﻿—or early. But I feel sure I’ve got a stranglehold on this mystery. If I get those papers from Riley in good time tomorrow I shall invite you and several others to a grand demonstration here tomorrow night. Don’t forget. Keep the whole evening free. It will be a big story.”

===Chinese===

赖利将信将疑地端详着名单，若有所思地挠着下巴。「这可难办，肯尼迪先生，长官。您想，这就得进那么多户不同的宅子和公寓。您总不打算凭一张搜查令去办这事吧，长官？当然不。那么，咱们怎么进得去呢？」

「赖利，你自己就是个体面漂亮的小伙子。」肯尼迪说。「依我看，事急的时候，你尽可以去哄哄人家的女仆。再不济，找辖区上的巡警代办也成——只要他没先叫人堵在厨房里。嗐，弄几张信纸么，我看得出一打法子。」

「嗬，迷死女人的角色正是区区在下，长官。」赖利咧嘴一乐。「出来办这种差事的时候，我就是块活的巧言石（Blarney stone）。放心，明儿一早我就能给您送几份来。」

「不论弄到多少，哪怕只有寥寥几份，明儿一早头一件事就给我送来。」肯尼迪说。赖利整了整领带，拿袖子掸了掸帽子，出门去了。

「好了，沃尔特，今晚你也得体谅我。」克雷格说。「我有一大堆事要做，不到夜深——不如说不到天亮——回不了公寓。不过我有十足把握，这个谜已经叫我扼住咽喉了。明天只要赖利的纸送来得及时，明晚我就邀请你和另外几位，到这里来看一场盛大的演示。别忘了，把整个晚上空出来。那可是条大新闻。」

===Original===

Kennedy’s laboratory was brightly lighted when I arrived early the next evening. One by one his “guests” dropped in. It was evident that they had little liking for the visit, but the coroner had sent out the “invitations,” and they had nothing to do but accept. Each one was politely welcomed by the professor and assigned a seat, much as he would have done with a group of students. The inspector and the coroner sat back a little. Mrs. Parker, Mr. Downey, Mr. Bruce, myself, and Miss La Neige sat in that order in the very narrow and uncomfortable little armchairs used by the students during lectures.

At last Kennedy was ready to begin. He took his position behind the long, flat-topped table which he used for his demonstrations before his classes. “I realise, ladies and gentlemen,” he began formally, “that I am about to do a very unusual thing; but, as you all know, the police and the coroner have been completely baffled by this terrible mystery and have requested me to attempt to clear up at least certain points in it. I will begin what I have to say by remarking that the tracing out of a crime like this differs in nothing, except as regards the subject-matter, from the search for a scientific truth. The forcing of man’s secrets is like the forcing of nature’s secrets. Both are pieces of detective work. The methods employed in the detection of crime are, or rather should be, like the methods employed in the process of discovering scientific truth. In a crime of this sort, two kinds of evidence need to be secured. Circumstantial evidence must first be marshalled, and then a motive must be found. I have been gathering facts. But to omit motives and rest contented with mere facts would be inconclusive. It would never convince anybody or convict anybody. In other words, circumstantial evidence must first lead to a suspect, and then this suspect must prove equal to accounting for the facts. It is my hope that each of you may contribute something that will be of service in arriving at the truth of this unfortunate incident.”

The tension was not relieved even when Kennedy stopped speaking and began to fuss with a little upright target which he set up at one end of his table. We seemed to be seated over a powder magazine which threatened to explode at any moment. I, at least, felt the tension so greatly that it was only after he had started speaking again, that I noticed that the target was composed of a thick layer of some putty-like material.

===Chinese===

第二天傍晚我早早赶到时，肯尼迪的实验室里灯火通明。他的「来宾」们陆陆续续到齐。看得出，他们对这一趟都没什么兴致，可「请柬」是验尸官发出的，他们除了接受别无选择。教授客客气气地迎接每一位，安排座位，同对待一群学生没什么两样。警督和验尸官稍稍坐得靠后。帕克夫人、唐尼先生、布鲁斯先生、我，还有拉·内日小姐，依次坐在学生们听课时用的那种又窄又不舒服的小扶手椅上。

「女士们，先生们，」他一本正经地开了场，「我明白，我即将做的是一件极不寻常的事；但诸位都知道，警方和验尸官已被这桩可怕的疑案弄得一筹莫展，故而请我尝试至少澄清其中的若干环节。我想先说明一点：像这样一桩罪案的追索，除了题材不同，与寻求一项科学真理毫无二致。逼出人类的秘密，就如同逼出自然的秘密，两者都是侦探工作。侦破犯罪所用的方法，或者说应当用的方法，就是发现科学真理的过程中所用的方法。在这类罪案中，需要取得的证据有两种：首先必须把间接证据一一排布妥当，然后必须找到动机。我一直在收集事实。可是撇开动机、满足于纯粹的事实，是得不出结论的——那样既说服不了任何人，也定不了任何人的罪。换句话说，间接证据必须先指向一名嫌疑人，而这名嫌疑人随后又必须能对全部事实作出交代。我希望，诸位每一位都能贡献一点什么，帮助我们查明这桩不幸事件的真相。」

即使肯尼迪住了口，转而去摆弄一座小立靶——他把它支在桌子一头——紧张的气氛也丝毫未减。我们仿佛坐在一座随时会炸的火药库上。至少我紧张得很，直到他重新开口之后，才发现那靶子是用厚厚一层油灰似的材料做成的。

===Original===

Holding a thirty-two-calibre pistol in his right hand and aiming it at the target, Kennedy picked up a large piece of coarse homespun from the table and held it loosely over the muzzle of the gun. Then he fired. The bullet tore through the cloth, sped through the air, and buried itself in the target. With a knife he pried it out.

“I doubt if even the inspector himself could have told us that when an ordinary leaden bullet is shot through a woven fabric the weave of that fabric is in the majority of cases impressed on the bullet, sometimes clearly, sometimes faintly.”

Here Kennedy took up a piece of fine batiste and fired another bullet through it.

“Every leaden bullet, as I have said, which has struck such a fabric bears an impression of the threads which is recognisable even when the bullet has penetrated deeply into the body. It is only obliterated partially or entirely when the bullet has been flattened by striking a bone or other hard object. Even then, as in this case, if only a part of the bullet is flattened the remainder may still show the marks of the fabric. A heavy warp, say of cotton velvet or, as I have here, homespun, will be imprinted well on the bullet, but even a fine batiste, containing one hundred threads to the inch, will show marks. Even layers of goods such as a coat, shirt, and undershirt may each leave their marks, but that does not concern us in this case. Now I have here a piece of pongee silk, cut from a woman’s automobile-coat. I discharge the bullet through it﻿—so. I compare the bullet now with the others and with the one probed from the neck of Mr. Parker. I find that the marks on that fatal bullet correspond precisely with those on the bullet fired through the pongee coat.”

===Chinese===

肯尼迪右手握着一支点三二口径的手枪，瞄着靶子，又从桌上拿起一大块粗糙的手织粗呢（homespun），松松地罩在枪口上。随即击发。子弹撕裂布面，破空而过，扎进靶子里。他用刀把它撬了出来。

「恐怕连警督自己也未必说得上来：一枚普通的铅弹射穿织物时，那织物的织纹在多数情况下会印在弹头上，有时清晰，有时浅淡。」

说着，肯尼迪拿起一块细薄的巴里纱（batiste），又射出一颗子弹穿过它。

「正如我方才所说，凡是打上过这类织物的铅弹，都带着纱线的印痕，即便子弹已深深钻进人体，那印痕依然辨认得出。只有当子弹撞上骨头或其他坚硬物体、本身被撞扁时，印痕才会局部或全部消失。即便如此，正如本案这颗，只要子弹只扁掉了一部分，余下的部分仍可能显出织物的痕迹。织纹厚重的织物，比如棉天鹅绒，或是我这块手织粗呢，会在弹头上印得清清楚楚；但纵然是每英寸一百根纱的细巴里纱，也会留下痕迹。就连外衣、衬衫、汗衫这样一层层衣物，也可能各自留下印记——不过这与本案无关。我这里有一块茧绸，是从一件女式汽车外套上剪下来的。我让子弹穿过它——如此。现在，我把这颗子弹同其余几颗、同从帕克先生颈中探出的那颗作比对。我发现，那颗致命子弹上的痕迹，与穿过茧绸外套射出的子弹上的痕迹精确吻合。」

===Original===

Startling as was this revelation, Kennedy paused only an instant before the next.

“Now I have another demonstration. A certain note figures in this case. Mr. Parker was reading it, or perhaps rereading it, at the time he was shot. I have not been able to obtain that note﻿—at least not in a form such as I could use in discovering what were its contents. But in a certain wastebasket I found a mass of wet and pulp-like paper. It had been cut up, macerated, perhaps chewed; perhaps it had been also soaked with water. There was a washbasin with running water in this room. The ink had run, and of course was illegible. The thing was so unusual that I at once assumed that this was the remains of the note in question. Under ordinary circumstances it would be utterly valueless as a clue to anything. But today science is not ready to let anything pass as valueless.

“I found on microscopic examination that it was an uncommon linen bond paper, and I have taken a large number of microphotographs of the fibres in it. They are all similar. I have here also about a hundred microphotographs of the fibres in other kinds of paper, many of them bonds. These I have accumulated from time to time in my study of the subject. None of them, as you can see, shows fibres resembling this one in question, so we may conclude that it is of uncommon quality. Through an agent of the police I have secured samples of the notepaper of everyone who could be concerned, as far as I could see, with this case. Here are the photographs of the fibres of these various notepapers, and among them all is just one that corresponds to the fibres in the wet mass of paper I discovered in the scrap-basket. Now lest anyone should question the accuracy of this method I might cite a case where a man had been arrested in Germany charged with stealing a government bond. He was not searched till later. There was no evidence save that after the arrest a large number of spitballs were found around the courtyard under his cell window. This method of comparing the fibres with those of the regular government paper was used, and by it the man was convicted of stealing the bond. I think it is almost unnecessary to add that in the present case we know precisely who﻿—”

At this point the tension was so great that it snapped. Miss La Neige, who was sitting beside me, had been leaning forward involuntarily. Almost as if the words were wrung from her she whispered hoarsely: “They put me up to doing it; I didn’t want to. But the affair had gone too far. I couldn’t see him lost before my very eyes. I didn’t want her to get him. The quickest way out was to tell the whole story to Mr. Parker and stop it. It was the only way I could think of to stop this thing between another man’s wife and the man I loved better than my own husband. God knows, Professor Kennedy, that was all﻿—”

===Chinese===

这一揭石破天惊，肯尼迪却只顿了一瞬，便转入下一项。

「现在做另一项演示。本案里牵涉着一封信。帕克先生中枪时，正在读它，或者正在重读。我一直没能弄到那封信——至少没能弄到一种可用来查明其内容的形态。但在某一只字纸篓里，我发现了一团湿漉漉、烂浆似的纸。它被撕碎过、泡软过，兴许还嚼过；也可能又用水浸过——那间屋里有一只带自来水的洗脸盆。墨水已经洇散，自然无法辨认。这东西太不寻常了，我当即断定：它便是那封信的残骸。换在寻常情形下，它作为任何线索都一文不值。可是今天，科学已不肯把任何东西当作无用之物轻易放过。

「在显微镜下检验，我发现那是一种不常见的亚麻证券纸（linen bond paper）。我对它的纤维拍了大量显微照片，全部彼此一致。我这里还有约莫一百张别的纸种的纤维显微照片，其中不少也是证券纸，是我研究这个课题时陆续积攒下的。诸位可以看到，没有一张的纤维同这团纸的相像，因此我们可以断定：这是一种品质罕见的纸。通过警方的一名代办，凡依我之见可能与本案有牵连的人，其信纸样品我均已到手。这里便是那些信纸纤维的照片，而其中恰有一种，与我在字纸篓里发现的那团湿纸的纤维互相吻合。为免有人质疑这种方法的准确性，我不妨援引一个案子：德国有个人因盗窃政府债券被捕，被捕时未及搜身。当时别无证据，只因逮捕之后，他囚室窗下的院子里发现了大量嚼湿的纸团（spitball）。当时用的正是这种把纤维同正规政府纸张纤维比对的方法，凭它，那人被定了盗窃债券的罪。我想，几乎无需赘言：在眼下这桩案子里，我们已确切地知道是谁——」

到了这一步，紧绷的空气终于绷断了弦。坐在我旁边的拉·内日小姐不由自主地一直前倾着身子。那句话仿佛是从她身上硬拧出来的，她声音沙哑地低语道：「是他们撺掇我干的；我本来不愿意。可事情已经走得太远。我不能眼睁睁看着他毁掉。我不要她得到他。最快的了局，是把整件事向帕克先生和盘托出，好叫它停止。要斩断另一个男人的妻子同我爱他胜过爱自己丈夫的那个男人之间的这桩事，我想得出的唯有这一个法子。老天知道，肯尼迪教授，我要说的就这么多——」

===Original===

“Calm yourself, madame,” interrupted Kennedy soothingly. “Calm yourself. What’s done is done. The truth must come out. Be calm. Now,” he continued, after the first storm of remorse had spent itself and we were all outwardly composed again, “we have said nothing whatever of the most mysterious feature of the case, the firing of the shot. The murderer could have thrust the weapon into the pocket or the folds of this coat”﻿—here he drew forth the automobile coat and held it aloft, displaying the bullet hole﻿—“and he or she (I will not say which) could have discharged the pistol unseen. By removing and secreting the weapon afterward one very important piece of evidence would be suppressed. This person could have used such a cartridge as I have here, made with smokeless powder, and the coat would have concealed the flash of the shot very effectively. There would have been no smoke. But neither this coat nor even a heavy blanket would have deadened the report of the shot.

“What are we to think of that? Only one thing. I have often wondered why the thing wasn’t done before. In fact I have been waiting for it to occur. There is an invention that makes it almost possible to strike a man down with impunity in broad daylight in any place where there is sufficient noise to cover up a click, a slight *Pouf!* and the whir of the bullet in the air.

“I refer to this little device of a Hartford inventor. I place it over the muzzle of the thirty-two-calibre revolver I have so far been using﻿—so. Now, Mr. Jameson, if you will sit at that typewriter over there and write﻿—anything so long as you keep the keys clicking. The inspector will start that imitation stock-ticker in the corner. Now we are ready. I cover the pistol with a cloth. I defy anyone in this room to tell me the exact moment when I discharged the pistol. I could have shot any of you, and an outsider not in the secret would never have thought that I was the culprit. To a certain extent I have reproduced the conditions under which this shooting occurred.

“At once on being sure of this feature of the case I despatched a man to Hartford to see this inventor. The man obtained from him a complete list of all the dealers in New York to whom such devices had been sold. The man also traced every sale of those dealers. He did not actually obtain the weapon, but if he is working on schedule-time according to agreement he is at this moment armed with a search-warrant and is ransacking every possible place where the person suspected of this crime could have concealed his weapon. For, one of the persons intimately connected with this case purchased not long ago a silencer for a thirty-two-calibre revolver, and I presume that that person carried the gun and the silencer at the time of the murder of Kerr Parker.”

===Chinese===

「定一定神，夫人。」肯尼迪宽慰地打断她。「定一定神。做过的事收不回。真相必须水落石出。定神吧。」待最初那阵悔恨的风暴发作过去、我们大家表面上都恢复了平静，他才接着说：「本案最神秘的环节，也就是那声枪响是如何击发的，我们还只字未提。凶手完全可能把武器捅进这件外套的口袋或者褶层里」——说到这里，他取出那件汽车外套，高高举起，亮出弹洞——「然后，他或者她（我不说是哪一位），就能在人不知鬼不觉中把枪击发。事后把武器取走藏匿，一件极其重要的证据便被湮灭了。这个人用的，可以是我手上这种装无烟火药的子弹，外套足能把射击的火光掩得严严实实，硝烟一丝也不会有。可是，别提这件外套，哪怕一床厚毯子，也闷不住那一声枪响。
